  use (pre,during,post): Baseline lifeline facility and utility-territory layers (cell towers to localise DIRS county percentages, electric retail service territories to assign each structure to Duke/co-op/municipal before applying outage shares, substations). Verify each layer's current URL; prefer NC OneMap/NCDOT equivalents; keep DataLumos archives as fallback.
  caveats: Several HIFLD Open layers were withdrawn or moved in 2024-2025; licence per layer varies; electric retail service territory layer status not confirmed.
### insights
- FCC DIRS Helene report of Oct 1, 2024 (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DOC-406055A1.pdf) shows how severe telecom loss is in the mountains: 48.69% of 1,452 NC cell sites in the 21-county activation area were out (Yancey 20/22 = 90.9%, Mitchell 18/20 = 90.0%, Henderson 116/158 = 73.4%, Transylvania 21/30 = 70.0%, Buncombe 215/348 = 61.8%); causes were overwhelmingly transport (303) and power (338) rather than physical damage (3). This supports treating 'out due to transport' as a corridor/fiber-cut indicator for road-isolation E8 and treating cell-site power loss as a proxy for grid outage where utility feeds are missing.
- DIRS reports also enumerate PSAP status by name: on Oct 1, 2024 Yancey County was 'rerouted without ALI' and Beech Mountain PD, Cherokee, Clay, EBCI Primary, Madison, Mitchell 911 and Swain were 'rerouted with ALI'. This is the only public, machine-parsable 911 status source; FCC NORS filings are confidential (https://www.fcc.gov/general/network-outage-reporting-system-nors).
- DIRS reports are PDFs posted per activation day on docs.fcc.gov; fcc.gov HTML pages blocked this fetch (HTTP 403), so NC Recon needs a PDF-table parser plus a watcher for new DOC-*.pdf links (or NCEM's ESF-2 liaison) rather than an API.
- ReadyNC states 'Power outage data from these utilities is automated and updates approximately every 30 minutes' (https://www.readync.gov/power-outages), meaning NCEM already operates a statewide outage aggregation across Duke, Dominion, 26 co-ops/municipals and ElectriCities. Requesting read access to that feed is the shortest path to complete county outage coverage; the only open machine feed today is Duke's ArcGIS service (20-minute, county polygons with customer_served/customer_out/percent_out/etr).
- The NC Electric Cooperatives outage map is a Leaflet app that calls undocumented same-origin endpoints (/outages/county/info, /outages/cooperative/info, /outages/details, counties.geojson, cooperatives.geojson) and refreshes every 15 minutes (https://www.ncelectriccooperatives.com/outages-safety); the site is 'All rights reserved' and bare GETs return Not Found, so any automated use requires NCEMC permission. Dominion's map is confirmed to run on KUBRA Storm Center (kubra.io product 5.54.1).
- EAGLE-I live access is restricted to DOE-approved accounts; NASEO/NARUC FAQ: 'State Energy Officials and Public Utility Commission staff can request an account' (https://awea.naseo.org/Data/Sites/1/documents/tk-news/naseo-naruc-gis-datafaq_v3.pdf) and DOE's State Energy Security Action List instructs states to 'Sign-up for EAGLE-I account' (https://www.energy.gov/sites/default/files/2023-05/StateEnergySecurityActionList_0.pdf). Design implication: NC Recon should not embed federal credentials; provide an operator-side connector/CSV import run inside NCEM's enclave, and treat EAGLE-I-derived layers as responder-only.
- EAGLE-I's public historical release (ORNL DOI 10.13139/ORNLNCCS/1975202; county-level 15-minute customers-out 2014-2022, figshare v3 to 2023) does not include Hurricane Helene (Sept 2024), so Helene nighttime-lights calibration must use Duke's live service snapshots or PowerOutage.us historical data (paid). ORNL caveat: 'the number of customers does not necessarily equate to the number of people affected'.
- DOE-417 reporting thresholds are 50,000 or more customers or 300 MW or more, with 1-hour/6-hour/72-hour filings; Schedule 1 is public and Schedule 2 confidential (https://doe417.pnnl.gov/files/DOE417_Form_Instructions.pdf). It is an event catalogue, not a live feed.
- NASA Black Marble: VIIRS DNB source is 750 m; VNP46A2 is a daily 15 arc-second (~500 m) gap-filled, lunar-BRDF-adjusted product in HDF5 (~40 MB/tile) requiring Earthdata Login; the project page states NRT products arrive 'within a three-hour window' (https://www.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/projects/black-marble; https://ladsweb.modaps.eosdis.nasa.gov/missions-and-measurements/products/VNP46A2). Tract-level radiance ratio vs. a monthly baseline is the practical outage-proxy design; it cannot resolve individual structures.
- Waze for Cities is 'a free, two-way data exchange' with 'nearly 1,000 partners' providing system-generated jams and user-reported incidents (jams, crashes, hazards, construction, potholes, roadkill, stopped vehicles, objects on road, missing signs) (https://web-assets.waze.com/partners/ccp/Waze-for-Cities-v01.pdf); NCDOT already pushes TIMS closures to Waze via a 'CIFS v2 data feed' and is in the two-way program (https://www.transportationops.org/case-studies/ncdot-waze-automated-road-closures), so NC Recon should piggyback on NCDOT's or NCEM's partner feed.
- DriveNC's developer API is throttled to 'Ten calls every 60 seconds' per key and requires an account (https://www.drivenc.gov/developers/doc); the TIMS traffic-prod v1 endpoints (https://eapps.ncdot.gov/services/traffic-prod/v1/...) expose county/road/event-scoped incidents and conditions (https://tims.ncdot.gov/tims/V2/webservices). Polling design must cache and fan out by county.
- NCDOT publishes event lifeline layers on its public ArcGIS Online org (NuWFvHYDMVmmxMeM): post-Helene structure inspection status with values 'Inspected - No Damage, Inspection Scheduled, Likely Repair, Likely Replace, No Inspection Scheduled' and Detour_Length; BridgeWatch alert layers (gauge/NEXRAD-driven scour fields, labelled DEV, last edited July 2023); a temporary-cell-tower planning map; Helene high-water marks; an airport runway Open/Closed layer (2018). A generic 'import an AGOL feature layer' capability would let analysts add these on day one.
- The quarterly NCDOT GIS data layers release schedule is May, August, November and February (https://connect.ncdot.gov/resources/gis/Pages/GIS-Data-Layers.aspx); NBI ASCII submissions ended with 2025 and move to SNBI, published by June 15 each year (https://www.fhwa.dot.gov/bridge/nbi/ascii.cfm).
- FEMA's ESF6-SS Open Shelters service (formerly NSS) is 'synchronized every morning with the American Red Cross shelter database' with 20-minute incremental checks and exposes shelter_status, evacuation_capacity, post_impact_capacity and total_population (https://gis.fema.gov/arcgis/rest/services/NSS/OpenShelters/MapServer/info/iteminfo). Pairing total_population with NC Recon's displaced-persons estimate gives an unmet-sheltering gap per county.
- HHS emPOWER's public REST service provides monthly county and ZIP counts of Medicare beneficiaries dependent on electricity-powered DME (over 3 million nationally) with no authentication (https://empowerprogram.hhs.gov/empower-rest.html); multiplying by county outage share is a direct, defensible need-index term for prolonged outages.
- There is currently no public facility-level hospital bed status: HHS's facility dataset stopped May 3, 2024 (https://healthdata.gov/api/views/anag-cw7u.json). NC's SMARTT tracks daily hospital/EMS beds and resources statewide but is restricted; hospital status in NC Recon must come via an OEMS/NCEM MOU and be responder-only.
- Water: EPA SDWIS (via https://data.epa.gov/dmapservice/) and NC OneMap water sources are inventory only; no public NC boil-water-advisory list or wastewater plant status feed was found (NC Drinking Water Watch returned 403; DEQ Helene pages 404). Water/wastewater status will need a DEQ PWS data-sharing arrangement or field reports.
- Fuel: GasBuddy's tracker is crowd-sourced and station-level with no API; EIA has no NC gasoline price series and no availability data (https://www.eia.gov/petroleum/gasdiesel/). Fuel availability stays a manual link, not an evidence channel.
- Airports: FAA's nasstatus.faa.gov airport-status-information XML needs no key and reports closures/ground stops by airport code; NOTAMs need a free api.faa.gov client_id/secret and the legacy API is being replaced by the NMS-API (request via notams@faa.gov).
- Security/governance implication across this dimension: the highest-value feeds (EAGLE-I, SMARTT, HIFLD Secure, Waze partner feed, NCEM aggregate) are restricted or agreement-bound, so NC Recon needs data-tier tagging per layer (public / partner-restricted / responder-only), per-tenant connector credentials stored outside the container, and the public tract summary must exclude restricted-derived layers.
- HIFLD Open's continuity is in doubt: the Data Rescue Project archived 'HIFLD OPEN Fire and EMS Stations' on Aug 26, 2025 and DHS's HIFLD page now describes only HIFLD Secure (GII account, Login.gov, DUA). Prefer NC OneMap (NC1Map_Health, NC1Map_Emergency_Services, NG911/NC911_Regions, NC1Map_Water_Sources) and NCDOT services for NC lifeline baselines.
### gaps
- FCC DIRS programme page and report index on fcc.gov returned HTTP 403; only a docs.fcc.gov report PDF was verified. Whether the 2025 FCC order making DIRS reporting mandatory changes report content/cadence was not verified (search budget exhausted).
- PowerOutage.us pricing (and any government/non-profit tier) is not published; poweroutage.us/products returned 403, details came from poweroutage.com/products.
- EAGLE-I: whether county EM offices (not only state energy officials/PUC) qualify, whether account holders get an API, and redistribution terms - eagle-i.doe.gov is a JS shell with no public docs.
- NC Electric Cooperatives outage map: calling convention and terms for the internal JSON endpoints; vendor not confirmed; the '26 co-ops, 93 counties' figure is from a search snippet, not the fetched page.
- Dominion Energy (KUBRA) and ElectriCities outage maps: data endpoint schemas, refresh intervals and member coverage not confirmed.
- NCEM's automated 30-minute statewide outage aggregation referenced on ReadyNC: no public endpoint or documentation located.
- NCDOT TIMS incidents ArcGIS feature layer (used by the GO!NC dashboard): REST URL not found (metadata PDF 404); TIMS web services page does not state rate limits, auth or licence.
- NCDOT BridgeWatch: meaning of the alert fields, whether a production (non-DEV) live service exists, and update cadence.
- NC boil-water advisories and water/wastewater system operational status: no public list or feed found (NC Drinking Water Watch 403; deq.nc.gov/helene and /hurricane-helene 404; pws.ncwater.org index minimal). No NC DEQ sanitary sewer overflow or WWTP status feed located.
- NC SMARTT: NC DHHS/OEMS pages returned 404; access policy and any export capability unverified (secondary sources only).
- Facility-level hospital bed/diversion status for NC after May 2024: no public source found; CDC NHSN facility-level respiratory data availability not checked (search budget exhausted).
- KCER current public site (esrdncc.org/en/kcer) rendered blank; dialysisunits.com open/closed status feature during emergencies not confirmed on its page.
- FAA NOTAM API: endpoint parameters, response format (AIXM/GeoJSON), rate limits and retirement date; FAA developer portal is a JS shell.
- NASA Disasters Mapping Portal REST root and any Black Marble HD / power-outage product for Hurricane Helene: maps.disasters.nasa.gov redirects to a JS hub; /arcgis/rest/services and /ags04/rest/services returned 404. VNP46A2-specific NRT latency not stated on the LAADS product page.
- Black Marble vs. EOG: EOG registration requirement for nightly mosaics not confirmed.
- HIFLD Open: current dataset list (DCAT feed HTTP 500; hub page JS shell; NASA NCCS mirror timed out); status of the Electric Retail Service Territories, Cellular Towers and Substations layers unknown.
- Waze for Cities feed format and 2-minute cadence not confirmed from a primary Waze page; redistribution/public-display restrictions unknown.
- School closures: no NC DPI or state data feed exists; only media lists (WRAL/ABC11) and ad hoc DPI tallies during Florence - not fetched as primary.
- Rail: no public Norfolk Southern/CSX/short-line service status feed; Amtrak alerts have no API.
- Mail: USPS has no API or RSS; regional free-text alerts only.
- 911: NC 911 Board has no public PSAP status page; FCC NORS is confidential; content of NC OneMap NG911 'NC911_Regions' not described on the service.
- OE-417 annual summary download page (oe.netl.doe.gov) was unreachable from this environment (IPv6 connection refused / DNS failure) so years/fields were not verified directly.
- Telecom site locations: no public per-site outage data exists (DIRS is county aggregate); FCC ASR/HIFLD cellular tower inventories not verified this session.
- Web search budget was exhausted mid-task (200/200), so several items (ReadyNC shelter sourcing, NC 911 GIS, ElectriCities map, NHSN hospital data, DEQ wastewater, DIRS index) rest on direct fetches of guessed URLs or secondary sources.

## Live hazard monitoring feeds for North Carolina (rivers, rain, wind, landslides, dams, fire, tornado/wind damage, earthquake) with their APIs
- (high/medium) **NC FIMAN gauge feed (NCEM ArcGIS REST: FIMAN/GAGES_ALL)** (NC Division of Emergency Management (NCDPS/NCEM); service copyright 'ESP Associates' (contractor)) https://spartagis.ncem.org/arcgis/rest/services/FIMAN/GAGES_ALL/MapServer/0
  Point layer of every FIMAN gauge (882 records on 2026-10-07) with live stage, elevation, flow, 1/6/12/24-h rain, FIMAN condition (Normal..Major Flooding), minor/moderate/major thresholds, trend, NWS forecast peak, NWM forecast peak, next inundation-library stage (NEXTFIM_*), coastal flag, and FIMAN-T road/bridge impact counts (Int_Miles, NC_Miles, AfBridgeCount, DOT_Monitor). Owners by count: USGS 346, NCEM 242, NCDOT 118, Town of Cary 37, Duke Energy 18, Haywood Co 16, SECOORA 16, New Hanover 17, NOAA 6, Lake Lure 4, Hohonu 2, TVA 2, others.
  access: ArcGIS MapServer query (JSON/GeoJSON/PBF), maxRecordCount 1000, supports statistics/pagination; fiman.nc.gov web app returns HTTP 403 to non-browser clients | auth: none observed (public REST); not documented as a supported API | licence: unstated (service description literally reads 'Test Gages NC FIMAN'; copyright field 'ESP Associates'); FIMAN fact sheet gives no reuse terms | cadence: gauge telemetry 15-30 min; REST layer refreshed continuously (observed timestamps within ~40 min of query) | NC: Statewide, 882 gauges incl. coastal sounds; readings every 15-30 min per fact sheet; LAST_UPDATED values current to the minute on 2026-10-07
  use (pre,during,post): Primary live river/rain layer for the Conditions map (adds ~300 non-USGS NCEM/NCDOT/county gauges that USGS and NWPS do not carry); trigger for pre-positioning assessments when CONDITION reaches Moderate/Major; NEXTFIM_STAGE and FIMAN-T road-miles fields seed road-isolation and flood-depth evidence; rain 24H field feeds landslide threshold alerts
  caveats: Undocumented service; description says 'Test'; no SLA; could be renamed or secured without notice. Confirm with NCEM (fimanhelp@ncdps.gov, 919-715-5711) before production use and ask for the production endpoint and terms.
- (high/medium) **NC FIMAN / FIMAN-T web application and inundation libraries** (NCEM (FIMAN) and NCDOT (FIMAN-T); administered by NC Floodplain Mapping Program) https://fiman.nc.gov/
  Flood Inundation Mapping and Alert Network: 700+ rain/stage gauges, real-time inundation maps in Current, Scenario and Forecast modes (FIMAN-T adds Historic), building/road/bridge impact tables, email and SMS alerts, system-wide reports. Inundation libraries are pre-computed extents per stage increment with impacted buildings, roads and bridges (and depth/damage in the web app).
  access: Web app (browser only; HTTP 403 to scripted fetch); KMZ export of inundation modes from the UI; email/SMS alert subscription; no public API or bulk download found | auth: account for alerts; none for viewing | licence: unstated | cadence: real time (15-30 min gauge reporting); forecast mode uses NWS forecasts | NC: Statewide gauges; inundation libraries at selected gauges and whole basins (Tar, Neuse etc.); NWPS metadata credits 'NC State Partner' for NWS FIM at TARN7, indicating NC libraries feed NWS FIM at some sites
  use (pre,during,post): Not machine-readable today; request an MOU/data share with NCEM for the inundation library depth grids (the missing water-surface source for NC Recon flood-depth evidence). Until then link out to fiman.nc.gov per gauge and use the GAGES_ALL REST layer.
  caveats: fiman.nc.gov blocks non-browser requests (403). Inundation-library depth/damage internals not accessible programmatically; FIMAN-T quick guide at fimant.nc.gov/Documents/FIMAN_T_Quick_Guide.pdf not fetched.
- (high/high) **USGS Water Data OGC API (api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi)** (US Geological Survey) https://api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi/v1/
  OGC API Features (pygeoapi) exposing monitoring-locations, continuous (15-min IV), latest-continuous, daily, latest-daily, field-measurements, peaks, time-series-metadata and code lookups. v0 and v1 both live; 338 NC gage-height (00065) latest-continuous series and 2,196 NC stream monitoring locations returned on 2026-10-07.
  access: REST/OGC API Features (GET, f=json/GeoJSON); OpenAPI at /ogcapi/v1/openapi | auth: none required; free api_key (query parameter) raises rate limit; signup at https://api.waterdata.usgs.gov/signup/ | licence: US Government work, public domain (CC0 per API landing page) | cadence: continuous data typically 15-min; latest-continuous near real time | NC: All USGS NC sites incl. rapid-deployment gauges; telemetry sites update 'within minutes of collection'
  use (pre,during,post): Already used for Conditions gauges; move to v1 and register a key; use latest-continuous for a one-call statewide refresh and peaks for post-event crest validation
  caveats: Rate-limit numbers are not published in the docs (OpenAPI only shows 429 over_rate_limit and 403 api_key_missing examples); secondary sources cite 100/h without key and 1000/h with key. numberMatched is null, so counts need full pulls.
- (low/high) **USGS NextGen WaterAlert** (US Geological Survey) https://accounts.waterdata.usgs.gov/wateralert/
  Subscription service that emails or texts when a USGS monitoring location crosses a user-set threshold (stage, flow, precipitation, etc.). Legacy WaterAlert decommissioned after 2022-09-30; 60,000+ users and 13.5 M alerts in 2025.
  access: web UI subscription only | auth: USGS account | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: near real time | NC: All USGS NC sites
  use (pre,during): Not an API; document in Help as the way an analyst gets personal gauge alerts. NC Recon should implement its own threshold alerts from the OGC API instead.
  caveats: No programmatic subscription; water.usgs.gov/wateralert redirects to the National Water Dashboard
- (high/high) **NOAA NWPS API (National Water Prediction Service)** (NOAA/NWS Office of Water Prediction) https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/docs/
  REST API for NWS river forecast locations: /gauges (bbox, srid filters), /gauges/{lid}, /gauges/{lid}/ratings, /gauges/{lid}/stageflow[/{product}], /gauges/{lid}/riverflow, /gauges/raag, /products/stageflow/{lid}/{pedts}, /reaches/{reachId}[/streamflow] (NWM). Gauge metadata includes flood categories, flood impact statements, datums, upstream/downstream LIDs and an 'inundation' block pointing to the NWS_FIM MapServer. 579 NC gauges returned (275 with forecast PEDTS). Swagger at /nwps/v1/docs/swagger.json.
  access: REST (OpenAPI 3 / Swagger 2 JSON) | auth: none in practice (spec declares BasicAuth definition but public GETs succeed) | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: observations as received (TARN7 obs issued 03:15Z on 2026-10-07); official forecasts typically 1-2x daily, more in floods; HEFS/NWM APIs experimental, may change without notice | NC: 579 gauges in NC, SERFC/RAH/GSP/MHX/ILM WFOs; forecasts at ~275
  use (pre,during,post): Already used; extend to pull flood impact statements and inundation.url per gauge, and NWM reach streamflow for ungauged towns
  caveats: No published rate limits or terms; no historical data beyond crests; HEFS and NWM APIs flagged experimental 'not supported 24/7'; per-gauge FIM download zips referenced in metadata (water.noaa.gov/resources/downloads/fim/tarn7/*.zip) return 404.
- (medium/high) **NWS River Gauges ArcGIS service (riv_gauges) and NWPS national shapefiles** (NOAA/NWS National Water Center) https://mapservices.weather.noaa.gov/eventdriven/rest/services/water/riv_gauges/MapServer
  Point service of ~4,000 NWS forecast locations with observed flood category and 24-336 h forecast stage layers; updated every 30 minutes. NWPS also publishes national observed/forecast shapefiles every 15 minutes (national_shapefile_obs.tgz).
  access: ArcGIS MapServer/FeatureServer; tgz shapefile download | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: 30 min (service), 15 min (shapefiles) | NC: All NC NWS forecast points
  use (during): Fallback/secondary channel to NWPS API for Conditions map; the shapefile is a cheap bulk pull for offline packs
  caveats: none noted
- (high/high) **NWS API alerts (api.weather.gov) incl. Flash Flood Warning polygons** (NOAA National Weather Service) https://api.weather.gov/alerts/active?area=NC
  GeoJSON FeatureCollection of CAP alerts. Storm-based warnings (Flash Flood, Tornado, Severe Thunderstorm, etc.) carry Polygon geometry; properties include event, severity, certainty, urgency, areaDesc, geocode (SAME/UGC), onset/expires/ends, parameters (VTEC, flashFloodDetection, AWIPSidentifier). Also /alerts (history), /alerts/active/zone/{id}, /points and /gridpoints (NDFD windGust, QPF).
  access: REST (GeoJSON, JSON-LD, CAP/ATOM) | auth: none; User-Agent with contact required | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: real time; cache-control max-age=5 s; undisclosed rate limit, retry after ~5 s | NC: All NC zones/counties; 0 active alerts at fetch time; FFW sample showed 20-vertex polygon
  use (pre,during): Already used for alerts; add FFW polygon ingestion as an evidence channel (flood extent prior) and as an assessment trigger; flashFloodDamageThreat/Considerable tags via IEM feed
  caveats: Rate limits not published; county/zone-based alerts have null geometry (join on UGC); grid coordinates can change so re-resolve /points periodically
- (medium/high) **NWS Watches/Warnings/Advisories ArcGIS FeatureServer (CurrentWarnings polygons)** (NOAA/NWS (CloudGIS, mapservices.weather.noaa.gov)) https://mapservices.weather.noaa.gov/eventdriven/rest/services/WWA/watch_warn_adv/FeatureServer/0
  Layer 0 'CurrentWarnings' (polygon, Tornado/Severe Thunderstorm/Flash Flood/Snow Squall/Special Marine warnings) and layer 1 'WatchesWarnings'; fields phenom, sig, prod_type, issuance, onset, ends, expiration, cap_id, wfo; maxRecordCount 4000; export to GeoJSON/shapefile/CSV. CloudGIS also exposes WMS/WFS/WCS.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer/MapServer; OGC WMS/WFS | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: event-driven (search result cites ~5-min WMS refresh; not confirmed on service page) | NC: National incl. NC
  use (during): Redundant polygon source if api.weather.gov is rate-limited; simplest layer for the Conditions map
  caveats: Update interval not stated in layer metadata
- (high/high) **Iowa Environmental Mesonet NWS warning archive and real-time GeoJSON (sbw.geojson, lsr.geojson)** (Iowa State University, Iowa Environmental Mesonet (third party)) https://mesonet.agron.iastate.edu/request/gis/watchwarn.phtml
  Archive of VTEC watches/warnings with polygons back to 2002-01-01 (county-based from 1986), downloadable as shapefile+CSV/KML/Excel, plus real-time GeoJSON endpoints: /geojson/sbw.geojson (active storm-based warnings with tags floodtag_damage, is_emergency, is_pds, windtag, hailtag) and /geojson/lsr.geojson?states=NC&hours=N (Local Storm Reports; fields type, magnitude, remark, city, county, lat, lon, valid).
  access: HTTP download, GeoJSON API, documented backend scripting | auth: none | licence: not stated; university service, data are NWS public domain | cadence: LSR real time every 5 min; archive zips nightly 02:00 Central; 1-year max per query | NC: All NC WFOs; LSRs since 2003
  use (during,post): Ground-truth channel: LSRs (flash flood, tornado, wind damage) and FFW damage tags for validation and for seeding the review queue; historical polygons for training/backtesting
  caveats: 'not complete or official'; no SLA; third party
- (medium/high) **NWS Local Storm Reports ArcGIS service (official)** (NOAA/NWS) https://mapservices.weather.noaa.gov/vector/rest/services/obs/nws_local_storm_reports/MapServer
  Point layers Last_24_Hours / Last_48_Hours / Last_72_Hours of LSRs from all WFOs, updated every 30 minutes.
  access: ArcGIS MapServer | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: 30 min; 72-h rolling window | NC: National incl. NC
  use (during,post): Official counterpart to IEM LSR feed for the ground-truth/field-report ingest
  caveats: Only 72 hours retained; use IEM or Storm Events for history
- (high/high) **NOAA MRMS on AWS Open Data (QPE, PrecipRate, FLASH)** (NOAA NSSL/NCEP via NODD; AWS Open Data bucket noaa-mrms-pds (us-east-1)) https://registry.opendata.aws/noaa-mrms-pds/
  1-km CONUS GRIB2 (gzip) products on a 2-minute cycle: RadarOnly_QPE_{15M,01H..72H,Since12Z}, MultiSensor_QPE_{01H..72H}_Pass1/Pass2, PrecipRate, FLASH_QPE_FFG{01H,03H,06H,MAX} (QPE-to-FFG ratio), FLASH_QPE_ARI{30M..24H,MAX}, FLASH_CREST/SAC MAXUNITSTREAMFLOW and MAXSOILSAT. Path: CONUS/{product}_00.00/{YYYYMMDD}/MRMS_{product}_00.00_{YYYYMMDD}-{HHMMSS}.grib2.gz. Archive begins 2020-10-14.
  access: S3 anonymous (aws s3 ls --no-sign-request s3://noaa-mrms-pds/ or HTTPS listing) | auth: none | licence: NOAA open data; attribution requested, derivatives may not be presented as original NOAA data | cadence: 2-min cycle; RadarOnly QPE ~every 2 min; MultiSensor Pass1 latency 20 min, Pass2 60 min (SCN v12.1) | NC: CONUS grid covers NC; latest Pass1 01H file at 03:00Z when queried 04:08Z, RadarOnly 01H at 04:06Z, FFG ratio at 04:02Z
  use (during,post): Rainfall evidence channel: 24/72-h QPE totals per structure/tract, QPE-to-FFG ratio and ARI as flash-flood triggers, and the NCGS 5 in/24 h landslide threshold; CREST unit streamflow for ungauged creeks
  caveats: GRIB2 decoding needs eccodes/cfgrib or pygrib; NSSL product-table page has a TLS certificate error; mountain radar beam blockage in WNC makes gauge-corrected Pass2 preferable
- (low/high) **NEXRAD Level II archive on AWS (unidata-nexrad-level2)** (NOAA/NWS via Unidata and AWS Open Data) https://registry.opendata.aws/noaa-nexrad/
  Real-time and archived Level II radar volumes; bucket moved to unidata-nexrad-level2 (old bucket and SNS topic deprecated 2025-09-01). Keys YYYY/MM/DD/{SITE}/{SITE}YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS_V06. NC-relevant sites KGSP, KRAX, KMHX, KLTX, KFCX, KMRX.
  access: S3 anonymous; SNS topic arn:aws:sns:us-east-1:684042711724:NewNEXRADLevel2Archive | auth: none | licence: NOAA open data | cadence: ~4-10 min per volume | NC: Full
  use (during,post): Low: MRMS already mosaics radar. Only needed for custom QPE or tornado-debris-signature work.
  caveats: Old bucket noaa-nexrad-level2 deprecated
- (medium/high) **NWS Radar Base Reflectivity ArcGIS service (MRMS-derived)** (NOAA/NWS CloudGIS) https://mapservices.weather.noaa.gov/eventdriven/rest/services/radar/radar_base_reflectivity/MapServer
  1x1 km quality-controlled CONUS base reflectivity mosaic image service, updated every 5 minutes; also a time-enabled variant radar_base_reflectivity_time.
  access: ArcGIS MapServer (export), WMS | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: 5 min | NC: CONUS
  use (during): Conditions map overlay only
  caveats: none
- (high/high) **WPC Excessive Rainfall Outlook (ERO) GIS feeds** (NOAA/NWS Weather Prediction Center) https://www.wpc.ncep.noaa.gov/qpf/excessive_rainfall_outlook_ero.php
  Day 1-5 probability that rainfall exceeds flash flood guidance within 40 km (Marginal >=5%, Slight, Moderate, High). Delivered as ArcGIS layers 0-4 of hazards/wpc_precip_hazards, GeoJSON Day{1..5}_Latest.geojson (properties OUTLOOK, ISSUE_TIME, START_TIME, END_TIME, VALID_TIME) and shapefiles EXCESSIVERAIN_Day{N}_latest.zip; QPF in precip/wpc_qpf.
  access: ArcGIS REST, GeoJSON, shapefile (HTTPS ftp.wpc.ncep.noaa.gov) | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: Day 1 issued several times daily (archive page JS: 01Z, 09Z, 15Z ... after May 2015; observed 2357Z issuance); Days 2-5 ~2x daily; full current schedule not confirmed | NC: CONUS; Day 1 issued 2026-10-06 23:57Z valid 01-12Z
  use (pre): Pre-event trigger: Moderate/High ERO over NC counties starts pre-event imagery baseline capture and alerts analysts; feeds the NCGS landslide 'areas of concern' logic
  caveats: Issuance schedule only partially confirmed; GeoJSON path is under /exper/ (experimental)
- (high/high) **NHC GIS products (cone, track, wind radii, wind speed probabilities, P-Surge, inundation, arrival time)** (NOAA National Hurricane Center) https://www.nhc.noaa.gov/gis/
  Per-advisory shapefile/KMZ for forecast track+cone+watches/warnings, wind field and forecast radii, preliminary best track, arrival time of TS winds (KMZ/GRIB2), wind speed probabilities (shp/KML/GRIB), probabilistic storm surge and Potential Storm Surge Flooding (inundation) when US watches/warnings are up, storm surge watch/warning, peak surge, breakpoints. RSS feed gis-at.xml lists live file URLs (TD Nine active at fetch). Archive al{NN}{YYYY}_5day_{ADV}.zip from 2008. ArcGIS tropical/NHC_tropical_weather service has 15 layers updated 6-hourly (3-hourly near landfall).
  access: HTTP download, RSS, ArcGIS MapServer; also nowCOAST WMS | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain ('provided as a convenience') | cadence: per advisory (6 h, 3 h near land); surge products within ~30 min of advisory (observed 02:57Z vs 03:33Z) | NC: Atlantic basin incl. NC coast; surge inundation products cover NC when watches/warnings issued
  use (pre,during): Already shows storms; add wind swath (best wind radii) as wind-damage evidence prior, surge inundation as coastal flood-depth prior, and cone intersection as pre-event trigger
  caveats: Archive directory page is >10 MB; post-storm H*Wind analyses are commercial (not NHC)
- (medium/medium) **nowCOAST (GeoServer WMS/WFS)** (NOAA National Ocean Service) https://nowcoast.noaa.gov/geoserver/ows?service=WMS&request=GetCapabilities
  Modernized nowCOAST on GeoServer with 73 named layers: alerts:watches_warnings_advisories, weather_radar:conus_base_reflectivity_mosaic, tropical_cyclones (cone, track, wind extent, watches/warnings), tropical_storm_surge:inundation and tidalmask (P-Surge), lightning_detection:ldn_lightning_strike_density, STOFS water disturbance, satellite imagery. The legacy nowcoast.noaa.gov/arcgis/rest directory no longer returns JSON.
  access: OGC WMS/WFS (GeoServer) | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: layer dependent; not confirmed (help page is JS-rendered) | NC: National
  use (pre,during): One WMS endpoint for several Conditions overlays (lightning density is the only lightning source found without a license fee)
  caveats: ArcGIS REST endpoints retired; update cadence and terms not fetched
- (medium/high) **NC State Climate Office CLOUDS API (ECONet, CoCoRaHS, COOP, RAWS, AWOS, USGS)** (NC State Climate Office (NCSU)) https://api.climate.ncsu.edu/
  CLOUDS (CLimate Office Unified Data System) API at https://api.climate.ncsu.edu/data.php with hash, loc, var, start, end, int, obtype (D/H/O minute/S), output (csv, json, geojson, xls, html). 16 networks: ECONet 49 stations (46 active; 1-min data since mid-2000s; rain, wind, soil moisture, temp), CoCoRaHS 2,784 (714 active), COOP 579, RAWS 82, AWOS 83, USGS 247, ECOExt 4. Replaces CRONOS.
  access: REST (GET) with user hash; query builder | auth: free NCSCO account + API hash (self-signup accounts.climate.ncsu.edu); tiers Public 300k points/month and 25k/request, Government/Education 750k and 35k, NCSU/NC A&T 1M and 40k | licence: Restricted: no commercial display/distribution; API hash and raw data may not be redistributed to third parties; attribution required ('data provided by the North Carolina State Climate Office' + link) | cadence: ECONet 1-min/hourly; CoCoRaHS daily; monthly point quotas reset each calendar month | NC: Statewide; near-real-time (start=-10 minutes&end=now supported)
  use (pre,during,post): Rain/wind/soil-moisture evidence and landslide-threshold inputs (ECONet soil moisture; CoCoRaHS 24-h totals for QPE validation). Government tier fits NCEM. Raw-data redistribution ban means NC Recon may display and derive but must not export raw CLOUDS records in public products.
  caveats: Non-commercial and no-redistribution terms; monthly quota; account per user (hash not shareable)
- (medium/high) **NOAA CO-OPS Tides and Currents Data API (coastal water level)** (NOAA National Ocean Service CO-OPS) https://api.tidesandcurrents.noaa.gov/api/prod/
  datagetter endpoint for 6-min water_level (preliminary/verified), predictions, wind, air_pressure, etc. with datum choice (MLLW, NAVD, MSL). Six NC water-level stations: 8651370 Duck, 8652587 Oregon Inlet Marina, 8654467 USCG Station Hatteras, 8656483 Beaufort, 8658120 Wilmington, 8658163 Wrightsville Beach.
  access: REST (json/csv/xml); metadata API mdapi | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: 6-min; 1-min data limited to 4 days per request, 6-min to 1 month, hourly to 1 year | NC: 6 NC tide gauges (sparse; FIMAN/SECOORA/Hohonu fill sounds)
  use (pre,during,post): Storm-tide stage for coastal towns and NAVD88 water-surface elevation for coastal flood depth; pair with NHC surge inundation
  caveats: Only 6 NC stations
- (high/high) **NC DEQ Dam Safety inventory (ArcGIS Online FeatureServer dam_inv_20201012)** (NC DEQ Division of Energy, Mineral and Land Resources, Dam Safety Program) https://services2.arcgis.com/kCu40SDxsCGcuUWO/arcgis/rest/services/dam_inv_20201012/FeatureServer/0
  7,543 dam records from DEQ's IBEAM database: STATE_ID, NID_ID, hazard potential (DAM_HAZARD_POTENTIAL_DESCRIPTI), EAP and EAP_DATE, CONDITION_ASSESSMENT, LAST/NEXT_INSPECTION_DATE, NOD/DSO enforcement dates, heights, storage, spillways, owner. Underlies the public web app 'North Carolina Dam Safety' (item 9ac80a9a...). Companion Lowhead Dams layer (175). DEQ page says the program oversees 3,000+ dams; Helene-affected count 41 state-regulated dams failed or significantly damaged (press, citing DEQ).
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer (query JSON/GeoJSON); web app | auth: none | licence: DEQ disclaimer only (no liability, not a legal document); reuse terms unstated | cadence: static inventory, refreshed irregularly (last edit 2026-09-18) | NC: Statewide; data last edited 2026-09-18 (editingInfo); service name suggests 2020 snapshot schema but data are maintained
  use (pre,during,post): Pre: map high-hazard dams with EAP status upstream of each town; during: cross-reference NWS 'dam failure' FFW text and FIMAN gauges below dams; post: prioritise inspection of structures in dam-break inundation paths
  caveats: No real-time dam monitoring or alerts exist; EAP documents and inundation maps are not public; count discrepancy (7,543 rows vs '3,000+ regulated' vs press '7,000'/'27,000' dams) needs DEQ clarification
- (medium/medium) **USACE National Inventory of Dams (NID) API** (US Army Corps of Engineers) https://nid.sec.usace.army.mil/api/query?sy=@stateKey:NC
  Public JSON API: /api/query?sy=@stateKey:NC (3,687 NC dams) with addProps (hazardId/publicHazardId, eapId, conditionAssessId, damHeight, county, nidStorage ...), /api/dams/{nidId}/inventory (90+ fields incl. inspection, EAP revision/exercise dates, max discharge, storage), /api/query?nr={lon},{lat},{radius_mi}, and /api/nation/csv (67 MB national CSV, downloaded without login). NC breakdown: publicHazardId 4=1,678, 2=1,349, 3=660; eapId 2=2,409, 1=1,252, 3=26.
  access: REST JSON; CSV download; developer docs at /api/developer/static/index.html (Swagger SPA) | auth: none observed for query/inventory/nation CSV | licence: US Government; redistribution terms unstated (a secondary source claims non-gov users can no longer download, but /api/nation/csv returned 200) | cadence: annual-ish inventory updates (dataUpdated field) | NC: 3,687 NC dams (vs 7,543 in DEQ layer: NID omits small/exempt dams)
  use (pre,post): Pre: national schema for hazard/EAP/condition joins and nearby-dam lookups (nr radius query); post: dam attributes in briefs
  caveats: Code lookups (hazard/EAP/condition id meanings) endpoint not found (/api/lookups 404); Swagger page is JS-only; /api/nation/gpkg redirects
- (high/high) **NC Geological Survey Landslide Inventory and hazard models** (NC DEQ / North Carolina Geological Survey) https://services2.arcgis.com/kCu40SDxsCGcuUWO/arcgis/rest/services/NC_Landslide_Inventory/FeatureServer
  Live AGOL FeatureServer (item 10209ea1...): layer 0 Observations 7,070 points (Impact, Damage, AssociatedStorm, Fatalities), layer 1 Ground Ruptures 156, layer 2 Landslide Features 28,485; being edited (lastEditDate 2026-10-06). DEQ ArcGIS Server models: DEMLR/North_Carolina_Probability_of_Sliding_Model and DEMLR/North_Carolina_Channelized_Debris_Flow_Model ('susceptible during 5 inches or more of rain in 24 hours'; many false positives). NCGS responded to 496 Helene slides and is building forecast-based 'areas of concern' alerts using WPC rainfall forecasts and a 5 in/24 h threshold with 9 rain/soil-moisture stations (3 staff, $3M 2025).
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer/MapServer (JSON/GeoJSON); NCGS products 'public information, may be distributed or copied' | auth: none | licence: public per NCGS metadata; DEQ disclaimer | cadence: inventory edited continuously; models static; real-time alert system under development, not operational | NC: Western NC counties (19-county 2005 mandate expanding post-Helene)
  use (pre,during,post): Pre: susceptibility and debris-flow path layers to weight structures; during: compute 24-h MRMS/ECONet rain vs 3/5/10-inch thresholds per the NCGS/USGS findings as a landslide trigger; post: inventory points as ground truth for slide-destroyed structures
  caveats: Legacy maps.deq.nc.gov DEMLR/North_Carolina_Landslide_Inventory and NCGS_Landslide_Read_Vector MapServers return 404 (dead); AGOL schema is 'latest' and older features are still being migrated
- (medium/high) **USGS Hurricane Helene landslide inventory, hazard estimates and US Landslide Inventory** (US Geological Survey Landslide Hazards Program (LASER)) https://doi.org/10.5066/P14CHGKS
  2,217 Helene-triggered landslide points (Source, Impact attributes) as shapefile/GeoJSON/CSV (CC0) and an AGOL FeatureServer USGS_Hurricane_Helene_Landslide_Inventory (2,217 features); dashboard; preliminary hazard estimate maps (doi 10.5066/P134ERB9); aerial photos; OFR 2025-1028 (30.8 in 72-h max rain, saturated antecedent soils, heightened susceptibility up to 25 years). US Landslide Inventory ~613,724 records (v3, Feb 2025).
  access: ScienceBase download, ArcGIS FeatureServer, dashboard | auth: none | licence: CC0 / US Government public domain | cadence: static (event release Jan 2025) | NC: WNC plus TN/VA/GA/SC AOI
  use (post,pre): Ground truth and training labels for landslide-destroyed structures; hazard estimate map as pre-event susceptibility layer
  caveats: No USGS real-time landslide monitoring stations in NC were found; LASER response ended Oct 2024
- (medium/medium) **NIFC WFIGS wildland fire locations and perimeters** (National Interagency Fire Center (WFIGS group, IRWIN-sourced)) https://services3.arcgis.com/T4QMspbfLg3qTGWY/arcgis/rest/services/WFIGS_Incident_Locations_Current/FeatureServer/0
  Authoritative interagency feeds: WFIGS_Incident_Locations_Current / _Last24h / _YearToDate (points, POOState='US-NC' filter) and WFIGS_Interagency_Perimeters_Current / _YearToDate (polygons, 119 fields). Includes incidents categorized WF or RX in IRWIN that are not yet contained/controlled/out.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer (maxRecordCount 2000) and data-nifc.opendata.arcgis.com downloads | auth: none | licence: NIFC disclaimer ('shall not be held liable...'); attribution to IRWIN/USDA FS/DOI agencies/NASF | cadence: not confirmed (service metadata empty); IRWIN-driven near real time | NC: National; 0 current NC incidents at fetch
  use (pre,during,post): Wildfire incident trigger and perimeter layer; perimeters define the AOI for burned-structure assessment (xView2 covers wildfire)
  caveats: NC state-only fires reported via NCFS may not all enter IRWIN; update frequency unconfirmed
- (medium/high) **NASA FIRMS active fire API (VIIRS/MODIS/Landsat)** (NASA LANCE/FIRMS) https://firms.modaps.eosdis.nasa.gov/api/area/
  /api/area/csv/{MAP_KEY}/{SOURCE}/{west,south,east,north}/{1-5 days}[/{YYYY-MM-DD}] with sources VIIRS_SNPP_NRT, VIIRS_NOAA20_NRT, VIIRS_NOAA21_NRT, MODIS_NRT, LANDSAT_NRT (US/Canada), plus SP (standard) variants; data_availability and KML footprints endpoints. VIIRS 375 m pixels.
  access: REST CSV (also WMS/KML in FIRMS) | auth: free MAP_KEY by email; 5,000 transactions per 10 minutes | licence: NASA open data (explicit licence text not on fetched pages) | cadence: URT <60 s after overpass for much of US; NRT up to 60 min; 2-4 overpasses/day | NC: Global; NC bbox -84.4,33.8,-75.4,36.6
  use (during): Thermal-anomaly trigger and burn footprint evidence for wildfire assessments
  caveats: country endpoints 'currently not available' notice; cloud and small/cool fires missed
- (low/medium) **NC Forest Service wildfire situation report and Wildfire Public Viewer** (NC Department of Agriculture, NC Forest Service) https://www.ncagr.gov/divisions/nc-forest-service/fire-control-and-prevention/sit-report
  Daily (10 a.m.) statewide fire-control activity summary from the 'Signal 14' database (fires and acres by district; approximate, excludes federal lands) as PDF; North Carolina Wildfire Public Viewer at ncfspublic.firesponse.com shows reported/active wildfires (controlled/out not shown); burn-ban status.
  access: web page/PDF; Nuxt SPA map viewer; no public API or ArcGIS endpoint discovered in page or JS bundles | auth: none | licence: unstated | cadence: daily report; viewer near real time (unconfirmed) | NC: Statewide state-protected lands
  use (during): Link-out only until NCFS provides a feed; ask NCFS/firesponse for an export or rely on WFIGS/FIRMS
  caveats: No machine-readable feed found; firesponse.com is a vendor platform
- (high/high) **NWS Damage Assessment Toolkit (DAT) damage survey data** (NOAA National Weather Service) https://services.dat.noaa.gov/arcgis/rest/services/nws_damageassessmenttoolkit/DamageViewer/FeatureServer
  Layer 0 Damage Points (structure-level survey points: damage/damage_txt = Damage Indicator, dod/dod_txt = Degree of Damage, efscale incl. EF0-EF5/TSTM/Tropical, windspeed, damage_dir, injuries, deaths, comments, image attachments, surveydate, office, qc), layer 1 Damage Lines (tornado tracks with length, width, start/end time), layer 2 Damage Polygons. 1,208 points in the NC bbox since 2024-01-01 and 98 in the Helene window (26-30 Sep 2024).
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer/MapServer query (JSON, GeoJSON, PBF), Generate KML; maxRecordCount 2000 | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain; 'quality controlled but still considered preliminary' | cadence: populated during/after NWS surveys (days); preliminary until Storm Data publication | NC: All NWS survey events in NC
  use (post): Highest-value structure-level ground truth for wind/tornado: map DI/DoD to FEMA damage levels to validate the CNN; polygons define tornado AOIs
  caveats: Preliminary; coverage limited to events NWS surveys (mostly tornado/straight-line wind; little flood)
- (medium/high) **NOAA Storm Prediction Center outlooks, watches and storm reports** (NOAA/NWS Storm Prediction Center) https://www.spc.noaa.gov/gis/
  Day 1-8 convective outlooks (categorical, tornado, wind, hail) as GeoJSON (e.g. day1otlk_cat.nolyr.geojson), KMZ and shapefile; fire weather outlooks; ActiveWW.kmz watches; ActiveMD.kmz mesoscale discussions; preliminary storm reports CSV (today.csv, today_torn/wind/hail.csv, yymmdd_rpts.csv: Time, F_Scale/Speed/Size, Location, County, State, Lat, Lon, Comments) back to 1999-06-01. ArcGIS: outlooks/SPC_wx_outlks and outlooks/spc_mesoscale_discussion. Day 1 issued 0600Z, 1300Z, 1630Z, 2000Z, 0100Z.
  access: HTTP download (GeoJSON/KMZ/shp/CSV), ArcGIS MapServer | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: outlooks 5x daily (Day 1); reports continuous, preliminary | NC: CONUS
  use (pre,during,post): Pre: Enhanced/Moderate risk over NC as tornado/wind pre-event trigger; post: preliminary tornado/wind reports as point ground truth before DAT surveys land
  caveats: Reports preliminary and duplicate-prone
- (medium/medium) **NCEI Storm Events Database and Severe Weather Data Inventory (SWDI) web service** (NOAA National Centers for Environmental Information) https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/swdi/stormevents/csvfiles/
  Bulk gzipped CSV per year: StormEvents_{details,fatalities,locations}-ftp_v1.0_d{YYYY}_c{YYYYMMDD}.csv.gz with event_type (48 NWS Directive 10-1605 types), begin/end lat/lon, damage_property, damage_crops, injuries/deaths direct/indirect, episode narrative. 2026 file last modified 2026-09-18, 2025 on 2026-08-19. SWDI web service https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/swdiws/{json|csv|xml|shp|kmz}/{dataset}/{dateRange} with datasets warn (polygon warnings), nx3tvs, nx3meso, nx3hail, nx3structure, plsr; 1-year max range, up to 10,000,000 results.
  access: HTTPS bulk download; SWDI REST | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: monthly-ish file refresh; publication lag of weeks to months (exact lag not confirmed; pages are JS-rendered) | NC: All NC counties/zones; tornadoes from 1950, all events from 1996
  use (post,pre): Official damage/injury statistics and event locations for backtesting and for the brief's historical context; SWDI 'warn' for archived FFW polygons
  caveats: Not real time; damage values are text like '10.00K'; www.ncdc.noaa.gov URLs 301 to ncei.noaa.gov
- (low/high) **USGS Earthquake Hazards FDSN event service and GeoJSON feeds** (US Geological Survey) https://earthquake.usgs.gov/fdsnws/event/1/
  query/count endpoints (geojson, csv, kml, quakeml; bbox/radius/time/magnitude filters; limit 20,000) and summary feeds /earthquakes/feed/v1.0/summary/{all|1.0|2.5|4.5|significant}_{hour|day|week|month}.geojson updated every minute. NC bbox count last 30 days: 6 events.
  access: REST | auth: none | licence: US Government, public domain | cadence: 1 min | NC: Global; NC seismicity low (Sparta M5.1 2020)
  use (during,post): Low-priority trigger (M>=4.5 within NC) and ShakeMap/PAGER link in briefs
  caveats: none
- (high/high) **NWS National Water Center HydroVIS: NWM inundation, high-flow, rapid-onset flooding, RFC-based inundation and stage-based CatFIM** (NOAA/NWS National Water Center (Office of Water Prediction)) https://maps.water.noaa.gov/server/rest/services/nwm?f=pjson
  Experimental ArcGIS services: nwm/ana_inundation_extent (hourly NWM analysis inundation + high flow magnitude + AEP, covers area with 30% of US population), srf_18hr_max_high_flow_magnitude, srf_18hr_rapid_onset_flooding, srf_12hr_rapid_onset_flooding_probability, mrf_gfs/nbm 5- and 10-day high flow, rfc/rfc_based_5day_max_inundation_extent (official RFC forecast routed through NWM), fim_libs/static_stage_based_catfim (HAND inundation for every NWS flood category and 1-ft intervals up to 5 ft above major, 60% population area, 5 miles up/downstream of gauges). Static per-gauge NWS FIM MapServers at mapservices.weather.noaa.gov/static/rest/services/NWS_FIM/FIM_{lid} with depth_grids: 15 NC sites (chin7, clyn7, efdn7, gldn7, hawn7, hokn7, kinn7, loun7, mann7, pgvn7, smfn7, tarn7, tksn7, trvn7, wlln7).
  access: ArcGIS MapServer/FeatureServer (most public; flash folder and srf_18hr_max_inundation_extent return 'Token Required') | auth: none for public services; token for partner-only services | licence: US Government, public domain; marked EXPERIMENTAL | cadence: ana hourly; srf/mrf per NWM cycle; CatFIM static | NC: NC partially inside the 30%/60% population FIM domains (reference/static_public_fim_domain service gives boundary); 15 static FIM sites, mostly eastern NC rivers
  use (pre,during,post): Best available water-surface/flood-depth source for NC Recon's empty flood-depth channel: sample CatFIM or ana_inundation_extent depth at structures during/after the crest; RFC 5-day max inundation as the pre-event impact estimate
  caveats: Experimental, 'not for legal purposes'; HAND extents are approximate, coastal processes not captured; WNC coverage uncertain (check domain layer); some services token-gated
- (medium/medium) **NCEM ReadyNC and FRIS public services (evacuation orders, Disaster Recovery Centers, effective flood zones)** (NC Emergency Management (spartagis.ncem.org)) https://spartagis.ncem.org/arcgis/rest/services/Public?f=pjson
  Public MapServers: ReadyNC_PROD_Mandatory_Evacuations_180 (zones/counties under mandatory or voluntary evacuation, Know Your Zone), ReadyNC_CountyImpact_StateOfEmergency, ReadyNC_DRC (WebEOC-sourced Disaster Recovery Centers), ReadyNC_EvacuationRoutesZones, HurricaneEvacStudyZones, FRIS_FloodZones (effective flood hazard areas, lines, stream centerlines), FRIS_FloodZones_Prelim, FRIS_HydraModel, PrelimStatus.
  access: ArcGIS MapServer | auth: none | licence: copyright NCEM; terms unstated | cadence: event-driven (WebEOC) | NC: Statewide
  use (pre,during,post): Live state-of-emergency and evacuation layers for the Conditions map and brief; DRC locations in public summary; FRIS zones as pre-event flood exposure prior
  caveats: Undocumented; confirm with NCEM GIS before relying on it
- (low/high) **Legacy NWS AHPS (water.weather.gov) - DECOMMISSIONED** (NOAA/NWS) https://water.weather.gov/ahps/
  Former Advanced Hydrologic Prediction Service site and XML/shapefile feeds. Hostname water.weather.gov now returns NXDOMAIN; replaced by NWPS (water.noaa.gov) and the NWPS API.
  access: none (dead) | auth: n/a | licence: n/a | cadence: n/a | NC: n/a
  use (during): Do not reference AHPS URLs in docs or code; all hydrograph links should use water.noaa.gov/gauges/{lid}
  caveats: DNS lookup failed (NXDOMAIN) on 2026-10-07
### insights
- FIMAN is operated by NCEM (NC Floodplain Mapping Program, fimanhelp@ncdps.gov) with 700+ gauges per the fact sheet (https://secoora.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/06/FACT_SHEET_FIMAN_and_FIMAN-T.pdf); the live ArcGIS layer actually holds 882 gauge records, of which only 346 are USGS; NCEM owns 242, NCDOT 118, Town of Cary 37, Duke Energy 18, Haywood County 16, SECOORA 16 (https://spartagis.ncem.org/arcgis/rest/services/FIMAN/GAGES_ALL/MapServer/0). That is roughly 500 gauges that neither the USGS OGC API nor NWPS provides.
- FIMAN inundation libraries are not exposed through any discovered API; the web app refuses scripted access (HTTP 403). The GAGES_ALL layer does carry NEXTFIM_ID/NEXTFIM_STAGE (next library stage) and FIMAN-T road/bridge impact counts, and the fact sheet confirms Current/Scenario/Forecast(/Historic) modes export as KMZ. NWPS gauge metadata credits an 'NC State Partner' for the NWS FIM at Tar River at Tarboro (TARN7), so NC libraries already flow to NWS for some sites (https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/TARN7).
- NWPS API: 579 NC gauges, 275 with a forecast PEDTS; swagger at https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/docs/swagger.json lists 11 paths; no rate limits or terms are documented anywhere official; the per-gauge FIM download URLs returned in gauge metadata (water.noaa.gov/resources/downloads/fim/{lid}/...) are 404.
- USGS OGC API v1 is live (https://api.waterdata.usgs.gov/ogcapi/v1/collections) with 38 collections; NC has 2,196 stream monitoring locations and 338 live gage-height series in latest-continuous. The OpenAPI only documents the 429 'over_rate_limit' response and the api_key parameter; actual request quotas are not published (secondary sources: 100/h anonymous, 1,000/h keyed).
- MRMS latency confirmed from the NCO SCN v12.1 PDF: MultiSensor QPE Pass1 latency 20 minutes (was 60) and Pass2 60 minutes (was 120); RadarOnly QPE files land every 2 minutes; the AWS archive starts 2020-10-14 and includes FLASH QPE-to-FFG ratio, ARI and CREST unit-streamflow grids (https://www.nco.ncep.noaa.gov/pmb/changes/MRMS_SCN_v12.1_Upgrade_Supplemental.pdf).
- Landslide thresholds for WNC from NCGS/USGS Helene analysis: 3 in/24 h for modified slopes, 5 in/24 h for forested slopes, 10 in/24 h for widespread sliding; 88% of Helene slides occurred where >=10 in fell and none below 5 in. NCGS is building a forecast-based 'areas of concern' product using WPC QPF and a 5 in/24 h trigger with 9 rain/soil-moisture stations and 3 staff; it is not yet operational (https://www.wfae.org/2026-01-09/helene-landslide-hazard-mapping-program-emergencies). NCGS debris-flow model metadata states the same 5 in/24 h trigger (https://maps.deq.nc.gov/arcgis/rest/services/DEMLR/North_Carolina_Channelized_Debris_Flow_Model/MapServer).
- USGS mapped 2,217 Helene landslides (1,064 flagged as impacting rivers, roads or structures); NCGS responded to 496, more than the 358 it handled 1990-2023 (https://pubs.usgs.gov/publication/ofr20251028/full; https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/item/674634a1d34e6d1dac3abddc). The NCGS AGOL inventory now has 7,070 observations and 28,485 landslide features and was edited the day of this research.
- Dams: NC DEQ's dam inventory layer has 7,543 rows with EAP, hazard and condition fields and was last edited 2026-09-18; USACE NID lists 3,687 NC dams (publicHazardId 4: 1,678; 2: 1,349; 3: 660). 41 state-regulated dams failed or were significantly damaged in Helene; Lake Lure overtopped and NWS GSP issued a flash flood emergency for imminent dam failure that did not occur (https://www.wfae.org/energy-environment/2025-06-02/not-a-silver-bullet-nc-dams-problematic-during-helene-flooding-future-role-unclear). The 2026 budget gives DEQ $20M for Helene dam repair/removal. No real-time dam monitoring feed exists; EAPs are not public; dam-failure alerts arrive only as NWS FFW text ('dam failure') and county IPAWS/alerts.
- NC State Climate Office CLOUDS API terms prohibit commercial display/distribution and redistribution of raw data to third parties, and quota is 300k-1M data points per month by tier (https://api.climate.ncsu.edu/usage); NC Recon can use it internally under the Government tier but must not re-export raw ECONet/CoCoRaHS records in public products.
- NWS DAT publishes structure-level Damage Indicator and Degree of Damage points (EF-scale DI/DoD), tornado track lines and damage polygons via a public FeatureServer; 1,208 points fall in the NC bounding box since 2024-01-01 (https://services.dat.noaa.gov/arcgis/rest/services/nws_damageassessmenttoolkit/DamageViewer/FeatureServer/0). This is the only official structure-level wind damage ground truth found.
- NWC HydroVIS gives NC Recon a flood-depth source it lacks: nwm/ana_inundation_extent (hourly, experimental, domain with 30% of US population), rfc_based_5day_max_inundation_extent, and fim_libs/static_stage_based_catfim (HAND, every flood category and 1-ft steps, 60% population domain); 15 static NWS FIM depth-grid services exist for NC gauges (https://mapservices.weather.noaa.gov/static/rest/services/NWS_FIM?f=pjson). Some services (flash folder, srf_18hr_max_inundation_extent) require a token.
- NWS CloudGIS (mapservices.weather.noaa.gov) folders enumerated: eventdriven {radar, water/riv_gauges, WWA}, vector {aviation, fire_weather, hazards/wpc_precip_hazards, obs/nws_local_storm_reports, obs/NWM_Stream_Analysis, outlooks/SPC_wx_outlks, precip/wpc_qpf}, static {NWS_FIM, nws_reference_maps}, tropical {NHC_tropical_weather}. River gauges update every 30 min, radar every 5 min, LSRs every 30 min, NHC every 6 h (3 h near landfall).
- Legacy AHPS host water.weather.gov no longer resolves (NXDOMAIN); the old nowCOAST ArcGIS REST directory is gone and nowCOAST now serves 73 GeoServer WMS/WFS layers; the NEXRAD Level II bucket moved to unidata-nexrad-level2 on 2025-09-01; legacy USGS WaterAlert was retired 2022-09-30 (NextGen has no API).
- Already used by NC Recon: USGS OGC API (recommend v1 + key), NWPS API, NWS API alerts, NHC storms. New in this dimension: FIMAN GAGES_ALL, MRMS QPE/FLASH, WPC ERO, NWS WWA FeatureServer, IEM real-time LSR/SBW GeoJSON, NWS LSR service, HydroVIS/CatFIM inundation, NC DEQ dam inventory + NID API, NCGS landslide inventory and models, USGS Helene inventory, WFIGS, FIRMS, SPC outlooks/reports, DAT, Storm Events/SWDI, CO-OPS, nowCOAST, NC SCO CLOUDS, ReadyNC evacuation/DRC layers.
### gaps
- FIMAN: no documented public API, data export or licence; fiman.nc.gov returns 403 to non-browser clients; inundation library depth/damage tables are not exposed; the GAGES_ALL service description reads 'Test Gages NC FIMAN' and its production status/terms are unknown. FIMAN-T quick guide (fimant.nc.gov) not fetched.
- NWPS API: no published rate limits, SLA or terms of use anywhere official; swagger declares BasicAuth but its purpose is unknown; per-gauge FIM download zips in gauge metadata are 404.
- USGS OGC API: numeric rate limits with/without key are not in the official docs or OpenAPI (only secondary sources give 100/1000 per hour).
- MRMS: the NSSL operational product table (https://www.nssl.noaa.gov/projects/mrms/operational/tables.php) could not be fetched (TLS certificate error) so product definitions were taken from the SCN PDF and bucket listing.
- WPC ERO: full current issuance schedule (UTC) not confirmed from an official page; only an archive-page code comment (01Z/09Z/15Z Day 1) and the observed 2357Z issuance were found. ERO GeoJSON lives under an /exper/ path.
- NC DEQ dams: no official DEQ statement of Helene dam failure counts was fetched (41 figure is from WFAE/Carolina Public Press citing DEQ); EAP public-records status, inundation maps and whether any NC dam has telemetry were not found; the count discrepancy between DEQ layer (7,543), DEQ page ('3,000+' regulated) and NID (3,687) is unexplained; NID hazard/EAP/condition code lookups (id meanings) endpoint not found.
- Landslides: no operational real-time landslide warning feed exists for WNC (NCGS system under development); no USGS landslide monitoring stations in NC were found; no NCGS rainfall-threshold API.
- Wildfire: NCFS Wildfire Public Viewer (ncfspublic.firesponse.com) exposes no discoverable API; NIFC WFIGS update frequency not stated in service metadata; FIRMS licence text not on fetched pages.
- Storm Events Database: typical publication lag (commonly cited 75-120 days) could not be confirmed because the NCEI pages are JavaScript-rendered; FAQ not readable.
- nowCOAST: help/update-cadence page is JS-rendered; per-layer refresh rates unconfirmed.
- NC State Climate Office: ECONet reporting interval for the API (1-minute availability) inferred from the networks page and obtype=O docs, not from an explicit latency statement; number of ECONet stations differs between page (46) and API (49, 46 active).
- Not researched due to exhausted web-search budget (200/200): Duke Energy/Alcoa lake-level feeds, TVA reservoir API, Synoptic Data (MesoWest) terms, CoCoRaHS direct API, NASA LHASA landslide nowcast, Hohonu/SECOORA coastal gauge APIs feeding FIMAN, IPAWS/WEA archive for county dam alerts, HRRR/NBM wind gust grids beyond api.weather.gov gridpoints, and NC OneMap wildfire-risk service terms.
- NHC GIS archive directory (>10 MB) only sampled; per-product file naming beyond al{NN}{YYYY}_5day_{ADV}.zip and wsp_* confirmed only from the first 300 KB.

## How North Carolina emergency management operates and buys software (organisation, information systems, damage-assessment doctrine, FEMA PDA process, Helene after-action findings, funding paths, state IT governance and security, procurement, legal/public-records/accessibility/retention, EMAC and mutual aid, owning offices and RFP patterns)
- (high/medium) **NCEM State EOC Activation Levels (Normal Operations / Enhanced Watch / SERT Activation) and 24-Hour Watch** (NC Emergency Management (NCEM), NC Department of Public Safety) https://www.ncdps.gov/our-organization/emergency-management/em-operations/state-eoc-activation-levels
  Defines the three State EOC postures: Normal Operations (24-Hour Watch only), Enhanced Watch (event largely within local capability, state agencies notified), and SERT Activation (multi-jurisdiction, multi-operational-period event likely to exceed local capability, possible federal support). The State Emergency Response Team (SERT) operates under the NCEM Director with Operations, Logistics, Information & Planning, JIC, Risk Management and Business EOC sections.
  access: Public web page (returned HTTP 403 to automated fetch; content taken from search index and the NCDPS SERT blog) | auth: none | licence: US state government web content, unstated | cadence: static doctrine | NC: Statewide; 24-Hour Watch 919-733-3300 / 800-858-0368, NCEM HQ 1636 Gold Star Drive Raleigh
  use (pre,during,post): Trigger and UI state: NC Recon should expose an 'activation mode' aligned to SERT activation (e.g., auto-start collection planning when SERT activates), and route outputs to the SEOC Information & Planning section and Risk Management section, which are the natural owners of a damage decision-support tool.
  caveats: Activation page blocked automated fetch; level names confirmed only via search snippet. SERT section names confirmed from 2020 NCDPS blog.
- (high/high) **NCEM Branches and Area Coordinators (NCEM_Branches_Coordination FeatureServer)** (NC Emergency Management (published to ArcGIS Online; mirrored on AmeriGEOSS)) https://services1.arcgis.com/YBWrN5qiESVpqi92/arcgis/rest/services/NCEM_Branches_Coordination/FeatureServer
  Two layers: NCEM Branches (layer 0) and NCEM Area Coordinators (layer 1). NC is divided into three branches (Eastern Branch, Kinston; Central Branch, Butner; Western Branch, Conover) each with five areas of 6-8 counties; each area has an Area Coordinator who is the state liaison and on-scene coordinator for state resources. Branch offices operate as Regional Coordination Centers (RCC East/Central/West) during activations.
  access: ArcGIS REST FeatureServer (JSON/GeoJSON query); also CSV/Shapefile/KML download via AmeriGEOSS | auth: none | licence: No license provided | cadence: static; refreshed when staffing changes | NC: All 100 counties; dataset last updated 2024-09-30 (AmeriGEOSS)
  use (pre,during,post): Map layer and routing table: attach the responsible Area Coordinator and RCC to every town/county assessment so briefs and exports go to the right branch office; use branch boundaries for coverage-gap and tasking views.
  caveats: Named coordinators in the 2006 NCEM brochure (ncleg doc 52270) are outdated; use the live service or ncdps.gov for current contacts.
- (high/high) **G.S. 166A-19.15 County and municipal emergency management (NC Emergency Management Act)** (NC General Assembly) https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_166A/GS_166A-19.15.html
  Makes each county's governing body responsible for emergency management within the county, requires a county that establishes an EM agency to appoint a coordinator with direct responsibility for the program, allows joint county/municipal agencies, and ties state/federal financial assistance to local plans meeting Division (NCEM) standards.
  access: Public statute (HTML/PDF) | auth: none | licence: Public law | cadence: None | NC: Statewide
  use (pre,during,post): Defines the primary user/owner persona (county EM coordinator/director) and the legal basis for county-led initial damage assessment; NC Recon's county workspace and role model should mirror county EM agency + municipalities coordinated by the county.
- (high/medium) **NC SPARTA (North Carolina State Preparedness and Resource Tracking Application) on Juvare WebEOC** (NCEM NCSPARTA team (sparta@ncdps.gov); software by Juvare) https://www.ncema.net/wp-content/uploads/2023/11/NCEM-WebEOC-Updates-and-2024-Roadmap.pdf
  NCEM's statewide crisis information management system, commonly called 'WebEOC'. Counties run their own WebEOC instances (e.g., harnettcountyem.webeocasp.com, carteret.webeocasp.com, charlotte-mecklenburg.webeocasp.com) fused to NCSPARTA for statewide visibility. Boards include Resource Request (mission requests), Situation Report, Shelter, Organization Profiles, Event Reporting; NCEM has used the WebEOC API (BEOC vendor microsite), Google Maps API and an ArcGIS Online Extension; 2024 roadmap: rebuild Resource Tracker and County Summary Response boards, improve Fusion. FEMA Region 4 treats WebEOC as the 'official database of record'.
  access: Login-only (FOUO, individual credentials issued by county EM or NCEM); WebEOC API exists but is not publicly documented for NC | auth: account | licence: Proprietary (Juvare) | cadence: real time during activations | NC: All 100 counties connected; disaster-recovery site tested May 2023
  use (during,post): Distribution channel and system of record: NC Recon outputs (county damage summaries, structure-level tables, briefs) must be posted into WebEOC boards by analysts today; a later integration via the WebEOC API (with an NCEM data-sharing agreement) could push damage summaries directly. Do not try to replace WebEOC.
  caveats: Helene AAR found WebEOC struggled under load, agencies failed to close requests, and WebEOC/iCAM were not integrated; a resource-request module released months before Helene caused confusion. No public API documentation or data-sharing terms found.
- (high/medium) **NCEM Survey123 Damage Assessment surveys and NCEM Damage Assessment Dashboard (as adopted in county EOP annexes)** (NC Emergency Management (ArcGIS Survey123 / Dashboards); documented by Alexander County EM) https://alexandercountync.gov/pdf/EOP/Damage%20Assessment.pdf
  Alexander County's Damage Assessment Functional Annex (updated Aug 11, 2025) states 'NCEM provides access to Survey123 Damage Assessment Surveys to assist with damage assessments' (public app, 'Continue without signing in'); fields include Event Name, Team Type, Team Name, County, geolocated structure point, FEMA PDA Pocket Guide damage level, up to 5 photos ('all 4 sides'); results appear in an NCEM Damage Assessment Dashboard. The county Damage Assessment Officer is the Tax Assessor; reports go to the NCEM Western Branch Office. Henderson County's public PDA page follows FEMA's five-step process.
  access: Survey123 mobile app with NCEM-shared survey (QR 'Quick Share'); dashboard access via NCEM | auth: account (survey can be used without sign-in once shared) | licence: Unstated | cadence: per event | NC: Statewide offering; example dashboard screenshot Lincoln County 7/31/2024
  use (post): Ground-truth source and export target: ingest NCEM Survey123 field records (point, FEMA level, photos) as labels for validation, and export NC Recon structure calls in the same schema so county teams can load them into the state dashboard. Also the Survey123 field sheet NC Recon already exports should match these field names.
  caveats: No NCEM-published page describing the statewide survey or its schema was found; evidence is a county annex. Alexander's annex still cites the May 2020 PDA Guide and Aug 2021 Pocket Guide.
- (high/high) **FEMA Preliminary Damage Assessment Guide (July 1, 2025 edition)** (FEMA Office of Response and Recovery, Recovery Directorate) https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_rd_pda-guide_07012025.pdf
  Current national doctrine for damage assessment; effective for incidents from July 1, 2025 and supersedes the 2024 Operational Draft. Five steps: local IDA, state verification, joint PDA planning, joint PDA (in person or virtual), declaration request within 30 days of incident-period end (60 days for Tribal Nations). Methodologies: self-reporting, door-to-door, windshield, digital surveys, validation tools (Field Maps loading state IDA data exported as Excel or geodatabase), virtual sensing (satellite 0.41 m or better; airborne 10-inch nadir, 6-inch oblique best; SAR/LiDAR for flood extent; geotagged CAP photos; geolocated media/social photos), GIS, HAZUS/POST modeling, desktop assessments. Adds a State Geospatial and Data Analysis Lead role. Lists FEMA's Field Assessment Collection Tool (FACT) as mapping software and FEMA.gov/PDA as template source.
  access: Portal download (PDF); templates at FEMA.gov/PDA | auth: none | licence: US Government public domain | cadence: revised roughly annually (May 2022 to July 2025 change log) | NC: National doctrine applied to NC declarations
  use (pre,post): Core evidence-standard reference: NC Recon's five damage levels, its imagery acceptance thresholds, and its export schema should cite this guide; the 'desktop assessment' and 'virtual sensing' sections are the authority that lets FEMA validate imagery-derived IDA data, which is exactly NC Recon's output.
  caveats: FEMA must still 'validate IDA damage estimates with visual confirmation and supporting documentation'; imagery AI output alone is not accepted without photos/geotags.
- (high/high) **FEMA Individual Assistance Damage Matrix (PDA Guide appendix)** (FEMA) https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_pda_individual-assistance-damage-matrix.pdf
  Degree-of-damage criteria for Affected, Minor, Major, Destroyed and Inaccessible for manufactured and conventionally built homes. Flood criteria are keyed to waterline relative to the floor system, electrical outlets and ceiling of an essential living space (e.g., conventional home Major = waterline at or above outlets, or any first-floor water if basement fully submerged; Destroyed = waterline at or above ceiling, only foundation remains, or imminent danger such as landslide). Non-flood criteria cover roof/structural failure, foundation cracks, displaced from foundation. Inaccessible = cannot be visually verified due to blocked access.
  access: Portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: US Government public domain | cadence: static (updated July 2025) | NC: National
  use (post): Label definitions for the classifier and review queue; map NC Recon's flood-depth channel to the outlet/ceiling thresholds (e.g., roughly 1-1.5 ft first-floor depth for Major, ceiling height for Destroyed) and add an explicit 'Inaccessible' state for structures with no usable imagery.
  caveats: The 2025 revision replaced numeric waterline measurements with outlet/ceiling references; older county SOPs cite the 2020/2021 editions.
- (high/medium) **FEMA Virtual Joint PDA and sharing IDA geospatial datasets (FACT system) and FACT decommissioning** (FEMA Recovery Directorate / FEMA Region 4) https://www.fema.gov/node/628208
  FEMA Regions may conduct virtual joint PDAs when states supply adequate geospatial IDA datasets and imagery. FEMA accepts state IDA geospatial datasets, recommended as file geodatabase (.gdb, preferred because photos stay attached) or CSV/Excel, uploaded into the Field Assessment and Collection Tools (FACT) system (ArcGIS Enterprise portal, FISMA Moderate). In July 2026 FEMA awarded a $405,000 sole-source contract to Ardent to restore and decommission FACT (remediation July 1 to Sept 30, 2026), migrating data to FEMA's Response Geospatial Mapping and Analysis Center.
  access: State-to-FEMA data submission via Region 4 (r4-gis@fema.dhs.gov, coordinated through the state EM GIS office) | auth: MOU | licence: US Government | cadence: per event | NC: Applies to NC via FEMA Region 4; NC state GIS POCs Daniel Madding and John Lay (NC DPS)
  use (post): Distribution format: the existing FileGDB export should carry FEMA's IDA attribute set (location, damage level, ownership/insurance where known, assessment date, collector, photo attachments) so NCEM can forward it for a virtual joint PDA; watch the FACT replacement for a new submission path.
  caveats: FACT is being retired in 2026; the successor intake mechanism and schema are not yet published. NAPSG deck (2023) could not be fetched directly (403).

Also publishes per-declaration PDA reports (e.g., DR-4827-NC).

  Configurable ArcGIS Online solution with Survey123 forms (Public Damage Report, Individual Assistance Survey, Public Assistance Survey, PA Damage Inventory), a QuickCapture windshield project, Experience Builder manager app and Hub site; v6.0 updated forms to FEMA 4.1.3 Public Assistance data standards aligned to the July 2025 PDA Guide; Spanish-language surveys added.

- (high/high) **FEMA Region 4 Geospatial Resource Center and remote sensing coordination ('Navigating Disaster: FEMA Resources for Disaster Response', Sept 2025)** (FEMA Region 4 Response, Recovery and Mitigation (presented via NC DIT/GICC)) https://it.nc.gov/fema-disaster-response-resources/open
  Describes how FEMA R4 coordinates GIS with NC: WebEOC is the official database of record; GIS coordination calls daily during RRCC activation; ArcGIS Online 'Region 4 Situational Awareness Journal' carries storm surge/wind models, US&R, multi-agency damage assessment data, remote sensing and PDA data; 'FEMA R4 RSC Viewer' for imagery AOI development and deconfliction; 'FEMA R4 RCD Validation Apps' for damage validation and SLTT data exchange; key datasets ACS, HIFLD, National Risk Index, USA Structures, CRCI. Mitigation guidance for Substantial Damage Assessment: Minor <10%, Major 10-50%, Substantial >=50% of pre-damage value, with SDE tool; recommends offline mobile collection and parcel/footprint/lidar pre-staging. Contacts: r4-gis@fema.dhs.gov, remote sensing travis.potter@fema.dhs.gov, PDA lee.edge@fema.dhs.gov; NC state POCs daniel.madding@ncdps.gov, John.Lay@ncdps.gov.

- (medium/high) **FEMA-4827-DR North Carolina (Tropical Storm Helene) declaration record and expedited PDA report** (FEMA) https://www.fema.gov/disaster/4827
  Declared Sept 28, 2024 (incident period Sept 25 to Dec 18, 2024) on an expedited basis: the joint PDA requirement was waived under 44 CFR 206.33(d)/206.36(d) and the PDA report shows no residence counts ('-'). 39 counties plus the Eastern Band of Cherokee Indians designated for IA. As of July 14, 2026: IA approved $567,412,314 (161,042 applications), PA obligated $2,379,760,937, Hazard Mitigation $201,485,386.
  access: Public web page and PDF; OpenFEMA API carries the same declaration data | auth: none | licence: US Government public domain | cadence: updated as obligations change | NC: 39 NC counties + EBCI
