- (high/high) **North Carolina SERT Hurricane Helene After Action Review (McChrystal Group, final report April 21, 2025)** (NCEM (commissioned); McChrystal Group (author)) https://www.ncdps.gov/division/emergency-management/ts-helene-after-action-review/open
  Third-party AAR based on 65 interviews and 100+ surveys (Nov 15 to Dec 13, 2024). Three Critical Areas: SERT Organization and Staffing; Interoperability, Communications and Data; Logistics and Resource Allocation. Data findings: no centralized database/common operating picture for SERT partners; WebEOC (NCSPARTA) overloaded and not integrated with iCAM inventory; two disparate SAR platforms (SARCOP, SARTopo) and no unified missing-persons system; validation capacity at RCC West overwhelmed; legislative constraints and privacy rules limited inter-agency data sharing; total communications blackout, Starlink effective; gaps in WebEOC/ICS proficiency; NCEM relies on federal grants for information systems. Recommendation #3 calls for a centralized database with dashboards integrating mission-critical systems, NCDIT as partner, a Validation Team at the RCC, and a systematic review of data-sharing agreements. Workshop top-5 included 'Create a unified database for information sharing and coordination across all SERT partners' and 'Establish permanent ESF liaison positions at RCC West'.
  access: Portal download (PDF, 2.33 MB) | auth: none | licence: NC state government report, unstated | cadence: static | NC: Statewide with focus on western NC (RCC West)
  use (pre,during,post): Primary justification document: position NC Recon as a component of the recommended COP/dashboard integration (feeding WebEOC and NCDIT-hosted data), include offline/low-bandwidth operation (72-hour comms loss), a validation queue that mirrors the recommended RCC Validation Team, and training/exercise material because WebEOC proficiency gaps were a finding.
  caveats: Covers preparedness and response phases only; recovery and damage-assessment process specifics are not reviewed in depth.
- (medium/medium) **NC Office of the State Auditor: Hurricane Helene One Year Review (Rapid Response, Sept 25, 2025)** (NC Office of the State Auditor) https://www.auditor.nc.gov/documents/reports/rapid-response/hurricane-helene-one-year-review/open
  One-page dashboard-style review: total state Helene funding $2,128,150,000 across S.L. 2024-51 ($273M), 2024-53 ($604.15M), 2024-57 ($227M), 2025-2 ($524M), 2025-26 ($500M); NCDOT spent $1,137,880,231 on roads/bridges, 1,426 roads closed immediately after the storm, 97% of state roads reopened by May 2025; ~550 homes built by nonprofits; agency 'percent unspent' chart ranges 16% to 95% with Department of Public Safety among listed agencies. OSA notes it 'has not independently audited the above information'.
  access: Portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state government report | cadence: one-off | NC: Statewide Helene spending
  use (post): Procurement/political context: large unspent state balances exist in multiple agencies; a tool that speeds documentation of eligible damage supports faster disbursement and should be pitched that way. Also OSA maintains a Helene expenditure dashboard (auditor.nc.gov/dashboards/helene).
  caveats: Per-agency unspent percentages could not be reliably mapped to agency names from the extracted text.
- (high/high) **OSBM Hurricane Helene Damage and Needs Assessment (Dec 13, 2024; $59.6B)** (NC Office of State Budget and Management with NCEM, DEQ, Commerce) https://osbm.nc.gov/hurricane-helene-dna/open
  Statewide damage/needs estimate. Methodology: 'top-down' from FEMA IA applications and inspections scaled against Hurricane Florence; 'bottom-up' from Search and Rescue structure categorizations (FEMA + NCEM through Nov 19, 2024, using FEMA destroyed/major/minor/affected criteria) combined with NCEM modeled flood inundation, DEQ/USGS landslide data and county parcel data from NC OneMap; damage value = improved parcel value x 100% (destroyed), 55% (major), 20% (minor), 10% (affected); parcels in the inundation layer without SAR assessment assumed 10% (Wisconsin EM method). Approximately 73,700 homes expected damaged; estimated 25,000-30,000 households receiving IA housing assistance; 5.2% of homes with FEMA-verified flood damage had flood insurance. Notes SAR teams categorized only a subset of damaged buildings and modeling underestimated mountain flooding.
  access: Portal download (PDF, 133 pages); slides also published | auth: none | licence: NC state government report | cadence: one-off with funding-request updates through March 2026 | NC: 39 FEMA-designated counties + EBCI
  use (post): Validates the NC Recon approach (fusing structure-level damage categories with inundation and parcel value) and supplies the state's accepted damage-to-value factors (100/55/20/10%) for a loss-estimate export; identifies the data gap NC Recon fills (SAR categorized only a subset of structures).

Applications via the Salesforce-based NC Emergency Management Preparedness Grants Portal, reviewed by the county's Area Coordinator; county performance period July 1, 2025 to Dec 31, 2026. Guidance explicitly lists 'paying for damage assessment software or a notification system annual subscription' as an allowable sustainment cost; equipment must be on the FEMA Authorized Equipment List (public alerting platforms AEL 04AP-09-ALRT must be IPAWS-capable). Contact: Derek Dorazio, Derek.dorazio@ncdps.gov; NCEM 919-710-8885.

- (high/high) **NC Statewide Information Security Manual (SISM), March 2025 (signed April 1, 2025)** (NC Department of Information Technology, State Chief Information Security Officer) https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/statewide-information-security-manual
  Statewide security policy required by G.S. 143B-1376; adopts NIST SP 800-37 RMF and NIST SP 800-53 Rev 5 (18 control families incl. Supply Chain Risk Management). Two security categorizations: Low (public data only) and Moderate (any Restricted/Highly Restricted data; 'Any system that stores, processes, or transfers or communicates PII ... is classified as a Moderate system, at a minimum'). Applies to all state information systems including those operated by contractors on behalf of the State; use by local governments is 'encouraged to the extent allowed by law'. Deviations go through the DIT Exception Process. Companion policies SCIO-SEC-301 to -318.
  access: Portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state policy | cadence: annual review | NC: All executive-branch agencies (NCEM/DPS included)
  use (pre): Security baseline: NC Recon handling structure-level damage with addresses/occupancy and any resident data will be a Moderate system; map its controls (MFA, audit logging, encryption, incident response, SR) to the NIST 800-53 Moderate baseline and document them in a System Security Plan before any state hosting or contract.
- (high/high) **NCDIT Vendor Readiness Assessment Report (VRAR) for non-state-hosted solutions and Cloud Vendor Exception Workflow** (NCDIT Enterprise Security and Risk Management Office (ESRMO)) https://it.nc.gov/documents/files/vendor-readiness-assessment-report-non-state-hosted-solutions/open
  Mandatory vendor security questionnaire for any state-hosted or cloud (SaaS/IaaS/PaaS) solution processing state data, required with the initial purchase request. Key requirements: independent third-party attestation (FedRAMP, SOC 2 Type 2, ISO 27001 or HITRUST) before contract award for systems with Restricted/Highly Restricted data; FIPS 140-2/140-3 validated cryptography for data at rest and in transit (TLS per NIST SP 800-52r2); MFA for administrative accounts; 14-character complex passwords; role-based access; online audit records retained at least 90 days; authenticated OS/web/database vulnerability scans at least monthly; incident response plan and testing; NIST SP 800-88 data destruction with certificate; policies/procedures for each NIST family. Workflow (June 13, 2018): vendor completes VRAR; agency approves and completes a Privacy Threshold Analysis; vendors without attestation must complete CSET, provide vuln/pen scan within 30 days, IR/BC plans, and contractually commit to attestation within 1 year or be rejected.
  access: Downloadable DOCX/PDF forms; submitted through the agency to ESRMO | auth: none | licence: NC state forms | cadence: per procurement; attestation renewed annually | NC: All executive-branch procurements
  use (pre): Pre-deployment checklist: build the VRAR answers into NC Recon's security documentation (FIPS-validated TLS/at-rest crypto, admin MFA, 90-day log retention, monthly authenticated scans, 800-88 wipe procedure). An open-source self-hosted deployment inside NCDIT or a county avoids the third-party-attestation gate that a SaaS offering would face.
  caveats: Workflow diagram dated 2018; GovRAMP adoption (below) is superseding the attestation path for cloud components from 2026.

  Effective April 1, 2026, new executive-branch contracts containing a cloud component must include GovRAMP-aligned (NIST 800-53 Rev 5) risk assessments, with an on-ramp for vendors to reach the required status (Core, Ready, or Authorized/Provisional); from April 1, 2027 full compliance is mandatory without exception and existing contracts must align at renewal. Continuous monitoring is required for the contract lifecycle and the State may request the security package.

Onboarding requires agency CIO and security-liaison approval, a Network Architecture Diagram and Network Security Request Form, and NCID enrollment; security and design review scheduled 2-3 days after prerequisites; agencies pay NCDIT rates plus pass-through cloud consumption.
  access: Service request via NCDIT Support Center | auth: account | licence: State service | cadence: continuous | NC: Statewide
  use (pre): Preferred state hosting option for a Moderate-category deployment: run the NC Recon container in an NCDIT landing zone (which also simplifies NCID and GovRAMP questions); confirm GovCloud/Government-region use with NCDIT since the page does not state it.
  caveats: Whether NCDIT uses AWS GovCloud or Azure Government regions is not stated; rates not published on the page.
- (high/high) **State IT procurement rules: G.S. 143B-1350, $100,000 cumulative delegation, 10-step process, Statewide IT Procurement Office** (NCDIT Statewide IT Procurement Office (PGT@nc.gov; submit2.StatewideIT@its.nc.gov)) https://it.nc.gov/blog/2025/07/02/it-purchasing-delegation-raised-100000
  G.S. 143B-1350 gives the State CIO authority over all state-agency IT procurement (can cancel unapproved buys) and lets local governments use statewide IT contracts and GSA cooperative purchasing. Since February 2025 agencies may buy IT up to $100,000 cumulatively over the contract term without Statewide IT Procurement Office authorization; above that, authorization is required. The NC eProcurement 10-step IT procurement process must document NCDIT project management, security, privacy and legal reviews; IT contract amendments/renewals go through the NC eProcurement Contracts Tool. General P&C small-purchase threshold $25,000 and $100,000 general delegation (01 NCAC 05B).
  access: NC eProcurement (agency side) and electronic Vendor Portal eVP (vendor side) | auth: account | licence: Public statute and policy | cadence: thresholds updated periodically | NC: All executive-branch agencies; statewide IT contracts open to local governments
  use (pre): Pricing and contracting strategy: keep any NCEM support/hosting contract under $100,000 cumulative for a pilot to stay within agency delegation, expect security/privacy/legal reviews, and register the maintaining entity in eVP. For statewide scale, pursue a statewide IT contract so counties can piggyback.
  caveats: The 'IT Procurement Process Overview' document itself was not retrieved.
- (high/high) **Local government procurement: G.S. 143-129 formal bidding, G.S. 143-131 informal bidding, G.S. 143-129.8 IT RFP option, G.S. 143-135.9 best value (UNC SOG CGCIO course, Jan 2025)** (NC General Assembly; UNC School of Government (Crista Cuccaro)) https://sog.unc.edu/sites/default/files/course_materials/IT%20Procurement%20and%20Contracting--CIO%20Jan%202025.pdf
  Counties/cities must formally bid purchases of apparatus/supplies/equipment at $90,000+ (informal $30,000-$90,000) and construction at $500,000+; services (including most software subscriptions and professional services) are not subject to competitive bidding under state law ('State Law Does Not Require Competitive Bidding Process'). G.S. 143-129.8 offers an optional IT RFP method (formal advertisement, award to 'best overall proposal', negotiation allowed, proposals confidential until award, no board approval required). G.S. 143-135.9 allows best-value, solution-based and government-vendor partnership procurements. Exceptions: sole source (board approval), piggyback on state/federal contracts, cooperative purchasing, emergency. Federal Uniform Guidance (2 CFR 200.320) applies when grant-funded (EMPG/HMGP).
  access: Public statutes; SOG course material (PDF) | auth: none | licence: Public law; SOG material copyright UNC | cadence: static | NC: All NC counties and municipalities
  use (pre): County adoption path: an NC Recon support/hosting subscription is a service that a county can contract without bidding under state law, but if paid with EMPG it must follow 2 CFR 200.320 (micro-purchase/small purchase thresholds and price analysis). Offer an IT-RFP-ready response package and a sole-source/open-source justification template.
- (medium/medium) **NC statewide Esri Enterprise License Agreement and local-government Master Purchase Agreement** (NCDIT (Kristen Burnette, Contract and Vendor Manager, 919-754-6678, kristen.burnette@nc.gov)) https://it.nc.gov/services/enterprise-license-agreements/esri-license-agreement
  Executive-branch agencies get unlimited Esri software under an ELA (term shown July 2021 to June 30, 2025) with an annual support fee recovering NCDIT's payment; includes Tier 1 help desk, 120 training days/year and 30 conference registrations; local governments buy at GSA-equivalent prices through an Esri-NC Master Purchase Agreement. NCDIT's ELA page currently lists Esri and SAS agreements.
  access: Through NCDIT; local governments via MPA | auth: paid | licence: Proprietary (Esri) | cadence: multi-year agreement | NC: All executive-branch agencies; local governments by MPA
  use (pre,post): Confirms every NC state agency and most counties have ArcGIS Online/Enterprise, Survey123 and Field Maps; NC Recon should treat ArcGIS feature services and FileGDB as first-class exchange formats rather than require Esri licenses itself.
  caveats: Page still shows a term ending June 30, 2025; renewal status after that date not confirmed.
- (high/high) **NC Public Records Law: G.S. 132-1, 132-1.7 (sensitive public security information), 132-1.10 (identifying information), 132-6.2 (electronic copies), 132-10 (GIS commercial-use exception)** (NC General Assembly) https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_132/GS_132-1.7.html
  All records made or received in public business, 'regardless of physical form', including maps, photographs and electronic data, are public and 'the property of the people' (132-1). 132-1.7 exempts specific public security plans, detailed drawings of public buildings and infrastructure 'including detailed plans and drawings contained in, or capable of being produced from ... geographic information system databases', critical-infrastructure vulnerability/design information, plans to respond to terrorist activity that reveal vulnerabilities or specific emergency procedures, and mobile numbers of emergency personnel, while general adoption of plans and budgets stay public. 132-1.10 bars collecting/disclosing SSNs and other identifying information and sends agency breaches to G.S. 75-65. 132-6.2 lets requesters choose the electronic medium and allows a 'special service charge' for extensive IT work. 132-10 lets counties/cities require a no-commercial-use agreement before releasing GIS database copies.
  access: Public statutes | auth: none | licence: Public law | cadence: static | NC: All NC agencies and subdivisions
  use (pre,during,post): Design for disclosure: every assessment, image and model output stored by a county/state instance is presumptively a public record; keep an export that is safe to release (already the suppressed public tract summary), tag structure-level records containing occupant PII or infrastructure vulnerability as potentially exempt, never store SSNs, and implement records export in requester-chosen formats.
  caveats: No statute specifically exempts damage-assessment records; damage levels tied to addresses are likely public unless they reveal exempt security or identifying information.
- (medium/high) **NC Identity Theft Protection Act (G.S. 75-61 definitions, 75-65 breach notification)** (NC General Assembly / NC Attorney General Consumer Protection Division) https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_75/GS_75-65.html
  Defines personal information (first name/initial + last name with identifying information per G.S. 14-113.20(b); excludes lawfully available government records) and security breach (unauthorized access and acquisition of unencrypted/unredacted records where misuse occurred or is reasonably likely). Requires notice to affected persons 'without unreasonable delay', notice to the Attorney General, and to consumer reporting agencies when 1,000+ affected; substitute notice allowed above $250,000 cost or 500,000 persons. Government agencies are bound via G.S. 132-1.10.
  access: Public statutes | auth: none | licence: Public law | cadence: static | NC: Statewide
  use (pre): Incident-response requirement: if NC Recon stores reviewer accounts or any resident-identifying data (field report ingest), encrypt at rest (encryption removes the breach trigger) and include an AG-notification step in the security runbook.
- (high/high) **ADA Title II web accessibility rule (WCAG 2.1 AA) and NC Digital Accessibility and Usability Standard** (US DOJ (rule); NCDIT Digital Accessibility program (state standard)) https://www.ada.gov/resources/2024-03-08-web-rule/
  DOJ's April 2024 Title II rule adopts WCAG 2.1 Level AA for all web content and mobile apps of state and local governments, including content provided through contractors; an April 2026 Interim Final Rule extended deadlines to April 26, 2027 (entities 50,000+, which includes state government) and April 26, 2028 (under 50,000 and special districts). Exceptions: archived content, pre-existing documents not used for services, third-party posts, password-protected individualized documents, pre-existing social posts. North Carolina's Digital Accessibility and Usability Standard (April 2024) applies to all state-agency public-facing sites and services including those run by contractors, and adds HTTPS, MFA for public-facing logins, nc.gov subdomains, plain language, mobile-first, privacy notice, and digital-first forms; NCDIT provides free automated monitoring and a Digital Accessibility Community of Practice.
  access: Public regulation and state standard | auth: none | licence: Public law / state standard | cadence: deadline-driven (2027/2028) | NC: All NC state and local government web content
  use (pre): Hard requirement for the React UI and exported PDFs/briefs: WCAG 2.1 AA (map alternatives, keyboard operation, contrast, accessible tables for the public tract summary), plus the NC standard's MFA/HTTPS/plain-language items; schedule an accessibility audit before the 2027 deadline.
  caveats: NCDIT's Nov 2024 deck shows the original April 24, 2026 date; the FAQ and ADA.gov now show April 26, 2027 after the DOJ extension.
- (medium/medium) **NC records retention: Functional Schedule for NC State Agencies (2025), 16. Risk Management / 16.2 Emergency Management; General Records Schedule for Local Government Agencies (Oct 1, 2021)** (NC Department of Natural and Cultural Resources, Division of Archives and Records) https://archives.ncdcr.gov/risk-management/open
  State schedule (dated April 20, 2026 revision of the 2025 edition) series: 1621 Agency Disaster Planning (retain until superseded; confidentiality G.S. 126-24, 132-1.7); 1622 Alerts (complete + 1 year); 1623.P Disaster Recovery Records for major efforts PERMANENT; 1623.3 minor recovery operations (complete + 3 years); 1623.A DPS Disaster Recovery Records 'also includes Flood Risk Information System data' PERMANENT (archival, transfer to State Archives); 1625 SAR Mission Records (complete + 5 years). Schedules apply 'to records in all media'. Local governments use the General Records Schedule: Local Government Agencies (issued Oct 1, 2021) plus 18 function-specific schedules; destruction requires governing-board approval of the schedule.
  access: Portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state records schedules | cadence: schedules revised periodically | NC: All state agencies; all local governments
  use (post): Retention design: treat major-event assessment datasets, model versions, imagery provenance and review decisions as permanent records (export an archival package: GeoPackage + PDF brief + audit log) and avoid auto-deletion; county instances should map to the local schedule's emergency management series.
  caveats: The local General Records Schedule PDF (>10MB) could not be fetched, so the exact local emergency-management series and retention periods are unconfirmed.
- (medium/high) **Emergency Management Assistance Compact (EMAC) and NC enabling statute (G.S. 166A Article 4)** (National Emergency Management Association (EMAC); NC General Assembly) https://www.emacweb.org/index.php/what-is-emac
  Congressionally ratified interstate mutual aid compact (Public Law 104-321, 1996) administered by state EM agencies; supports personnel, equipment and 'virtual missions (such as GIS mapping)'. NC's enactment (G.S. 166A-42 to -49) requires party states to maintain plans and 'free exchange of information, plans, and resource records', honors out-of-state licenses, makes aid providers agents of the requesting state with good-faith liability protection, and provides reimbursement. The Helene AAR credits EMAC as a success and recommends expanding standardized EMAC packages (including communications, logistics).
  access: State-to-state requests through NCEM (EMAC A-Team) | auth: MOU | licence: Public law | cadence: per event | NC: NC is a member state
  use (during,post): Out-of-state analysts deployed under EMAC (or NC analysts deployed elsewhere) can run NC Recon as a virtual GIS mission; design multi-tenant/other-state deployment (non-NC data sources optional) and a data-handling note that EMAC does not itself waive NC public-records or privacy law.
- (medium/medium) **NC Statewide Emergency Management Mutual Aid and Assistance Agreement (G.S. 166A-19.72) and WebEOC request flow** (NCEM; UNC School of Government (Kara Millonzi, Oct 2024)) https://canons.sog.unc.edu/2024/10/local-governments-assisting-other-local-governments-during-a-declared-disaster/
  G.S. 166A-19.72 authorizes the Governor and local chief executives (with governing-board concurrence) to enter mutual aid agreements covering supplies, equipment, facilities, personnel, services and reimbursement. All 100 counties and many municipalities have signed the Statewide Mutual Aid Agreement; requests flow through county EMs into NCEM's WebEOC and regional coordination centers; FEMA PA reimburses the requesting entity only when there is a declaration, a formal request, documentation and an agreement. NC Constitution's public-purpose clause bars donating funds to another unit.
  access: Agreement signed by each unit; requests via WebEOC | auth: MOU | licence: Public law / agreement | cadence: static | NC: All 100 counties
  use (during,post): Supports regional/shared deployments: a county or COG hosting NC Recon can assist neighbors under the statewide agreement; keep per-request logs so assessment labor is documentable for PA reimbursement.
  caveats: The 2023 Statewide Mutual Aid Agreement PDF on ncdps.gov returned 404.
- (medium/high) **NC OneMap imagery services (CGIA) incl. Orthoimagery_Helene_2025 and the Statewide Orthoimagery Program cycle** (NC Center for Geographic Information and Analysis (CGIA), NCDIT Enterprise Data Office (Ben Shelton, 919-754-6377, Ben.Shelton@nc.gov)) https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/Imagery?f=pjson

The Statewide Orthoimagery Program flies about a quarter of the state each year on a 4-year cycle (2025: 26 counties, 15,141 sq mi, four vendors, delivery Nov-Dec 2025; 2026: 26 counties with 25% 'Mountain Acquisition', delivery Nov-Dec 2026); the NC 911 Board funds it. CGIA operates on a cost-recovery basis under G.S. 143B-1321(a)(32); the State CIO supervises CGIA, GICC, GDAC and the 911 Board (G.S. 143B-1322(c)(19)).
  access: ArcGIS REST ImageServer (public endpoints under /secure/rest/services) | auth: none | licence: Unstated on service; Helene service copyright 'NC Collaboratory at UNC Chapel Hill' | cadence: annual deliveries; emergency acquisitions ad hoc | NC: Statewide on 4-year cycle; Helene 2025 imagery for 13 counties
  use (pre,post): Already a core imagery source; for this dimension it identifies CGIA/GICC as the governance body to engage for a standing 'emergency imagery' agreement and as the host of statewide services NC Recon depends on.
  caveats: GICC FY2024-25 annual report and nconemap.gov pages could not be fetched (size/JS); GICC statute section not located in Article 15 extract.
- (high/high) **NCEM Risk Management geospatial holdings: spartagis.ncem.org public services, NC_Risk_Building_Footprints, Spatial Data Download (sdd.nc.gov), FRIS, FIMAN** (NCEM Risk Management (Daniel Madding, IT Manager, Daniel.Madding@ncdps.gov; John Lay, GIS Analyst, John.Lay@ncdps.gov)) https://spartagis.ncem.org/arcgis/rest/services/Public?f=pjson

The NC_Risk_Building_Footprints FeatureServer (copyright 'NCEM Risk Management') holds statewide footprints digitized 2009-2012 with first-floor elevations (field-collected in SFHAs, lidar-derived elsewhere); NCEM's 'Datapalooza' deck describes just under 5 million buildings with FFE, foundation type, roof shape/slope, stories, occupancy, value, year built and HAZUS attributes, ~4,500 HM acquisition sites, QL1/QL2 lidar and DEMs via sdd.nc.gov, and FRIS/FIMAN/flood.nc.gov web apps. FIMAN serves 700+ gauges with inundation mapping; FIMAN-T adds roadway flooding depths with NCDOT.

- (medium/high) **NC electronic Vendor Portal (eVP) and NCDPS/NCEM solicitation pattern (e.g., RFQ 19-RFQ-2172540211-RMT, RFP 19-RFP-2063949214-RMT)** (NC Department of Administration Division of Purchase & Contract (eVP); NC DPS Purchasing (contract specialists, e.g., morgan.rilling@ncdps.gov)) https://evp.nc.gov/
  eVP is 'the State of North Carolina's system to connect vendors with state government organizations'; vendors must register and hold an eVP number (kept confidential under G.S. 132-1.10(b)); solicitations are browsed in eVP and responses collected in the NC eProcurement Sourcing Tool with a message board for questions. NCEM RFQs use the Nov 2025 state template, 1-year initial terms, award to lowest responsive price (G.S. 143-52) or, for RFPs, best value; examples in 2026 include an NCEM training/exercise software RFQ (issued May 20, 2026) and an Individual Assistance/Public Assistance personnel RFP (opening June 5, 2026).

  caveats: Local governments generally do not post in eVP; county RFPs appear on county sites or BidNet.

NCEM's county EM directory links each county's own alert sign-up; vendors vary by county (e.g., ReadyBrunswick on Everbridge with voice/SMS/email and the Everbridge mobile app). No statewide Everbridge contract was found; EMPG allows IPAWS-capable alerting platforms (AEL 04AP-09-ALRT) for counties that are Public Alerting Authorities.

  By Oct 7, 2024, about 100 CAP members from 31 wings completed more than 90,000 remote damage assessments and identified over 40,000 damaged structures across the six Helene-declared states using satellite, airborne and open-source imagery; CAP launched a Damage Assessment Training Course qualification for remote analysts.

  caveats: Secondary (CAP news) source; the geodatabase of these assessments is not publicly released.

  County assessors must recognize value changes from damage as of the next January 1 (G.S. 105-287) and are expected to join local damage assessment teams, using property records and appraisal skill; Dare County publishes modified FEMA damage matrices and photo-based damage descriptions; assessor staff availability for other counties is coordinated through WebEOC. Alexander County designates the Tax Assessor as Damage Assessment Officer.

### insights
- NCEM organizes the state into three branches (Eastern Branch at Kinston, Central Branch at Butner, Western Branch at Conover) and 15 areas of 6-8 counties, each with an Area Coordinator who is the county's liaison and the on-scene coordinator for state resources; branch offices function as Regional Coordination Centers (RCC West was the Helene focal point). Source: https://data.amerigeoss.org/dataset/ncem-area-coordinators and https://www.ncdps.gov/division/emergency-management/ts-helene-after-action-review/open
- State EOC postures are Normal Operations (24-Hour Watch only), Enhanced Watch, and SERT Activation; the SERT works under the NCEM Director with Operations, Logistics, Information & Planning, JIC, Risk Management and Business EOC sections. Source: https://www.ncdps.gov/our-organization/emergency-management/em-operations/state-eoc-activation-levels (search index; page blocked automated fetch) and https://www.ncdps.gov/blog/2020/07/31/responding-all-hazards-work-state-emergency-response-team
- Counties, not the state, own emergency management and initial damage assessment under G.S. 166A-19.15; the county EM coordinator/director is the statutory program head and state/federal funding eligibility depends on meeting NCEM standards. Source: https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_166A/GS_166A-19.15.html
- NC SPARTA (WebEOC by Juvare) is the system of record for mission/resource requests, situation reports and shelters across all 100 counties via county WebEOC instances fused to the state; FEMA Region 4 also calls WebEOC the 'official database of record'. NCEM's NCSPARTA team (sparta@ncdps.gov) has used the WebEOC API and an ArcGIS Online Extension. Sources: https://www.ncema.net/wp-content/uploads/2023/11/NCEM-WebEOC-Updates-and-2024-Roadmap.pdf and https://it.nc.gov/fema-disaster-response-resources/open
- NCEM already distributes a statewide Survey123 Damage Assessment survey (team type, event name, county, geolocated point, FEMA level, up to 5 photos) and an NCEM Damage Assessment Dashboard; county annexes instruct teams to classify using the FEMA PDA Pocket Guide and to report to the branch office. Source: https://alexandercountync.gov/pdf/EOP/Damage%20Assessment.pdf (updated Aug 11, 2025)
- The current FEMA PDA Guide (July 1, 2025) accepts virtual sensing and desktop assessments for validation when imagery meets thresholds (satellite about 0.41 m, airborne 10-inch nadir, 6-inch oblique best; SAR/LiDAR for flood extents) and IDA data is delivered as Excel or file geodatabase with photos; it created a State Geospatial and Data Analysis Lead role; states must request a declaration within 30 days of incident-period end. Source: https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_rd_pda-guide_07012025.pdf
- FEMA IA damage levels are Affected, Minor, Major, Destroyed and Inaccessible; flood criteria are keyed to waterline relative to the floor system, electrical outlets and ceiling of an essential living space (2025 revision replaced numeric depths). Source: https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_pda_individual-assistance-damage-matrix.pdf
- FEMA's FACT system that received state IDA geodatabases is being decommissioned under a $405,000 contract (July 1 to Sept 30, 2026) with data migrating to FEMA's Response Geospatial Mapping and Analysis Center; the successor state-submission path is not yet published. Source: https://www.cdomagazine.tech/us-federal-news-bureau/fema-awards-405k-contract-to-restore-and-decommission-fact-data-system
- Helene's NC declaration (DR-4827, Sept 28, 2024) was expedited with the joint PDA waived under 44 CFR 206.33(d); as of July 14, 2026 FEMA had approved $567.4M IA for 161,042 applications, obligated $2.38B PA and $201.5M HMA across 39 counties plus EBCI. Sources: https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/PDAReport_FEMA4827DRexpedited-NC.pdf and https://www.fema.gov/disaster/4827
- The state's Helene AAR (McChrystal Group, Apr 21, 2025) found no centralized database/common operating picture for SERT partners, WebEOC overloaded and un-integrated with inventory (iCAM), duplicated SAR well-checks from fragmented data, validation capacity overwhelmed at RCC West, and privacy/legislative constraints on inter-agency data sharing; it recommends a unified database with dashboards (NCDIT as partner), an RCC Validation Team, review of data-sharing agreements, year-round LEO satellite comms, and notes NCEM funds information systems from federal grants rather than state appropriations. Source: https://www.ncdps.gov/division/emergency-management/ts-helene-after-action-review/open
- OSBM's Helene Damage and Needs Assessment ($59.6B) built its structure-damage estimate from FEMA/NCEM SAR categorizations plus NCEM inundation and DEQ/USGS landslide layers joined to NC OneMap parcels, applying 100%/55%/20%/10% of improved value for destroyed/major/minor/affected and 10% for unassessed inundated parcels; it acknowledges SAR teams categorized only a subset of damaged buildings. Source: https://osbm.nc.gov/hurricane-helene-dna/open
- Only 4% of FEMA's incident management workforce was available to deploy as of Nov 1, 2024 after Helene/Milton, and FEMA lost about 2,446 staff Jan-Jun 2025 (GAO-25-108598). Source: https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-25-108598
- EMPG is the routine county funding channel: NC's FY25 allocation is $8,558,068 with ~51% passed to counties in population tiers (about $20.6k/$35k/$62.5k federal with 50% match), applied for through NCEM's Salesforce 'NC Preparedness Grants Portal' and reviewed by Area Coordinators; the guidance names 'damage assessment software' subscriptions as allowable sustainment costs. Sources: https://www.ncdps.gov/empg and https://ncdps.gov/fy25-empg-20-program-guidance/open
- FEMA's HMGP 5% Initiative explicitly funds 'GIS software, hardware, and data acquisition whose primary aim is mitigation' and 'new, unproven mitigation techniques, technologies' with a narrative instead of a BCA; DR-4827 made HMGP available statewide. Source: https://www.fema.gov/hmgp-appeal-categories/5-initiative
- BRIC was terminated April 4, 2025, the termination was permanently enjoined Dec 11, 2025, and a March 6, 2026 order required FEMA to issue the FY2024 NOFO; no FY25/FY26 NOFO existed as of March 2026. Source: https://www.everycrsreport.com/files/2026-03-10_IN12609_78ea9dfb9eb809875111eb861d2febebe5da2eee.html
- The General Assembly appropriated $2.128B in state Helene funds across five Disaster Recovery Acts (S.L. 2024-51, 2024-53, 2024-57, 2025-2, 2025-26) with no line item for damage-assessment technology; S.L. 2025-2 routed $100M private roads/bridges through NCEM and $120M home repair through Commerce's Division of Community Revitalization. Sources: https://www.auditor.nc.gov/documents/reports/rapid-response/hurricane-helene-one-year-review/open and https://ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/SessionLaws/HTML/2025-2026/SL2025-2.html
- The NC Collaboratory (created 2016 by the General Assembly) funded 21 Helene micro-grants at App State, WCU and UNC Asheville and paid for the 6-inch 4-band spring-2025 orthoimagery of 13 western counties now served on NC OneMap as Orthoimagery_Helene_2025; it has no open Helene call today. Sources: https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/Imagery/Orthoimagery_Helene_2025/ImageServer?f=pjson and https://projects.collaboratory.unc.edu/
- State security policy (SISM, March 2025) is NIST 800-53 Rev 5 with Low/Moderate categorization; any system with PII is Moderate at minimum; it binds contractors operating systems for the State and is 'encouraged' for local governments. Source: https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/statewide-information-security-manual
- Before award, NCDIT's VRAR requires third-party attestation (FedRAMP, SOC 2 Type 2, ISO 27001 or HITRUST) for Restricted/Highly Restricted data, FIPS 140-2/3 validated crypto, admin MFA, 90-day online audit logs, monthly authenticated vulnerability scans and NIST 800-88 destruction; from April 1, 2026 new cloud contracts need GovRAMP-aligned assessments and from April 1, 2027 full GovRAMP compliance. Sources: https://it.nc.gov/documents/files/vendor-readiness-assessment-report-non-state-hosted-solutions/open and https://it.nc.gov/programs/cybersecurity-risk-management/esrmo-initiatives/govramp-adoption
- NCID (Simeio cloud platform since Sept 17, 2023) is required for state agencies and suggested for local governments, integrating via SAML, web services, HTTP proxy or LDAP; NCDIT Cloud Services offers managed AWS/Azure/GCP landing zones to state and local agencies. Sources: https://it.nc.gov/support/ncid/ncid-citizen-identity-project and https://itservices.nc.gov/services/hosting/cloud-services
- State agencies may buy IT up to $100,000 cumulative per contract without Statewide IT Procurement Office authorization (since Feb 2025) but must document project-management, security, privacy and legal reviews in NC eProcurement's 10-step process; local governments may use statewide IT contracts and GSA cooperative purchasing (G.S. 143B-1350). Sources: https://it.nc.gov/blog/2025/07/02/it-purchasing-delegation-raised-100000 and https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_143B/GS_143B-1350.html
- For counties, services and software subscriptions are not subject to state competitive-bidding statutes (formal bidding applies to purchases of $90,000+ and construction $500,000+; informal $30,000-$90,000), and G.S. 143-129.8 offers a best-overall-proposal IT RFP route; federal 2 CFR 200.320 applies when grant funds are used. Source: https://sog.unc.edu/sites/default/files/course_materials/IT%20Procurement%20and%20Contracting--CIO%20Jan%202025.pdf
- The state Esri ELA (listed term July 2021 to June 30, 2025) gives executive agencies unlimited Esri software and local governments GSA-equivalent pricing via a Master Purchase Agreement, so ArcGIS Online/Survey123/Field Maps are ubiquitous among NC EM users. Source: https://it.nc.gov/services/enterprise-license-agreements/esri-license-agreement
- Public records law makes assessment data public by default (G.S. 132-1) with exemptions for detailed infrastructure drawings 'capable of being produced from ... geographic information system databases', critical-infrastructure vulnerability data, specific emergency procedures and responders' mobile numbers (G.S. 132-1.7); agencies may not disclose SSNs/identifying information and must follow G.S. 75-65 breach notice (G.S. 132-1.10); electronic copies in the requester's chosen medium with special service charges for heavy IT work (G.S. 132-6.2). Sources: https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_132/GS_132-1.7.html and https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_132/GS_132-6.2.html
- WCAG 2.1 AA is mandatory for NC state and local government web content and apps under ADA Title II, with DOJ's April 2026 interim final rule moving deadlines to April 26, 2027 (entities 50,000+, including the state) and April 26, 2028 (smaller entities); NC's own Digital Accessibility and Usability Standard (April 2024) also requires HTTPS, MFA for public-facing logins and nc.gov subdomains. Sources: https://www.ada.gov/resources/2024-03-08-web-rule/ and https://it.nc.gov/faq-partnering-for-compliance
- State records schedules make major disaster recovery records PERMANENT (series 1623.P) and DPS disaster recovery records including Flood Risk Information System data permanent/archival (1623.A); agency disaster plans are retained until superseded and flagged confidential under G.S. 132-1.7. Source: https://archives.ncdcr.gov/risk-management/open
- EMAC (P.L. 104-321; NC G.S. 166A-42 to -49) supports 'virtual missions (such as GIS mapping)' and requires 'free exchange of information, plans, and resource records' among party states; the Helene AAR rated EMAC a success and all 100 NC counties have signed the Statewide Mutual Aid Agreement, with requests flowing through WebEOC. Sources: https://www.emacweb.org/index.php/what-is-emac, https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/ByArticle/Chapter_166A/Article_4.html and https://canons.sog.unc.edu/2024/10/local-governments-assisting-other-local-governments-during-a-declared-disaster/
- Likely owning offices for a tool like NC Recon: NCEM Risk Management (owns the ~5M-building footprint/FFE inventory, FRIS, FIMAN and the FEMA R4 GIS relationship; contacts Daniel.Madding@ncdps.gov, John.Lay@ncdps.gov), NCEM Operations/Information & Planning (SEOC COP), NCEM Hazard Mitigation (Asst. Director Steve McGugan; HMGP 5%), NCEM Plans (Katie Webster), the Office of Partnership Engagement (Persia Payne-Hurley) for university partners, and CGIA/GICC for statewide geospatial governance. Sources: https://rebuild.nc.gov/ncem-datapalooza/open, https://www.ncdps.gov/our-organization/emergency-management/emergency-management-leadership, https://it.nc.gov/fema-disaster-response-resources/open
- FEMA Region 4 (Atlanta; RA Robert P. Ashe; r4-gis@fema.dhs.gov) runs daily GIS coordination calls during activations, an AGOL Situational Awareness Journal, an RSC Viewer for imagery AOI deconfliction and RCD Validation Apps for SLTT damage data exchange, and insists requests come through the state EM GIS point of contact. Sources: https://it.nc.gov/fema-disaster-response-resources/open and https://www.fema.gov/about/organization/region-4
- NCEM procures through NC eProcurement/eVP with standard RFQ/RFP templates (Nov 2025 version), 1-year initial terms and lowest-price or best-value awards; 2026 examples include an EM training/exercise software RFQ and an IA/PA surge-personnel RFP, showing NCEM already buys EM software and recovery staffing from vendors. Source: https://evp.nc.gov/_entity/annotation/9288f715-5c54-f111-bec6-001dd80bcb64/863ea987-6d3e-ed11-9daf-001dd805ec0b
### gaps
- NCDPS pages for State EOC activation levels, the SERT page, EM Operations and 24-Hour Watch returned HTTP 403/404 to automated fetch; activation-level definitions rest on search-index text only.
- No NCEM-published damage assessment SOP, form set or Survey123 schema was found; NCEM's statewide Survey123 Damage Assessment survey and dashboard are evidenced only through a county EOP annex (Alexander County) and a county PDA page (Henderson County).
- WebEOC/NC SPARTA API documentation, data-sharing agreement templates and the terms under which a third-party tool could post to NC SPARTA boards are not public.
- FEMA's successor to FACT (Response Geospatial Mapping and Analysis Center) and the post-2026 state IDA geodatabase submission schema are not yet published; the FEMA PDA reports listing page returned HTTP 503 on repeated attempts, and the FEMA Geospatial Resource Center hub is JavaScript-only.
- The NC GICC statute section number could not be located (G.S. 143B-1370/-1371/-1372/-1380 are other topics or reserved) and the GICC FY2024-25 annual report (>10MB) and nconemap.gov pages could not be fetched; GICC committee structure and any Helene imagery/data-sharing lessons therefore come from search-index text.
- Esri ELA renewal status after June 30, 2025 is unconfirmed on NCDIT's page.
- NCID: whether OIDC/OAuth 2.0 is supported (only SAML, web services, HTTP proxy, LDAP listed), the NCDIT rate schedule amounts, and the onboarding path for a non-state-hosted application were not confirmed.
- NCDIT Cloud Services page does not state whether AWS GovCloud or Azure Government regions are used; GovRAMP level-to-data-classification mapping for NC is not published on the fetched pages.
- The Local Government Agencies General Records Schedule (Oct 1, 2021) PDF exceeded the fetch limit, so the county emergency-management records series and retention periods are unconfirmed (state Functional Schedule series were confirmed).
- No DHS OIG audit specific to FEMA's Helene damage assessment or IA inspections was found; no NC General Assembly committee report (House Select Committee on Helene Recovery) with emergency-management or data-sharing findings was located; Rep. Edwards' 'Fix FEMA' report PDF was not retrieved.
- NC Collaboratory: no open Helene or emergency-management technology funding call exists as of Oct 2026 and its 2025 Annual Legislative Report (which mentions Helene initiatives) was not retrieved.
- NCDPS Hazard Mitigation program pages (HMGP Helene amounts, application portal, 5% Initiative project list) returned 404; the HMGP 5% examples for NC come from search-index text of an NCDPS document.
- FIMAN (fiman.nc.gov) and services.gis.nc.gov returned 403; FIMAN API/terms of use and the Orthoimagery_2025 item metadata were not confirmed (services.nconemap.gov endpoints did work).
- No statewide alerting contract (Everbridge or other) was found; alerting is county-by-county. The fate of the ReadyNC mobile app is unknown.
- The 2023 NC Statewide Mutual Aid Agreement PDF returned 404; the 'all 100 counties signed' statement relies on the UNC SOG blog.
- No NC statute or policy was found that specifically governs confidentiality of address-level damage assessment records or FEMA IA applicant data held by the state; treatment under G.S. 132-1.7/132-1.10 is inferred.
- Per-agency 'percent unspent' figures in the State Auditor's Helene review could not be reliably paired with agency names from the extracted text.
- EMPG FY25 tier dollar amounts appear as 'TBD' in the fetched guidance PDF; the $20,625/$35,000/$62,500 figures come from search-index text of the NCDPS EMPG page.
- The NAPSG 'From State to National' FACT presentation (2023) and the NCDIT 'IT Procurement Process Overview' document were not retrievable (403/404).

## Satellite imagery and emergency activation programs that an NC agency can use at no cost (optical, SAR, derived flood/disturbance/nightlights products, activation mechanisms, access layers, Hurricane Helene satellite timeline, commercial tasking as context)
- (high/high) **Maxar / Vantor Open Data Program (ARD STAC + S3)** (Vantor (formerly Maxar Technologies; maxar.com/open-data now 301-redirects to vantor.com/company/open-data-program)) https://vantor.com/company/open-data-program
  Pre- and post-event 30 cm-class WorldView/Legion imagery released as analysis-ready COG tiles for selected sudden-onset disasters. Legacy events 2015-2025 (54 collections, incl. HurricaneHelene-Oct24, Texas Flooding Jul-25, LA Wildfire Jan-25, Hurricane Melissa Oct-25) live under s3://maxar-opendata/events/catalog.json; 2026 events are published under a new catalog at https://vantor-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/events/catalog.json.
  access: Static STAC 1.0 catalog over public S3 (no-sign-request), us-west-2; browse at discover.vantor.com; also mirrored on Source Cooperative (s2.source.coop/maxar/maxar-opendata) | auth: none | licence: CC BY-NC 4.0 (STAC license field CC-BY-NC-4.0). NonCommercial = 'not primarily intended for or directed towards commercial advantage or monetary compensation' (CC legal code 1(9)); the licence has no government carve-out, so NCEM/county use for response is non-commercial in practice but any paid redistribution or use inside a paid product/contract is not covered. Attribution to Maxar/Vantor required. | cadence: Per event; released when (a) sudden-onset disaster, (b) categorized major by scope/humanitarian impact, (c) a Vantor FirstLook event is activated. Helene imagery released from ~2024-09-27 onward. | NC: HurricaneHelene-Oct24 collection: 42 acquisition collections / 616 ARD items; temporal extent 2019-11-09 to 2024-10-06. Post-event acquisitions over the Southeast: 2024-09-27 (1), 09-30 (2), 10-02 (5), 10-05 (9), 10-06 (5); pre-event back to 2019. AOI bboxes in western NC include Asheville/Swannanoa (-82.64..-82.48, 35.52..35.66), Chimney Rock/Lake Lure/Bat Cave (-82.29..-82.06, 35.48..35.69), Spruce Pine (-82.11..-82.03, 35.88..35.94), Boone/Blowing Rock (-81.80..-81.58, 36.13..36.32), Burnsville/Bakersville area (-82.47..-82.35, 36.10..36.18), Marion/Old Fort (-81.99..-81.73, 35.19..35.42), Shelby/Lincolnton area (-81.82..-81.56, 35.60..35.83), plus Newport/Erwin TN and Atlanta/FL AOIs. Already an NC Recon source.
  use (post): Primary post-event evidence channel for the xView2 CNN and change detection (already integrated). Add: watch both catalog roots (maxar-opendata for history, vantor-opendata for 2026+) and surface the CC BY-NC constraint in the export/brief footer; request activation for an NC event by emailing opendata@vantor.com if FirstLook has not already triggered.
  caveats: Non-commercial licence; event selection is at Vantor's discretion (no guaranteed activation for a smaller NC event); the detailed 'activation protocol' page at vantor.com/resources/vantor-open-data-program-activation-protocol/ renders client-side and could not be read; 30 cm optical is cloud-limited (first WNC post-event collects came 1 day after landfall but most were Oct 2-6).
- (medium/high) **Planet Disaster Data / Crisis Response Program (Source Cooperative catalog)** (Planet Labs PBC) https://www.planet.com/disasterdata/
  Select PlanetScope (3 m) and SkySat (~0.8 m) imagery for major disasters released as pre-event / post-event STAC catalogs on Source Cooperative (source.coop/planet/disasterdata). Current repos: Hurricane Melissa Jamaica 2025, Philippines/Venezuela/Colombia earthquakes 2026, Nepal flash flood 2026, Gironde wildfire 2026.
  access: Static STAC 1.1 (Portolan profile) over HTTPS at source.coop; credential-free but requires a real User-Agent header; bucket listing disabled (follow STAC links). Eligibility statement: NGOs, government authorities and international organizations assisting response. | auth: none for published catalogs; no self-serve request form found (contact via Planet Community / sales for new activations) | licence: CC BY-NC 4.0 ('© Planet Labs PBC' attribution required); non-commercial only | cadence: Per event, at Planet's discretion; updates announced on Planet Community / social media | NC: No Hurricane Helene 2024 dataset is listed in the Source Cooperative disasterdata account as of 2026-10-06. Planet publicly showed Asheville before/after PlanetScope frames in Oct 2024 (secondary reports only); no open download located.
  use (post): Secondary optical evidence channel (3 m daily PlanetScope fills gaps between 30 cm collects); ingest via STAC when a Southeast event is published. Request activation early through Planet's emergency-management contacts; do not plan on it as guaranteed.
  caveats: Non-commercial licence; no formal request mechanism published; Helene not in catalog; U.S. events rarely published (none in current list).
- (low/medium) **Planet NICFI Satellite Data Program** (Planet Labs PBC / Norway's International Climate and Forest Initiative) https://developers.google.com/earth-engine/datasets/catalog/projects_planet-nicfi_assets_basemaps_americas
  4.77 m monthly/biannual tropical basemaps (Dec 2015 - Jan 2025 monthly) for tropical forest monitoring; tropics only.
  access: Google Earth Engine (sign-up required) and Planet basemaps API | auth: account + NICFI sign-up | licence: Restricted non-commercial, tropical-forest purposes | cadence: Monthly mosaics ended Jan 2025 per GEE catalog listing | NC: None. Coverage is tropical latitudes only; North Carolina is outside the footprint.
  use (pre): Not usable. Note only so it is not re-evaluated.
  caveats: planet.com/nicfi now redirects to the generic Planet homepage; program status beyond Jan 2025 not confirmed from a primary page.
- (low/high) **Planet Education and Research Program** (Planet Labs PBC) https://www.planet.com/industries/education-and-research/
  Basic tier: 3,000 km2/month PlanetScope & RapidEye for university-affiliated users; Research $15k/yr; Campus $35k/yr.
  access: Planet Explorer / API after application | auth: university email + application | licence: Non-commercial research; no value-added products that might be sold | cadence: Daily PlanetScope archive | NC: Global archive incl. NC, but only for university users.
  use (pre,post): Not available to NCEM or counties ('Nonprofit and government employees are not eligible'). Only path is a partnership where a university (e.g., UNC/NCSU/Appalachian State researcher) pulls imagery for a joint research study, not for operations.
  caveats: Government ineligible; operational use would violate the research licence.
- (medium/high) **Umbra Open Data Program (SAR)** (Umbra Lab Inc.) https://umbra.space/open-data/
  Very high resolution X-band SAR spotlight imagery (25 cm-1 m; 16 cm in registry text) over 20+ repeat time-series sites and 1000+ one-off collects including emergency-response collects (e.g. sar-data/tasks/Texas_Floods_2025-08/).
  access: Public S3 bucket s3://umbra-open-data-catalog (us-west-2, no-sign-request); static STAC 1.1 catalog at https://s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/umbra-open-data-catalog/stac/catalog.json (children 2024, 2025, by month/day); also in GEE community catalog | auth: none | licence: CC BY 4.0 (registry and umbra.space pricing page) — government and commercial use allowed with attribution | cadence: Irregular; time-series sites updated frequently, disaster collects at Umbra's discretion | NC: No Hurricane Helene, Asheville, or North Carolina collects found in the 2024-09, 2024-10 catalogs or task prefixes. Time-series sites listed are outside NC.
  use (during,post): Opportunistic SAR evidence channel (works through cloud, day/night) if Umbra tasks an NC event; also the best free training set for a SAR-based change/debris detector. No request path for free collects is published.
  caveats: Spotlight footprints are small (5x5 km); no NC/Helene data; no guaranteed activation.
- (medium/medium) **Capella Space Open Data (SAR)** (Capella Space) https://registry.opendata.aws/capella_opendata/
  ~1000+ Capella SAR collects (Spotlight, Sliding Spotlight, Stripmap; SLC/GEO/GEC/SICD/SIDD/CPHD) organised by product type, mode, use case and capital city; 'Disaster Response' collection has 508 items with collects through 2026-06-29 (2021 Marshall fire, 2023 Turkey quakes, 2023 Derna flood, volcanoes).
  access: Public S3 s3://capella-open-data/data/ (us-west-2, no-sign-request); static STAC 1.0 catalog https://capella-open-data.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/stac/catalog.json; Felt map; also via Capella Console with free account | auth: none for S3/STAC; Console account for cart download | licence: CC BY 4.0 per AWS registry; NOTE the STAC collection 'license' field reads 'proprietary' — confirm with opendata@capellaspace.com before relying on CC BY | cadence: Quarterly additions per registry; latest items dated June 2026 | NC: No North Carolina or Helene collects identified; disaster items are global.
  use (during,post): SAR training/validation data and opportunistic evidence if Capella tasks an NC event. Data Cooperative / Data Grant tiers (research/nonprofit) exist per Capella press but tier pages could not be fetched.
  caveats: Licence discrepancy (registry CC BY 4.0 vs STAC 'proprietary'); support articles on Open Data access and pricing returned 404.
- (medium/high) **ICEYE Flood Insights / Flood Rapid Impact (incl. Hurricane Helene releases)** (ICEYE) https://iceye.com/solutions/government/flood-insights
  Commercial SAR-derived flood extent, flood depth and building-level impact (GIS-ready) within hours of flood peak, daily updates, via ISS app, API, and Esri integrations; South Carolina EMD cited as public-sector user. For Helene ICEYE acquired 350+ SAR images, first flood extent/depth release 2024-09-28 (FL west coast), third release 2024-09-30 included Asheville, NC extent/depth; final (9th) release counted >160,000 buildings impacted across FL/NC/TN/GA/VA/SC/WV, ~17,000 with >5 ft water.
  access: Commercial subscription (ISS app / API / Esri partner); public PDF briefings and media images only | auth: paid | licence: Proprietary; public bulletins for media use only | cadence: Event-driven, daily updates during a flood | NC: Helene 2024 flood extent/depth for Asheville and the Southern Appalachians (Sept 30 release); NC not a listed customer (SCEMD is).
  use (during,post): Only free artefacts are PDFs, so no ingest path without a contract. If NCEM procures Flood Insights, NC Recon's flood-depth channel (currently without a water-surface source) could ingest building-level depth via the ISS API. Flag as paid.
  caveats: Paid; no pricing published; depth is modelled from SAR extent + DEM + ground data; blog says 150+ images while the Sept 30 PDF says 350+.
- (high/high) **Copernicus Data Space Ecosystem - Sentinel-1 GRD/SLC and Sentinel-2 L1C/L2A (STAC, OData, S3, Sentinel Hub, openEO)** (European Commission / ESA (operated by T-Systems, CloudFerro, Sinergise, VITO)) https://documentation.dataspace.copernicus.eu/APIs/STAC.html
  Authoritative free archive and live feed of Sentinel-1 (GRD, SLC, OCN; GRD global from Oct 2014; SLC global from Feb 2021) and Sentinel-2 (L1C from 2015; L2A with SCL cloud mask; quarterly mosaics), plus Copernicus DEM. STAC API v1.1 root https://stac.dataspace.copernicus.eu/v1/ (legacy catalogue.dataspace.copernicus.eu/stac deprecated 2025-11-17). Constellation (SentiWiki, 2026): Sentinel-2B + 2C nominal (2A extended campaign since Mar 2025), 5-day equatorial revisit, 290 km swath; Sentinel-1A/1C/1D in transition — 1C acquiring since 2025-03-26, 1D data from 2026-04-17 (ASF: downloadable from 2026-06-03), 1C repositioned 2026-06-09..23, 1A terminated 2026-06-29, leaving 1C+1D with 6-day exact repeat (pattern shifted 1 day). Sentinel-1 GRD land products published within 24 h, in practice a few hours.
  access: STAC API (search anonymous), OData, S3 (eodata endpoint with token), Sentinel Hub process/OGC APIs, openEO, Copernicus Browser | auth: free account for downloads/S3/Sentinel Hub; search is anonymous | licence: Copernicus Sentinel data licence: free, full and open; attribution 'Contains modified Copernicus Sentinel data (year)' | cadence: Sentinel-1 GRD within ~hours to 24 h of sensing; Sentinel-2 L1C/L2A systematic (hours-to-day scale; exact CDSE timeliness figure not stated on fetched pages) | NC: Full NC coverage. Sentinel-1 IW VV+VH over North America every 12 days per satellite (6 days with two satellites); Sentinel-2 ~5 days at the equator, more often at NC latitude from overlapping swaths. Helene: S1 passes over NC pre 2024-09-14/16, post 2024-09-26, 09-28, 10-03 (NASA DRCS); first mostly clear Sentinel-2 over Asheville 2024-10-02 (6 days after landfall).
  use (pre,during,post): Pre: baseline S1 backscatter/coherence stacks and S2 cloud-free composites per county (pre-computed and cached offline). During/post: S1 GRD change detection for flood extent (open water) and debris/landslide hotspots at 10-20 m; S2 for NDVI change, tarp/roof colour at 10 m once clear. Quotas (free tier): 12 TB/30 days transfer, 20 MB/s, 4 concurrent connections, 50,000 OData/STAC requests/month, 2,000 S3 req/min, 10,000 Sentinel Hub PU/month — enough for county-scale pulls, not statewide reprocessing.
  caveats: Sentinel-2 L2A publication latency not confirmed from a primary page; cloud cover in the week after a hurricane is the binding constraint (NASA: 'clouds have blocked most optical imagery' for Helene through Oct 2); S1 6-day cadence means a 0-6 day wait for the first post-event pass; the S1 constellation transition in June 2026 shifted pass days.
- (medium/high) **Copernicus Emergency Management Service - Rapid Mapping (on-demand mapping)** (European Commission DG ECHO / JRC; activations routed through the ERCC) https://mapping.emergency.copernicus.eu/about/who-can-request-the-service/
  24/7 satellite-based delineation and grading maps (flood extent, damage) delivered within hours to days after an authorised request; products publicly downloadable except sensitive activations. Historic US activations requested by FEMA: Hurricane Harvey 2017 (17 flood delineation maps), Irma 2017 (EMSR241, 65 products), Florence 2018 (EMSR311) and Michael 2018 (EMSR322) — 54 maps to FEMA in 2018; US State Department publicly praised the service.
  access: Online Service Request Form at https://mapping.emergency.copernicus.eu/srf/ (Authorised Users only), auto-forwarded to ERCC; products downloadable from activation pages | auth: Authorised User status (EU member-state national focal points, participating-state focal points, EC DGs, EEAS Situation Room, EU delegations). A US state cannot request directly; 'must coordinate first with the relevant Authorised User and channel your request through them' — in practice FEMA has been the US requester. | licence: Products free to view/download (sensitive activations excepted); source imagery licence varies by provider | cadence: Per activation; first products within hours to days | NC: Hurricane Florence 2018 (EMSR311) covered the Carolinas. No evidence found of an activation for Hurricane Helene 2024 (activation list is client-rendered and could not be scraped).
  use (during,post): Ground-truth / comparison layer: ingest EMS flood delineation and grading vectors when FEMA triggers an activation for an NC event; NC Recon should include an 'ask FEMA Region IV to request Copernicus EMS' step in its activation checklist.
  caveats: Not directly requestable by NC; FEMA discretion; Helene activation status unknown.
- (high/high) **International Charter 'Space and Major Disasters' (via USGS as US Authorized User)** (Charter member space agencies (17+ incl. USGS, NOAA, ESA, CNES, DLR, JAXA, ISRO; Planet is a data contributor since 2018)) https://www.usgs.gov/emergency-operations-portal/activation-process
  Voluntary mechanism that priority-tasks member satellites (optical and SAR) and delivers imagery plus value-added maps for a major disaster, at no cost, over an activation lasting typically several weeks. USGS 'as an Authorized User (AU) for the International Charter, may request activations on behalf of Federal, State, local, or international agencies' and submits US domestic activations 'on behalf of responsible emergency management authorities'.
  access: NC agency emails eo_liaison@usgs.gov with point of contact, nature of event, coordinates or KML/KMZ/SHP, and event date (English); USGS activates; imagery is distributed to the response community (historically via HDDS) and products posted on disasterscharter.org activation pages | auth: request through USGS (no account for NC); Charter End User agreement handled by USGS | licence: Satellite data provided at no cost under each provider's Charter terms; redistribution of raw commercial imagery is restricted to the activation's users; derived maps usually public | cadence: Per activation; first acquisitions typically within 1-3 days; activation closes after 'several weeks' | NC: Hurricane Florence 2018 and other Carolinas events have been Charter activations historically; a Helene 2024 activation could not be confirmed (Charter site is client-rendered). Secondary sources indicate the Charter was activated for Hurricane Milton on 2024-10-10.
  use (during,post): Trigger + evidence channel: build an 'Activation checklist' in the Operations screen that drafts the eo_liaison@usgs.gov request with the town AOI as KML; ingest resulting imagery/maps from HDDS once posted. Also the only free route to tasked commercial SAR (ICEYE/Capella contribute to the Charter via CSDA/members).
  caveats: USGS decides whether to request; data access for county staff depends on HDDS restricted-dataset approval; no SLA.
- (high/high) **USGS Emergency Operations Portal: HDDS Explorer, Collection Management Tool (CMT), Event Support** (U.S. Geological Survey, EROS / National Geospatial Program) https://www.usgs.gov/emergency-operations-portal/event-support
  Single point of distribution for USGS-hosted pre- and post-event imagery per emergency event: moderate-resolution satellite (Landsat), high-resolution commercial satellite (restricted), high-resolution aerial, lidar, county orthos as baseline. HDDS Explorer (hddsexplorer.usgs.gov) supports search by event/date/location, KML/SHP/CSV/FGDC export, RSS, standing requests, WMS creation, bulk download client. CMT (cmt.usgs.gov) shows events currently supported and lets users submit new AOI collection requests; 'one agency may submit the initial request for support, but the imagery that becomes available on HDDS can be shared across the response community'. Adhoc and Restricted Adhoc datasets have moved into the USGS Data Download Service (DDS).
  access: Web portal + bulk download client; CMT for requests; email eo_liaison@usgs.gov (Emergency Response Liaison) | auth: free USGS/EROS account to download; separate approval for restricted (commercial/NextView-licensed) datasets — typically government responders | licence: USGS data public domain; commercial imagery restricted per vendor/NextView licence to approved government users | cadence: Per event; data posted as collected (hours to days) | NC: Historic NC hurricane events (Florence, Matthew, etc.) exist in HDDS; whether a Hurricane Helene event was stood up could not be confirmed (explorer requires login; no search budget left).
  use (pre,during,post): Distribution and evidence channel: (1) NC Recon's activation checklist should submit the town AOI through CMT/eo_liaison so USGS can task/collect; (2) poll HDDS RSS for the event and ingest aerial/satellite deliveries; (3) restricted 30 cm commercial scenes can be used inside the agency but not re-exported in public products.
  caveats: Portal pages are terse; eligibility of county staff for restricted datasets must be confirmed with eo_liaison; parts of HDDS are being consolidated into DDS.
- (high/high) **NASA Disasters Program - Disaster Response Coordination System (DRCS) and Disasters Mapping Portal** (NASA Earth Action Disasters Program (LaRC project office; GSFC, MSFC, JSC, JPL, Ames)) https://science.nasa.gov/earth-science/earth-action/disasters/
  Request-driven system that produces open geospatial products for federal, state, local and NGO responders and publishes them on the Disasters Mapping Portal (maps.disasters.nasa.gov -> disasters-nasa.hub.arcgis.com; ArcGIS Hub API and REST services). Helene 2024: DRCS supported 2024-09-24 to 10-24, working directly with FEMA, NC/FL/GA/TN state EM agencies, Red Cross, USGS, NOAA. Products: Sentinel-1 landslide density heatmap (GSFC Landslides Team, post 2024-09-28, GEE method Handwerger 2022); Sentinel-1 RGB/RTC flood extents for NC/SC (pre 09-16; post 09-26, 09-28, 10-03) by ASF; OPERA DIST-ALERT-HLS disturbance from HLS of 10-02 (ARIA/OPERA, published as 'aria_dist'); Black Marble nighttime lights daily from 09-18 with 09-22 vs 10-02 comparison and experimental zip-code radiance-change power-outage map; Sentinel-2 colour-infrared 2023-10-18 vs 2024-10-14; UAVSAR airborne flood collects; ISS handheld photos. Hub items also include 'Hurricane Helene NOAA' ImageServer and a 'Helene Damage Flood' raster layer.
  access: ArcGIS Hub (disasters-nasa.hub.arcgis.com; API v3 datasets?q=helene), ArcGIS REST MapServer/ImageServer on maps.disasters.nasa.gov (ags03/ags04 folders, e.g. hurricane_helene_2024/aria_dist), story maps; request support via the Disasters program (contact path not published on fetched pages) | auth: none for portal/REST; DRCS support by request | licence: NASA products open; Sentinel-derived layers 'Contains modified Copernicus Sentinel data (2024) processed by ESA' | cadence: Per activation; products within 1-3 days of satellite passes; Black Marble daily | NC: Explicit western NC products for Helene listed above; DRCS is not currently activated (Oct 2026) but supported 7 storms in 2024.
  use (during,post): Evidence and map layers: subscribe to the Hub API for an active event folder and pull landslide heatmap, S1 flood extent, DIST disturbance and Black Marble change as ArcGIS REST/COG layers into the fusion model (landslide/flood priors) and the Conditions map. Add DRCS to the activation checklist (ask NCEM GIS to request support via FEMA/NASA liaison).
  caveats: Direct REST folder listings returned 404 to scripted requests (services exist but discovery is via Hub); request-support contact page is client-rendered; products are rapid-response, uncorrected guidance.
- (medium/medium) **NASA ARIA Damage Proxy Maps (JPL/Caltech) - Helene status** (NASA JPL ARIA project with OPERA) https://maps.disasters.nasa.gov/ags04/rest/services/hurricane_helene_2024/aria_dist/MapServer/1/iteminfo
  ARIA normally produces SAR-coherence Damage Proxy Maps (e.g., Hurricane Ian 2022 aria_dpm service). For Helene the ARIA/OPERA teams instead delivered an 'aria_dist' disturbance map derived from OPERA DIST-ALERT-HLS (vegetation cover loss, HLS scene of 2024-10-02) plus RTC-S1 and DSWx-S1 mosaics and DSWx-S1 water-change maps; no classic coherence DPM for Helene was found.
  access: ArcGIS REST on maps.disasters.nasa.gov; ARIA products otherwise via aria-share.jpl.nasa.gov (client-rendered) and PO.DAAC | auth: none | licence: NASA open; contains modified Copernicus Sentinel data | cadence: Per event, typically 1-5 days after post-event pass | NC: Helene 2024 disturbance (aria_dist) over the affected Southeast incl. WNC, dated 2024-10-02 HLS.
  use (post): Evidence prior: structure-level damage probability can be conditioned on the 30 m disturbance/DPM class around each footprint; keep as optional layer because 30 m is coarse for single houses.
  caveats: Helene map is DIST-based (optical, needs clear sky) not SAR coherence; exact service metadata could not be fetched (404 to scripted requests).
- (high/high) [verdict: confirmed] **NASA Harmonized Landsat Sentinel-2 (HLS) v2.0 - HLSL30 / HLSS30** (NASA (LP DAAC, IMPACT)) https://www.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/projects/hls
  30 m surface reflectance harmonised across Landsat 8/9 OLI and Sentinel-2A/B/C MSI with Fmask cloud/shadow mask; global land; ~1.4-1.6 day revisit with five satellites; 2-3 day latency; vegetation-index products added Feb 2025; low-latency products targeted ~2027.
  access: CMR-STAC (https://cmr.earthdata.nasa.gov/stac/LPCLOUD), Earthdata Search, direct S3 (us-west-2, temporary credentials), AppEEARS | auth: free Earthdata Login | licence: NASA EOSDIS data use policy: free and open | cadence: Daily tiles; 2-3 day latency | NC: Full NC; HLS of 2024-10-02 was the first usable post-Helene optical over WNC (used for DIST-ALERT and DSWx-HLS).
  use (pre,during,post): Pre-event cloud-free baseline composites per town; post-event NDVI/NBR change and water index at 30 m as a coarse evidence channel and as a trigger for which towns to review first; feeds coverage-gap analysis (where no clear optical yet).
  caveats: Cloud-limited; 30 m too coarse for per-structure calls.
  verifier: Could not refute. Every substantive field matches primary NASA sources fetched today (2026-10-07): both v2.0 collections are ACTIVE with granules produced within the last 24 h; known-issues docs are dated April 2026; CMR-STAC, Earthdata Search, direct S3 (us-west-2, temporary creds) and AppEEARS all verified live; Earthdata Login gating verified by 302/307 to URS; licence is CC0 per the linked NASA data-use guidance. Refinements rather than corrections: (a) revisit - the cited Earthdata page gives 1.6 d (1.4 d with S2C) as a mean, while hls.gsfc.nasa.gov gives "global median repeat frequency o
- (medium/high) **Landsat Collection 2 (USGS LandsatLook STAC / EarthExplorer / AWS usgs-landsat)** (USGS EROS) https://registry.opendata.aws/usgs-landsat/
  Landsat 8/9 OLI/TIRS 30 m (15 m pan) Level-1 and Level-2 (surface reflectance, surface temperature), ARD tiles; 16-day repeat per satellite, 8-day combined; Level-1 available within ~6 h via EarthExplorer; Level-2 24-72 h after Tier processing; Landsat 7 decommissioned 2025-06-04; ~1,500 scenes/day.
  access: STAC API https://landsatlook.usgs.gov/stac-server/collections; EarthExplorer/M2M API; S3 s3://usgs-landsat/collection02/ (requester-pays, us-west-2); also Earth Search and Planetary Computer mirrors | auth: EarthExplorer login for downloads; AWS requester-pays | licence: No restrictions; courtesy credit to USGS | cadence: 8-day combined revisit; L1 ~6 h latency | NC: Full NC; pre-Helene Landsat 8 of 2024-08-30 used by NASA Earth Observatory as the baseline for Asheville.
  use (pre,post): Baseline optical and thermal (surface temperature anomalies for debris/mud fields); redundant with HLS so medium priority; use LandsatLook STAC (no cost) rather than requester-pays S3.
  caveats: Cloud-limited; AWS bucket is requester-pays.
- (high/high) **OPERA DSWx-HLS (Dynamic Surface Water Extent from HLS) v1** (NASA JPL OPERA / PO.DAAC) https://www.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/projects/nsite/solutions/opera-near-global-dswx-product-suite
  30 m surface water extent classification (open water, partial water, confidence, diagnostic layers; 10 GeoTIFF layers per MGRS tile) from HLS; validated record from April 2023, back-processed to 2016; ~3-day revisit; near-global land.
  access: Earthdata Search / CMR (C2617126679-POCLOUD), PO.DAAC S3 podaac-ops-cumulus-protected/OPERA_L3_DSWX-HLS_V1 (us-west-2), podaac data-subscriber, NASA Worldview | auth: free Earthdata Login | licence: CC BY 4.0 (AWS registry) / NASA open | cadence: ~3-day revisit; product latency not stated on fetched pages (HLS input is 2-3 days) | NC: Full NC; of limited use for Helene because of clouds (NASA: DSWx-HLS 'had limited utility due to cloud cover').
  use (during,post): Flood-extent evidence channel for the (currently empty) flood-depth input: intersect WTR layer with structure footprints and a DEM to derive inundation; use as a trigger for review queues.
  caveats: Optical, cloud-limited; 30 m misses narrow mountain streams.
- (high/high) **OPERA DSWx-S1 (Dynamic Surface Water Extent from Sentinel-1) v1** (NASA JPL OPERA / PO.DAAC) https://cmr.earthdata.nasa.gov/search/collections.umm_json?short_name=OPERA_L3_DSWX-S1_V1&page_size=1
  30 m radar-based open-water extent over MGRS tiles, 6-12 day revisit, 'irrespective of cloud conditions and daylight'; maps inland water bodies > 3 ha and > 200 m wide; 3 GeoTIFF layers (water classification + confidence); forward production began September 2024 (i.e., during Helene) and record starts 2023-12-01. ARIA/OPERA used RTC-S1, DSWx-S1 and DSWx-S1 difference (water change) maps for Helene (comparison 2024-09-14 vs 2024-09-26).
  access: Earthdata Search / CMR (C2949811996-POCLOUD), PO.DAAC S3 podaac-ops-cumulus-protected/OPERA_L3_DSWX-S1_V1, Worldview | auth: free Earthdata Login | licence: CC BY 4.0 (AWS registry) / NASA open; contains modified Copernicus Sentinel data | cadence: 6-12 day revisit (6-day with 1C+1D after June 2026); product latency after RTC availability not stated in fetched spec text | NC: Full NC; Helene products exist for the Southeast (Sept 26 and later passes).
  use (during,post): Cloud-independent flood-extent channel; most valuable in the first week when optical is blocked. Limitations (3 ha / 200 m minimum) mean it captures the French Broad/Swannanoa/Catawba floodplains but not small creeks — treat as lower-bound inundation.
  caveats: Steep terrain causes radar shadow/layover in WNC valleys; narrow rivers below size threshold; latency unknown.
- (medium/high) **OPERA DIST-ALERT-HLS (and DIST-S1, DIST-ANN)** (NASA JPL OPERA / LP DAAC) https://www.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/projects/nsite/solutions/opera-near-global-surface-dist-product-suite
  30 m per-pixel vegetation disturbance alerts (19 layers: status, anomaly, confidence, dates, counts) from HLS, in production since Feb 2023 with record from 2016; sub-weekly revisit; annual DIST-ANN 2023-2025. DIST-S1 (Sentinel-1 radar disturbance, 30 m, 6-12 day) is scheduled for production in 2026 (JPL product page: March 2026; sample products for LA fires Jan 2025).
  access: Earthdata Search / CMR (C2746980408-LPCLOUD), LP DAAC S3 lp-protected/OPERA_L3_DIST-ALERT-HLS_V1.001 | auth: free Earthdata Login | licence: NASA data use policy (free and open); CC BY 4.0 per registry | cadence: Sub-weekly (HLS); DIST-S1 6-12 day once operational | NC: Full NC; Helene disturbance from HLS 2024-10-02 delivered via DRCS (aria_dist).
  use (post): Landslide/debris-flow and tree-fall proxy: vegetation loss polygons near structures raise prior for Major/Destroyed and flag road isolation candidates; DIST-S1 (when live) gives the same without clouds.
  caveats: Detects vegetation loss, not building damage; cloud-limited until DIST-S1 ships; DIST-S1 CMR collection not yet present (short_name query returned none).
- (high/high) **OPERA RTC-S1 and ASF HyP3 on-demand Sentinel-1 processing (RTC, InSAR, OPERA RTC-S1, ARIA GUNW)** (NASA JPL OPERA (product) / Alaska Satellite Facility DAAC (archive + HyP3)) https://hyp3-docs.asf.alaska.edu/
  RTC-S1: 30 m radiometrically terrain-corrected Sentinel-1 burst backscatter (Copernicus GLO-30 DEM), near-global, record from 2016-04-14, distributed by ASF. HyP3: cloud processing that turns Sentinel-1 GRD/SLC into RTC, InSAR, burst InSAR, OPERA RTC-S1 and ARIA GUNW on demand; HyP3 Basic gives 8,000 free credits/month per Earthdata user; HyP3+ credits $0.05 each; outputs retained 14 days (30 for HyP3+); jobs take tens of minutes to hours.
  access: ASF Vertex (search.asf.alaska.edu), asf_search Python, hyp3_sdk, REST API; Earthdata Search | auth: free Earthdata Login | licence: NASA EOSDIS free and open; Sentinel attribution | cadence: RTC-S1 follows Sentinel-1 SLC availability; HyP3 jobs minutes-hours | NC: Full NC; ASF produced the Sentinel-1 RTC/RGB flood composites for NC/SC used by NASA DRCS for Helene.
  use (pre,during,post): Lets NC Recon run its own SAR change detection without hosting SNAP: queue pre/post RTC pairs for the town AOI via hyp3_sdk, difference backscatter for flood/debris, and (later) coherence change for structural damage. Fits the 'no paid services' constraint within the 8,000-credit monthly budget.
  caveats: HyP3 job names are public; outputs expire; credit budget caps statewide processing.
- (medium/high) **Microsoft Planetary Computer (free data catalog + STAC API)** (Microsoft) https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1/collections/sentinel-1-rtc
  Free STAC API (https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1) over Azure-hosted Sentinel-2 L2A (COG, West Europe), Sentinel-1 RTC (Catalyst-processed, CC-BY-4.0, global from 2014), Sentinel-1 GRD, Landsat C2 L2, NAIP, 3DEP lidar/DEM, Copernicus DEM, HLS and others. The hosted Hub (JupyterHub) was retired 2024-06-06; Microsoft stated data and APIs 'will remain available and unchanged' but gave no long-term guarantee. Planetary Computer Pro (GA 2026) is a separate paid Azure service for hosting your own data.
