  access: STAC API + Azure Blob with short-lived SAS tokens from the planetary-computer package / token API; STAC search anonymous | auth: none for search; SAS token (free; sentinel-1-rtc requires a Planetary Computer account to obtain tokens) | licence: Per collection: Sentinel data (Copernicus terms, marked 'proprietary' in STAC), sentinel-1-rtc CC-BY-4.0, Landsat/NAIP/3DEP public domain | cadence: Collections updated as upstream data arrive (Sentinel-2 L2A ongoing); sentinel-1-rtc ongoing | NC: Full NC in all listed collections.
  use (pre,during,post): Access layer: the only free source of analysis-ready Sentinel-1 RTC as COGs with a STAC API (no processing step), plus 3DEP lidar COPC for the planned lidar DEM-of-difference. Treat as a mirror, not the system of record, given the Hub retirement and 'no guarantee' statement.
  caveats: SAS tokens expire; Sentinel-2 assets stored in West Europe (latency); platform future beyond Microsoft's statement is not guaranteed.
- (high/high) **AWS Open Data - Earth Search STAC API (Element 84) and Sentinel buckets** (Element 84 (Earth Search, sentinel-cogs), Sinergise/AWS (Sentinel-1 GRD bucket), AWS Open Data Sponsorship Program) https://earth-search.aws.element84.com/v1
  STAC 1.0 API over public AWS datasets: sentinel-2-l2a and sentinel-2-c1-l2a (COGs in s3://sentinel-cogs, us-west-2, ingested within hours of Copernicus publication), sentinel-2-l1c, sentinel-1-grd (s3://sentinel-s1-l1c, eu-central-1, GRD only, static STAC at sentinel-s1-l1c-stac.s3.amazonaws.com), landsat-c2-l2, naip, cop-dem-glo-30/90.
  access: STAC API (anonymous) + S3 no-sign-request | auth: none | licence: Copernicus free and open (Sentinel); USGS/USDA public domain (Landsat, NAIP) | cadence: Within hours of Copernicus publication (Sentinel); Landsat daily | NC: Full NC for all collections; NAIP gives 60 cm-1 m pre-event baseline statewide.
  use (pre,during,post): Default access layer for Sentinel-2 L2A and Sentinel-1 GRD and NAIP (no account, no quota), avoiding CDSE download quotas; use CDSE as fallback and for SLC.
  caveats: Sentinel-1 bucket is GRD only (no SLC for InSAR/coherence) and in eu-central-1 (egress latency); Earth Search is community-run without an SLA.
- (low/high) **Google Earth Engine (noncommercial eligibility and paid plans)** (Google Cloud) https://earthengine.google.com/noncommercial/
  Hosted analysis platform with Sentinel-1/2, Landsat, HLS, NICFI, VIIRS catalog. Noncommercial (free) use for government agencies is limited to agencies from Least Developed Countries, Indigenous governments, or scholarly research; explicitly excluded: 'repeated production of data products', 'tooling for management, policy, or web applications', 'datasets, apps, services that are maintained on an on-going basis', and 'operational workloads'. Paid plans (cloud.google.com/earth-engine/pricing): Limited (usage fees only), Basic $500/month (100 batch + 33 online EECU-hours, 100 GB), Professional $2,000/month (500 batch + 166 online EECU-hours, 1 TB, SLA), Premium (contact sales); compute $0.40/EECU-hour (first 10,000 h), storage ~$0.0000356/GiB-hour.
  access: Earth Engine API/Code Editor via Google Cloud project | auth: Google Cloud account; paid plan for operational government use | licence: Google Cloud terms; underlying datasets per provider | cadence: n/a (platform) | NC: Global catalog incl. NC; NASA GSFC used GEE for the Helene landslide heatmap (research use).
  use (pre,during,post): Not a no-cost path for NCEM operations: an operational NC Recon deployment would need at least the Limited/Basic plan. Keep GEE unconfigured in the product; optionally let a university partner run research analyses.
  caveats: Paid for government operational use; vendor lock-in.
- (medium/medium) **NOAA/NESDIS-CIMSS VIIRS and ABI near-real-time flood products (RealEarth / floods.ssec.wisc.edu)** (NOAA NESDIS STAR with UW-Madison SSEC/CIMSS (George Mason University algorithm)) https://floods.ssec.wisc.edu/
  Satellite flood extent (water fraction per pixel) for situational awareness: VIIRS 375 m NRT flood map from Suomi-NPP/NOAA-20 (2-3 daytime passes/day, ~3 h latency), daily and 5-day composites; GOES ABI 1 km hourly flood map (America 135W-17W); joint VIIRS/ABI 375 m-1 km daily product (available ~07Z). Built for emergency-response stakeholders. 2020 user guide is the fetched primary document; site is live (HTTP 200) in Oct 2026.
  access: RealEarth web viewer (floods.ssec.wisc.edu) and SSEC product pages; GeoTIFF/shapefile/KML downloads per the user guide | auth: none | licence: US Government / NOAA public data (not stated on fetched pages) | cadence: VIIRS ~3 h after each daytime pass; ABI hourly; composites daily | NC: CONUS incl. NC; mountains and clouds limit usefulness for WNC but the ABI composite fills cloud gaps.
  use (during,post): Statewide 'during' trigger on the Conditions map: VIIRS/ABI flood fraction identifies which counties/towns are flooded within hours so analysts can pre-stage structure pulls before 30 cm imagery exists. Too coarse for structure-level evidence.
  caveats: Daytime only; false positives from tides/marsh/agriculture; water reference map from 2015-era masks; machine-readable endpoint details not re-verified in 2026.
- (medium/medium) **NASA GIBS / Worldview (browse imagery tiles)** (NASA EOSDIS) https://nasa-gibs.github.io/gibs-api-docs/access-basics/
  Open WMTS/WMS/TWMS/XYZ tile services at https://gibs.earthdata.nasa.gov for 1,200+ visualised products (MODIS/VIIRS true colour, Black Marble, HLS, OPERA DSWx via Worldview), many 'available within hours of being acquired'; projections EPSG:4326/3857/3413/3031.
  access: WMTS/WMS/TWMS endpoints (no auth); Worldview UI (Earthdata Login only for downloads) | auth: none for tiles | licence: NASA open | cadence: NRT layers within hours; standard layers daily | NC: Global incl. NC.
  use (during,post): Low-cost Conditions-map basemap overlays (daily VIIRS true colour, Black Marble, DSWx) via WMTS in MapLibre — no processing, just situational context and cloud-cover check before scheduling pulls.
  caveats: Visualisation tiles, not analysis-grade data; specific layer latencies not confirmed.
- (medium/medium) **NASA Black Marble nighttime lights (VNP46A2) - as satellite outage proxy** (NASA GSFC Black Marble Science Team / LAADS DAAC) https://cmr.earthdata.nasa.gov/search/collections.umm_json?short_name=VNP46A2&page_size=1
  Daily moonlight- and atmosphere-corrected VIIRS DNB nighttime lights at 15 arc-second (~500 m) with gap-filled layer and quality flags (HDF5). NASA DRCS published Black Marble daily from 2024-09-18 for Helene and built an experimental zip-code radiance-change power-outage map (Aug baseline vs 2024-10-02).
  access: LAADS DAAC / Earthdata Search; GIBS visualisation; DRCS ArcGIS services during activations | auth: free Earthdata Login | licence: NASA open | cadence: Daily (standard); NRT availability not confirmed here | NC: Full NC; Helene outage visualisations for NC exist on the Disasters portal.
  use (during,post): Input for the planned 'nighttime-lights outage' feature: daily radiance change per tract as a displacement/outage indicator and a trigger for which towns to assess first.
  caveats: Cloud and moonlight artefacts; 500 m resolution; belongs mainly to the outage dimension.
- (low/medium) **NASA Commercial Smallsat Data Acquisition (CSDA) program** (NASA Earth Science Division) https://science.nasa.gov/earth-science/csda/
  NASA purchases commercial data (Planet, Maxar/Vantor, Umbra, Capella, ICEYE, BlackSky, Airbus, Spire, GHGSat, Wyvern, OroraTech, PlanetiQ and others; 23 vendors) under three EULA tiers: US Government (Title 5 U.S.C. 101-105 federal entities, EOP, Congress; unlimited copies and derived products within authorised users, no commercial use), US Government Plus (adds foreign/intergovernmental partners for USG purposes), and Public EULA for releasable items. Access through the Satellite Data Explorer after Authorized User registration.
  access: CSDA Satellite Data Explorer / SMCE with Earthdata Login after approval | auth: Authorized User registration; federal affiliation or NASA funding | licence: Vendor EULAs as above; data 'does not become public domain' | cadence: Archive and limited tasking by NASA | NC: Archive is global/opportunistic; no NC-specific holdings confirmed.
  use (pre,post): Not directly available to a state agency (US Government tier is federal). Path: federal partner (FEMA, USGS, NASA DRCS) pulls CSDA commercial scenes for the NC event and shares derived products; NC Recon should accept derived rasters/vectors from them rather than raw CSDA imagery.
  caveats: Eligibility of state/local users not stated; redistribution restricted.
- (low/medium) **Commercial tasking price context (Umbra public price list; SkySat/Capella via resellers)** (Umbra (public pricing), Planet SkySat and Capella via SkyWatch/UP42/Apollo Mapping resellers) https://umbra.space/pricing/
  Context only. Umbra per-scene SAR pricing (CC BY 4.0 licence on purchased data): 5x5 km spotlight 1 m $675-2,200; 0.5 m $950-2,600; 0.35 m $1,750-3,500; 0.25 m $3,250-4,900 (price rises with number of looks); larger 'natural footprint' $1,500-5,650; dwell $4,750. Secondary reseller listings: Planet SkySat 50 cm tasking ~$12/km2 (25 km2 minimum), Capella 50 cm spotlight ~$166/km2 (25 km2 min), 1.2 m stripmap ~$13.85/km2 (100 km2 min); SkyFi lists 16-30 cm optical tasking ~$30/km2 (25 km2 min) — the SkyFi PDF URL now returns 404.
  access: Vendor web stores and reseller platforms (SkyWatch, UP42, SkyFi, Apollo Mapping) | auth: paid | licence: Umbra CC BY 4.0; others proprietary per-licence tiers | cadence: Tasking turnaround typically 1-3 days (not verified) | NC: Tasking available anywhere incl. NC on request.
  use (post): Procurement planning only: a town-scale (25 km2) SAR or 50 cm optical task costs on the order of $700-4,000 per scene; state could pre-negotiate an emergency BPA. Not for the no-cost baseline.
  caveats: Only Umbra figures come from a primary page; reseller prices are secondary and change often.
- (low/high) **NASA Earth Observatory - 'Flash Floods Swamp North Carolina' (Helene optical timeline record)** (NASA Earth Observatory) https://science.nasa.gov/earth/earth-observatory/flash-floods-swamp-north-carolina-153416
  Published 2024-10-05: Landsat 8 OLI 2024-08-30 (pre) vs Sentinel-2 MSI 2024-10-02 (post) over Asheville, Old Fort, French Broad and Swannanoa rivers; states 'clouds have blocked most optical imagery of the flooding collected by NASA satellites so far'.
  access: Web article, images downloadable | auth: none | licence: NASA images public; Sentinel imagery under Copernicus terms | cadence: static | NC: Asheville / Swannanoa / Old Fort, NC.
  use (post): Documentary evidence for the plan: expect ~6 days before the first clear Sentinel-2 over WNC after a late-September hurricane; design the first-week workflow around SAR, aerial (NOAA NGS) and 30 cm commercial collects.
  caveats: Single-event anecdote, not a climatology.
### insights
- Maxar's Open Data Program is now Vantor's; maxar.com/open-data 301-redirects to https://vantor.com/company/open-data-program. Legacy events (2015-2025, 54 collections incl. HurricaneHelene-Oct24) remain at https://maxar-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/events/catalog.json; 2026 events are published at https://vantor-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/events/catalog.json. NC Recon must poll both roots.
- Activation criteria for the Vantor Open Data Program (vantor.com/company/open-data-program): the event is a sudden-onset disaster, categorized as major by geographic scope, humanitarian impact and expected need, and a Vantor FirstLook event service has been activated; requests go to opendata@vantor.com.
- Hurricane Helene satellite timeline over western NC (sources: Maxar Helene STAC collection; NASA DRCS AGU poster ntrs.nasa.gov/api/citations/20240015495; NASA Earth Observatory 153416; ICEYE bulletin 2024-09-30; earthdata.nasa.gov OPERA data-in-action): landfall 2024-09-26 late evening (FL). Sentinel-1 pre-event passes 2024-09-14 and 09-16; post-event S1 passes 09-26 (storm day), 09-28, 10-03 — NASA GSFC landslide-density heatmap from the 09-28 pass, ASF S1 flood composites for NC/SC from 09-26/09-28/10-03, OPERA RTC-S1/DSWx-S1 mosaics and water-change maps (09-14 vs 09-26). Black Marble nighttime lights daily from 09-18 (09-22 pre vs 10-02 post comparison; zip-code outage map). ICEYE: 350+ SAR images, first flood extent/depth 09-28 (FL), Asheville NC extent/depth in the 09-30 release. Maxar 30 cm post-event acquisitions in the Helene collection: 2024-09-27 (1 strip), 09-30 (2), 10-02 (5), 10-05 (9), 10-06 (5), with pre-event archive from 2019-11-09 to 2024-09-22; 616 ARD tiles over 42 acquisitions with WNC AOIs (Asheville/Swannanoa, Chimney Rock/Lake Lure, Spruce Pine, Boone, Burnsville, Marion/Old Fort). Optical: NASA stated on 10-05 that clouds had blocked most optical imagery; first mostly clear Sentinel-2 over Asheville 2024-10-02 (also the HLS date used for OPERA DIST-ALERT disturbance); Sentinel-2 colour-infrared comparison 2023-10-18 vs 2024-10-14. NASA DRCS activation ran 2024-09-24 to 10-24 and worked directly with NC emergency management. USGS landslide team flew aerial reconnaissance 10-03 to 10-05 and had mapped 450+ landslides by 10-08. Planet, Umbra and Capella open-data catalogs contain no Helene datasets.
- OPERA DSWx-S1 forward production began in September 2024, i.e., Helene was effectively its first operational hurricane; it maps water bodies larger than 3 ha and wider than 200 m at 30 m, 6-12 day revisit (CMR collection C2949811996-POCLOUD). DSWx-HLS 'had limited utility due to cloud cover' for Helene (earthdata.nasa.gov OPERA data-in-action).
- Sentinel-1 constellation in 2026 (ASF news asf.alaska.edu/?p=26648; SentiWiki s1-mission): Sentinel-1C acquiring since 2025-03-26; Sentinel-1D data from 2026-04-17 (ASF download from 2026-06-03); 1C repositioned 2026-06-09..23 (no data); 1A terminated 2026-06-29; resulting 1C+1D constellation gives a 6-day exact repeat but the 6-day pattern shifted by one day — any NC Recon pass-prediction logic must be re-baselined after June 2026. GRD land products are published within 24 h, in practice a few hours.
- Sentinel-2 constellation in 2026 (SentiWiki s2-mission): 2B and 2C nominal, 2A moved to an extended campaign in March 2025; 5-day equatorial revisit, 290 km swath. CDSE free-tier quotas (documentation.dataspace.copernicus.eu/Quotas.html): 12 TB per rolling 30 days, 20 MB/s, 4 concurrent connections, 50,000 OData/STAC requests per month, 2,000 S3 requests per minute, 10,000 Sentinel Hub processing units per month, 10-minute tokens. The CDSE STAC API root is https://stac.dataspace.copernicus.eu/v1/ (legacy catalogue endpoint deprecated 2025-11-17).
- Copernicus EMS Rapid Mapping can only be triggered by Authorised Users (EU/participating-state national focal points, EC DGs, EEAS, EU delegations) through the Service Request Form routed to the ERCC; non-authorised authorities must channel requests through an Authorised User. FEMA has been that channel for US hurricanes: Harvey 2017 (17 flood maps), Irma 2017 (EMSR241, 65 products), Florence 2018 (EMSR311) and Michael 2018 (EMSR322) with 54 maps delivered to FEMA in 2018 (mapping.emergency.copernicus.eu news 2018-12-03). A North Carolina agency cannot request EMS directly.
- International Charter path for NC: USGS 'as an Authorized User (AU) for the International Charter, may request activations on behalf of Federal, State, local, or international agencies'; requests go to eo_liaison@usgs.gov with POC, event description, AOI coordinates or KML/KMZ/SHP, and event date, in English (usgs.gov/emergency-operations-portal/activation-process). Charter data are no-cost; activations typically last several weeks (disasterscharter.org how-the-charter-works). Universal Access registration is for national authorities only, so NC must go through USGS.
- USGS Event Support: imagery requests are submitted through the Collection Management Tool (cmt.usgs.gov) or eo_liaison@usgs.gov; 'one agency may submit the initial request for support, but the imagery that becomes available on HDDS can be shared across the response community' (usgs.gov/emergency-operations-portal/event-support). HDDS Explorer requires login to download; Adhoc/Restricted Adhoc data and the HDDS Directory Viewer have been integrated into the USGS Data Download Service (hddsexplorer.usgs.gov banner).
- Licensing matrix for free satellite sources: Maxar/Vantor ODP and Planet Disaster Data are CC BY-NC 4.0 (no government exemption; NonCommercial = 'not primarily intended for or directed towards commercial advantage or monetary compensation'); Umbra ODP and Capella ODP are CC BY 4.0 per AWS registries (Capella's STAC collection license field says 'proprietary' — needs confirmation); Copernicus Sentinel is free, full and open with attribution; NASA (HLS, OPERA, Black Marble) and USGS Landsat are open/public domain; Planetary Computer sentinel-1-rtc is CC-BY-4.0 but requires an account for SAS tokens.
- Google Earth Engine is not free for NCEM operational use: noncommercial eligibility excludes 'repeated production of data products', 'tooling for management, policy, or web applications' and 'operational workloads' (earthengine.google.com/noncommercial); paid plans are Limited (usage fees only), Basic $500/month (100 batch + 33 online EECU-hours, 100 GB), Professional $2,000/month (500 batch + 166 online EECU-hours, 1 TB, SLA), Premium by quote; compute $0.40 per EECU-hour for the first 10,000 hours (cloud.google.com/earth-engine/pricing).
- Microsoft retired the Planetary Computer Hub on 2024-06-06 citing company-wide security requirements; 'the Planetary Computer Data and APIs will remain available and unchanged' with 'no anticipated impact' but no long-term guarantee (github.com/microsoft/PlanetaryComputer/discussions/347). Planetary Computer Pro (GA 2026) is a separate pay-as-you-go Azure service for hosting an organisation's own data, not a free data source.
- Planet's Education and Research Program explicitly excludes nonprofit and government employees and points federal researchers to NASA CSDA; Basic tier is 3,000 km2/month for university users only (planet.com/industries/education-and-research). NICFI basemaps cover the tropics only and monthly mosaics ended January 2025, so neither is a path for NC.
- NASA CSDA EULA tiers (science.nasa.gov/earth-science/csda/end-user-license-agreements): 'US Government' covers Title 5 U.S.C. 101-105 federal entities, EOP and Congress; 'US Government Plus' adds foreign/intergovernmental partners; 'Public EULA' items are released but 'do not become public domain'. State agencies are not named, so CSDA commercial imagery (Planet, Vantor, Umbra, Capella, ICEYE, BlackSky and 17 others) reaches NC only through federal partners' derived products.
- ASF HyP3 gives every Earthdata user 8,000 free credits per month for on-demand Sentinel-1 RTC, InSAR, burst InSAR, OPERA RTC-S1 and ARIA GUNW processing (HyP3+ credits $0.05 each; outputs retained 14 days; job names are public) — a no-cost way for NC Recon to run its own SAR change detection without GEE or local SNAP (hyp3-docs.asf.alaska.edu).
- NOAA VIIRS flood product: 375 m, 2-3 daytime passes per satellite, available ~3 hours after pass; GOES ABI flood product 1 km hourly for the Americas; joint VIIRS/ABI daily product available ~07Z; daytime only, false positives from tides/marsh/agriculture (NOAA/SSEC quick guide 2020-06-22; floods.ssec.wisc.edu live in Oct 2026).
- HLS v2.0 (LP DAAC): 30 m, global land, ~1.4-1.6 day revisit, 2-3 day latency, COG, CMR-STAC and direct S3; Landsat 8/9 together revisit every 8 days with Level-1 scenes available within ~6 hours via EarthExplorer and Level-2 24-72 hours after Tier processing; Landsat 7 was decommissioned 2025-06-04.
- OPERA roadmap (jpl.nasa.gov/go/opera/products): in production DSWx-HLS, DIST-HLS, RTC-S1, CSLC-S1, DSWx-S1, DISP-S1, TROPO; in development DIST-S1 (March 2026), DSWx-NI (June 2026), DISP-NI (Sept 2026). DIST-S1 is the product that would give cloud-independent landslide/debris disturbance for WNC; its CMR collection did not exist as of 2026-10-06.
- NASA Disasters Mapping Portal discovery works through the ArcGIS Hub API (https://disasters-nasa.hub.arcgis.com/api/v3/datasets?q=helene returns Helene items such as 'Hurricane Helene NOAA' ImageServer and 'Helene Damage Flood' raster) even though direct REST folder listings on maps.disasters.nasa.gov returned 404 to scripted requests; the Hub RSS reports 'The NASA Disasters Program is not currently activated' and lists 2026 activations (Hurricane Nolo Sept 2026, Ohio Floods Aug 2026, Texas Floods July 2026, Louisiana Floods June 2026, US Winter Storm Jan 2026).
### gaps
- International Charter: could not confirm whether an activation was issued for Hurricane Helene 2024 (disasterscharter.org activation list is client-rendered; web-search budget exhausted). Secondary results indicate a Charter activation for Hurricane Milton on 2024-10-10 only.
- Copernicus EMS: could not confirm whether a Rapid Mapping activation (EMSR code) was requested by FEMA for Hurricane Helene; EMS activation list/API not reachable by scripted fetch.
- USGS HDDS: could not verify that a Hurricane Helene event folder exists in HDDS Explorer or which imagery (aerial, Charter, commercial) was posted; explorer requires login and the USGS Helene topic page does not mention HDDS. Eligibility rules for county (non-federal) staff to access restricted commercial datasets were not found in writing.
- Vantor Open Data Program activation protocol page (vantor.com/resources/vantor-open-data-program-activation-protocol/) renders client-side; the full protocol text (timing of release, attribution wording, government-use notes) could not be read.
- Planet: no published request form or contact for the Disaster Data / Crisis Response Program; the Source Cooperative catalog has no Hurricane Helene dataset and Planet's Pulse tag page for Helene is empty. Whether Planet released open Helene imagery anywhere else is unconfirmed.
- Umbra and Capella: no Helene or North Carolina collects found in their open-data STAC catalogs; Capella's Data Cooperative / Data Grant eligibility pages and the 'Getting Access to Open Data' support article returned 404; Capella STAC license field ('proprietary') conflicts with the AWS registry (CC BY 4.0).
- ICEYE: no pricing, no public-sector no-cost program, and no machine-readable Helene flood layers found; only PDF bulletins are public.
- OPERA DSWx-S1 and DSWx-HLS product latency (hours/days from acquisition to availability) is not stated on the fetched JPL, Earthdata, PO.DAAC/CMR pages or in the DSWx-S1 product spec text extracted; PO.DAAC dataset pages timed out.
- Sentinel-2 L1C/L2A publication timeliness on CDSE (hours from sensing) not stated on the fetched CDSE or SentiWiki pages.
- Cloud-free odds for Sentinel-2 over NC in a hurricane week: no climatology or official statistic found; only the Helene anecdote (first mostly clear Sentinel-2 over Asheville 6 days after landfall) is documented.
- NASA Disasters program: the request-support contact/form for state agencies was not found on fetched pages (science.nasa.gov and the ArcGIS Hub 'about' page are client-rendered); NASA ARIA-share directory listing is client-rendered so the full Helene product list on ARIA-share could not be enumerated; no classic ARIA coherence Damage Proxy Map for Helene was found.
- GEE: whether Google offers a discounted public-sector or disaster-response plan was not found on the pricing page.
- Commercial tasking prices for Planet SkySat, Capella and SkyFi come from reseller pages (secondary); the SkyFi August 2025 price PDF now returns 404; Vantor/Maxar tasking prices were not located.
- NASA GIBS near-real-time layer latencies and Black Marble NRT availability were not confirmed from the fetched pages.
- Web-search budget (200 calls) was exhausted mid-task; remaining items were verified by direct fetches only, so some secondary sources (SkyWatch, Apollo Mapping) could not be replaced with primary pages.

## Aerial and drone imagery, and the drone operations ecosystem in North Carolina
- (high/high) **NOAA NGS Emergency Response Imagery (ERI) - storms.ngs.noaa.gov viewer, WMTS and noaa-eri-pds S3 bucket** (NOAA National Geodetic Survey, Remote Sensing Division (distribution via NOAA Big Data Program on AWS)) https://storms.ngs.noaa.gov/
  Post-event aerial RGB orthomosaics flown with the DSS v6 camera at 15-30 cm GSD, mosaicked and tiled per flight. Public S3 bucket noaa-eri-pds holds per-flight folders (e.g. 2024_Hurricane_Helene/20241005a_RGB/) with COG GeoTIFF tiles (EPSG:4326, LZW, RGB + alpha mask, 512x512 blocks, overviews), a cogs_<flight>_RGB.vrt virtual mosaic, tile_index_<flight>_RGB.shp/.tar, raw/ JPEG frames with OSSIM .geom exterior-orientation files, and downloads/<flight>_RGB.tar + <flight>_jpgs.tar bundles. Per-storm WMTS at https://storms.ngs.noaa.gov/storms/<storm>/services/WMTSCapabilities.xml with tile template https://stormscdn.ngs.noaa.gov/<flight>-rgb/{TileMatrix}/{TileCol}/{TileRow} (GoogleMapsCompatible, zoom 0-21, image/jpgpng). Bucket also has 2022-2026 *_Pre_Event folders (pre-event baseline imagery).
  access: S3 anonymous (aws s3 ls --no-sign-request s3://noaa-eri-pds/; HTTPS GET; GDAL /vsicurl/ on tiles and VRT), WMTS/XYZ tiles, web viewer, NOAA Data Access Viewer custom download (coast.noaa.gov/dataviewer id=10182) | auth: none | licence: CC0-1.0 per InPort record; AWS registry says public domain, attribution requested | cadence: Per flight; tiles appear in S3 the same day as the flight (20241005a tiles LastModified 2024-10-05T23:13Z; downloads tar 23:36Z). InPort 'Update frequency: None Planned' for a finished event. | NC: Helene: coastal/FL-GA flights 2024-09-27 to 2024-10-02 (folders 20240927a..20241002c); NC mountain flights 20241005a, 20241005b, 20241006a, 20241006b, 20241007a, 20241008a (tile names encode lon/lat, e.g. 20241005aC0813815w360900n = 81°38'15"W 36°09'00"N, Ashe/Alleghany). Hurricane Erin 2025 flights 20250822a, 20250823a, 20250823b over the Outer Banks (tiles at 75°27'W 35°34'N). Earlier NC events in bucket: Matthew 2016, Florence 2018, Dorian 2019, Isaias (Henri 2021 n/a), Arthur 2014, Irene 2011.
  use (during,post): Primary rapid 'after' evidence channel (already integrated); add per-flight WMTS layers to the Conditions map on the day of flight; use the tile_index shapefile and VRT to compute coverage gaps per town; use *_Pre_Event folders and the 2016-2019 storm folders as extra 'before' baselines; use raw JPEG + .geom for oblique viewing. Trigger: poll S3 prefix list for new <date><letter>_RGB folders.
  caveats: 'For informational and planning purposes only... no accuracy or completeness guarantee'. Coverage is corridor/target based, not wall-to-wall; mountain flights began 9 days after landfall. WMTS layer list I retrieved showed flights through 20241005a; confirm later layers exist in WMTS (S3 has them).
- (high/high) **NOAA ERI Hurricane Helene 2024 dataset record (InPort 73570 / data.gov)** (NOAA National Geodetic Survey) https://www.fisheries.noaa.gov/inport/item/73570
  Formal metadata for the Helene ERI collection: DSS v6 sensor, 15-30 cm GSD, 8-bit RGB, acquisition 2024-09-27 to 2024-10-02 plus 2024-10-05 onward for the mountain region; two extents (FL/coastal -85.1..-82.2, 27.51..30.42 and mountains -83.4..-81.6, 35.1..36.2). Lists distribution: storms.ngs.noaa.gov, noaa-eri-pds S3 (raw imagery, orthorectified mosaic tiles, VRT, tile index, sqlite footprint files), NOAA Data Access Viewer.
  access: portal download / S3 / web viewer | auth: none | licence: CC0-1.0 | cadence: static | NC: Mountain region bbox -83.4 to -81.6 W, 35.1 to 36.2 N (western NC and adjacent TN), flown from 2024-10-05
  use (post): Authoritative provenance record to cite in exports and the brief; use the stated GSD and dates for evidence weighting and for the public tract summary's data lineage.
  caveats: Temporal coverage in data.gov record lists only Sept 27-Oct 2; the mountain flights are described in the InPort text but not in the catalog temporal field.
- (high/high) **FEMA / Civil Air Patrol imagery feature services and fema-cap-imagery S3 bucket** (FEMA (Response Geospatial Office / FEMA_HQ_IDM on ArcGIS Online) with Civil Air Patrol as collector) https://services.arcgis.com/XG15cJAlne2vxtgt/arcgis/rest/services/Image_Points_view/FeatureServer
  Public ArcGIS feature service indexing every CAP photo uploaded to FEMA: layer 0 Ground, layer 1 Aerial Oblique, layer 3 Aerial Nadir. Fields: longitude, latitude, altitude, heading, exif_photo_date, exif_camera_maker/model, exif_focal_length, url (direct S3 link), image_type, project, mission, flight, exif_hash. Photos live in S3 bucket fema-cap-imagery which allows anonymous GET and bucket listing: Images/CAP_H_TS_Helene_2024/Source/<mission>/<flight>_Aerial_Oblique_Nikon_VIRB_GoPro/<timestamp>/DSC_xxxx.JPG and Images/CAP_H_TS_Helene_2024/Finished Products/<JOB>/ORTHO/<JOB>_Orthomosaic.tiff. Project 'CAP - H-TS Helene 2024' has 10,692 oblique and 99,857 nadir photo points; obliques dated 2024-09-28 14:48Z to 2024-10-18. Camera Nikon D7200 for obliques; orthomosaics from WaldoAir XCAM Ultra 50.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer REST query (JSON/GeoJSON), anonymous S3 HTTPS GET and list | auth: none | licence: Unstated on the service; FEMA GeoPlatform CAP_Ortho item says 'No specific terms of use are required'; FEMA hub page says 'This dataset is intended for public access and use'. Treat as US Government work (public domain) but confirm with FEMA RGO. | cadence: Near real time during an activation: a photo taken 2024-09-28 14:48Z had S3 Last-Modified 2024-09-28 16:20Z (single sample, ~1.5 h) | NC: Helene obliques cover western NC (first sample 2024-09-28 near Lake Lure -82.163, 35.437, heading 235). Also Florence 2018, Matthew 2016 era imagery under other project names; 'CAP - H-TS Erin 2025' project (1,847 obliques) was USVI/PR, not NC.
  use (during,post): Oblique ground-truth channel: query layer 1 by bbox + project to attach geotagged obliques (with heading/altitude) to each structure for the review queue; use nadir points to estimate CAP flight-line coverage; ingest Finished Products orthomosaics (8-10 cm) as a second 'after' raster where NOAA has no coverage; watch for a new project name as a trigger that CAP has been tasked.
  caveats: S3 keys contain spaces ('Finished Products'); 'filename' field is null in samples; the oblique layer's overall extent includes bad coordinates (xmax 82.06, ymin -36.5) so filter to a sane bbox; FEMA Image Uploader itself (imageuploader.geoplatform.gov) requires login and its REST host imageryuploader.geoplatform.gov no longer resolves (NXDOMAIN).
- (high/high) **FEMA Disasters GeoPlatform imagery servers: CAP_Ortho ImageServer, CAP_Ortho_footprint, Hurricane_Helene_2024 folder** (FEMA (disasters.geoplatform.gov ArcGIS Enterprise)) https://disasters.geoplatform.gov/image/rest/services/CAP_Ortho/ImageServer
  Public ArcGIS Image Service mosaicking CAP WALDO orthomosaics since Aug 2022 (4-band U8, Web Mercator, mosaic methods incl. Seamline/LockRaster, Catalog+Mensuration capabilities; filter by 'event'). Companion CAP_Ortho_footprint MapServer/FeatureServer (702 footprints; 89 with event = 'CAP - H-TS Helene 2024', e.g. LENOIR_NE 0.09 m, SPRUCE_PINE 0.08 m, DUNNS_ROCK_NE) with url field pointing to the S3 .tiff. Folder Hurricane_Helene_2024 holds Hurricane_Helene_NOAA ImageServer (0.10 m pixel, 4-band) and Hurricane_Helene_Worldview_20241006 (0.36 m). Other folders: Hurricane_Milton, Debby, Francine, Texas_Flood_2025, Wildfire_SC_MAR_2025, etc. A public CAP imagery browser app exists at https://disasters.geoplatform.gov/imagerybrowser/?wemap=2c7dabb4a0594861a8bac3944b931d2d.
  access: ArcGIS ImageServer / MapServer / FeatureServer REST (exportImage, query, catalog) | auth: none (public endpoints returned JSON without token) | licence: Item says 'No specific terms of use are required'; otherwise unstated | cadence: Per event; footprints added as CAP finishes products (Helene orthos dated 2024-10-03 onward) | NC: 89 Helene orthomosaic footprints across WNC (Lenoir, Spruce Pine, Dunns Rock, Swannanoa, Asheville, McDowell, Hendersonville, Brevard, Canton, Bearwallow, Blowing Rock, Waynesville, Elk Shoal, Cherokee, Yadkin, Robbinsville, Murphy, Andrews, Marble, Monroe, Salisbury, Statesville, Elkin, Concord, Bryson City) plus TN; NOAA Helene mosaic also served here.
  use (post): Query CAP_Ortho_footprint by town bbox to discover available 8-10 cm orthomosaics and their S3 URLs; stream CAP_Ortho via exportImage as an alternate 'after' layer; use the 'event' attribute as the activation trigger and footprint union for coverage-gap analysis.
  caveats: No copyright text or formal licence on services; FEMA may reorganize folders; some services are hosted on 'Hosted' folder with different lifecycles.
- (high/high) **Civil Air Patrol Helene orthomosaics on OpenAerialMap** (Humanitarian OpenStreetMap Team (OAM) hosting Civil Air Patrol uploads (provider 'US Civil Air Patrol' / 'Civil Air Patrol')) https://api.openaerialmap.org/meta?provider=US%20Civil%20Air%20Patrol&bbox=-84.5,35.0,-80.5,36.6&limit=100
  69 CAP Helene orthomosaics in the NC/TN bbox (titles 'CAP Imagery - Helene - <JOB>' and 'CAP Imagery - CAP_H_TS_Helene_2024 - <JOB>'), acquisition 2024-10-03 to 2024-10-23, GSD 0.08-0.10 m (Hendersonville tiles 0.37 m), 0.05-2.2 GB each, 3-band RGB GeoTIFF with TMS tiles. Duplicate entries exist for several jobs (e.g. ASHEVILLE_W and 'Asheville West').
  access: REST API (api.openaerialmap.org/meta with bbox, provider, acquisition_from/to, platform, gsd_from/gsd_to, page, limit), direct S3/HTTPS download of .tif (oin-hotosm / oin-hotosm-temp buckets), TMS https://tiles.openaerialmap.org/{id}/0/{file}/{z}/{x}/{y} | auth: none for read; JWT/OSM login for upload | licence: CC-BY 4.0 (attribution 'contributors of Open Imagery Network') | cadence: As uploaded (Helene uploads Oct 2024) | NC: Spruce Pine, Lenoir, Dunns Rock/Brevard, Swannanoa, Asheville, McDowell, Hendersonville, Canton, Bearwallow, Blowing Rock, Waynesville, Elk Shoal, Happy Valley, Cherokee, Bryson City, Robbinsville, Andrews, Murphy, Marble, Yadkin, Elkin, Statesville, Salisbury, Concord, Monroe, plus TN (Elizabethton, Roan Mountain, Hampton, Bumpus Cove, Valley Forge)
  use (post): Second 'after' raster channel at 8-10 cm for xView2 and blue-tarp detection where NOAA is 15-30 cm; TMS endpoint can be used directly in the MapLibre viewer; also the natural publication channel for NC Recon partners' own drone orthomosaics (upload gives a COG + TMS + CC-BY licence).
  caveats: Platform is tagged 'uav' although CAP flies manned aircraft; 'sensor' is 'Unknown'; some files sit in the oin-hotosm-temp bucket; heavy API use should be coordinated with HOT per terms.
- (high/high) **OpenAerialMap platform and Open Imagery Network (general)** (Humanitarian OpenStreetMap Team) https://openaerialmap.org/
  Open catalogue and hosting for openly licensed aerial/satellite imagery: browser at map.openaerialmap.org, catalog API (hotosm/oam-api: /meta, /meta/:id, /tms, /uploads, /user), uploader (Dropbox, public URL or local file; TIFF; metadata title, sensor, provider, platform, dates, license). All imagery is CC-BY 4.0 and stored in S3 bucket oin-hotosm (us-east-1, no-sign-request). Post-Helene NC holdings (92 records since 2024-09-26): 69 CAP, 17 Maxar Helene strips (0.31 m; some CC BY-NC 4.0, some CC-BY 4.0), NC State Extension farm flights, French Broad River Greenway (CC BY-NC).
  access: REST API, S3, TMS; web uploader | auth: none to read; account (OSM/Google login) to upload | licence: CC-BY 4.0 for OIN imagery (a few items marked CC BY-NC 4.0) | cadence: continuous, community driven | NC: Statewide where contributed; 92 records in western/central NC since Helene
  use (pre,during,post): Imagery discovery API for any new event (bbox + acquisition_from); distribution channel for county/NCDOT/volunteer drone orthomosaics so they become citable COGs; the OIN metadata JSON is the schema NC Recon should emit for its own drone ingests.
  caveats: API docs site (hotosm.github.io/oam-api) is JS-only and did not render; parameters confirmed by live queries and the GitHub README. Non-commercial Maxar entries cannot be used in commercial products.
- (high/high) **NC OneMap Orthoimagery_Helene_2025 Image Service (NC Collaboratory Helene Priority 1 imagery)** (NC Center for Geographic Information and Analysis (NC OneMap) hosting data produced for the NC Collaboratory at UNC Chapel Hill by AECOM) https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/Imagery/Orthoimagery_Helene_2025/ImageServer
  6-inch (0.5 ft pixel) post-Helene leaf-off orthoimagery over 13 WNC counties (~5,323 sq mi), 6,768 GeoTIFF tiles 5,000 x 5,000 ft, source 4-band RGBIR, served 3-band; NAD83(2011) State Plane NC ftUS (WKID 103122 / EPSG 6543); mosaic methods NorthWest/Center/LockRaster/ByAttribute/Nadir/Viewpoint/Seamline; capabilities Catalog, Mensuration, Image, Metadata; max export 15,000 x 4,100 px. Collected spring 2025 (flights Jan to early March 2025 per UNC); ASPRS-based accuracy, 39 of 49 checkpoints usable; seamline editing concentrated on transportation corridors. Mirrored at services.gis.nc.gov. Also referenced by AGO items from NC OneMap (892b6918...), Asheville GIS (AVLGIS) and UNC Asheville.
  access: ArcGIS ImageServer REST (exportImage, identify, catalog); tile download via NC OneMap imagery download app | auth: none | licence: 'No formal license'; users asked to acknowledge source; copyright text 'NC Collaboratory at UNC Chapel Hill'; NC OneMap terms at https://www.nconemap.gov/pages/terms (text not retrieved) | cadence: static (one-off post-event collection, published 2025) | NC: Counties covered in full: Alexander, Ashe, Avery, Buncombe, Burke, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, McDowell, Mitchell, Rutherford, Watauga, Yancey (UNC article lists Davie instead of Alexander; service metadata lists Alexander)
  use (post): Already integrated as 'after' imagery; use as the high-accuracy reference for registering drone orthomosaics (GCP picking) and for recovery-phase (months later) change detection; note it is leaf-off so compare with leaf-off 2020-2023 flights where possible.
  caveats: Acquired 4-5 months after the event (not rapid response); areas away from major transportation features may show seamline displacement; 4-band analysis service for Helene not confirmed.
- (high/medium) **NC Collaboratory Helene Priority 1 lidar and imagery project (lidar component)** (North Carolina Collaboratory (UNC Chapel Hill) with AECOM; partners NC DPS/NCEM, NC Geodetic Survey, UNC Institute for Risk Management and Insurance Innovation) https://www.unc.edu/posts/2025/10/02/collaboratory-maps-helene-damage-to-speed-recovery/

Imagery is uploaded to the FEMA Image Uploader (imageuploader.geoplatform.gov, login required) and is 'automatically accessible through HDDS'; non-public imagery must be deleted from CAP equipment within 72 h; uploads logged in WMIRS. NC Wing flew 56 sorties / 159 hours for Helene supporting FEMA, NC Emergency Management, NC National Guard, NCDOT and local agencies, and staffed the state EOC 80+ hours. CAP's AGO org (cap-gis, orgid U0vgiXgwLpyQDfbW) exposes public items: web map 'CAP - H-TS Helene 2024' (e69ef7715b9d4d09baca8ea5d1399875), 'Helene Image Viewer' attachment viewer app, Damage_Assessment dashboard view, CAP_Aircraft_Status_and_Locations and FEMA_Request_Feature_Layer_view services, and dozens of wildfire orthomosaic tile services. CAP took 'close to half a million pictures' in the 2018 hurricane season.

- (high/high) **OpenDroneMap ecosystem (ODM, WebODM, NodeODM, ClusterODM)** (OpenDroneMap community (AGPL-3.0); WebODM maintained separately (webodm.org) with free installers) https://docs.opendronemap.org/
  Open-source photogrammetry for drone images: outputs georeferenced orthophoto (odm_orthophoto.tif, optional --cog), DSM/DTM (dsm.tif, dtm.tif), point cloud (.laz; --pc-las for LAS), textured mesh (.obj), OGC 3D Tiles (--3d-tiles), camera/shot JSON, PDF report. GCP file format (projection header + geo_x geo_y geo_z im_x im_y image_name), POSM GCPi and GCP Editor Pro interfaces for picking GCPs on a basemap; --align 'Path to a GeoTIFF DEM or a LAS/LAZ point cloud that the reconstruction outputs should be automatically aligned to. Experimental.' (coarse DSM feature match + fine ICP, translation and rotation, scale fixed). Hardware: 40 images 4 GB RAM, 250 images 16 GB, 500 images 32 GB, 1,500 images 64 GB, 2,500 images 128 GB, 5,000 images 256 GB; 100 GB disk recommended; GPU only speeds SIFT (~2x). NodeODM REST API (POST /task/new, GET /task/{uuid}/info, download all.zip), docker run -p 3000:3000 opendronemap/nodeodm (GPU variant opendronemap/nodeodm:gpu), token auth. WebODM: Docker-based web UI with REST API, plugins, 3D viewer, Gaussian splats; 'official up-to-date WebODM installers are free'; Lightning is a paid hosted processing option.
  access: Self-hosted Docker containers; REST API | auth: none (self-hosted); token optional | licence: AGPL-3.0 (ODM, WebODM, NodeODM) | cadence: software releases; processing time hours per few hundred images on CPU | NC: n/a (software)
  use (during,post): Add a NodeODM container to the NC Recon compose stack so county/volunteer drone photo sets become COG orthomosaics + DSM in the NC datum, then feed xView2/change detection; use --align with NC DEM03 or Helene lidar and GCPs picked on NC OneMap orthos for before/after registration; export 3D Tiles for structure-level 3D review.
  caveats: AGPL obligations if NC Recon modifies and distributes ODM; --align is experimental; without GCPs horizontal error is at the level of consumer GNSS (metres) unless RTK/PPK drones are used; memory scales steeply with image count.
- (medium/high) **CODEM multi-modal DEM/point-cloud co-registration (used by ODM --align)** (NCALM / University of Houston with Hobu Inc. (GitHub NCALM-UH/CODEM)) https://github.com/NCALM-UH/CODEM
  Apache-2.0 tool that registers an area-of-interest 3D dataset (LAS/LAZ/BPF point cloud, PLY/OBJ mesh or GeoTIFF DSM) to a larger foundation dataset by generating normalized DSMs, coarse feature matching, then ICP fine registration, and applies the transform to the original data; also offers lidar vertical change detection with raster/mesh/point cloud outputs.
  access: pip/conda install, CLI | auth: none | licence: Apache 2.0 | cadence: software | NC: n/a (software)
  use (post): Register drone DSM/point clouds to NC statewide lidar or the Helene 2025 lidar for DEM-of-difference and to correct drone orthomosaic placement before structure alignment.
  caveats: Needs overlapping terrain relief/features; both inputs assumed metric with scale fixed.
- (medium/high) **AROSICS automatic raster co-registration** (GFZ German Research Centre for Geosciences (Daniel Scheffler)) https://github.com/GFZ/arosics
  Python package (Apache-2.0) for automatic sub-pixel co-registration of two GDAL-readable rasters using frequency-domain phase correlation: global mode computes one X/Y shift and corrects geocoding without resampling; local mode builds a dense tie-point grid with multi-stage false-positive filtering and warps with an affine/cubic resampling. Handles clouds/outliers; conda-forge and PyPI; Scheffler et al. 2017, Remote Sensing 9(7):676.
  access: pip/conda install, CLI and Python API | auth: none | licence: Apache 2.0 | cadence: software | NC: n/a (software)
  use (post): Snap drone orthomosaics, CAP orthos and NOAA tiles to the NC OneMap 6-inch basemap before pixel change detection and roof-outline alignment; log the computed shift as a QA metric per image.
  caveats: Designed for satellite imagery; large relief displacement and seasonal differences between a leaf-on drone image and leaf-off NC orthos can reduce tie-point quality; use masks (water, vegetation).
- (high/high) **FAA Special Governmental Interest (SGI) process, disaster TFRs and Helene airspace actions** (Federal Aviation Administration (System Operations Support Center, SOSC)) https://www.faa.gov/uas/advanced_operations/emergency_situations
  Expedited Part 107/COA amendments for emergency operations incl. 'Damage Assessments Supporting Disaster Recovery Related Insurance Claims', SAR, firefighting, infrastructure restoration, media. Apply via the TSA/FAA Waiver & Airspace Access Program web portal ('Part 107 Special Government Interest'); time-sensitive requests by phone to SOSC 202-267-8276 (SOSC email 9-ator-hq-sosc@faa.gov staffed 0600-2400 ET, phone 24/7 per 2022 FAA briefing to NC government UAS group); approvals 'may be issued in minutes' for VLOS, while BVLOS approvals 'typically require the establishment of a TFR' and take longer; process in JO 7210.3EE (formerly JO 7200.23A). Disaster TFRs issued under 14 CFR 91.137(a)(2). Helene: FAA issued TFRs over NC/SC/TN (Oct 1-4, 2024), stated TFRs 'do not ban aircraft, including drones, from providing disaster relief' if coordinated with emergency responders, warned of fines/criminal prosecution; FAASTeam notice NOTC3963 (2024-10-01, CLT FSDO) warned pilots of heavy public-safety drone activity in WNC; press reported a 300% increase in WNC air traffic and 30 mid-air close calls in one day. FAA Hurricane Toolkit: established drone programs should obtain waivers/COAs ahead of season.
  access: web portal (TSA/FAA WAAP), email form, 24/7 phone | auth: Part 107 Remote Pilot Certificate or public COA required | licence: n/a (regulation) | cadence: real time during events | NC: Statewide; Helene TFRs covered western NC

- (low/medium) **USACE ERDC UAS team Helene mapping (Watauga, Avery, Buncombe)** (U.S. Army Engineer Research and Development Center (ERDC) supporting USACE road clearing) https://www.army.mil/article/280680/with_birds_eye_view_army_engineer_researchers_soldiers_respond_to_hurricane_helene
  Fixed-wing VTOL drone plus quadcopter produced orthomosaics, 4K video and digital elevation models of impassable roads, damaged bridges and debris in three WNC counties, delivered to USACE within 24 hours of collection; approval to on-site in 48 hours.
  access: Not public; USACE-held | auth: federal partner request | licence: US Government work (public domain) but not published | cadence: per deployment | NC: Watauga, Avery, Buncombe counties, Oct 2024
  use (post): Precedent for 24-hour drone ortho/DEM turnaround; potential data request through the FEMA/USACE mission assignment channel for road-isolation analysis.
  caveats: No public distribution; aircraft model not named.
- (low/medium) **Contracted and volunteer drone mapping during Helene (WithersRavenel for NCDOT; Virtual Sandtable at Spruce Pine)** (WithersRavenel (NC engineering firm, NCDOT contractor); Virtual Sandtable with AgEagle (volunteer)) https://withersravenel.com/portfolio/ncdot-hurricane-helene-uas-response/
  WithersRavenel flew Wingtra fixed-wing UAS for orthomosaics and a Riegl UAS lidar along NC-9 Bat Cave to the Buncombe line and US-64/US-74A Bat Cave to Chimney Rock ('more than 4 miles'), producing orthomosaics, planimetrics, topographic surveys and lidar of channel changes (Grassy, Hickory, Reedy Patch Creeks, Broad River) on survey control shared with other consultants for emergency road design. Virtual Sandtable flew loaned eBee Tac mapping drones around Spruce Pine (Mitchell County) with Starlink, producing a damage model and thermal data for local EM and volunteer tasking.
  access: Not public (client deliverables) | auth: NCDOT/county agreement | licence: unstated | cadence: per project | NC: Rutherford/Henderson (Chimney Rock, Bat Cave), Mitchell (Spruce Pine), Oct 2024
  use (post): Shows that professional-grade RTK drone orthos and UAS lidar exist for the worst-hit corridors; NC Recon's ingest should accept contractor deliverables (GeoTIFF/LAS with control reports) and record accuracy metadata; volunteer groups need the OAM upload path.
  caveats: Company and trade-press sources; no data access.
- (medium/medium) **Vexcel Gray Sky / Geospatial Insurance Consortium post-catastrophe aerial imagery (commercial)** (Vexcel Data Program (GIC domain redirects to Vexcel)) https://vexceldata.com/?p=14651
  Rapid-response aerial program: for Helene, five aircraft crews began collecting 2024-09-27, >10,000 sq km across FL, GA, TN, SC and NC (Asheville), 7.5-10 cm ortho and multispectral plus 7.5 cm obliques in populated areas, with pre-event comparisons; delivered via Vexcel Platform/GIC viewer, APIs and ArcGIS Image Services. Access by request form; pricing not stated. NCDOT separately maintains a 'Nearmap Images of Helene Impact Area' web map, showing commercial subscriptions are already in use in NC government.
  access: commercial platform, API, ArcGIS Image Services | auth: paid / request access (first-responder terms unknown) | licence: proprietary | cadence: days after event | NC: Asheville area for Helene (other NC towns not listed)
  use (post): Optional paid 'after' channel where the free sources have gaps; keep as a pluggable ImageServer connector but do not depend on it; flag cost and licence in procurement.
  caveats: Paid; redistribution in public exports not allowed; GIC free-access terms for government not confirmed.
- (high/high) **North Carolina drone statutes affecting imagery collection and dissemination** (North Carolina General Assembly (NCDOT laws and regulations page summarizes)) https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_15A/GS_15A-300.1.html
  G.S. 15A-300.1: no person, entity or state agency may use a UAS to conduct surveillance of a person or occupied dwelling and curtilage, or private property, without consent, or photograph an individual for publication without consent (newsgathering/newsworthy/public-event exceptions); explicit exception for emergency management under G.S. 166A-19.3 for incident command, reconnaissance, SAR, damage assessment, hazard management and floodplain mapping; law-enforcement exceptions; civil remedy up to $5,000 per published photo/video plus fees and injunction; illegally obtained evidence inadmissible. 15A-300.2: no launch/recovery from state or private property without consent; municipalities may regulate their property. 15A-300.3: no UAS within 500 ft horizontal/250 ft vertical of correctional facilities (emergency responders exempt). 15A-300.4: no UAS within 3,000 ft of a forest fire without the managing official's consent (Class A1 misdemeanor to Class D felony). 14-401.25: publishing thermal images revealing people/activities inside a structure without owner consent is a Class A1 misdemeanor. 14-280.3: interfering with a manned aircraft by UAS is a Class H felony. S.L. 2024-15 s.21 repealed Article 10 of Chapter 63 (the NC UAS operator permit) effective 2024-12-01.
  access: public statutes | auth: none | licence: public domain | cadence: static (check each legislative session) | NC: Statewide
  use (pre,during,post): Security/privacy design constraints: drone imagery ingested under the 166A-19.3 emergency-management exception should be tagged with the authorizing agency; public exports must not publish identifiable individuals or interior thermal detail; keep per-structure imagery behind the responder token and apply small-count suppression; collection planner should warn near prisons and wildfires.
  caveats: NC public records law (G.S. 132) implications for imagery held by agencies were not researched here.
- (high/high) **Open format standards for drone and aerial products: OGC COG 1.0, OGC GeoTIFF 1.1, ASPRS LAS 1.4 R15 / LAS 1.5, OGC 3D Tiles 1.1** (Open Geospatial Consortium; ASPRS; cogeo.org community) https://docs.ogc.org/is/21-026/21-026.html
  COG (OGC 21-026, v1.0, 2023-07-14): tiled IFDs (256/512/1024), reduced-resolution overviews, GeoTIFF keys, HTTP range requests with CORS 'Access-Control-Allow-Headers: range'; five conformance classes; create/validate with GDAL COG driver, rio-cogeo, Radiant Earth/Java validators. GeoTIFF 1.1 (OGC 19-008r4) formalizes geokeys with EPSG. LAS: ASPRS LAS 1.4 R15 (2019) referenced by USGS Lidar Base Specification; LAS 1.5 R00 now published at lasformat.org (point record formats 6-10, OGC WKT CRS required, EVLRs). 3D Tiles 1.1 (OGC 22-025r4) for streaming photogrammetry/point clouds (glTF-based, vendor neutral). ODM can emit COG (--cog), LAS (--pc-las) and 3D Tiles (--3d-tiles).
  access: public specifications | auth: none | licence: open standards (OGC document licence; ASPRS spec freely downloadable) | cadence: static | NC: n/a
  use (pre,during,post): Mandate COG for every raster NC Recon stores or exports (enables range-request streaming to the browser and QGIS), LAS/LAZ 1.4+ with CRS WKT for point clouds, 3D Tiles for the 3D review view; validate on ingest with rio-cogeo.
  caveats: ASPRS legacy LAS page returned 404; LAS 1.5 intro page could not be read (Read the Docs 404), so its approval date is unknown; LAZ compression is a LASzip de-facto standard, not covered here.
- (medium/high) **Drone image metadata: EXIF/XMP (DJI tags) and OIN imagery metadata schema** (Phil Harvey ExifTool documentation (DJI tag table); Open Imagery Network (HOT)) https://exiftool.org/TagNames/DJI.html
  DJI XMP/EXIF tags needed for photogrammetry and georeferencing: GpsLatitude/GpsLongitude, AbsoluteAltitude, RelativeAltitude, Flight{Roll,Yaw,Pitch}Degree, Gimbal{Roll,Yaw,Pitch}Degree, RtkFlag, RtkStdLon/Lat/Hgt, CalibratedFocalLength, DewarpData, thermal parameters (K1-K4, emissivity, object distance for ZH20T/M3T). OIN metadata JSON (oin-meta-generator): uuid, title, projection, bbox, footprint, gsd, file_size, acquisition_start/end, platform, provider, contact, properties{sensor, license, thumbnail, tags}. FEMA's CAP point schema (longitude, latitude, altitude, heading, exif_photo_date, exif_camera_maker/model, exif_focal_length, url, image_type, project, mission, flight, exif_hash) is the operational equivalent for obliques.
  access: documentation; tools (exiftool, GDAL) | auth: none | licence: ExifTool: Perl Artistic/GPL docs; OIN tool: open source (licence not stated) | cadence: static | NC: n/a
  use (during,post): Define the drone-photo ingest schema: parse EXIF/XMP (incl. gimbal pitch to classify nadir vs oblique and RTK flags for accuracy), compute exif_hash for deduplication, emit OIN JSON for OAM publication and FEMA-style point features for the review queue.
  caveats: Skydio/Autel tag names differ from DJI; iPhone photos (as in the USGS set) carry only basic GPS EXIF.
### insights
- NOAA ERI imagery is posted the same day it is flown: Helene flight 20241005a (NC mountains) tiles have S3 Last-Modified 2024-10-05T23:13Z and the tar bundles 23:36Z (https://noaa-eri-pds.s3.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&prefix=2024_Hurricane_Helene/downloads/). NOAA flew the NC mountains on Oct 5, 5b, 6, 6b, 7 and 8, 2024, i.e. 9-12 days after landfall, after six days of coastal FL/GA flights (Sept 27-Oct 2).
- NOAA ERI tiles are COGs in EPSG:4326 with per-flight VRTs and shapefile tile indexes; GDAL /vsicurl/ on cogs_<flight>_RGB.vrt lets a client subset a 200+ GB flight in seconds without downloading (NOAA BDP guide: https://github.com/NOAA-Big-Data-Program/bdp-data-docs/blob/main/NGS/NOAA_ER_Pre_Event_Data.pdf). Raw frames ship with OSSIM .geom exterior-orientation files.
- NOAA ERI bucket also holds 2022_Pre_Event, 2023_Pre_Event, 2024_Pre_Event, 2025_Pre_Event and 2026_Pre_Event folders (pre-season baseline flights) and flew Hurricane Erin over the NC Outer Banks on 2025-08-22/23 (https://noaa-eri-pds.s3.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&prefix=2025_Hurricane_Erin/&delimiter=/).
- Civil Air Patrol Helene volume (FEMA Image_Points_view FeatureServer): 10,692 aerial obliques (Nikon D7200, 2024-09-28 14:48Z to 2024-10-18) and 99,857 aerial nadir frames, plus 89 WALDO orthomosaics indexed in CAP_Ortho_footprint; all files are anonymously readable in S3 bucket fema-cap-imagery (HEAD 200 on sample oblique and orthomosaic). The first sampled oblique was taken 2024-09-28 14:48Z and stored by 16:20Z (https://services.arcgis.com/XG15cJAlne2vxtgt/arcgis/rest/services/Image_Points_view/FeatureServer/1/query?where=project+LIKE+%27%25Helene%25%27&returnCountOnly=true&f=pjson).
- FEMA's own GeoPlatform republished NOAA Helene imagery as a 10 cm 4-band ImageServer (Hurricane_Helene_2024/Hurricane_Helene_NOAA) and a Maxar WorldView 2024-10-06 strip (0.36 m) at https://disasters.geoplatform.gov/image/rest/services/Hurricane_Helene_2024?f=pjson; the folder list there (Milton, Debby, Francine, Texas_Flood_2025, Wildfire_SC_MAR_2025, ...) is a de facto index of federally supported imagery events.
- The legacy FEMA/CAP REST host imageryuploader.geoplatform.gov (ImageEvents MapServer and WMS) advertised on the FEMA hub page no longer resolves (NXDOMAIN); the uploader app at imageuploader.geoplatform.gov requires login. The live public equivalents are the Image_Points_view FeatureServer (org XG15cJAlne2vxtgt) and disasters.geoplatform.gov services.
- OpenAerialMap holds 69 CAP Helene orthomosaics for NC/TN (2024-10-03 to 10-23, 8-10 cm, CC-BY 4.0, 0.3-2.2 GB each) and 17 Maxar Helene strips; 92 NC records exist since 2024-09-26 in total (https://api.openaerialmap.org/meta?bbox=-84.5,35.0,-80.5,36.6&acquisition_from=2024-09-26&limit=100). CAP entries are mis-tagged platform 'uav'.
- NC Wing CAP flew 56 sorties / 159 hours for Helene tasked by FEMA, NC Emergency Management, NC National Guard and NCDOT and staffed the state EOC 80+ hours (https://www.cap.news/north-carolina-wing-assists-in-hurricane-response). AFNORTH rules require geotagged imagery uploaded to the FEMA Image Uploader, automatic availability through USGS HDDS, and deletion of non-public imagery from CAP equipment within 72 hours (https://www.gocivilairpatrol.com/programs/emergency-services/operations-support/afnorth-imagery-requirements).
- NCDOT reported about 200 UAS missions in the first week and over 240 missions with CAP and NOAA, using three imagery types: rapid situational awareness fixed-wing flights within 24 hours (with CAP), site-specific drone imagery, and photogrammetry (https://www.ncdot.gov/news/press-releases/Pages/2024/ncdot-capturing-impacts.aspx). NCDOT Aviation published 6,745 drone photo/video points publicly (https://services.arcgis.com/NuWFvHYDMVmmxMeM/arcgis/rest/services/NCDOT_Drone_Locations_2024_Hurricane_Helene/FeatureServer/0).
- NCDOT Photogrammetry's rapid 'direct georeferenced' orthomosaics (0.5 ft planned GSD) carry 2-3 pixel (about 1.5 ft) XY error before orthorectification and were orthorectified to 2017 lidar bare earth, adding error where 2024 terrain changed; they are marked 'not to be used for pay items' and 'For INTERNAL NCDOT USE ONLY' although published as public AGO items (https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/096163b7a48743358a908405eb097fb3?f=pjson).
- The NC Collaboratory redirected $4 million to fly 13 WNC counties with lidar and 6-inch imagery in Jan-early March 2025 (leaf-off); AECOM coordinated acquisition; lidar was delivered progressively to the Collaboratory and NCEM through May 2025; data went to NC OneMap and to counties on hard drives (https://www.unc.edu/posts/2025/10/02/collaboratory-maps-helene-damage-to-speed-recovery/, https://it.nc.gov/smac-lidar-april-10-2025/open).
- NC Orthoimagery Program status (SMAC update 2025-10-07): 2025 phase = 26 counties, 15,141 sq mi, 16,885 tiles, four vendors, delivery Nov-Dec 2025; 2026 phase = 26 counties, 12,349 sq mi with '25% of Project Area Mountain Acquisition', flights Feb-Apr 2026, delivery Nov-Dec 2026; 116 active reviewers resolved 443 QC issues in 2025 (https://it.nc.gov/smac-ortho-program-update-oct-7-2025/open). The program is funded by the NC 911 Board and has collected 60.2 TB / 203,434 tiles since 2010 (https://www.nconemap.gov/pages/imagery).
- FAA SGI approvals for VLOS operations 'may be issued in minutes'; BVLOS approvals via SOSC 'will typically require the establishment of a TFR' and take longer; requests go through the TSA/FAA Waiver & Airspace Access Program portal, time-sensitive ones by phone to SOSC 202-267-8276; approval is an amendment to the COA or Remote Pilot Certificate (https://www.faa.gov/uas/advanced_operations/emergency_situations). SOSC email is staffed 0600-2400 ET and the phone 24/7 (FAA briefing to NC government UAS group, 2022).
- During Helene the FAA issued TFRs over NC/SC/TN and stated that TFRs 'do not ban aircraft, including drones, from providing disaster relief' when coordinated with emergency responders, while warning of fines or criminal prosecution (Oct 3-4, 2024); FAASTeam notice NOTC3963 (2024-10-01, CLT FSDO) alerted pilots to heavy public-safety drone traffic in WNC; press reported a 300% rise in WNC air traffic and 30 mid-air close calls in one day (https://www.faasafety.gov/spans/noticeView.aspx?nid=13963, https://www.popsci.com/environment/drones-milton-helene/).
- North Carolina repealed its state UAS operator permit (Article 10 of Chapter 63) effective 2024-12-01 via S.L. 2024-15 s.21; drone operators in NC now follow FAA rules plus NC statutes 15A-300.1 to 15A-300.4, 14-401.25 and 14-280.3. G.S. 15A-300.1 expressly allows emergency management use for 'damage assessment' and 'floodplain mapping' under G.S. 166A-19.3 and sets a civil penalty up to $5,000 per unlawfully published photo (https://www.ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_15A/GS_15A-300.1.html).
- Asheville-led UAV operations after Helene: 13 UAV teams from four states, 850+ flights, DroneSense feed aggregation, Skydio-installed Starlink, SD cards as the only data path when networks failed, and repeated survey requests of the same areas by multiple agencies (https://dronelife.com/2025/03/11/lessons-from-hurricane-helene-how-uavs-supported-emergency-response-in-western-north-carolina/); APD later fielded 13 DJI Docks with Ovrwatch (https://enterprise-insights.dji.com/user-stories/ashevilles-blueprint-how-drones-redefined-disaster-response-during-hurricane-helene).
- USACE ERDC's UAS team delivered orthomosaics and DEMs to USACE within 24 hours of collection in Watauga, Avery and Buncombe counties, 48 hours from approval to on-site (https://www.army.mil/article/280680/with_birds_eye_view_army_engineer_researchers_soldiers_respond_to_hurricane_helene).
- OpenDroneMap memory guidance: 40 images 4 GB, 250 images 16 GB, 500 images 32 GB, 1,500 images 64 GB, 5,000 images 256 GB RAM; GPUs give no benefit except ~2x faster SIFT (https://docs.opendronemap.org/installation/, https://github.com/OpenDroneMap/ODM). ODM --align accepts a GeoTIFF DEM or LAS/LAZ reference and performs coarse DSM matching then ICP (translation + rotation, scale fixed) (https://github.com/OpenDroneMap/ODM/blob/master/opendm/align.py).
- Registering drone orthomosaics to the NC OneMap basemap can be done three ways with open tools: (1) GCPs picked on NC OneMap 6-inch orthos in WebODM's GCP interface (5-10 GCPs, each in 5+ images), (2) ODM --align / CODEM against NC DEM03 (3.125 ft lidar DEM) or the Helene 2025 lidar for 3D placement, (3) AROSICS raster-to-raster phase correlation against the NC ortho for residual sub-pixel shifts. NCDOT's experience shows uncontrolled GNSS/IMU orthos carry 2-3 pixel error and terrain-change error when the DEM predates the event.
- ArcGIS Online's public search API (https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/search?q=Helene%20imagery&f=pjson) is an effective discovery channel for post-event imagery in NC: it surfaced NCDOT Photogrammetry and Aviation services, the NCEM clearinghouse, CAP's org items, WCU/UNCA mirrors and USFS/TNC canopy-loss layers that no agency web page lists.
- Commercial imagery was present in the Helene response: Vexcel/GIC collected 7.5-10 cm ortho and oblique imagery over Asheville (https://vexceldata.com/?p=14651) and NCDOT Traffic Safety maintains a 'Nearmap Images of Helene Impact Area' web map, indicating existing NC state Nearmap access.
- Lessons for NC Recon's drone module from the ecosystem: ingest must work offline (SD card), parse EXIF/XMP incl. gimbal pitch and RTK flags, deduplicate by exif_hash, store COGs, publish OIN metadata to OpenAerialMap for licence clarity, and expose a shared tasking/coverage layer (as FEMA's request layer and CAP's collection polygons did) to stop duplicate flights.
### gaps
- No public NC Emergency Management UAS program page, team roster or imagery product was found; the NCEM Risk Management page lists FRIS, FIMAN, SDD and lidar but no drone program (https://www.ncdps.gov/our-organization/emergency-management/risk-management). The WebSearch budget was exhausted before targeted NCEM/NCDPS queries could run.
- NC State Highway Patrol aviation (helicopter/aircraft) imagery from Helene: not found; no fetched source mentions SHP imagery products.
- NC National Guard aerial imagery: only press coverage of Black Hawk/Chinook reconnaissance and supply flights (https://www.wfae.org/2024-10-07/helene-national-guard-food-deliveries-aerial-searches-western-nc); no public imagery dataset or service found.
- County and municipal drone teams in WNC other than Asheville PD (e.g. Buncombe, Henderson, Haywood, Watauga, Rutherford, Yancey sheriff/fire/EM): not confirmed; Buncombe County emergency services page returned 403 and the search budget ran out before per-county queries.
- NC OneMap terms of use text (https://www.nconemap.gov/pages/terms) could not be extracted (ArcGIS Hub JS page); licence for NC orthoimagery is inferred as freely available public data.
- NC Collaboratory Helene lidar: quality level, point density, LAS/LAZ download URL and whether it is on sdd.nc.gov or NC OneMap were not confirmed (sdd.nc.gov and collaboratory.unc.edu/helene returned no content or 404).
- NCDOT drone photo access: ImageUrl values point to ncconnect.sharepoint.com; anonymous access was not tested and is likely blocked.
- NCDOT Photogrammetry Helene orthomosaics: licence status is contradictory (public AGO item, 'INTERNAL NCDOT USE ONLY' text); permission path not identified. The Jan 2025 NC Airports Association PDF with Helene aviation statistics returned 404.
- Exact CAP posting latency during Helene: only one sample (photo 14:48Z, stored 16:20Z on 2024-09-28); no official statement on turnaround. Public CAP GIS hub (public-cap-gis.hub.arcgis.com) and CAP aerial photography program page returned no text/404.
- USGS HDDS: whether a 'Hurricane Helene 2024' event exists and what it contains was not verified (JS app); M2M API applicability to HDDS unconfirmed.
- FAA: number of SGI approvals issued for Helene and the full text of JO 7210.3 chapter on SGI were not obtained; faa.gov blocks automated fetches (Hurricane Toolkit and emergency page were read only via a browser user-agent download).
- DRONERESPONDERS membership cost and the NCGRU's internal resources are behind a login; the Esri/NASA public-safety drone program directory was not confirmed.
- Vexcel/GIC pricing and whether first responders get free Gray Sky access were not stated; Nearmap NC licence terms unknown.
- LAS 1.5 approval date and change list could not be read (lasformat.org intro page 404 on Read the Docs); ASPRS legacy LAS page returned 404.
- OpenAerialMap API documentation site is JavaScript-only; query parameters were confirmed only by live API calls and the GitHub README.
- TDOT's Helene imagery MapServer (spatial.tdot.tn.gov Helene_TDOT_Imagery_WeekOfOct14th_v2) now returns 404 (decommissioned); the FEMA hub's imageryuploader.geoplatform.gov host is NXDOMAIN.
- NC OneMap Orthoimagery_20242027 service description appears copied from the 2020-2023 service; actual loaded phases were not verified.
- County list discrepancy for the Helene 2025 orthoimagery: service metadata lists Alexander, the UNC article lists Davie; unresolved.
- NOAA Helene WMTS capabilities: my layer listing showed flights through 20241005a; whether 20241005b-20241008a are also in the WMTS was not confirmed (they exist as COGs in S3).
- No evidence was found of a statewide NC drone imagery sharing portal or standard for counties to submit orthomosaics to NCEM/NC OneMap; sharing appears ad hoc (AGO items, SharePoint, hard drives, OAM uploads).

## Human-impact data and structure-level ground truth for validating/calibrating automated damage assessment in North Carolina (Helene DR-4827-NC as the reference event)
- (high/high) **OpenFEMA Individuals and Households Program - Valid Registrations (v2)** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/openfema-data-page/individuals-and-households-program-valid-registrations-v2
  Applicant-level IHP registrations with FEMA inspection outcomes: rpfvl/ppfvl (verified real/personal property loss), destroyed flag, habitabilityRepairsRequired, floodDamage/foundationDamage/roofDamage and amounts, waterLevel (inches), ownRent, residenceType, insurance flags, haAmount/onaAmount, plus damagedCity, damagedZipCode, county and a 12-digit censusGeoid (block group). Queried live: 282,711 rows for disasterNumber 4827; 100,547 with inspnReturned=true; 1,087 destroyed=true; 28,416 floodDamage=true; 2,439 inspected with rpfvl > $20,000.
  access: REST API https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v2/IndividualsAndHouseholdsProgramValidRegistrations (OData-style $filter/$top<=10000/$skip, $inlinecount) plus CSV/Parquet bulk files | auth: none (no API key) | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions: free use, mandatory citation of endpoint+version+access date and the 'not endorsed by FEMA' statement; statistical use only, no re-identification, no logo use | cadence: R/P1D (daily); weekly refresh for disasters in last 18 months; disasters appear 30+ days after declaration | NC: DR-4827-NC Helene fully present (282,711 registrations as of 2026-10-06); all NC declarations since DR-1439 (2002)
  use (post): Primary calibration/validation source at block-group level: aggregate inspected rpfvl, destroyed and habitabilityRepairsRequired by censusGeoid/zip and compare to NC Recon's predicted damage distribution; use waterLevel as flood-depth ground truth; use ownRent/residenceType/householdComposition to calibrate the displacement model; never a per-structure label because PII (address) is removed
  caveats: Raw NEMIS data, self-reported fields, 'not an official federal report'; only applicants appear (undercounts uninsured non-applicants and over-represents renters/low-income); location generalized to block group/zip; file too large for Excel
- (high/high) **OpenFEMA Housing Assistance Program Data - Owners (v2) and Renters (v2)** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/openfema-data-page/housing-assistance-program-data-owners-v2
  Aggregated per disaster x state x county x city x zip: validRegistrations, totalInspected, FEMA-inspected damage bands (none, $1-10k, $10-20k, $20-30k, >$30k) and averageFemaInspectedDamage for owners; for renters totalInspectedWithNoDamage/Moderate/Major/Substantial; approvals and IHP/repair/rental/ONA dollars. Live query: 1,401 owner rows and 1,434 renter rows for DR-4827.
  access: REST API https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v2/HousingAssistanceOwners and https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v2/HousingAssistanceRenters; bulk CSV/Parquet | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions (citation required) | cadence: R/P1D (daily) | NC: DR-4827-NC present; all NC IA declarations since DR-1439
  use (post): Zip-level validation table and the public 'small-count-safe' comparator for NC Recon's tract/zip summaries; damage-dollar bands can be mapped to FEMA damage categories for calibration curves per county
  caveats: County may be inferred or overridden by applicant; zip rows include out-of-area zips (e.g., a row with zip 20821); financial figures differ from USAspending
- (medium/high) **OpenFEMA Registration Intake and Individuals Household Program (RI-IHP) v2** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/openfema-data-page/registration-intake-and-individuals-household-program-ri-ihp-v2
  City/zip-level aggregates of valid registrations by channel (call center, web, mobile) and IHP/HA/ONA referrals, eligibility and dollars. 2,029 rows for DR-4827.
  access: REST API https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v2/RegistrationIntakeIndividualsHouseholdPrograms | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions | cadence: R/P1D | NC: DR-4827-NC present
  use (post): Early-recovery demand signal (registrations per zip within days of declaration) to compare against predicted affected-structure counts; registration channel mix flags connectivity-poor areas
  caveats: Aggregated only; registrations are not damage
- (low/high) **OpenFEMA Individual Assistance Housing Registrants - Large Disasters (v1)** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/openfema-data-page/individual-assistance-housing-registrants-large-disasters-v1
  Curated applicant-level set for selected large disasters with waterLevel, floodDamage, destroyed, rpfvl, ppfvl, habitabilityRepairsRequired, damagedZipCode, censusBlockId. Live query: 0 rows for 4827; highest disasterNumber present is 4611 (2021).
  access: REST API https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v1/IndividualAssistanceHousingRegistrantsLargeDisasters | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions | cadence: irregular | NC: Helene not included; NC presence for Florence/Matthew unconfirmed
  use (post): Not usable for Helene; v2 Valid Registrations supersedes it. Note only.
  caveats: Geocoding may snap to polygon centers; irregular updates; no Helene
- (medium/high) **OpenFEMA Public Assistance Funded Projects Details (v2)** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/openfema-data-page/public-assistance-funded-projects-details-v2

  133-page revised assessment: $59.6B total ($44.4B direct, $9.4B indirect, $5.8B mitigation); housing need $15.4B incl. $12.7B residential structure damage; ~73,700 homes expected damaged; 274,900 IA applications projected; 25,000-30,000 households expected to receive Housing Assistance; 5.2% of flood-damaged applicants had flood insurance; 2,000+ landslides with 260 damaging multiple homes; 82 public schools closed 20+ days; methodology combines 'top-down' FEMA applicant-level IA data (as of Dec 2) scaled from Florence with 'bottom-up' NCEM/FEMA SAR structure categories (destroyed/major/minor/affected/unaffected through Nov 19) joined to NC OneMap county parcels and NCEM inundation and DEQ/USGS landslide layers, valuing damage at 100%/55%/20%/10% of improved value (Wisconsin EM method) and 10% for inundated parcels without SAR assessment.

  caveats: Statewide only; claims counts not comparable to structures (multiple coverages per home)
