  GROW NC: temporary housing placements (5,720 households Mar 2025), debris cubic yards, % roads reopened, small-business loans, parks reopened; quarterly reports to NCGA. Renew NC Single-Family Housing ($807M of $1.4B CDBG-DR): daily dashboard of applications by county and review stage; press/legislative figures: ~7,924 applications, 5,366 inactive/ineligible, 3,676 active (Mar 2026), 89 completed and 71 under construction (Jul 2026), 159 completed (Sep 2026); county actives e.g. Buncombe 765 of 2,151, Henderson 340 of 958, McDowell 163 of 626, Yancey 118 of 474; award types 65% reconstruction ($365k avg), 24% rehabilitation ($32k), 11% replacement ($191k).
  access: web dashboards (ArcGIS/Power BI style, JS-rendered); PDF quarterly reports via webservices.ncleg.gov | auth: none | licence: NC state (unstated) | cadence: Renew NC dashboard 'updated daily'; GROW NC periodic; quarterly NCGA reports | NC: 29 CDBG-DR eligible counties + one zip; HUD MID 28 counties
  use (post): County-level recovery-progress benchmark (eligible/ineligible/completed homes) to compare with NC Recon's Major/Destroyed owner-occupied counts; the reconstruction vs rehabilitation split is a proxy for Destroyed vs Major shares
  caveats: Dashboards are JS-only (figures above came from press releases, the April 2 2026 DCR legislative presentation and Carolina Journal); no download; applicant-level data is confidential
- (low/medium) **NC Office of the State Auditor Helene Recovery dashboard** (NC Office of the State Auditor) https://dashboards.ncauditor.gov/helene-recovery/
  Weekly dashboard of FEMA-derived displacement and housing metrics by county across the 28 HUD-designated counties (displaced persons, housed, still seeking assistance, state temporary housing units) plus state spending by agency and expenditure transactions; launched Feb 11 2025.
  access: web dashboard (JS), expenditure transaction viewer | auth: none | licence: NC state (unstated) | cadence: weekly (per launch announcement) | NC: 28 HUD MID counties
  use (post): Secondary county-level displacement benchmark for the displaced-persons estimator
  caveats: Re-publishes FEMA data; no download; methodology for 'displaced' not documented
- (medium/medium) **County property tax reassessment of Helene-damaged structures (Legal Aid NC county protocol compilation; Buncombe 2025 damage notices)** (33 county tax offices in DR-4827; compiled by Legal Aid of North Carolina (Feb 5 2025)) https://legalaidnc.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Tax-Value-Reassessment-and-Appeals-by-County-v1.pdf
  Per-county protocols for revaluing Helene-damaged property for tax year 2025: Buncombe mailed appeal forms to all owners whose property was damaged (deadline Mar 31) and delayed general reappraisal to 2026; McDowell 'has access to the initial first response North Carolina Emergency Management database of homes damaged' and hired an appraisal firm; Watauga, Wilkes and Union were 'given a list of names and homes that were damaged'; Iredell received damaged-property lists from FEMA and fire departments; Avery, Graham, Forsyth sent appraisers to damaged properties; Rutherford reappraises buildings still damaged/destroyed on Jan 1 2025 at lower or zero value. No county publishes a parcel-level Helene damage adjustment dataset.
  access: portal download (PDF); county notices by mail; parcel values via county GIS/NC OneMap parcels | auth: none | licence: Legal Aid NC publication; county data unstated | cadence: annual (tax year 2025 values as of Jan 1 2025; Buncombe 2026 general reappraisal notices Feb 2026) | NC: 33 WNC/Piedmont counties contacted
  use (post): Latent ground truth: year-over-year building-value drops in county parcel data (NC OneMap statewide parcels) between 2024 and 2025 assessments identify structures the assessor judged damaged; propose MOUs with Buncombe/McDowell tax offices for the damage-adjusted parcel list as a label set
  caveats: Buncombe county pages (buncombenc.gov) returned 403 to fetches; counts of revalued parcels unknown; G.S. 105-380 prevents tax forgiveness so adjustments appear only in the following tax year
- (medium/medium) **Building permit data for rebuild tracking (Buncombe County Reduce to Rebuild; Asheville Accela permits)** (Buncombe County Permits & Inspections; City of Asheville) https://media.buncombenc.gov/common/Commissioners/20250506/NB%201_Preso%20Rev_Reduce%20to%20Rebuild%20Proposal.pdf
  Buncombe reports Helene-related building permit applications at 1,515 unique locations and inspections at 1,256 locations (May 20 2025) and ~400 Reduce-to-Rebuild fee-reduction permits; eligibility requires the dwelling be 'documented by a Buncombe County or Federal Emergency Disaster Agency Damage Assessment with a rating of Destroyed, Major, Moderate, or Minor' (so the county keeps an internal address-level damage-rating list). Disaster Recovery Act of 2024 waived permit fees 9/26-12/31/2024 statewide in designated counties. Buncombe's public ArcGIS services (permits, Accela) expose only base layers, not permit records; Asheville's AccelaPermitsView exposes 66,143 permit points.
  access: Asheville: ArcGIS MapServer query; Buncombe: no public permit records found (Accela portal only) | auth: none for Asheville layer | licence: unstated | cadence: Asheville layer live; Buncombe counts in monthly commission briefings | NC: Asheville and Buncombe; other WNC counties not found
  use (post): Rebuild-progress channel: permits issued after the event at predicted Major/Destroyed structures confirm damage and track recovery; request Buncombe's Helene permit/inspection location list under MOU
  caveats: Permits lag damage by months and skip uninsured/informal repairs; Asheville permits are not Helene-tagged
- (medium/high) **Crisis Cleanup (open-source disaster work-order platform) public worksites API** (Crisis Cleanup (nonprofit); client code Apache-2.0 on GitHub) https://api.crisiscleanup.org/docs/
  Incident 171 'Hurricanes Helene & Milton' has 54,937 worksites in the unauthenticated endpoint https://api.crisiscleanup.org/worksites_public?incident=171 returning id, case_number, point location (lat/lon), created/updated and work_types with status (debris, trees, muck_out, tarp, mold_remediation, rebuild, fence, heating, other) and claimed_by; address/name stripped. Full worksite records (address, damage notes, photos) require an authenticated account held by verified relief organizations; Buncombe EOC and NC VOADs used it (hotline 844-965-1386).
  access: REST API (public worksites_public and incidents endpoints; authenticated /worksites); Scalar API docs | auth: none for public endpoints; organization account for full data | licence: Web app Apache-2.0; data terms unstated (platform is 'open and transparent among participating organizations', not public) | cadence: live | NC: Helene incident spans NC/SC/GA/FL/TN/VA; NC share must be derived spatially
  use (during,post): Household-level 'need' signal within days: density of muck_out/rebuild/tarp work orders vs predicted damage; late-stage open work orders flag under-served areas for the needs ranking; ask Crisis Cleanup for an NCEM data-sharing account to ingest damage notes as labels
  caveats: Self-requested help, biased to volunteer-served areas; work type is not a FEMA damage level; public endpoint undocumented so terms of use for bulk pulls should be confirmed with Crisis Cleanup
- (low/medium) **NC 211 / 211 Counts and United Way of NC Helene call data** (United Way of North Carolina (NC 211); 211 Counts platform) https://nc211.org/data/
  NC 211 publishes caller-need data through 211 Counts (nc.211counts.org) by zip, county, region and legislative district, 'as recently as yesterday'. Helene: 30,000+ calls in Oct 2024 (vs ~15,000 typical monthly), nearly 16,000 welfare-check requests over 5 months via call and text 'PERSON' to 40403; NC 211 staffed the State EOC for 26 days.
  access: web dashboard (211 Counts); no API documented; data requests to UWNC | auth: none for dashboard | licence: unstated | cadence: daily (211 Counts) | NC: statewide
  use (during,post): Zip-level demand signal (shelter, housing, food requests) to compare with predicted displacement; welfare-check requests as a proxy for isolation
  caveats: nc.211counts.org and 211counts.org returned 404/522 during this research; no geographic Helene breakdown published; call counts are not damage
- (low/low) **American Red Cross detailed damage assessments (Helene) and casework** (American Red Cross) https://www.redcross.org/local/north-carolina/about-us/news-and-events/news/the-american-red-cross-continues-to-support-affected-communities.html
  Red Cross reported nearly 14,000 door-to-door detailed residential damage assessments for Helene (search snippet of NC Red Cross news page) and an early estimate of 10,000+ homes destroyed; assessments use smartphone apps and GPS (RC View) and drive financial assistance casework. No public dataset exists; data is shared with partners case-by-case.
  access: request-only (Red Cross disaster partnership / state EM liaison) | auth: MOU / partner agreement | licence: proprietary | cadence: per event, weeks after | NC: WNC Helene response area
  use (post): Potential structure-level label source via NCEM-Red Cross data sharing; not available openly
  caveats: redcross.org pages returned 403 to fetches; 14,000 figure is from a search-result snippet, unverified on the primary page
- (low/medium) **Humanitarian OpenStreetMap Team / OSM Helene mapping** (HOT (Tasking Manager) / OpenStreetMap community) https://tasking-manager-production-api.hotosm.org/api/v2/projects/?textSearch=Helene
  HOT Tasking Manager API returns zero projects for 'Helene' and for 'North Carolina'; OSM wiki pages '2024_Hurricane_Helene' and 'Hurricane_Helene' do not exist. No HOT activation or coordinated OSM damage tagging for Helene in NC was found. (GISCorps PhotoMappers geolocated crowd photos for FEMA/NAPSG but its Helene data page was not reachable.)
  access: REST API (Tasking Manager v2) / OSM API | auth: none | licence: ODbL (OSM) | cadence: n/a | NC: none for Helene
  use (post): Note only: OSM building footprints remain a structure source but no OSM damage labels exist for Helene
  caveats: Negative finding; archived/private TM projects would not appear in textSearch
- (low/medium) **Academic Helene datasets: StEER PVRR and Annotated Media Repository (DesignSafe PRJ-5669), Recov-Vision street-view study, ECU/PSABC Montreat historic survey, Richmond Fed housing analysis** (NSF StEER network; Texas A&M/UNC authors (Recov-Vision); East Carolina University with Preservation Society of Asheville and Buncombe County; Federal Reserve Bank of Richmond) https://researchonline.stthomas.edu/esploro/outputs/report/StEER-Hurricane-Helene-Preliminary-Virtual-Reconnaissance/991015267328903691
  StEER activated Level 1 (virtual VAST only, no FAST door-to-door ratings) producing the PVRR (DOI 10.17603/ds2-rgnq-e383) and a geolocated annotated media repository (DOI 10.17603/ds2-mxeh-7712) on DesignSafe. Recov-Vision (arXiv 2509.20628, CC BY 4.0) applies vision-language models to post-Helene street-view surveys in Swannanoa/Black Mountain to infer occupancy/recovery attributes. ECU/PSABC surveyed ~400 historic Montreat structures (pre-storm June 2024 vs post-storm, severe/moderate/minor). Richmond Fed (Mar 2025) analysed OpenFEMA HA data for 24 WNC counties ($347M assessed FEMA damage; highest totals Buncombe, Henderson, Yancey; highest per-unit McDowell, Avery, Ashe, Yancey).
  access: DesignSafe portal (account for download; pages returned 403 to automated fetch); arXiv; ECU news; Richmond Fed web | auth: DesignSafe free account | licence: StEER data typically ODC-By/CC BY on DesignSafe; arXiv paper CC BY 4.0; Montreat survey unpublished | cadence: static | NC: WNC (Asheville area, Swannanoa, Black Mountain, Montreat); PVRR multi-state
  use (post): Geotagged annotated photos as spot ground truth for specific structures; Recov-Vision approach is a candidate street-level evidence channel; Montreat survey could be requested from PSABC as 400 labeled historic structures
  caveats: No StEER FAST building-by-building ratings for Helene; Recov-Vision dataset release not confirmed; DesignSafe pages blocked automated fetch
- (low/medium) **Hospital, school and nursing-home impact records (NCDHHS fatalities page; OSBM education section; EdNC school damage reporting)** (NC DHHS Office of the Chief Medical Examiner; NC OSBM; EdNC/NC DPI) https://www.ncdhhs.gov/assistance/hurricane-helene-recovery-resources/hurricane-helene-storm-related-fatalities
  NCDHHS: 107 verified storm-related deaths by county (Buncombe 42, Henderson 12, Yancey 11, Avery 6, Haywood 5; 21 counties) as of June 17 2025. OSBM: 1,100+ public schools in the 39 counties, 82 schools in six LEAs plus two community colleges and one UNC campus closed 20+ days; 11 districts closed 10+ days. EdNC: four schools relocated (Atkinson Elementary, Old Fort Elementary, Valle Crucis School 46,000 of 66,000 sq ft flooded, Micaville Elementary permanently closed). No facility-level dataset of damaged hospitals, nursing homes or adult care homes was found; NC DHHS reached out to all licensed facilities and HHS deployed ~200 personnel to assess hospitals/nursing homes/dialysis.
  access: web pages / PDF | auth: none | licence: NC state (unstated) | cadence: fatalities updated only on change since Dec 19 2024 | NC: statewide/WNC
  use (post): Critical-facility validation list (named schools) and fatality-by-county context for the brief; facility-status feeds would need NC DHHS/NCEM WebEOC access
  caveats: No open facility-damage dataset; NC DPI facility damage reports not located
- (medium/high) **NHC Tropical Cyclone Report AL092024 Hurricane Helene (county damage narrative) and FEMA DR-4827 disaster page** (NOAA National Hurricane Center; FEMA) https://www.nhc.noaa.gov/data/tcr/AL092024_Helene.pdf
  NHC TCR (107 pp, Mar 2025) compiles county-level impacts: Watauga 118 buildings destroyed, 479 major, 538 minor/moderate; Yancey ~1,400 destroyed, 2,300 severely damaged, 2,000 minor-moderate (media-sourced); Haywood ~600 homes flooded, a couple hundred destroyed; Chimney Rock/Lake Lure majority of buildings destroyed; Nash County EF-3 damaged 11 buildings. FEMA DR-4827 page (updated Jul 14 2026): $567.4M IA approved to 161,042 applicants ($294.3M housing, $273.1M ONA), $2.38B PA obligated ($1.31B emergency, $877M permanent), $201.5M HMGP; FEMA fact sheets: 29,000+ home inspections by Oct 24 2024, $320M to 146,000 households by Jan 24 2025.
  access: portal download (PDF 17 MB); web page | auth: none | licence: US Government public domain | cadence: static report; FEMA page updated periodically | NC: WNC counties narrative; statewide program totals
  use (post): County-total benchmarks (destroyed/major/minor) for Watauga, Yancey, Henderson, Haywood to check NC Recon county tallies; program totals for the brief
  caveats: County counts are quoted from local officials/media in the TCR, methods unstated; Henderson's windshield counts (299 destroyed/1,400 major) 'tracked closely' with FEMA's 335/1,391
- (low/medium) **USACE Operation Blue Roof (Helene)** (US Army Corps of Engineers) https://www.usace.army.mil/
  No evidence found that Operation Blue Roof was activated for DR-4827-NC; Helene/Milton Blue Roof activity found only for Florida (DVIDS, Florida DEM). USACE's NC Helene missions were temporary power (generators) and debris. No public Blue Roof installation dataset exists for any state (addresses are PII).
  access: request-only (USACE/FEMA mission data) | auth: MOU | licence: n/a | cadence: n/a | NC: none for Helene
  use (post): Note only: blue-tarp detection cannot be validated against Blue Roof rolls in NC; a future wind event (coastal hurricane) would make this relevant via FEMA Region IV
  caveats: Negative finding based on searches; USACE Wilmington District pages not fetched directly
### insights
- No FEMA dataset gives structure- or address-level damage for DR-4827-NC: IHP Valid Registrations is applicant-level but generalized to block group (censusGeoid) and zip; the FEMA Historical Geospatial Damage Assessment Database (https://services.arcgis.com/XG15cJAlne2vxtgt/arcgis/rest/services/FEMA_Historical_Geospatial_Damage_Assessment_Database/FeatureServer) was last edited 2021-06-22 and contains no Helene rows; FEMA RGO/Dewberry/NGA/Civil Air Patrol imagery assessments for Helene (90,000+ assessments, 40,000+ damaged structures across six states per https://www.cap.news/p/geospatial-team-assessing-hurricane-destruction) were never published.
- The only public structure-level Helene labels with full FEMA categories including negatives are a Buncombe County extract of NCEM/FEMA SARCOP waypoints (12,891 points: unaffected 10,081, affected 635, minor 697, major 907, destroyed 564) at https://services6.arcgis.com/VLA0ImJ33zhtGEaP/arcgis/rest/services/SARCOP_v9_SIFS_NC_Damage/FeatureServer; the statewide NCEM SAR damage database exists (OSBM DNA methodology; McDowell tax office cites access to it) but is not public and would need an NCEM MOU.
- NC's Helene damage assessment stack was Esri-based: NCEM SAR teams used SARCOP QuickCapture/Smart Forms; Buncombe used Survey123 ATC-45 (2,097 inspections) plus a UNCA NEMAC imagery grid tool (802 grids, 6,401 damage points) and NRCS EWP Damage Survey Reports in Survey123; Asheville ran a Survey123 Phase I property damage form (1,499 records with FEMA categories); Esri reported 64,000+ damage assessment points collected and GIS staff deployed to 10 counties (search snippet of ArcNews, primary page returned 403). NC Recon's field ingest should accept SARCOP, ATC-45 and ArcGIS Damage Assessment Solution schemas.
- OSBM's Dec 13 2024 Damage and Needs Assessment (https://www.osbm.nc.gov/hurricane-helene-dna/open) values structure damage as improved parcel value x 100%/55%/20%/10% for destroyed/major/minor/affected (Wisconsin EM method), joins NCEM SAR points and inundation to NC OneMap county parcels, and projects 73,700 homes damaged, 274,900 IA applications and $12.7B residential damage; total need $59.6B. This is the state's de facto loss-estimation standard and NC Recon's dollar export should mirror it.
- Live OpenFEMA counts for DR-4827 (queried 2026-10-06): 282,711 IHP valid registrations, 100,547 inspections returned, 1,087 destroyed flags, 28,416 flood-damage flags, 2,439 inspected losses over $20k; 1,401 owner and 1,434 renter zip aggregates; 3,029 PA project worksheets; 678 mission-assignment actions; 644 HMGP mitigated properties; 2,035 NFIP claims in NC with loss dates 2024-09-25..10-05. FEMA DR-4827 page: $567.4M IA to 161,042 applicants, $2.38B PA, $201.5M HMGP (https://www.fema.gov/disaster/4827).
- FEMA 'destroyed' flags (1,087) are far below county windshield counts (Yancey ~1,400 destroyed, Henderson 299, Watauga 118, Buncombe 372 per county briefings/NHC TCR), showing FEMA inspection 'destroyed' is a stricter and applicant-limited label; NC Recon validation must compare like with like (SAR/ATC-45 categories vs FEMA inspection categories separately).
- County tax offices used emergency-management and FEMA damaged-property lists to revalue 2025 assessments (Buncombe mailed appeal forms to all damaged owners; McDowell used the NCEM database; Watauga/Wilkes/Union/Iredell received lists) per https://legalaidnc.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Tax-Value-Reassessment-and-Appeals-by-County-v1.pdf; year-over-year building-value changes in NC OneMap parcels are therefore an untapped structure-level damage proxy.
- Several public county ArcGIS layers expose survivor PII: Asheville Phase I survey has names in all 1,499 rows; Buncombe DCM intake layer has 2,198 client names and 1,501 FEMA registration numbers (https://services6.arcgis.com/VLA0ImJ33zhtGEaP/arcgis/rest/services/DCMUpdateActive05122025/FeatureServer/0). NC Recon should strip PII at ingest, never redistribute, and the deployment plan should include a responsible-disclosure note to these GIS offices.
- Landslide ground truth is strong: USGS inventory 2,217 points (CC0; 55% damaged infrastructure or property; 260 slides damaged multiple homes) and NCGS Landslide Inventory of NC with 7,046 Helene observations (Dwelling 342, Building 180 impacts; collapse states) and ~20,400 Helene polygons; Buncombe alone had 145 slides affecting 246 residential properties.
- FEMA accepts state Initial Damage Assessment geospatial data via the PDA Operations Hub System and the ArcGIS Damage Assessment Solution collaboration group as feature service/shapefile/CSV with damage class, address, lat/lon and photos (https://www.fema.gov/fact-sheet/how-share-initial-damage-assessment-geospatial-datasets-fema); NC Recon exports should match this schema and the 2025 PDA Guide IA damage matrix.
- OpenFEMA API: base https://www.fema.gov/api/open/, no key, $top max 10,000, $skip paging, OData-like $filter, JSON/JSONA/JSONL/GeoJSON/CSV/Parquet, OpenAPI metadata at https://www.fema.gov/api/open/metadata/v3.0/OpenApi.json; terms require citation of endpoint+version+date and the statement that the product is not endorsed by FEMA; rate limits undocumented.
- Deprecations to plan for: PA Funded Projects v1 removed 2026-06-24 (use v2); NFIP Claims v2 frozen 2026-06-01 (use v3 NfipClaims); NFIP Policies v2 deprecated 2026-10-15 (v3 NfipPolicies); HMA Mitigated Properties v4 removed 2026-10-15 (HMA Subapplications Project Site Inventories); Mission Assignments v1 removed.
- Insurance context: NC DOI data call (final Sept 12 2025) shows 93,060 residential property claims ($1.42B incurred) but only 684 private flood claims statewide, and OSBM found 5.2% of IA applicants with verified flood damage had flood insurance; NFIP shows only ~2,035 NC claims for Helene, so insured-loss data cannot validate most WNC flood damage.
- Recovery-phase ground truth accrues slowly: Renew NC had 7,924 applications (5,366 ineligible/inactive, 3,676 active) with 89 homes completed by July 2026 and 159 by Sept 2026; Buncombe logged Helene permit applications at 1,515 locations by May 2025; Crisis Cleanup holds 54,937 Helene/Milton worksites with public point locations and work types.
- NWS DAT contributed only 13 NC points for Helene (tornado/wind, Blacksburg office) because its taxonomy is EF-scale; it will matter for NC tornado outbreaks, not flood events.
- Negative findings: no HOT/OSM Helene activation (Tasking Manager API returns nothing for Helene or North Carolina); no USACE Blue Roof in NC for Helene; no public NC DPI or NC DHHS facility-damage dataset; no county (Henderson, McDowell, Yancey, Watauga, Haywood) publishes parcel-level structure damage, only Buncombe/Asheville/Brevard/Avery layers exist.
### gaps
- Statewide NCEM SAR/IDA structure damage points (the dataset behind OSBM's bottom-up estimate and the HeleneEstimatedFloodInundation layer) are not public; only a Buncombe extract was found. Access terms and schema from NCEM GIS unknown.
- FEMA's imagery-based Helene damage assessments (RGO/Dewberry/NGA/CAP, 90,000+ assessments) were not located on GeoPlatform, ArcGIS Online or data.gov; the Historical Damage Assessment Database stops at 2020.
- FEMA PDA report for DR-4827-NC (county counts of destroyed/major/minor/affected) could not be confirmed: the PDA reports index returned 503/404 and the first page did not list 4827; the PDA Public Assistance dataset is 'restricted public' with no access instructions.
- Esri ArcNews article on NC Helene GIS (64,000 damage points, GIS staff to 10 counties) returned 403; the figure rests on a search snippet and the MTAS catalog record.
- County-level parcel/structure damage data for Henderson, McDowell, Yancey, Watauga, Haywood, Avery (beyond the Avery imagery-inspection layer) was not found on county GIS portals or ArcGIS Online; county totals exist only in press and the NHC report.
- Buncombe County web pages (buncombenc.gov news, MyValueBC reappraisal, Helene recovery pages) returned 403 to fetches, so the number of parcels revalued for Helene damage in 2025 and the county's internal damage-rating list format are unknown.
- No open dataset of hospitals, nursing homes, adult care homes or dialysis centers damaged/evacuated in Helene (NC DHHS, NCHA, HHS emPOWER not located); no NC DPI facility damage report located.
- Red Cross door-to-door detailed damage assessment counts (~14,000) and casework data: primary pages returned 403; data is partner-only.
- Crisis Cleanup: terms of use for the undocumented public worksites_public endpoint and the authenticated API data-sharing policy for government EM agencies were not retrievable (site is JS-rendered).
- NC 211 Counts dashboard (nc.211counts.org) returned 404/522; whether zip-level Helene need data can be exported is unknown.
- Renew NC and GROW NC dashboards are JS-only; applications-by-county figures were taken from press and legislative PDFs, not the live dashboard; no CSV export confirmed.
- USACE Operation Blue Roof activation status for NC Helene was inferred as negative from searches; USACE Wilmington District primary confirmation not fetched.
- StEER DesignSafe project pages (PRJ-5669, annotated media repository) returned 403; record counts, licence and exact photo geolocation precision unknown.
- LINC (linc.osbm.nc.gov/pages/nc-helene) Helene indicators could not be read (JS page).
- Brevard 'Helene SD Structures' layer fields and count were not retrieved (layer id 306 service); Avery_Imagery_Inspections field semantics (IMAGE/CHECKED) undocumented.
- Web search budget was exhausted mid-task, so Watauga/Haywood/Mitchell county GIS pages, NC DPI, HHS emPOWER, HUD CDBG-DR unmet-needs methodology and GISCorps PhotoMappers Helene data were not searched further.

## Security, privacy and resilience requirements for a self-hosted web tool (NC Recon) that holds building-level damage data and addresses for North Carolina responders
- (high/high) **NC Statewide Information Security Manual: SCIO-SEC-307 Identification and Authentication Policy (v4, reviewed 03/26/2025)** (NC Department of Information Technology (NCDIT), Enterprise Security and Risk Management Office) https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-identification-and-authentication/open
  Binding statewide policy implementing the NIST SP 800-53 IA control family under G.S. 143B-1376. Covers all State information systems including contractor-operated ones; local governments are 'encouraged' to use it.
  access: portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state government publication (public record) | cadence: reviewed annually (last 03/26/2025) | NC: Statewide; mandatory for executive-branch agencies and their vendors; voluntary for counties and municipalities
  use (pre,during,post): Controlling requirement set for authentication design. Concrete rules: no shared accounts (IA-2a), which rules out the single shared TERRATRIAGE_TOKEN for any state deployment; MFA for all privileged accounts (IA-2(1)); MFA for remote access, privileged and non-privileged, to systems holding Restricted/Highly Restricted data, with one factor on a separate device (IA-2(2)); replay-resistant auth (TLS, OTP) (IA-2(8)); accept PIV (IA-2(12)); identifiers disabled after 90 days inactivity, not reused for 7 years (IA-4); passwords >=14 chars with upper/lower/digit/special, stored with salted KDF, checked against breached-password lists, generic 'log-on failed' messages, lockout after N failures (IA-5, IA-5(1)); non-privileged password max lifetime 1 year with MFA / 180 days without; FIPS 140-2 validated modules for encrypted transmissions (IA-7); public users uniquely identified unless an AC-14 exception is documented (IA-8). Implement: per-user accounts via SSO, admin MFA, lockout, breached-password check if any local password exists.
  caveats: Password composition/rotation rules conflict with NIST SP 800-63B-4 (2025) and with NCDIT's own Digital Accessibility and Usability Standard (no periodic rotation for public-facing systems). Local governments are not legally bound; many adopt it anyway.
- (high/high) **SCIO-SEC-301 Access Control Policy (v4, 3/26/2025)** (NCDIT) https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-access-control/open
  Statewide AC-family policy: account management, least privilege, lockout, banners, session timeouts, remote access, permitted actions without authentication.
  access: portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state government publication | cadence: annual review | NC: Statewide (state agencies and vendors; local encouraged)
  use (pre,during,post): Concrete numbers for the responder UI: AC-7 limit unsuccessful logons to 3 in 120 minutes then disable, lock-out >=30 minutes, notify an administrator; AC-2 inactivity logout at 15 minutes and AC-12 terminate idle sessions at <=30 minutes; AC-8 display the standard State logon banner ('This is a government computer system and is the property of...') before access; AC-3/AC-6 role-based access as default method, deny-all default for Restricted data, documented review of standard users at least annually and of admin accounts; AC-14 public websites may be accessed without authentication when documented. Implement four roles (public, analyst, responder, administrator) with deny-all default on structure-level data, idle timeout 15-30 min, lockout, banner, and an exportable access-review report.
- (high/high) **SCIO-SEC-303 Audit and Accountability Policy (v4, 03/26/2025)** (NCDIT) https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-audit-accountability/open
  Statewide AU-family policy: auditable events, record content, storage, failure response, review, time stamps, protection and retention of audit records.
  access: portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state government publication | cadence: annual review | NC: Statewide (state agencies and vendors; local encouraged)
  use (pre,during,post): Defines what the audit.log must contain and how long to keep it: AU-3 each record must identify date/time, component, event type, user identity, source, outcome and security-relevant actions; AU-3(1) full text of privileged commands and individual identities behind group accounts; AU-4/AU-11 retain audit records at least two (2) years (per the DNCR General Schedule for State Agency Records, IT Records; 7 years for FTI); AU-5 immediate alert on audit failure or storage exhaustion; AU-6 designated staff review logs, SIEM integration; AU-8 time stamps in UTC or with offset, synchronized to a DIT-defined authoritative source; AU-9 protect logs from modification, back up to a physically different system, WORM media acceptable, AU-9(4) only a subset of privileged users manage audit functions. Implement: structured JSON audit records with outcome and object id, NTP, ship/copy logs to a separate store with >=2-year retention, write-failure alerting, admin-only log management.
- (high/high) **SCIO-SEC-306 Contingency Planning Policy (v4, 03/26/2025)** (NCDIT) https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-contingency-planning/open
  Statewide CP-family policy covering contingency plans, RTO/RPO, backups, recovery and annual testing; explicitly covers cloud-hosted environments.

  use (pre,during,post): CP-2 requires a written plan with RTO ('e.g., one business day (8 hours) or one day (24 hours)') and RPO (point to which data is recovered from the most recent backup); CP-9 backups of system and user-level information at a frequency consistent with the agency RTO/RPO, with confidentiality and integrity protected; CP-10 recovery to a known state within RTO/RPO; CP-4 test the plan at least annually with an after-action report and corrective actions. Implement: declare NC Recon RTO (target <=4 h on a spare host from image + backup repo) and RPO (<=1 h for labels, ops_registry, audit log; 24 h for re-derivable outputs), automate backups to that cadence, and run an annual restore drill documented in docs/ops.
- (high/high) **SCIO-SEC-316 System and Communications Protection Policy (v4, 03/26/2025)** (NCDIT) https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-system-and-communications-protection/open

Implement: Caddy TLS 1.2/1.3 (already), host-level volume encryption (LUKS/dm-crypt or cloud disk encryption) for /app/data and backups, AES-256/SHA-256/ECDHE only, documented key custody.
- (high/high) **Statewide Data Classification and Handling Policy v2.0 (February 2026)** (NCDIT) https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/statewide-data-classification-handling-policy/open
  NC's data classification scheme, renamed in Feb 2026 to four tiers: Public (formerly Low Risk), Internal (formerly Medium), Confidential (formerly Medium), Restricted (formerly High). Lists statutory confidentiality categories and handling requirements per tier.
  access: portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state government publication | cadence: reviewed annually | NC: Statewide (state data; local encouraged)
  use (pre,during,post): Classification map for NC Recon data: mixed systems default to the highest tier present; 'security features' of IT systems (passwords, configurations, security standards) are confidential under G.S. 132-6.1(c) (audit logs, Caddyfile, tokens, OIDC client secrets => Confidential); sensitive public security information under G.S. 132-1.7 is non-public, while 'general adoption' and budget information is Public; PII handling references NIST SP 800-122; in-transit requirements scale from basic transport encryption (Public) through TLS for internal, 'Strong encryption (TLS 1.2+)' for Confidential, to end-to-end encryption for Restricted. Recommended: structure-level damage records with addresses => Confidential (not statutorily regulated PII, but not for public release); tract-level suppressed summaries and Conditions map => Public; label the tier in every export's metadata.
  caveats: The fetched text shows the four renamed tiers; older NC policies (incl. SCIO-SEC-307/316) still say 'Restricted or Highly Restricted'. Map Highly Restricted -> Restricted, Restricted -> Confidential when reconciling.
- (high/high) **NC cyber incident reporting and ransomware statutes: G.S. 143B-1376, 143B-1379, 143-800; NCDIT and NCDPS reporting pages** (NC General Assembly; NCDIT; NC Department of Public Safety / NC Emergency Management (Joint Cybersecurity Task Force)) https://it.nc.gov/programs/cybersecurity-risk-management/cyber-incident-reporting
  G.S. 143B-1376 gives the State CIO authority for statewide security standards binding on state agencies (local governments not covered). G.S. 143B-1379 requires local government entities to report cybersecurity incidents to NCDIT; shared information is protected from disclosure under G.S. 132-6.1(c). G.S. 143-800 (S.L. 2021-180) prohibits any state agency or local government entity from paying or communicating with a ransomware actor and requires consultation with NCDIT. NCDIT's page sets the expectation of reporting within 24 hours of confirmation via the Statewide Cybersecurity Incident Report Form or DIT.ThreatManagement@nc.gov; the Joint Cybersecurity Task Force (law enforcement, NCEM, NC National Guard Cyber Security Response Force, Local Government IT Strike Team) is reached via the NCEM 24-hour Watch Center, NCEOC@ncdps.gov or 1-800-858-0368.
  access: portal (statute HTML, web form) | auth: none | licence: public law / state publications | cadence: statutes current; pages maintained | NC: All NC state agencies and local government entities (counties, municipalities, school units, community colleges)
  use (pre,during,post): Write the incident-response runbook into docs/ops: a compromised NC Recon instance at a county is a reportable incident within 24 hours; include the form URL and phone numbers; design for rebuild-from-image-plus-backup so that 'never pay' is operationally possible; mark incident reports and forensic logs as confidential (132-6.1(c)); keep local 5-year retention of finalized incident reports (see records schedule).
  caveats: The 24-hour window appears on NCDIT's page, not in the fetched statute text for local governments.
- (high/high) **NC public records exemptions relevant to damage and infrastructure data: G.S. 132-1.7, 132-6.1(c), 132-10, 132-11** (NC General Assembly (text via ncleg.gov); NC Geographic Information Coordinating Council deck summarizing GIS provisions) https://ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_132/GS_132-1.7.html
  G.S. 132-1.7(a) excludes from public records 'specific details of public security plans and arrangements or the detailed plans and drawings of public buildings and infrastructure facilities', including those 'capable of being produced from ... geographic information system databases'; (b) excludes plans to prevent or respond to terrorist activity to the extent they set forth vulnerability and risk assessments, potential targets, or specific security or emergency procedures; (b1) responder mobile numbers; (c) general adoption of plans and budgets remain public; (a1)/(a2) repealed 2022. G.S. 132-6.1(c): agencies need not disclose security features of IT systems (passwords, configurations, security standards). G.S. 132-10: county/city GIS databases are public records; electronic copies may be conditioned on a written no-resale/non-commercial agreement. G.S. 132-11: confidentiality restrictions expire after 100 years except (among others) detailed plans and drawings of public buildings and infrastructure.
  access: portal (statute HTML; GICC slide PDF) | auth: none | licence: public law | cadence: static (statutes) | NC: All NC public agencies including counties and municipalities
  use (pre,during,post): Structure-level damage grades with addresses are not obviously covered by any exemption (they are neither security plans nor building drawings), so a county running NC Recon should expect public-records requests for the full dataset; the defensible posture is (1) publish the suppressed tract summary proactively, (2) document per-field withholding rationale (addresses of responders' homes? none; critical-facility vulnerability exposure analyses may fit 132-1.7(b)), (3) classify configuration, tokens and audit logs as confidential under 132-6.1(c), and (4) provide a redaction/export path. Build a 'public records export' profile that strips confidential fields.
  caveats: Not legal advice; the county attorney decides. Federal 'Sensitive Security Information' (49 CFR 1520) is a transportation-security designation and does not apply; NC's analog is G.S. 132-1.7. No case law or AG opinion on structure-level damage data was fetched (the GICC deck cites Whitehurst v. Alexander County, 2016 N.C. App. 329, not read).
- (high/high) **NC personal information and breach statutes: G.S. 132-1.10, 14-113.20(b), 75-61, 75-65** (NC General Assembly) https://ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_132/GS_132-1.10.html
  G.S. 132-1.10 makes 'identifying information' confidential and non-public and, in (c1), requires government agencies that suffer a security breach to comply with G.S. 75-65. 'Identifying information' (G.S. 14-113.20(b)) is SSN/EIN, driver's licence/state ID/passport numbers, financial account and card numbers, PINs, electronic identification numbers, e-mail names or addresses, internet account numbers and identification names, digital signatures, anything usable to access financial resources, biometrics, fingerprints, passwords, parent's maiden surname. Street addresses, phone numbers and property records are not listed. G.S. 75-61: 'security breach' is unauthorized access to and acquisition of unencrypted and unredacted records containing personal information (first name/initial + last name + identifying information); encrypted data plus the key also counts. G.S. 75-65: notice to affected persons without unreasonable delay, notice to the Attorney General's Consumer Protection Division, and to consumer reporting agencies when more than 1,000 persons are notified.
  access: portal (statute HTML) | auth: none | licence: public law | cadence: static (statutes) | NC: Statewide; applies to state and local agencies via 132-1.10(c1)
  use (pre,during,post): Address + damage grade is not statutory 'identifying information', so a leak of the structure layer alone does not trigger the 75-65 regime; what would trigger it is a leak of analyst/responder account data (e-mail addresses and passwords are identifying information). Therefore: use SSO so NC Recon stores no passwords; store only an opaque subject id plus display name; encrypt the data volume (encrypted-at-rest records are outside the breach definition unless the key leaks); still ship a breach playbook citing 75-65 and the 24-hour NCDIT report.

- (high/medium) **Small-count suppression norms: CDC WONDER (<10) and US Census Bureau disclosure avoidance** (CDC National Center for Health Statistics; US Census Bureau Disclosure Review Board) https://wonder.cdc.gov/wonder/help/ucd.html
  CDC WONDER suppresses sub-national counts of 0-9 (fewer than 10), suppresses populations under 10, and flags rates based on fewer than 20 events as 'unreliable'. The Census Bureau applies disclosure avoidance (suppression, coarsening, noise) under Title 13 with a Disclosure Review Board; a June 2026 Commerce Department order made coarsening the preferred method with suppression only when necessary. (Secondary sources report CMS suppresses cells under 11.)
  access: portal (documentation pages) | auth: none | licence: US Government work | cadence: static | NC: Norms, not NC data; NC has no EM-specific suppression rule found

Adopt the CDC norm (suppress 1-9, show 0 and >=10), add complementary suppression so area totals cannot back out a suppressed tract, flag displaced-person rates with denominators under 20 as unreliable, and document the rule in the public summary metadata. Keep structure-level and small-geography detail responder-only.
  caveats: CMS <11 threshold is from secondary sources (HHS page returned 403). No North Carolina statute sets a numeric k for GIS/EM releases.

- (high/medium) **NCID (North Carolina Identity Management) and the NCID Citizen Identity Project** (NCDIT (external-user platform operated by contractor Simeio Solutions LLC)) https://itservices.nc.gov/services/security-privacy/nc-identity-management-ncid
  The State's standard identity and access platform: required for state agencies, suggested for local governments, businesses and individuals. Published integration standards: SAML v2 (Application Integration Requirements to NCID template), HTTP proxy, shared LDAP and web services; the Citizen Identity Project separated external (citizen/business) from workforce identities and moved them to a Simeio-run cloud platform with production cutover September 17, 2023 and application migrations through August 2024. MFA is user ID + password plus a code sent to a validated email or phone (MFA training released April 30, 2024). Attributes releasable to a SAML SP include GUID ('unique and does not change'), User ID ('can change'), name, email ('not unique for all users'), Employee Type, and User Type S (state employee), L (local employee), B (business), I (individual). Integration is requested through an NCDIT Support Center ticket (919-754-6000) and documented in the AIRN template (architecture, test cases incl. account lock after three unsuccessful attempts and session timeout).

Microsoft's emergency-access guidance (doc dated 2026-06-04) prescribes two or more cloud-only break-glass accounts with phishing-resistant passkeys (FIDO2) or certificates, excluded from Conditional Access policies that block sign-in, credentials stored in separate fireproof safes, alerts on every sign-in, post-mortem review of each use, and validation at least every 90 days; it explicitly cites federation/MFA outages and 'natural disaster emergency, during which a mobile phone or other networks might be unavailable'.

- (high/high) **NIST SP 800-63B-4 Digital Identity Guidelines: Authentication and Authenticator Management (final, August 26, 2025)** (NIST) https://pages.nist.gov/800-63-4/sp800-63b.html
  Current federal authentication standard. AAL2 requires two distinct factors; verifiers SHALL offer at least one phishing-resistant option at AAL2; AAL2 reauthentication at most every 24 hours with inactivity timeout recommended not to exceed 1 hour; passwords at least 15 characters when used alone or 8 with MFA, checked against blocklists, no composition rules, no periodic rotation unless compromise; SMS/PSTN out-of-band are 'restricted' authenticators; syncable passkeys permitted at AAL2. Supersedes SP 800-63B (rev 3).

- (high/high) **Self-hosted OIDC relying-party and local identity-provider options: oauth2-proxy, Caddy forward_auth, Authelia, Keycloak** (oauth2-proxy (CNCF sandbox); Caddy (ZeroSSL/Matt Holt); Authelia project; Keycloak (CNCF incubation, Red Hat)) https://oauth2-proxy.github.io/oauth2-proxy/
  oauth2-proxy: reverse proxy/forward-auth middleware authenticating against OIDC providers (generic OIDC, Entra ID, Okta, Keycloak, Google, GitHub) and passing X-Forwarded-User/Email/Groups headers; Caddy forward_auth proxies a copy of each request to an auth gateway and, on 2xx, copies headers such as Remote-User, Remote-Groups, Remote-Email to the backend; Authelia: Apache-2.0 authentication portal and OpenID Certified OIDC provider with TOTP, WebAuthn/passkeys, Duo, file or LDAP user backends, container under 20 MB and under 30 MB RAM; Keycloak: Apache-2.0 IAM with OIDC, OAuth 2.0 and SAML 2.0, identity brokering to external OIDC/SAML IdPs, LDAP/AD federation, two-factor authentication.

Phase 2: bundle Authelia in the compose stack as the local IdP for offline/air-gapped kits and break-glass (file backend, passkeys, no internet needed) with Caddy forward_auth in front of responder routes. Keycloak only if SAML 2.0 to NCID is mandatory, since it can broker NCID SAML and Entra OIDC into one OIDC token for NC Recon.
  caveats: Keycloak adds a JVM and database to the single-container footprint; Authelia is the lighter fit for laptop kits.

GovRAMP statuses: Security Snapshot (40 NIST controls), Progressing Snapshot, Core Verification (60 controls, PMO-validated, quarterly monitoring), Ready Verification (80 controls, independent 3PAO, monthly monitoring), Authorized/Provisional (300+ controls, 3PAO, monthly). Pricing (2025-26): provider membership $1,000 (revenue under $1M), $1,750 ($1M-$5M), $2,500 (over $5M), renewing June 1; PMO fees by revenue tier: Snapshot $1,073/$1,610/$2,683, Progressing Snapshot $9,660/$12,880/$20,607, Core $10,733/$13,953/$22,539, Ready $3,757/$9,123/$16,904 plus 3PAO, Authorized/Provisional $4,830/$11,806/$20,929 plus 3PAO. 1,200+ member organizations, 70+ governments, 330+ products.

- (high/high) **Caddy TLS defaults, automatic HTTPS and header directive; NIST SP 800-52r2; HSTS preload; .gov domains** (Caddy project; NIST; Chromium HSTS preload list; CISA (get.gov)) https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/tls
  Caddy defaults: minimum TLS 1.2, maximum TLS 1.3, curated cipher suites (not customizable for 1.3), curves including x25519mlkem768 (post-quantum hybrid), automatic ACME certificates from Let's Encrypt/ZeroSSL with HTTP->HTTPS redirect, internal CA for localhost/private names, client_auth modes up to require_and_verify (mTLS), header directive with '-Server' to strip the Server header. NIST SP 800-52 Rev 2 (Aug 2019): government servers must support TLS 1.2 with FIPS-based cipher suites and TLS 1.3 by January 1, 2024 (under review, expected May 2026). HSTS preload requires max-age >= 31536000, includeSubDomains and preload with HTTPS on all subdomains; all newly registered .gov domains are preloaded automatically and .gov registration is free.
  access: open-source software / documentation | auth: none | licence: Apache-2.0 (Caddy); US Government work (NIST, get.gov) | cadence: Caddy releases ongoing | NC: NC OneMap/NCDIT standard requires state sites on nc.gov subdomains; counties may hold .gov domains
  use (pre,during,post): Keep Caddy; set Strict-Transport-Security max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains (preload only once all subdomains are HTTPS); host under the agency's .gov or nc.gov name so HSTS is preloaded; optional mTLS (require_and_verify) on the /admin path; use 'tls internal' with the Caddy root CA distributed to field devices for air-gapped kits.
  caveats: Rate limiting in Caddy requires the unofficial mholt/caddy-ratelimit module built with xcaddy; NC Recon already rate-limits in the app (30 writes / 600 reads / 120 feed requests per minute per IP).
- (medium/high) **OWASP Secure Headers Project best-practice values (machine-readable, updated 2026-10-04)** (OWASP) https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-headers/ci/headers_add.json
  Recommended response headers: Cache-Control 'no-store, max-age=0'; Clear-Site-Data '"cache","cookies","storage"' (logout); Content-Security-Policy "default-src 'self'; form-action 'self'; base-uri 'self'; object-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; upgrade-insecure-requests"; Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy require-corp; Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy same-origin; Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy same-origin; Permissions-Policy denying camera, geolocation, microphone, payment, usb etc. (sync-xhr=(self)); Referrer-Policy no-referrer; Strict-Transport-Security 'max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains'; X-Content-Type-Options nosniff; X-DNS-Prefetch-Control off; X-Frame-Options deny; X-Permitted-Cross-Domain-Policies none. Companion headers_remove.json lists Server, X-Powered-By and similar to strip.

Add Permissions-Policy (allow geolocation only for the field-label route), Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy same-origin, Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy same-origin, X-Permitted-Cross-Domain-Policies none, Clear-Site-Data on logout, and strip Server via Caddy. Test COEP require-corp carefully: cross-origin basemap tiles (CARTO) and federal feeds must send CORP/CORS headers or COEP will break the map.
- (high/high) **NIST SP 800-190 Application Container Security Guide (Sept 2017) with Docker seccomp, Compose hardening attributes and Compose secrets** (NIST; Docker Inc.) https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.800-190.pdf
  800-190 countermeasures: 4.1.1 container-specific vulnerability scanning with CVSS-threshold build gates; 4.1.2 images configured to run as non-privileged users and validated against configuration standards; 4.1.4 secrets stored outside images and injected at runtime, encrypted at rest and in transit with FIPS 140 validated modules; 4.1.5 only trusted images/registries; 4.4.3 automate runtime configuration compliance (CIS Docker Benchmark), use mandatory access control (SELinux/AppArmor), run with at least the runtime's default seccomp profile, never privileged mode, avoid mounting sensitive host directories; run containers 'with their root filesystems in read-only mode' with writes isolated to defined directories; use container-specific host OSs. Docker's default seccomp profile blocks about 44 of 300+ syscalls (default.json in moby/profiles) and is applied unless --privileged or seccomp=unconfined. Compose attributes: read_only, cap_drop/cap_add, security_opt (e.g., no-new-privileges, seccomp, apparmor), user, tmpfs, pids_limit, mem_limit/deploy.resources, healthcheck, restart, logging, secrets; Compose secrets are mounted as files at /run/secrets/<name> (Linux containers only), avoiding environment-variable leakage into logs.
  access: portal (PDF); Docker documentation | auth: none | licence: US Government work (NIST); Docker docs Apache-2.0 | cadence: 800-190 static; Docker docs continuous | NC: Not geographic
  use (pre,during,post): Harden docker-compose.yml: user: nonroot UID, read_only: true with tmpfs for /tmp and only the ncrecon-data volume writable, cap_drop: [ALL], security_opt: [no-new-privileges:true] plus default seccomp (or a tighter custom profile), pids_limit and memory limits, healthcheck; move TERRATRIAGE_TOKEN and OIDC client secret from .env into Compose secrets read from /run/secrets; add docker-bench-security (CIS Docker Benchmark) to CI; document host hardening (automatic updates, firewall deny-inbound except 80/443, disk encryption).

  Distroless images contain only the application and runtime dependencies (no shell or package manager); current images are Debian 13 (trixie) with explicit -debian13 tags; language images include python3, nodejs, java, cc, base, static; :nonroot tags run unprivileged (default tags do not); all images are cosign-signed keyless and verified with 'cosign verify $IMAGE --certificate-oidc-issuer https://accounts.google.com --certificate-identity keyless@distroless.iam.gserviceaccount.com'; :debug tags add busybox. Sigstore cosign signs container images, blobs and attestations; keyless mode binds an ephemeral key to an OIDC identity via Fulcio with Rekor transparency log (GitHub, Google, Microsoft identities supported). SLSA v1.0 Build track: L1 provenance exists, L2 hosted build platform with signed provenance, L3 hardened builds isolating runs and signing keys.

- (high/high) **Dependency and image scanning toolchain: pip-audit, npm audit, Trivy, OpenSSF Scorecard, OpenSSF Best Practices badge** (PyPA/Trail of Bits; npm (GitHub); Aqua Security; OpenSSF) https://pypi.org/project/pip-audit/
  pip-audit scans environments, requirements files and pyproject/pylock files against the PyPI Advisory Database and OSV, outputs text/JSON/Markdown/CycloneDX, has an official GitHub Action, needs Python >= 3.10. npm audit reports vulnerabilities with --audit-level thresholds and --omit=dev; 'npm audit signatures' verifies registry ECDSA signatures and provenance attestations. Trivy (Apache-2.0) scans images, filesystems, repos and SBOMs for CVEs, misconfigurations, secrets and licenses, generates CycloneDX JSON and SPDX/spdx-json, has a GitHub Action. OpenSSF Scorecard (Apache-2.0) scores repos 0-10 on branch protection, pinned dependencies, signed releases, SAST, token permissions, security policy etc. OpenSSF Best Practices 'passing' badge requires a published vulnerability reporting process, initial response within 14 days, no publicly known medium+ vulnerabilities unpatched beyond 60 days, HTTPS for project sites, and NIST-keylength-compliant crypto.

Add: trivy image scan of the built image with a fail gate on critical/KEV, 'npm audit signatures', Scorecard GitHub Action with the badge in README, and complete the OpenSSF Best Practices passing questionnaire. These are the realistic attestations a small open-source tool can offer in place of SOC 2.
- (high/high) **Vulnerability disclosure: RFC 9116 security.txt, CISA BOD 20-01 and VDP template, NIST SP 800-216** (IETF; CISA; NIST) https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9116.html
  RFC 9116 (April 2022): serve /.well-known/security.txt over HTTPS as text/plain UTF-8 with required Contact and Expires (recommend under one year) and optional Encryption, Acknowledgments, Policy, Preferred-Languages, Canonical, Hiring; optionally OpenPGP-signed. BOD 20-01 (federal) requires a published VDP stating scope, how to report, that good-faith research will not draw legal action, and remediation transparency; CISA's template commits to acknowledging reports within 3 business days and prohibits DoS, physical and social-engineering testing. NIST SP 800-216 (May 2023) gives federal guidance for receiving, triaging, coordinating and disclosing vulnerabilities.

- (high/high) **Backup and disaster-recovery references: NIST SP 800-34 Rev 1, restic, SOPS** (NIST; restic project; SOPS (CNCF sandbox)) https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/34/r1/upd1/final
  SP 800-34 Rev 1 (May 2010, upd. Nov 2010) defines Maximum Tolerable Downtime, Recovery Time Objective ('maximum amount of time that a system resource can remain unavailable before there is an unacceptable impact') and Recovery Point Objective ('point in time, prior to a disruption ... to which mission/business process data can be recovered'), a seven-step contingency planning process (policy, BIA, preventive controls, strategies, plan, testing/training/exercises, maintenance), and full vs incremental backup and offsite storage guidance. restic is a BSD-2 single-binary backup tool with client-side encryption, deduplication, verification ('restic check') and local/SFTP/S3/B2/Azure/GCS/rclone backends. SOPS (MPL-2.0, CNCF sandbox since 2023) encrypts values in YAML/JSON/ENV/INI files with age, PGP, AWS/GCP/Azure KMS or Vault.

  Service workers intercept fetches and serve from the Cache API (cache-first with network fallback); IndexedDB for structured client data; Background Sync defers requests until connectivity returns but is limited (app must be open, browser caps retries, worker stops after about 5 minutes); Periodic Background Sync and Background Fetch are browser-controlled; push requires HTTPS and user permission; all service-worker features require HTTPS (localhost exempt); storage may be evicted.

Section 3 requires attribution, licence notice and indication of modifications.
