- (high/high) **OpenStreetMap ODbL 1.0 and OSMF Community Guidelines (Produced Work, Horizontal Layers, Collective Database)** (OpenStreetMap Foundation; Open Data Commons) https://osmfoundation.org/wiki/Licence/Community_Guidelines
  ODbL 1.0: a Derivative Database (alteration or extraction of a substantial part into a new database) publicly used must be licensed under ODbL (s.4.4) and the database or diff made available (s.4.6); a Produced Work (image, PDF, map) needs a notice that it derives from an ODbL database (s.4.3); a Collective Database of independent databases is not derivative. OSMF guidelines: a Produced Work must not be designed for extraction of the data; Horizontal Layers: no share-alike when a feature type is entirely non-OSM, but mixing OSM and non-OSM data within the same feature type (e.g., merging building footprints) creates a Derivative Database; Collective Database: non-OSM attributes attached to OSM features by reference ID do not trigger share-alike when the attribute data is entirely non-OSM, but merging duplicate records across sources likely does. Attribution: '© OpenStreetMap contributors' with ODbL notice.
  access: portal (wiki, licence text) | auth: none | licence: ODbL 1.0 (data); CC BY-SA 2.0 (OSM documentation) | cadence: static (guidelines endorsed 2012-2014) | NC: Global incl. NC; NC Recon uses OSM footprints to gap-fill USA Structures
  use (post): NC Recon merges OSM and USA Structures footprints within one feature type (buildings), so the merged footprint database is likely an ODbL Derivative Database and must be offered under ODbL (GeoPackage/GeoJSON exports already carry footprint_source=osm). The damage attributes are NC Recon's own: keep them in a separate table keyed by footprint id (Collective Database) so the county's damage data is not forced under ODbL; briefs/PDF maps are Produced Works needing the OSM notice and a pointer to the ODbL footprint file. Alternatively, drop OSM footprints where USA Structures exists to avoid mixing.
  caveats: Guidelines are OSMF interpretations, not legal advice; the Collective Database guideline's 'regional cut' condition should be checked for statewide merges.
- (medium/medium) **FEMA/ORNL USA Structures feature service licence (CC BY 4.0)** (FEMA Geospatial Response Office with Oak Ridge National Laboratory (ArcGIS item published by Esri_US_Federal_Data)) https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/0ec8512ad21e4bb987d7e848d14e7e24?f=pjson
  ArcGIS Online item 0ec8512ad21e4bb987d7e848d14e7e24 'USA Structures' (created March 28, 2022; modified April 5, 2026) states 'This work is licensed under a Creative Commons by Attribution (CC BY 4.0)', credits ORNL and the FEMA Geospatial Response Office, describes footprints for structures over 450 sq ft nationwide, updated for 36+ states/territories as of June 6, 2025; feature service https://services2.arcgis.com/FiaPA4ga0iQKduv3/arcgis/rest/services/USA_Structures_View/FeatureServer. State downloads are on the FEMA GIS hub (disasters.geoplatform.gov redirects to gis-fema.hub.arcgis.com/pages/usa-structures-data).
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer; portal downloads by state | auth: none | licence: CC BY 4.0 (per item licenseInfo); other re-hosts show CC BY 4.0 or CC0 | cadence: periodic state updates (2022-2026) | NC: Full NC coverage (NC among updated states, 2025)
  use (pre,post): Correct LICENSES.md from 'US Government work' to 'CC BY 4.0, credit ORNL/FEMA'; CC BY is compatible with public redistribution of the footprint/damage exports; keep address fields responder-only as a policy choice (not a licence requirement).
  caveats: The FEMA hub pages are JavaScript-rendered and could not be read; licence text is from the Esri-hosted federal item.
- (medium/low) **NC OneMap orthoimagery (2020-2023 program; Hurricane Helene 2025) terms of use** (NC Center for Geographic Information and Analysis (NCDIT) / NC OneMap) https://www.nconemap.gov/pages/terms
  ArcGIS item 892b6918ea7446ea97ce5fe65f32d73a 'NC Orthoimagery for Hurricane Helene 2025' (owner nconemap; 6-inch natural-color imagery acquired spring 2025 over 13 western counties; ImageServer at https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/Imagery/Orthoimagery_Helene_2025/ImageServer) and about 95% of NC OneMap datasets in the DCAT feed carry a 'custom license' pointing to https://www.nconemap.gov/pages/terms, some with the clause 'The State of North Carolina shall not be held liable for any errors in this dataset'. The terms page itself is JavaScript-rendered and could not be read.
  access: ArcGIS ImageServer / DCAT catalogue | auth: none | licence: NC OneMap terms (text not retrieved); treated as public state data with disclaimer | cadence: program imagery every 4 years by region; Helene 2025 one-off | NC: Statewide orthoimagery program; Helene 2025 imagery for 13 western counties
  use (pre,post): Record the terms URL and the liability disclaimer in LICENSES.md and in export metadata; credit NC OneMap/CGIA/NC 911 Board; confirm with CGIA that redistributing imagery chips in responder exports and briefs is permitted before procurement.
  caveats: Licence text unknown; G.S. 132-10 allows no-resale conditions on electronic GIS copies from counties/cities.
- (medium/medium) **USACE National Structure Inventory (NSI) technical documentation and API** (US Army Corps of Engineers, Hydrologic Engineering Center) https://www.hec.usace.army.mil/confluence/nsi/technicalreferences/latest/technical-documentation
  NSI 2026 Base is the current version (2022 and 2019 historical); built from Lightbox (HIFLD) parcels, HIFLD facilities, Microsoft/USA Structures/ORNL footprints, ACS, LEHD and BLS; distributed as GeoPackage, GeoParquet, GeoJSON by state and via API https://nsi.sec.usace.army.mil/nsiapi/ (structures by bbox, FIPS or posted polygon; stats endpoint; GeoJSON); 'private fields' (e.g., parcel APN, NAICS) are restricted to federal users and mission partners via an /internal/ path because of licensed or sensitive source data; documentation recommends downloading inventories rather than repeated API calls; no explicit licence or PII statement found.
  access: REST API and portal downloads | auth: none for public fields; account for private fields | licence: unstated (US Government work assumed; source parcel data commercially licensed) | cadence: versions 2019, 2022, 2026 | NC: Statewide NC structures with occupancy, foundation and value attributes
  use (pre,post): Pre-stage NC county extracts (pre phase) so assessments do not depend on the API during events; keep NSI-derived per-structure attributes (value, occupants) in the responder tier only; add to LICENSES.md that terms are unstated and confirm redistribution with HEC; note the 2026 Base version when re-pulling.
  caveats: No terms-of-use page was retrievable; NC Recon currently cites 'NSI 2022'.
- (medium/medium) **xView2 baseline code licence (CMU, BSD-3) and xBD dataset provenance** (Carnegie Mellon University Software Engineering Institute (baseline); Defense Innovation Unit (xView2 challenge, xBD)) https://github.com/DIUx-xView/xView2_baseline
  The xView2 baseline repository is BSD-3-style, Copyright Carnegie Mellon University (LICENSE.md; permission@sei.cmu.edu), with SpaceNet-derived localization code under its own licence and trained weights in releases. The xBD paper (Gupta et al., arXiv:1911.09296) states imagery was sourced from the Maxar/DigitalGlobe Open Data Program (CC BY-NC) and defines the Joint Damage Scale; the paper text does not state the dataset licence, and xview2.org is JavaScript-rendered. The first-place solution repo is MIT.
  access: GitHub; arXiv | auth: none | licence: BSD-3 (baseline code); xBD dataset licence unverified (widely cited as CC BY-NC-SA 4.0); underlying imagery CC BY-NC 4.0 | cadence: static | NC: Not geographic; model used statewide
  use (post): Record the provenance chain xBD (Maxar NC imagery) -> CMU baseline weights -> NC Recon ONNX export (SHA-256 pinned) in MODEL_CARD.md and the SBOM; treat the weights as non-commercial-tainted for conservative compliance (already stated in LICENSES.md); if a commercial hosting partner appears, retrain on licence-clear data or obtain permission.
  caveats: xBD licence text not retrieved.
- (high/high) **NC General Records Schedule: Local Government Agencies (effective October 1, 2021)** (NC Department of Natural and Cultural Resources, Division of Archives and Records, Government Records Section) https://archives.ncdcr.gov/general-records-schedule-local-government-agencies/open
  Mandatory retention and disposition schedule for NC counties, municipalities and regional entities (adopted by the governing board). Relevant items: 5.09 Network and System Security Records - finalized cyber incident reports destroyed 5 years after resolution, records of unauthorized attempted entry/probes after 2 years, firewall/anti-virus records after 1 year, confidential under G.S. 132-6.1(c); 5.13 System Access Records (access requests, authorizations, system access logs) 1 year after superseded or obsolete; 5.14 System Documentation 3 years after superseded, confidential under 132-1.1(g)/132-6.1(c); 5.16 Records Backups per the office's established backup plan; 5.07 Electronic Records and Security Backup policies 3 years after superseded; 8.02 Declarations of states of emergency permanent (G.S. 166A-19.22); 8.03 Disaster and Emergency Management Plans (incl. COOP/BCP) destroy when superseded, confidential under 132-1.7; 3.01 GIS core data - parcel, boundary, zoning and orthoimagery layers permanent, other datasets per GICC standards. State agencies instead follow the General Schedule for State Agency Records, which SCIO-SEC-303 cites for a two-year audit-record retention.
  access: portal download (PDF, 127 pages) | auth: none | licence: NC state publication | cadence: issued Oct 1, 2021; program schedules updated periodically | NC: All NC local government agencies
  use (pre,during,post): Retention design: keep audit.log at least 2 years (state) and treat access logs as 1 year minimum/2 years for intrusion records (local); ground-truth label CSVs and assessment outputs become county GIS/program records whose retention the county must schedule (likely 'retain until reference value ends' or per GICC); implement audit-log export by date range, a configurable purge job that honors litigation/audit holds (no destruction while audits or litigation are pending), and permanent export of any imagery-derived orthos the county adopts.
  caveats: The State Agency General Schedule PDF was not fetched; its two-year IT-records retention is cited via SCIO-SEC-303 AU-11.
- (low/high) **CISA Protected Critical Infrastructure Information (PCII) Program** (CISA) https://www.cisa.gov/resources-tools/programs/protected-critical-infrastructure-information-pcii-program
  Under the Critical Infrastructure Information Act of 2002, critical infrastructure information voluntarily submitted to DHS/CISA by private or state/local entities is protected from FOIA, state and local disclosure laws and use in civil litigation; only trained, certified SLTT employees with need-to-know and signed NDAs may access PCII under a PCII Officer.
  access: request-only (submission to CISA PCII Program) | auth: MOU/accreditation (PCII Officer, training) | licence: US federal program | cadence: static | NC: Available to NCEM and NC counties as submitters/accessors
  use (pre,post): Note only: if NC Recon critical-facility exposure or vulnerability outputs are shared upward to CISA, PCII marking can shield them from state public-records requests; it does not protect copies the county itself holds. Add a 'PCII-eligible' flag to critical-facility exposure exports.
### insights
- NC's Statewide Information Security Manual policies (SCIO-SEC-301/303/306/307/316, v4, reviewed 03/26/2025) are mandatory for state agencies and their vendors under G.S. 143B-1376 and only 'encouraged' for local governments; G.S. 143B-1376 does not cover local governments at all (https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-identification-and-authentication/open, https://ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_143B/GS_143B-1376.html).
- NC's identity policy forbids shared credentials (IA-2a) and requires MFA for privileged accounts and for remote access to systems holding Restricted data with a separate-device factor; NC Recon's single shared bearer token cannot pass an NCEM/state-agency review as-is (https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-identification-and-authentication/open).
- Concrete NC session/lockout numbers: 3 failed logons in 120 minutes disables the account, lockout at least 30 minutes, 15-minute inactivity logout, sessions terminated after no more than 30 minutes idle, and a mandatory State logon banner (SCIO-SEC-301 AC-7, AC-2, AC-12, AC-8) (https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-access-control/open).
- NC audit records must be kept at least two years (SCIO-SEC-303 AU-11, citing the DNCR General Schedule for State Agency Records), stamped in UTC or with offset, synchronized to an authoritative time source, and backed up to a physically different system (https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-audit-accountability/open).
- Local-government retention differs: the 2021 NC General Records Schedule for Local Government Agencies keeps finalized cyber incident reports 5 years, intrusion/probe records 2 years, firewall/AV records 1 year, system access logs 1 year after superseded, backups per the office's backup plan, orthoimagery GIS layers permanently (https://archives.ncdcr.gov/general-records-schedule-local-government-agencies/open).
- NC's SC policy says full FIPS mode is not required: 'FIPS 140-2, or 140-3 where available, approved algorithms ... Enablement of full FIPS mode in an application or operating system is not required', TLS per NIST SP 800-52 Rev 2, Restricted data at rest encrypted (SC-28(1)), and SC-28 is optional for LOW-risk systems (https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/scio-system-and-communications-protection/open).
- NC renamed its data classification tiers in February 2026 to Public, Internal, Confidential, Restricted (formerly Low/Medium/High Risk); mixed systems default to the highest tier; IT 'security features' are confidential under G.S. 132-6.1(c) and sensitive public security information under G.S. 132-1.7 is non-public while 'general adoption' information is Public (https://it.nc.gov/documents/statewide-policies/statewide-data-classification-handling-policy/open).
- Under NC law a street address plus damage grade is not 'identifying information' (G.S. 14-113.20(b) lists SSN, licence/passport numbers, account numbers, PINs, e-mail names/addresses, passwords, biometrics etc.), so the breach-notification regime of G.S. 75-61/75-65 (triggered for agencies by G.S. 132-1.10(c1)) attaches to leaked user e-mails/passwords rather than to the structure layer; encrypted data without the key is outside the breach definition (https://ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_14/GS_14-113.20.html, https://ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_75/GS_75-61.html).
- G.S. 132-1.7 shields detailed building/infrastructure drawings 'capable of being produced from ... geographic information system databases' and vulnerability/risk assessments in terrorism response plans, but not ordinary structure-level damage observations; G.S. 132-10 makes county/city GIS databases public records while allowing a written no-resale condition on electronic copies; G.S. 132-11 sunsets most confidentiality after 100 years (https://ncleg.gov/EnactedLegislation/Statutes/HTML/BySection/Chapter_132/GS_132-1.7.html, https://it.nc.gov/gicc-overview-council-role-and-certain-applicable-laws/open).
- FEMA's 2025 PDA Guide states PDA data collection should not include PII and that a Privacy Threshold Analysis lets FEMA and SLTT governments share IDA/PDA data without a formal data sharing agreement, a useful precedent for classifying address-level damage records as sensitive-but-shareable rather than PII (https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_rd_pda-guide_07012025.pdf).
- Public health suppression norms are k=10: CDC WONDER suppresses sub-national counts of 0-9 and marks rates based on fewer than 20 events unreliable; NC Recon's current public threshold of 5 is below that norm (https://wonder.cdc.gov/wonder/help/ucd.html).
- Local governments must report cyber incidents to NCDIT (G.S. 143B-1379; NCDIT expects within 24 hours of confirmation) and may not pay or communicate with ransomware actors (G.S. 143-800); the Joint Cybersecurity Task Force is reached through the NCEM 24-hour Watch Center, NCEOC@ncdps.gov or 1-800-858-0368 (https://it.nc.gov/programs/cybersecurity-risk-management/cyber-incident-reporting, https://www.ncdps.gov/cyber-incident-reporting).
- NCID is required for state agencies and 'suggested' for local governments; documented integration is SAML v2, HTTP proxy, LDAP and web services (no OIDC documented), external identities moved to a Simeio-operated cloud platform with production cutover September 17, 2023, MFA is an email/phone code, and the SAML attribute set includes an immutable GUID and a User Type of S/L/B/I (https://it.nc.gov/support/ncid/ncid-citizen-identity-project, https://it.nc.gov/documents/files/ncid-application-integration-requirements-airn-saml-based/open).
- NC executive-branch contracts with a cloud component must align to GovRAMP (NIST 800-53 Rev 5) from April 1, 2026 with an on-ramp and without exception from April 1, 2027; GovRAMP provider membership costs $1,000-$2,500 per year and PMO fees run from about $1,073 (single Snapshot) to $22,539 (Core) before 3PAO costs (https://it.nc.gov/programs/cybersecurity-risk-management/esrmo-initiatives/govramp-adoption, https://govramp.org/pricing-overview/).
- The DOJ ADA Title II web rule requires WCAG 2.1 AA; an interim final rule published April 20, 2026 moved the deadlines to April 26, 2027 (populations of 50,000+) and April 26, 2028 (under 50,000 and special districts); NCDIT's own standard (v1.1, Jan 2025) still shows the old April 24, 2026 date (https://www.ada.gov/resources/2024-03-08-web-rule/, https://it.nc.gov/documents/digital-accessibility-usability-standard/open).
- NCDIT's Digital Accessibility and Usability Standard requires state digital services to use nc.gov subdomains, the 'An official website of the State of North Carolina' identifier, HTTPS with .gov preload, a phishing-resistant MFA option on public systems, no forced periodic password rotation, and a FIPPs/Privacy-by-Design privacy policy (https://it.nc.gov/documents/digital-accessibility-usability-standard/open).
- NIST SP 800-63B-4 (final August 26, 2025) requires at least one phishing-resistant option at AAL2, allows passkeys, caps AAL2 sessions at 24 hours with a recommended 1-hour inactivity timeout, sets 15-character minimum for single-factor passwords (8 with MFA), bans composition rules and periodic rotation, and restricts SMS/voice OTP; NC's SCIO-SEC-307 still mandates 14-character complexity and 180-day rotation without MFA, so NC Recon should document which regime it follows (https://pages.nist.gov/800-63-4/sp800-63b.html).
- Microsoft's emergency-access guidance (2026-06-04) is the reference pattern for EOC break-glass: two or more cloud-only accounts with FIDO2 passkeys, excluded from Conditional Access, credentials in separate fireproof safes, alert on every sign-in, post-mortem after use, validation at least every 90 days; it explicitly anticipates natural-disaster loss of mobile networks (https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/entra/identity/role-based-access-control/security-emergency-access).
- CISA CPG 2.0 (December 11, 2025) folded security.txt deployment into goal 2.C 'Maintain Vulnerability Disclosure/Reporting Process', keeps 3.E MFA, 3.G least privilege, 3.L strong encryption, 3.R log collection and storage, and tells organizations to back up critical operations near-real-time, store backups offsite and offline, and test recovery (https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/2025-12/CPG_Report_2.0_508c.pdf).
- CISA BOD 26-04 (June 10, 2026) replaced BOD 22-01/19-02 with risk-based timelines (three days plus forensic triage for the highest-risk KEVs); the KEV catalog (1,734 entries at fetch) is published as JSON/CSV with a schema that now includes a forensicTriage field (https://www.cisa.gov/known-exploited-vulnerabilities-catalog, https://www.cisa.gov/news-events/directives/bod-26-04-prioritizing-security-updates-based-risk).
- CISA Cyber Hygiene vulnerability and web-application scanning is free to SLTT governments, enrolled by emailing vulnerability@cisa.dhs.gov, starts within about three business days, and delivers weekly/monthly reports; it is the zero-cost substitute for a paid penetration test for county deployments (https://www.cisa.gov/cyber-hygiene-services).
- MS-ISAC became member-funded on October 1, 2025 (single-organization or statewide memberships, quotes via the CIS portal, 20% discount before December 31, 2025); it is no longer a free default for NC counties (https://www.cisecurity.org/ms-isac/ms-isac-membership-faq).
- NIST SP 800-190 explicitly recommends read-only root filesystems with writes isolated to defined directories, images that run as non-privileged users, at least the runtime's default seccomp profile, mandatory access control, secrets injected at runtime and encrypted with FIPS 140 modules, and automated compliance with the CIS Docker Benchmark; Docker's default seccomp profile blocks about 44 of 300+ syscalls (https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/SpecialPublications/NIST.SP.800-190.pdf, https://docs.docker.com/engine/security/seccomp/).
- Google distroless images are now Debian 13 based, offer a python3 image and :nonroot tags, and are cosign-signed keyless (verify with --certificate-identity keyless@distroless.iam.gserviceaccount.com) (https://github.com/GoogleContainerTools/distroless).
- CISA's 2025 SBOM minimum-elements draft adds Component Hash, License, Tool Name and Generation Context, requires at least one software identifier, replaces Depth with Coverage, and names SPDX and CycloneDX as the formats; pip-audit emits CycloneDX directly and Trivy emits CycloneDX JSON and SPDX (https://www.cisa.gov/sites/default/files/2025-08/2025_CISA_SBOM_Minimum_Elements.pdf, https://pypi.org/project/pip-audit/, https://trivy.dev/latest/docs/supply-chain/sbom/).
- Caddy's defaults already satisfy NIST SP 800-52r2 (minimum TLS 1.2, maximum 1.3, post-quantum x25519mlkem768 curve available), and every newly registered .gov domain is HSTS-preloaded at no cost (https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/tls, https://get.gov/domains/security/).
- OWASP's machine-readable best-practice headers (updated 2026-10-04) recommend HSTS max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains, CSP default-src 'self' with frame-ancestors 'none', COOP same-origin, CORP same-origin, COEP require-corp, Referrer-Policy no-referrer, X-Frame-Options deny, Cache-Control no-store (https://owasp.org/www-project-secure-headers/ci/headers_add.json).
- FEMA/ORNL USA Structures is licensed CC BY 4.0 according to the ArcGIS item metadata (modified April 5, 2026), not 'US Government work' as NC Recon's LICENSES.md currently states (https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/0ec8512ad21e4bb987d7e848d14e7e24?f=pjson).
- OSMF's Collective Database guideline lets non-OSM attributes be attached to OSM features by reference ID without share-alike when the attribute data is entirely non-OSM, but the Horizontal Layers guideline says mixing OSM and non-OSM data within one feature type (as NC Recon does with building footprints) creates an ODbL Derivative Database (https://osmfoundation.org/wiki/Licence/Community_Guidelines/Collective_Database_Guideline_Guideline, https://osmfoundation.org/wiki/Licence/Community_Guidelines/Horizontal_Map_Layers_-_Guideline).
- Creative Commons says NonCommercial 'turns on the use, not the identity of the reuser' and acknowledges gray areas; Maxar Open Data (CC BY-NC 4.0, s3://maxar-opendata in us-west-2, no AWS account needed) therefore needs a use-based justification and attribution, not merely 'we are a government' (https://wiki.creativecommons.org/wiki/NonCommercial_interpretation, https://registry.opendata.aws/maxar-open-data/).
- The public Overpass API expects at most about 10,000 requests and 1 GB per day per client and answers 429 when exceeded; statewide or multi-county NC Recon runs should use pre-staged extracts or a self-hosted instance (https://dev.overpass-api.de/overpass-doc/en/preface/commons.html).
- USACE NSI 2026 Base is the current version; public API fields are open but 'private fields' (APN, NAICS) are restricted to federal users and mission partners because of licensed source data, and no public licence statement exists (https://www.hec.usace.army.mil/confluence/nsi/technicalreferences/latest/technical-documentation).
- NC Recon's current posture per docs/ops/DEPLOYMENT.md: fail-closed shared bearer token, public tract summaries suppress counts under 5, per-IP rate limits (30 writes/600 reads/120 feeds per minute), CSP and security headers, audit.log with client address, tar.gz manual backups, pip-audit/npm audit with CycloneDX SBOM; the gaps against the sources above are per-user SSO/MFA, RBAC roles, 2-year protected log retention, encrypted automated offsite backups with tested RTO/RPO, signed distroless images with SBOM attestations, VDP/security.txt, k=10 suppression, WCAG 2.1 AA conformance report, and an NIST 800-53 Moderate/CSF 2.0 control statement.
- FedRAMP (including 20x, Phase 3 with a Jul-Sep 2026 submission pipeline) and SOC 2 apply to hosted services, not to software a county installs itself; the realistic attestations for a small open-source tool are the OpenSSF Best Practices badge (vulnerability process, 14-day response, 60-day patch), Scorecard, SLSA provenance, and CISA Cyber Hygiene reports (https://www.fedramp.gov/20x/, https://www.bestpractices.dev/en/criteria/0).
### gaps
- NCID: no fetched NCDIT document confirms OpenID Connect support on the Simeio platform, the onboarding cost or lead time for a local-government relying party, or whether county EM staff without state NCID accounts can be provisioned; only the SAML v2 AIRN template and the LDAP/HTTP-proxy/web-services methods are documented.
- NC OneMap terms of use (https://www.nconemap.gov/pages/terms) are JavaScript-rendered and could not be read; the licence for the 2025 Helene orthoimagery and the 2020-2023 program imagery is therefore unverified beyond 'custom license, see terms' and a liability disclaimer in the DCAT feed.
- xBD dataset licence text could not be retrieved (xview2.org is JavaScript-rendered; the arXiv paper does not state a licence); the commonly cited CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 remains unverified.
- USACE NSI: no terms-of-use, licence or PII statement was found in the technical documentation or API reference; redistribution rights for NSI-derived attributes are unknown.
- MS-ISAC membership fee amounts are not published (quote-only in the CIS portal).
- GovRAMP: the mapping of verification statuses to Low/Moderate/High impact levels, any explicit exemption for on-premises or open-source software, and whether NC's GovRAMP policy will extend to local governments were not found in fetched pages; the NC adoption page is silent on counties.
- FedRAMP Rev 5 baseline control counts (Low/Moderate/High) page could not be fetched (404); only the 800-53B baselines themselves were confirmed.
- Starlink/satellite connectivity use after Hurricane Helene in NC: no primary FEMA, NCDIT or Starlink page with deployment facts was fetched (web search budget exhausted); offline-operation guidance relies on MDN PWA documentation and Microsoft's disaster-outage rationale.
- CISA 2025 SBOM Minimum Elements: final publication status after the October 3, 2025 comment deadline is unknown; only the August 2025 draft was fetched.
- The NC Statewide Information Security Manual index page could not be located (several URL guesses returned 404); individual policies 301, 303, 306, 307, 316 and the Data Classification policy were fetched directly, but the Incident Response, Configuration Management, Risk Assessment, System and Information Integrity (vulnerability management) and Supply Chain policies were not.
- The NC General Schedule for State Agency Records (which SCIO-SEC-303 cites for two-year IT-record retention) was not fetched directly.
- G.S. 143B-1379 as fetched does not itself state a 24-hour reporting window for local governments; the 24-hour expectation comes from NCDIT's and NCDPS's reporting pages.
- No NC statute, AG opinion or case law was found that decides whether structure-level damage assessments with addresses are public records or exempt; the GICC deck cites Whitehurst v. Alexander County (2016) which was not read.
- No North Carolina-specific small-count suppression or k-anonymity rule for emergency-management or GIS releases was found; CDC WONDER (k=10) and Census disclosure-avoidance practice were used as norms, and the CMS cell-suppression policy page returned 403.
- OWASP ASVS 5.0 level definitions (L1/L2/L3) could not be retrieved (GitHub raw paths 404); only the 5.0.0 release (May 2025) was confirmed.
- The CISA ESS Cybersecurity Framework Implementation Guidance contains no controls specific to EOC software, 911/dispatch systems or damage-assessment data sharing (zero mentions of EOC, 911 or Starlink), so EM-specific control expectations had to be derived from NIST 800-53/CPG rather than sector guidance.
- FEMA PDA Guide 2025: no explicit handling marking (e.g., FOUO) for PDA datasets was found; only the no-PII statement and the PTA-based sharing posture.
- Whether onnxruntime, GDAL and PROJ wheels run on gcr.io/distroless/python3-debian13 was not tested; the distroless recommendation needs a build spike.

## 3D, lidar, elevation, buildings and flood models for building-level damage work in North Carolina
- (high/high) **NC Emergency Management Spatial Data Download (SDD): statewide QL1/QL2 lidar, DEMs, building footprints** (NC Department of Public Safety, Division of Emergency Management (NCEM) Risk Management / GIS) https://sdd.nc.gov
  NCEM's distribution site for all statewide lidar point clouds (LAS, LAZ or zLAS by class), DEM mosaics (3.125, 10 and 20 ft TIF by tile or county), building footprints (2010 RISK dataset and 2020-2023 cycle, by county), 2001-2005 legacy lidar, and links to FRIS flood hazard data. Lidar lineage: 2001-2005 legacy (~1 pt per 3-5 m2, bare earth only); 2014-2018 cycle in 5 phases (Phases 1-2 2014 east/coast QL2 2 ppsm linear sensor; Phase 3 2015 central QL2; Phases 4-5 2016-2018 west, Geiger-mode QL1 8 ppsm flown at 28,000 ft); 2020+ cycle (Phases 1-2 2020 QL1 8 ppsm topobathy with NOAA/USGS, bare-earth and bridge classes only; Phase 3 flown early 2024 QL1; Phases 4-5 flown 2025 QL1 8 ppsm) plus the post-Helene NC Collaboratory counties.
  access: Web application only (React SPA behind Azure AD B2C login): map clip-and-ship of up to 6 tiles (~2,500 x 2,500 ft) per request for lidar (All Classes, Bare Earth or chosen classes) or DEM (3/10/20 ft); county-wide DEM, footprint and flood hazard downloads without login; 'large data request' for more than 6 tiles (county pick-list or uploaded KML/zipped shapefile AOI) delivered to the requester's Azure or AWS bucket or a mailed USB drive. No REST API or bulk S3 found. | auth: none for county DEMs, footprints and flood hazard files; account required for tile-level lidar/DEM and large requests (NC state government email/NCID, Microsoft Live, Google or Apple ID) | licence: No formal licence text; NC OneMap item points to NC OneMap Terms (free, unrestricted use, no written release agreements, no warranty) | cadence: Multi-year acquisition cycles (2001-05, 2014-18, 2020-26); Helene counties added 2025; remaining WNC counties expected early 2026 | NC: Statewide. Post-Helene: QL1 lidar for the NC Collaboratory counties (Ashe, Avery, Burke, Buncombe, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, McDowell, Mitchell, Rutherford, Watauga, Yancey) is on SDD now with an NCEM-specific point classification; SMAC slide says 2024 QL1 and 2025 QL1 collection areas exist, Phase 3 lidar on SDD 'early January 2026', and lidar for the remaining western NC counties 'early 2026'.
  use (pre,post): Primary 'before' and 'after' lidar for E6 (2017 Phase 5 Geiger QL1 vs 2025 Collaboratory QL1 over the 12 Helene counties) and for E3 building heights (all-return classes). Because there is no API, plan a one-time large data request (statewide LAS/LAZ + 3 ft DEMs delivered to an NC Recon S3/Azure bucket) and keep county DEMs in offline packs. Normalise datums (NAD83(2011) NC State Plane ftUS, NAVD88 Geoid12A/12B ft vs ASPRS 1.4 vs NCEM Helene classes) in the ingest step.
  caveats: Site is JavaScript-only and fronted by Cloudflare, so cannot be scraped; six-tile/≈10 GB per request; point classification schemes differ across the 2014-17 cycle, 2020+ ASPRS 1.4 cycle and the Collaboratory counties; login tenant is named 'dpsemsddstage'; point density/QL of the 2025 Collaboratory lidar not published on a fetched page.
- (high/high) **NC OneMap statewide 3 ft DEM image services (DEM03 and derivatives) and county DEM Direct Data Downloads** (NC Center for Geographic Information and Analysis (CGIA) serving NCEM/NCFMP data) https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/Elevation/DEM03/ImageServer
  Statewide bare-earth DEM mosaic built from NCFMP county DEMs (2014-2018 lidar), 3.125 ft pixels, 32-bit float, NAD83(2011) NC State Plane ftUS, with derived ImageServers (hillshade, slope, aspect, shaded relief, 1/2/4/20/100 ft raster contours), a 20 ft legacy hillshade, WMS and WCS endpoints, and county DEM zips on NC OneMap Direct Data Downloads.
  access: ArcGIS ImageServer REST (exportImage, identify, Download, Mensuration capabilities), OGC WMS (https://services.gis.nc.gov/secure/services/Elevation/DEM03/ImageServer/WMSServer) and WCS; county DEM files via https://assets.nconemap.gov/pages/hub/ddd/index.html | auth: none | licence: NC OneMap Terms: free and unrestricted use, cite 'NC OneMap (2019)…', no warranty | cadence: Static; updated when NCEM publishes new county mosaics | NC: Statewide; source lidar 2014-2018 (not yet the 2020-2025 cycle per item text)
  use (pre,during,post): E5 flood depth = water surface elevation minus DEM03 at each footprint; E3 ground reference to turn DSM/point-cloud roof heights into building heights; slope/aspect context for landslide flags; county DEM zips for offline PWA packs; WCS for server-side raster pulls without ArcGIS tooling.
  caveats: Extent/DEM date varies by county (2014-2018); not a DSM; maxImageWidth 15000 px per request.
- (high/high) **NC Collaboratory post-Helene QL1 lidar and 6-inch orthoimagery 2025 (Helene-North Carolina Priority 1 Lidar and Imagery project)** (NC Collaboratory at UNC Chapel Hill (funded by NC General Assembly), vendor AECOM team; distributed by NC OneMap (imagery) and NCEM SDD (lidar)) https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/Imagery/Orthoimagery_Helene_2025/ImageServer
  Leaf-off aerial lidar and 6 in GSD 4-band (RGBIR) orthoimagery flown January to early March 2025 over 13 western NC counties (about 5,323 sq mi, 6,768 tiles of 5,000 x 5,000 ft), $4M redirected appropriation; imagery published as NC OneMap ImageServer + WMS; lidar for the Collaboratory counties is on SDD (login) with an NCEM-specific classification; hard drives were given to counties.
  access: Imagery: ArcGIS ImageServer and WMS (https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/services/Imagery/Orthoimagery_Helene_2025/ImageServer/WMSServer). Lidar: SDD tile download or large data request. | auth: none for imagery services; SDD account for lidar | licence: NC OneMap Terms; credit 'NC Collaboratory at UNC Chapel Hill' | cadence: One-off (spring 2025) | NC: Alexander, Ashe, Avery, Burke, Buncombe, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, McDowell, Mitchell, Rutherford, Watauga, Yancey (imagery); same minus Alexander for lidar per NCEM storymap
  use (post): The 'after' surface for E6 lidar differencing against 2017 Phase 5 Geiger lidar / 3DEP 1 m DEM (collapsed roofs, debris deposits, scour, washed-out structures); DSM-derived roof heights for E3; already the 'after' imagery channel. Build a one-time DoD product per county and serve it as a raster layer and per-structure statistic.
  caveats: Lidar point density, QL and accuracy report not found on a fetched page; the ImageServer pixel values were adjusted for visualisation (not for pixel analysis); classification differs from ASPRS 1.4.
- (high/high) **USGS 3D Elevation Program (3DEP): 1 m DEMs, lidar point clouds and OPR DEMs over North Carolina via TNM Access API** (U.S. Geological Survey National Geospatial Program) https://tnmaccess.nationalmap.gov/api/v1/products
  Nationally consistent 1 m DEM Cloud Optimized GeoTIFFs, LAZ point-cloud tiles and original-resolution DEMs derived from the NCEM lidar. Over western NC the catalog returns NC_Phase5_2018_A18 (1 m DEM published 2022-05; LPC 2021), NC_Phase4_2017_A17 (1 m DEM published 2024-05), NC_Phase_4_CentralWestNC_GEIGER_A16 (collected 2016-02-27 to 2017-10-30, 1 m DEM published 2024-06), legacy NC_PHASE3_2005 LAZ, and along the SC line SC_Northern_D24 (QL1, collected 2024-12-05 to 2025-02-19, 0.5 m DEM, 1 m DEM published 2026-04). All products 'free of charge and without use restrictions'.
  access: REST API (GET /products?datasets=Digital Elevation Model (DEM) 1 meter|Lidar Point Cloud (LPC)|Original Product Resolution (OPR) Digital Elevation Model (DEM)&bbox=…), direct HTTPS from prd-tnm.s3.amazonaws.com/StagedProducts/Elevation/… and rockyweb.usgs.gov, The National Map downloader; 3DEP index MapServer exists (https://index.nationalmap.gov/arcgis/rest/services/3DEPElevationIndex/MapServer) but its query layers returned 'Failed to execute query' in tests. | auth: none | licence: US Government public domain | cadence: Per project; NC 1 m DEM refreshes published 2022 and 2024 | NC: Statewide 1 m DEM and LPC from the 2014-2018 NCEM cycle; no USGS post-Helene lidar over NC found (USGS Helene task order to Bowman/Merrick covers 8,800 sq mi in GA, TN, AL and SC; SC_Northern_D24 touches the NC border only)
  use (pre,post): Pre-event 1 m DEM baseline and LPC for E6 'before' surface where SDD bulk data are not yet ingested; E3 heights from LPC; national-standard fallback for counties outside NC OneMap mosaics; COGs are range-request friendly for on-demand clipping.
  caveats: Datum/projection differ from NC state products (UTM m vs State Plane ft); TNM API max page size ~500; 3DEP index queries failed.
- (high/high) **USGS 3DEP lidar as Entwine Point Tiles on AWS (usgs-lidar-public) with STAC catalog** (Hobu Inc. with USACE and AWS Open Data (USGS data)) https://registry.opendata.aws/usgs-lidar/
  Streamable lossless EPT (LAZ octree) copies of 3DEP projects in s3://usgs-lidar-public (us-west-2) plus requester-pays raw LAZ in s3://usgs-lidar; STAC catalog https://usgs-lidar-stac.s3-us-west-2.amazonaws.com/ept/catalog.json lists 2,280 items. NC items include NC_Phase5_<county>_2017 for 22 western counties (Ashe, Avery, Buncombe, Burke, Caldwell, Cherokee, Clay, Graham, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, Macon, Madison, McDowell, Mitchell, Polk, Rutherford, Swain, Transylvania, Watauga, Yancey), USGS_LPC_NC_Phase4_<county>_2016/2017, 2014 Phase 1/2 county sets, NC_HurricaneFlorence_1-11_2020 and NC_HurFlo_NOAA_12-15_2020, NC_SpartaEQ_1_2020.
  access: S3 public bucket (aws s3 ls --no-sign-request s3://usgs-lidar-public/), EPT readers (PDAL readers.ept, Potree, CesiumJS), STAC JSON | auth: none (requester-pays only for the raw LAZ bucket) | licence: US Government public domain | cadence: Periodic | NC: Statewide for 2014-2017 collections; no 2024-2025 NC resources in the catalog as of 2026-10-06
  use (pre,post): On-demand point-cloud reads for E3 (roof height, roof shape, nDSM per footprint) and the E6 'before' surface without bulk downloads; power a 3D inspection view (Potree/Cesium) in the review queue; PDAL pipelines can filter by class and bbox server-side.
  caveats: EPT bucket omits resources with incomplete CRS metadata (Buncombe STAC item has proj:epsg null); cross-region egress latency from us-west-2; STAC item pc:count values are very large (Buncombe lists 91,211,176,328) and should be verified before capacity planning.
- (high/medium) **NOAA Digital Coast Data Access Viewer and noaa-nos-coastal-lidar-pds S3 bucket (NC lidar redistribution with custom processing)** (NOAA Office for Coastal Management) https://coast.noaa.gov/dataviewer/
  NOAA redistributes the NCEM/NCFMP lidar (e.g. '2014 NCFMP Lidar: Statewide North Carolina (Phase 1)', ID 4954: 9,396 sq mi, 0.7 m NPS, 11.7 cm vertical accuracy at 95%, LAS 1.3 classes 1-18/25, 5,000 ft tiles) and USGS DEMs such as '2024-2025 USGS Lidar DEM: Northern SC' (QL1, 0.5 m, NVA 4.99 cm RMSE). The DAV builds custom products (reprojection, datum and unit changes, class filters, DEM/contour generation, format choice); bulk data sit in s3://noaa-nos-coastal-lidar-pds as EPT, LAZ/COPC and GeoTIFF with STAC endpoints. NC OneMap states the DAV disseminates the same NCEM lidar as SDD.
  access: Web DAV job (results emailed), S3 bucket (public), STAC | auth: none (email address for DAV jobs) | licence: Open; NOAA requests attribution and prohibits implying endorsement or passing modified data as original | cadence: Periodic as datasets are added | NC: NC 2014-2018 lidar and DEMs; 2020 coastal topobathy; coverage of the 2024-2025 NC collections not confirmed
  use (pre,post): Scriptable, login-free path to NC lidar normalised to NAVD88 metres/UTM (COPC) for the E3/E6 pipelines; alternative to SDD for bulk pulls; InPort metadata gives accuracy figures for uncertainty thresholds in DoD.
  caveats: DAV is JavaScript-only (not fetched); bucket index must be used to find dataset IDs; NC 2024-25 QL1 availability unknown.
- (high/medium) **NC Flood Risk Information System (FRIS): effective/preliminary flood data, hydraulic models, county lidar downloads and building-level risk** (NCEM / NC Floodplain Mapping Program (NCFMP)) https://fris.nc.gov/fris/
  Statewide system (all 100 counties) with effective and preliminary DFIRM data, FIS reports, index maps, per-stream hydraulic/hydrologic models (e.g. Buncombe lists Limited Detailed and Detailed HEC-RAS studies and LOMR models as zips with H&H reports), panel downloads (DFIRM PDF, shapefile, file geodatabase, LiDAR data types), public download directories (County_LIDAR_Data/<County>BE|D2|D5, LegacyLidar, Redelineations), discharge and floodway reports, and building-level risk: a 'Building Footprints with Risk' layer (Buncombe is 'one of the few counties' with it), a per-building table of depth above finished floor and damage % for the 10/4/2/1/0.2 % annual-chance events, Hazus occupancy types and mitigation cost calculator. FRIS also links 'Spatial Data Download: Lidar, DEMs and Building Footprints'.
  access: SPA plus undocumented JSON API: GET /api/flood/counties, /api/flood/CountyModels/{fips}/{isEffective}, /api/flood/CountyCoastalModels/{fips}/{eff}, /api/flood/CountyReports/{fips}, /api/flood/CountyPanels/{fips}, /api/flood/StreamsByCounty/{fips}/{eff}, /api/flood/PanelDownloadUrl/{fips}/{panel}/{eff}; POST /api/flood/EngineeringModels, /api/flood/GetFilesForDownload; Risk API GET /api/Risk/HazardProbability/{buildingId}/, /api/Risk/OccupancyTypes/, POST /api/Risk/RiskProjection, /api/Risk/MitigationData. Static files under https://fris.nc.gov/fris_hardfiles/nc/hardfiles/{DFIRM,EFIS,PFIS,EINDEX,PINDEX}/{fips}/ and https://fris.nc.gov/downloads/public/. Map services on https://spartagis.ncem.org/arcgis/rest/services/Public/ (FRIS_FloodZones, FRIS_FloodZones_Prelim, FRIS_Panels, FRIS_HydraModel). | auth: none observed | licence: State of NC data; nc.gov disclaimer/terms of use; no explicit open licence | cadence: Updated as studies become effective/preliminary; public download directories dated 2018-2021 (County_LIDAR_Data index 2025-06-10) | NC: Statewide; building risk layer only in some counties (Buncombe confirmed)
  use (pre,post): Pre-event exposure modelling: per-building first-floor-relative depth-damage for standard return periods, BFE/flood zone per footprint; download effective HEC-RAS models to generate scenario or historical WSE for E5; pull county bare-earth lidar/DEM from the public directory without SDD login; FIS/panel references for exports and briefs.
  caveats: API is undocumented and may change; model download base URLs come from a runtime config not retrievable directly; depth grids as rasters were not confirmed; FFE source (lidar vs survey) per building not verified.
- (high/medium) **NCEM building footprints with Hazus-style risk attributes (2010 RISK dataset, 2020-2023 regional sets, statewide Composite)** (NC Emergency Management GIS (NCDPS) with NC DOA Governor's Youth Involvement Program) https://services1.arcgis.com/YBWrN5qiESVpqi92/arcgis/rest/services/North_Carolina_Composite_Building_Footprints/FeatureServer
  Statewide 'North Carolina Composite Building Footprints' (5,435,529 polygons, BLDG_ID only, rooflines >800 sq ft digitised from the 2020-2023 statewide orthos: 2024 coastal 27 counties, 2021 eastern Piedmont 26, 2022 northern mountains/western Piedmont 26, 2023 southern mountains/western Piedmont 21; data edited 2026-09). Regional services '2020', '2021', '2022' (e.g. Building Footprints - Northwest Mountains 2022: 1,430,220 polygons) carry the NCEM RISK schema: OCCUP_TYPE, BUILD_TYPE, FLD_ZONE, STATIC_BFE, YEAR_BUILT, BLDG_VALUE, BLDGREPVAL, CONTREPVAL, INVREPVAL, HTD_SQ_FT, FFE, FFE_TYP, LIDAR_LAG, LIDAR_HAG, FOUND_TYPE, BASMENT_TY, NUM_STORY, RISE, ROOF_SHAPE, ROOF_SLOPE, roof cover/deck/connection fields, SHUTTERS, NUM_UNITS, ISCOASTAL, ISLIMWA. Also Risk_Buildings and All_Buildings_wm vector tiles. The 2022 Business Plan documents the 2009-2012 origin and the plan to maintain footprints from the quarterly ortho cycle with lidar-derived height attributes.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer (query JSON, maxRecordCount 2000), VectorTileServer; by-county downloads on sdd.nc.gov | auth: none | licence: Not stated on items (NCEM-owned; NC OneMap Terms by reference) | cadence: Regional refresh with the 4-year statewide ortho cycle; composite republished 2026 | NC: Statewide composite (2020-2024 imagery vintage by region); 2022 set covers the northern mountains incl. Helene counties
  use (pre,post): Authoritative NC footprint source to replace/augment FEMA USA Structures and OSM outlines; FFE, LIDAR_LAG/HAG and FOUND_TYPE feed E5 depth-above-first-floor; NUM_STORY/ROOF_SHAPE feed E3; BLDGREPVAL/CONTREPVAL for loss estimates; BLDG_ID as a join key for NCEM damage-assessment interoperability.
  caveats: Whether FFE/LAG/HAG and replacement values are populated in the 2020-2023 sets was not verified (schema inherited from the 2010 RISK dataset); outbuildings <800 sq ft omitted; licence not stated.
- (medium/high) **FEMA National Flood Hazard Layer (NFHL) ArcGIS services and MSC downloads** (FEMA) https://hazards.fema.gov/arcgis/rest/services/public/NFHL/MapServer
  Effective regulatory flood hazard database: Flood Hazard Zones (layer 28; fields FLD_ZONE, ZONE_SUBTY, SFHA_TF, STATIC_BFE, DEPTH, VELOCITY), BFEs, cross sections, LOMRs/LOMAs, FIRM panels, high water marks, levees, coastal transects, LiMWA; JSON/geoJSON/PBF queries, maxRecordCount 2000; state/county downloads via https://msc.fema.gov/portal/advanceSearch.
  access: ArcGIS REST MapServer, WMS (per FEMA GIS services guide), MSC portal downloads | auth: none | licence: US Government public domain (not stated on page) | cadence: Continuous | NC: Statewide (NC effective data originate from NCFMP)
  use (pre,post): SFHA/BFE per footprint as an E5 prior and for the brief; cross-check against FRIS (which also carries preliminary data); FIRM panel IDs in exports.
  caveats: Depth grids and non-regulatory Flood Risk Database products are separate MSC downloads where they exist.
- (high/high) **USA Structures (ORNL/FEMA) with HEIGHT and adjacent-grade elevations** (Oak Ridge National Laboratory and FEMA Response Geospatial Office (Esri-hosted view)) https://services2.arcgis.com/FiaPA4ga0iQKduv3/arcgis/rest/services/USA_Structures_View/FeatureServer/0
  Footprints >450 sq ft with fields BUILD_ID, OCC_CLS, PRIM_OCC, SEC_OCC, HEIGHT, SQFEET, H_ADJ_ELEV, L_ADJ_ELEV, SOURCE, IMAGE_DATE, VAL_METHOD, USNG, POP_MEDIAN; Esri item states data currency 2025-06-06 with North Carolina updated in that cycle and CC BY 4.0. A 2026 PE&RS study (Liu et al.) found USA Structures heights agree with 3DEP lidar at R² 0.86-0.87, MAE 1.8-2.2 m, versus Microsoft GBF heights at R² 0.54-0.60.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer; FEMA USA Structures state GDB download site | auth: none | licence: CC BY 4.0 (Esri item); FEMA data | cadence: Periodic state refresh cycles | NC: Statewide, updated June 2025
  use (pre,post): Already a structure source; additionally use HEIGHT as the E3 height prior/shadow model and L_ADJ_ELEV (lowest adjacent grade) as the E5 ground reference for depth-above-grade when no FFE exists.
  caveats: HEIGHT derivation method per record (VAL_METHOD) should be checked; occupancy from parcels is incomplete.
- (medium/high) **Microsoft Building Footprints (US set and GlobalMLBuildingFootprints with heights)** (Microsoft Maps / Bing) https://github.com/microsoft/USBuildingFootprints
  US set: 129.6M ML-derived footprints, North Carolina file 4,678,064 buildings (1.22 GiB GeoJSON), ODbL, ~73M from 2019-2020 imagery and the rest ~2012 vintage, precision 98.5 %/recall 92.4 %. GlobalMLBuildingFootprints: ~1.4B footprints 2014-2024 in line-delimited GeoJSON (.csv.gz) by quadkey, CDLA-Permissive 2.0, height estimates on ~174M structures (-1 = missing), latest refresh August 2026, also on Planetary Computer.
  access: GitHub-linked Azure blob downloads, dataset-links.csv, Planetary Computer | auth: none | licence: ODbL (US set); CDLA Permissive 2.0 (Global set) | cadence: Global set refreshed frequently (Aug 2026); US set static | NC: Statewide
  use (pre,post): Cross-check and gap-fill of footprints in rural WNC; imagery-based heights are weak (R² 0.54-0.60 vs lidar) so use only as a last-resort E3 prior.
  caveats: ODbL share-alike on the US set if merged with other data; no capture-date per building in the US set beyond the two vintages.
- (low/high) **Google Open Buildings (v3 and temporal/2.5D)** (Google Research) https://sites.research.google/gr/open-buildings/
  Footprints with confidence and Plus Codes for Africa, South/South-East Asia, Latin America and the Caribbean; CC BY 4.0 or ODbL; no height in v3. Does not cover North America.
  access: CSV by S2 cell, Earth Engine asset, gsutil | auth: none | licence: CC BY 4.0 or ODbL | cadence: v3 May 2023 | NC: None

- (medium/medium) **Overture Maps buildings theme (GeoParquet with height, floors, roof attributes, GERS IDs)** (Overture Maps Foundation (Linux Foundation)) https://docs.overturemaps.org/guides/buildings/
  Global conflated buildings (OSM highest priority, Esri Community Maps, authoritative sets, Microsoft and Google ML roofprints) released monthly as GeoParquet (s3://overturemaps-us-west-2/release/2026-09-23.1/theme=buildings/ and Azure mirror) under ODbL; schema includes height, min_height, roof_height, num_floors, roof_shape, roof_orientation, facade/roof material, sources[] with per-record licence, and GERS ids. Search results indicate Overture published 3DEP-lidar-derived heights for >6M US buildings (whitepaper too large to fetch).
  access: S3/Azure GeoParquet, overturemaps CLI, DuckDB spatial queries | auth: none | licence: ODbL | cadence: Monthly | NC: Statewide (quality lower where ML-derived)

- (medium/high) **Cesium ion platform (Cesium World Terrain, Cesium OSM Buildings 3D Tiles, 3D tiling and streaming)** (Cesium GS Inc. (Bentley)) https://cesium.com/platform/cesium-ion/pricing/
  Hosted 3D Tiles: global terrain, Cesium OSM Buildings (>350M OSM buildings, heights from tags or levels x 3 m, quarterly updates, attribution '© OpenStreetMap contributors'), tiling of user point clouds/meshes, and Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles pass-through. Pricing: Community $0 (individuals, 10 GB storage, 15 GB/month streaming, 1,000 Google 3D root tiles/month) but 'Government projects' require a paid plan; Commercial $149/month individual or $524 team; Premium $499/$874; Self-Hosted enterprise on request.

  use (pre,during,post): Optional 3D situational view (terrain + OSM buildings + NC Recon damage colours) in the EOC UI; tiling of NC lidar/EPT to 3D Tiles. Flag: not free for government deployments; prefer self-hosted 3D Tiles from NC lidar (open tools) if cost must be zero.

- (low/high) **Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles (Map Tiles API)** (Google Maps Platform) https://developers.google.com/maps/documentation/tile/policies
  Photorealistic mesh 3D Tiles for visualisation. Pricing (Enterprise SKU C6E1-98B2-DBD0): 1,000 root tileset requests free per month, then $6.00 per 1,000 (1-100k), $5.10 (100k-500k), $4.20, $3.30, $2.40 at higher volumes; 2D Map Tiles $0.60 per 1,000 after 100,000 free. Policies prohibit pre-fetching/caching/storing beyond limited conditions, offline use, image analysis, object detection or geodata extraction, and require aggregated on-screen attribution; third-party 3D objects may be overlaid but not derived from the tiles. Credits exist for crisis response and nonprofit organisations (eligibility not fetched).

The NCEM lidar storymap also cites a City of Raleigh 'Mirror City' 3D model built from NCEM lidar, but no public endpoint was found.

  use (pre): Demonstrator for a 3D damage view in an urban county; not relevant to WNC.
  caveats: Licence and attributes not published; open mapping data catalogue pages are JavaScript-only.
- (high/high) **USGS Preliminary Landslide Inventory for Hurricane Helene (point inventory, FeatureServer, dashboard)** (USGS Landslide Hazards Program, LASER project (with NCGS, TN/VA surveys, NCDOT)) https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/item/674634a1d34e6d1dac3abddc
  Reviewed point inventory of landslides triggered 25-27 Sep 2024 (2,217 features in the live FeatureServer; 1,155 at the October 2024 briefings), attributes Source and Impact (river, road, building, other, various, none, unclear), mapped from Sentinel-2 NDVI/RGB, Planet, aerial and oblique imagery; CC0 1.0; published 2025-01-27 (DOI 10.5066/P14CHGKS). Live services: https://services.arcgis.com/v01gqwM5QqNysAAi/arcgis/rest/services/USGS_Hurricane_Helene_Landslide_Inventory/FeatureServer/0 and Hurricane_Helene_Landslide_Locations/FeatureServer/1; dashboard https://www.arcgis.com/apps/dashboards/01b4f51fc0b64002bf7722a9acfc181d; oblique photo releases for 3-5 and 13-17 Oct 2024.
  access: ScienceBase download (zipped shapefile, GeoJSON, CSV), ArcGIS FeatureServer (JSON, maxRecordCount 2000), dashboard | auth: none | licence: CC0 1.0 (public domain) | cadence: Event-driven; reviewed version Jan 2025, no further revisions noted | NC: Western NC plus TN, VA, GA, SC
  use (post): Ground-truth and feature for a landslide/debris-flow damage mechanism: flag structures within a buffer of a point with Impact=building; prioritise E6 differencing tiles; event-response template (USGS LASER inventories are produced within days for major events).
  caveats: Points, not polygons; positional accuracy not systematically assessed; under-represents small or canopy-hidden slides.
- (high/high) **NC Geological Survey Post-Helene Landslide Inventory (polygons) and Landslide Inventory of North Carolina** (NC Geological Survey (NC DEQ DEMLR) with UNC Asheville NEMAC) https://services2.arcgis.com/kCu40SDxsCGcuUWO/arcgis/rest/services/North_Carolina_Post_Helene_Landslide_Inventory/FeatureServer
  NCGS polygon inventory of Helene slope movements: layer 2 'Landslides' 2,607 polygons (fields Existence_Confidence, Location_Confidence, Material_Type, Movement_Type, Pre_Failure_Slope, Associated_Storm, Impact/Impact2/Impact3, Damage, Fatalities, Watch, Site_Visit), layer 1 Ground Ruptures (20 lines), layer 0 Observations (603 points); data last edited May 2026. Statewide 'Landslide Inventory of North Carolina' (NC_Landslide_Inventory: 7,070 observations plus ground ruptures and landslide features) viewed at https://experience.arcgis.com/experience/cd122ec3a6414d8e80ca93f5efe6bcb5 and https://landslidesncgs.com. USGS reports NCGS has mapped >2,800 Helene landslide outlines with higher-resolution imagery, lidar and field visits; DEQ reports >4,500 landslides in the published inventory and >400 Helene responses (Nov 2025); WUNC reports 496 Helene responses vs 358 in 1990-2023 and $3M in the 2025 recovery bill for mapping 15 unmapped counties and updating 9.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer (JSON, maxRecordCount 1000), Experience Builder viewer with download | auth: none | licence: NC DEQ disclaimer: free access, no written release agreements required, attribution expected, no warranty | cadence: Continuously edited (last data edit 2026-05) | NC: Western NC; Helene counties
  use (pre,post): Best available polygon ground truth for E6 and for a 'destroyed by landslide/debris flow' label; Damage and Impact fields for validation of structure-level calls; pre-event inventory as a susceptibility feature; map layer in the Conditions view.
  caveats: Service descriptions are empty; polygon count differs from the '>2,800 outlines' quoted by USGS; some records are low existence/location confidence.
- (medium/medium) **NCGS county landslide hazard maps (Slope Movement Outlines, Deposit Polygons, Landslide Locations, Stability Index) hosted by UNCA NEMAC** (NC Geological Survey; hosted by UNC Asheville NEMAC) https://services1.arcgis.com/PwLrOgCfU0cYShcG/arcgis/rest/services/Slope_Movement_Outlines/FeatureServer/0
  Pre-Helene county landslide hazard mapping: Slope Movement Outlines (2,347 polygons delineated from field work, multi-vintage aerial photography and 6 m lidar DEMs), Deposit Polygons (3,268 debris-flow/slide deposits), Landslide Locations (1,188 points), Landslide Stability Index tile layer for Buncombe, and a WNC county progress layer.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer / tiled MapServer | auth: none | licence: North Carolina Geological Survey (DEQ disclaimer) | cadence: Updated as counties are completed (progress layer dated June 2021) | NC: Mapped WNC counties only (completion list not verified; progress layer exists)
  use (pre): Pre-event exposure: structures on mapped deposits or below slope movements get a landslide-susceptibility attribute; informs which tiles to difference first after a storm.
  caveats: Hosted on a university AGOL org, not a state endpoint; county coverage partial.
- (medium/high) **NCGS Channelized Debris Flow Model of Western North Carolina** (NC Geological Survey (DEQ DEMLR)) https://maps.deq.nc.gov/arcgis/rest/services/DEMLR/North_Carolina_Channelized_Debris_Flow_Model/MapServer
  Modelled source areas and transport paths for channelized debris flows (layer 0), applicable only during large storms (≥5 in of rain in 24 h or equivalent); does not model the deposit zone below the pour point (where most destruction occurs) and produces more false positives than true positives.
  access: ArcGIS MapServer (JSON, geoJSON, PBF; maxRecordCount 2000) | auth: none | licence: NC Geological Survey (DEQ disclaimer) | cadence: Static model output | NC: Western NC
  use (pre,during): Pre-event exposure: tag structures in or just below transport paths; during an event, combine with QPF/observed 24 h rain ≥5 in (NCGS 'areas of concern' threshold) as a trigger to pre-stage review for those structures.
  caveats: High false-positive rate; deposit zones not modelled.
- (medium/high) **USGS Preliminary Landslide Hazard Estimate Maps for Helene (USGS and NASA LHASA) and Sentinel-2 NDVI change map** (USGS Landslide Hazards Program (with NASA)) https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/item/66feded8d34e80be174ab8d4
  Rapid event hazard maps (published 2024-10-04, revised 2025-04-18): USGS model = National Landslide Susceptibility slope-relief thresholds x NWS GSP blended radar/gauge rainfall for 23-28 Sep 2024, normalised 0-1 at ~600 m and ~7 km; NASA LHASA machine-learning probability at 1 km with SMAP soil moisture; GeoTIFF. Companion 'NDVI Change Map between 9/22/2024 and 10/12/2024, Southern Appalachian Mountains' (ver. 1.1, March 2025): Sentinel-2 L2A, 10 m, GeoTIFF (1.45-5.36 GB files) with cloud masks and RGB composites.

  use (during,post): NDVI change is a cheap vegetation-loss evidence layer (debris flows, scour) and a scoping mask for E6 differencing; the rainfall x susceptibility recipe is reproducible in NC Recon as a 'during' trigger using NWS QPE/QPF and the NCGS model.
  caveats: Coarse (600 m-7 km) hazard grids; NDVI change affected by cloud and leaf-off timing.
- (low/high) **USGS Open-File Report 2025-1028: Preliminary Field Report of Landslide Hazards Following Hurricane Helene** (USGS (Allstadt, McBride, Godt, Slaughter, Baxstrom, Sobieszczyk, Stull)) https://pubs.usgs.gov/publication/ofr20251028
  June 2025 report documenting the interagency response: 1,155 landslide locations by the October 2024 briefings rising to 2,217 in the January 2025 inventory, helicopter overflights and imagery mapping (no systematic USGS field investigations), hazards from exposed scars and renewed slides in future rainfall; links the oblique aerial photo data releases.
  access: PDF/HTML publication | auth: none | licence: US Government public domain | cadence: One-off | NC: Nine western NC counties
  use (post): Narrative source for the brief and for defining a post-event landslide hazard flag (exposed scars) on nearby structures; not a data feed.
  caveats: Preliminary; no structure-level data.
- (high/medium) **DEM-of-Difference / repeat-lidar change detection methods for Helene (NCSU GRASS workflow, Davidson/USGS Wilson Creek study, OpenTopography differencing)** (NC State University Center for Geospatial Analytics (Corey White, OpenPlains); Davidson College/USGS/App State/WCU researchers; OpenTopography (NSF)) https://talks.osgeo.org/foss4g-na-2026/talk/QK3P7B/
  FOSS4G-NA 2026 workshop 'Geohazard Change Detection for Emergency Management with GRASS' uses post-Helene repeat lidar and SfM photogrammetry from the Blue Ridge Escarpment to teach: DEM differencing, vertical uncertainty quantification, spatial autocorrelation and error propagation, co-registration error diagnosis/correction, spatially variable uncertainty thresholds, and multi-date organisation in the GRASS temporal framework. GSA 2025 abstract (Johnson et al.) describes Wilson Creek Helene field work as ground truth for planned pre/post lidar differencing. OpenTopography lists 'Datasets with differencing' but restricts 3DEP access to academic users (non-academic API keys: 50 calls/day).
  access: Open-source GRASS GIS modules; conference abstracts; OpenTopography portal/API with key | auth: none for GRASS; OpenTopography key (3DEP 1 m academic only) | licence: GRASS GPL; abstracts copyrighted; OpenTopography terms | cadence: n/a | NC: Methods demonstrated on WNC Helene data
  use (post): Specification for the E6 channel: reproject both epochs to a common CRS/datum (ftUS NAVD88 Geoid12B vs metres), co-register on stable surfaces (roads, parking lots), compute DoD with a spatially variable level-of-detection (use the 9.25-19.6 cm NVA figures), summarise per footprint (roof-height loss, debris deposition) and per channel reach; implement with PDAL/GRASS/rasterio rather than OpenTopography.
  caveats: A 'repeat-lidar analysis of Hickory Nut Gorge' appeared only in a search snippet and was not fetched; no published DoD raster for Helene found.
- (high/medium) **FIMAN - NC Flood Inundation Mapping and Alert Network (gauges, inundation libraries, FIMAN-T)** (NC Emergency Management (NCDPS) with ESP Associates; NCDOT for FIMAN-T) https://fiman.nc.gov/
  Real-time stage/rain from the state gauge network (fact sheet: >700 gauges; GAGES_ALL service: 882 records incl. test/scenario) with pre-computed flood inundation libraries at gauges built from effective FIS models run iteratively at stage targets, Current/Scenario/Forecast modes (NWS forecasts), building/road/bridge impact statements, email/SMS alerts, EOC reports and KMZ export. FIMAN-T adds roadway/bridge inundation depths from lidar-derived road elevations and a Historic mode. The public gauge layer https://spartagis.ncem.org/arcgis/rest/services/FIMAN/GAGES_ALL/MapServer/0 ('Test Gages NC FIMAN', © ESP Associates) carries fields incl. MINOR/MODERATE/MAJOR thresholds, CURRENT_ELEVATION_MSL, FORECAST_PEAK_STAGE, NWMFORECAST_PEAK_STAGE, NEXTFIM_ID/STAGE, NAVD88_ELEVATION, road miles and bridges affected (current and forecast), NCEM_Branch, DOT_DIVISION.

  use (pre,during,post): Primary NC 'during' E5 source if NCEM shares libraries: depth at current/forecast stage per footprint; gauge MapServer already machine-readable for triggers (condition, forecast peak, NWM forecast); post-event peak-stage library extent as 'water reached' evidence; FIMAN-T road depths for the planned road-isolation module. Needs an MOU/data-sharing agreement for library depth grids.
  caveats: Scripted access to fiman.nc.gov is blocked; library data not open; FIMAN-T public domain dead; gauge service labelled 'Test'.

  NWPS gauge JSON includes an 'inundation' object with a per-gauge MapServer (e.g. https://mapservices.weather.noaa.gov/static/rest/services/NWS_FIM/FIM_bltn7/MapServer for Swannanoa River at Biltmore, USGS 03451000, zero datum 1976.29 ft NAVD88) exposing depth-grid layers at 1 ft stage increments, study extent, levees and flood categories, plus zipped shapefile depth grids, images and KMZ downloads (https://water.noaa.gov/resources/downloads/fim/bltn7/…). Libraries are steady-state hydraulic models for incremented discharges. NC static libraries (NWSLI ending n7): bltn7, bynn7, chin7, clyn7, efdn7, gldn7, hawn7, hokn7, kinn7, loun7, mann7, pgvn7, smfn7, tarn7, tksn7, trvn7, wlln7.

- (high/medium) **NWS/National Water Center experimental NWM-based flood inundation services (HAND) and stage-based CatFIM** (NOAA/NWS National Water Center (Office of Water Prediction)) https://maps.water.noaa.gov/server/rest/services/nwm/ana_inundation_extent/MapServer

High-water thresholds and AEPs come from the 40-year NWM reanalysis. water.noaa.gov states FIM services 'now cover nearly 100% of the U.S.'.

NWM v3.0 covers >2.7 million reaches; NOMADS/FTP also distribute.

  Near-worst-case surge inundation above ground for Category 1-5 as 8-bit GeoTIFFs in 1 ft bins (class 21 = 20+ ft) with world files and metadata; downloads at https://www.nhc.noaa.gov/gis/hazardmaps/ (US_SLOSH_MOM_Inundation_v4.zip, 1.5 GB, dated 2026-06-18; v3 2022; original 2017); also served as ArcGIS tile layers (Storm_Surge_HazardMaps_Category1-5_v3) catalogued on NC OneMap. During active storm-surge watches/warnings the P-Surge-based Potential Storm Surge Flooding Map (10 % exceedance) is issued every 6 h (03/09/15/21 UTC) as GeoTIFF and via nowCOAST WMS. SLOSH model code is in a private GitHub repository (request to arthur.taylor@noaa.gov under the SLOSH licence); MEOW/MOM inputs require contacting NHC/FEMA.

- (medium/medium) **HEC-RAS hydraulic models for western NC rivers (NCFMP effective models via FRIS; HydroLearn 2D Helene Swannanoa module)** (NCFMP/NCEM (effective models); HydroLearn/WMO Early Warnings for All (educational 2D module)) https://fris.nc.gov/api/flood/CountyModels/021/true

These are FEMA FIS models (predominantly 1D). A HydroLearn module 'Getting Started with 2D HEC-RAS: From Terrain to Flood Maps' demonstrates 2D rain-on-grid for Hurricane Helene over the Swannanoa River valley using USGS 3DEP terrain and MRMS/GOES rainfall (CC BY-NC-SA 4.0). No publicly released NCEM/FEMA 2D Helene model for the French Broad/Swannanoa was found.

- (low/low) **Post-Helene drone (sUAS) lidar and corridor lidar: USGS NUSO Blue Ridge Parkway and NCDOT/GPI US-19W** (USGS National Uncrewed Systems Office (for National Park Service); GPI Geospatial for NCDOT) https://www.usgs.gov/media/images/assessing-road-damage-caused-hurricane-helene

GPI Geospatial flew design-grade aerial lidar/imagery along a 22-mile US Highway 19 West corridor for NCDOT. Neither dataset was found as a public download; no open Gaussian-splat or drone photogrammetry datasets for NC were found.

North Carolina is a filterable state in the catalog.

- (low/high) **Eastern North Carolina 2014-2015 lidar-derived 20 ft canopy height** (US Fish and Wildlife Service (ScienceBase), from USGS/NCDPS QL2 lidar) https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/item/5a591b25e4b00b291cd6a949
  20 ft canopy height GeoTIFF (6.87 GB) for the 59 eastern counties aggregated from 5 ft products, NC State Plane ft NAD83(2011) EPSG:6543.
  access: ScienceBase download | auth: none | licence: Not stated (US Government hosted) | cadence: Static | NC: 59 eastern counties

### insights
- NC lidar lineage (NCEM LiDAR StoryMap https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/dc7044c9c8db4839a1b0d3dd56f40034/data?f=json; DEQ PDF https://deq.nc.gov/media/10455/download): 2001-2005 legacy by river basin (~1 pt/3-5 m2, bare earth only, not LAS); 2014-2018 by county in 5 phases (Phases 1-3 QL2 2 ppsm linear sensor, Phases 4-5 QL1 8 ppsm Geiger-mode flown at 28,000 ft, 2016 and 2018); 2020+ cycle QL1 8 ppsm (Phases 1-2 2020 topobathy with NOAA/USGS, Phase 3 early 2024, Phases 4-5 2025) adopting ASPRS 2023 Edition 2 accuracy standards and ASPRS 1.4 classes, while the 12 Collaboratory Helene counties use an NCEM-specific classification. Deliverables are NAD83(2011) NC State Plane US survey feet, NAVD88 Geoid 12A (Phases 1-3) or 12B (Phase 4+), 5,000 ft tiles (Phases 1-3) or 2,500 ft tiles (Phases 4-5); spec vertical accuracy 9.25 cm RMSEz NVA (DEQ) / 19.6 cm NVA at 95 % (NCDOT); 2014 Phase 1 achieved 11.7 cm at 95 % (NOAA InPort 49836). NC Recon's E6 must reproject and re-datum before differencing and should use these figures to set level-of-detection thresholds.
- Spatial Data Download has no API or bulk endpoint; county DEMs/footprints download without login, tile-level lidar needs an account (NC gov email/NCID, Microsoft, Google or Apple), 6 tiles (~10 GB) per request, and larger areas go through a 'large data request' delivered to the requester's Azure/AWS bucket or a mailed USB drive (SDD StoryMap https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/c8364f25e9c24d54bd6a99868669a45a/data?f=json). Plan a one-time statewide ingestion into NC Recon object storage rather than live calls.
- Post-Helene lidar status: the NC Collaboratory (UNC) funded $4M of leaf-off lidar + 6 in imagery over 13 counties in Jan-Mar 2025 (AECOM); imagery is on NC OneMap as an ImageServer/WMS and lidar is on SDD; SMAC's 2026 update says QL1 was collected in 2024 and 2025, Phase 3 lidar reaches SDD in early January 2026 and the remaining western counties in early 2026 (https://it.nc.gov/documents/gicc/smac-lidar-update/open). USGS 3DEP's Helene task order (Bowman/Merrick, 8,800 sq mi) covers GA, TN, AL and SC, not NC; the only 2024-25 USGS product touching NC is SC_Northern_D24 (QL1, Dec 2024-Feb 2025, 1 m DEM published April 2026).
- USGS 3DEP 1 m DEMs over WNC come from NC_Phase5_2018_A18 (published 2022), NC_Phase4_2017_A17 (published May 2024) and NC_Phase_4_CentralWestNC_GEIGER_A16 (collected Feb 2016-Oct 2017, published June 2024), all public domain, as COG GeoTIFF on prd-tnm.s3.amazonaws.com; the EPT mirror on s3://usgs-lidar-public has NC_Phase5_<county>_2017 for 22 western counties plus Phase 4, 2014 and 2020 Florence/Sparta sets, so point clouds can be streamed without bulk download (https://registry.opendata.aws/usgs-lidar/).
- NC's building footprint schema is Hazus-aligned: the 2010 RISK dataset and the 2020-2023 regional sets carry OCCUP_TYPE, FOUND_TYPE, NUM_STORY, FFE, FFE_TYP, LIDAR_LAG, LIDAR_HAG, BLDGREPVAL, CONTREPVAL, roof-shape/cover/deck fields and flood zone/BFE (https://services1.arcgis.com/YBWrN5qiESVpqi92/ArcGIS/rest/services/2022/FeatureServer/0?f=pjson; Business Plan https://it.nc.gov/media/3146/open). The statewide Composite Building Footprints service has 5,435,529 polygons >800 sq ft from 2020-2024 orthos but only BLDG_ID. NC Recon's displaced-population and loss outputs can map onto these fields and the NCEM BLDG_ID.
- FRIS exposes an undocumented JSON API (counties, CountyModels, CountyReports, CountyPanels, StreamsByCounty, PanelDownloadUrl, Risk/HazardProbability, Risk/RiskProjection, Risk/OccupancyTypes) and public download directories (County_LIDAR_Data, LegacyLidar, Redelineations) at https://fris.nc.gov/downloads/public/; its per-building risk report gives depth above finished floor and damage % for the 10/4/2/1/0.2 % events, but the 'Building Footprints with Risk' layer exists only in some counties (Buncombe is one) (https://www.guilfordcountync.gov/theincrediblefloodriskpdf/open).
- Landslide data after Helene: USGS reviewed point inventory 2,217 points (CC0, DOI 10.5066/P14CHGKS); NCGS post-Helene polygon inventory 2,607 landslides, 603 observations, 20 ground ruptures, with Material_Type, Movement_Type, Impact, Damage and Fatalities fields, edited to May 2026; NCGS statewide inventory >4,500 landslides and >7,000 observations; NCGS responded to 496 Helene landslides versus 358 in 1990-2023, Helene slides caused 31 deaths, 75 % impacted roads, structures or rivers, and the 2025 recovery bill allocated $3M to map 15 unmapped counties and update 9 (https://www.wunc.org/term/news/2026-01-09/helene-landslide-hazard-mapping-program-emergencies). NCGS treats 24 h forecast rainfall over 5 in as the 'areas of concern' trigger, the same threshold the Channelized Debris Flow Model assumes.
- Building heights: a 2026 PE&RS study found USA Structures heights agree with 3DEP lidar (R² 0.86-0.87, MAE 1.8-2.2 m) while Microsoft Global Building Footprints heights are weaker (R² 0.54-0.60, 2.5-3.1 m error) (https://www.usgs.gov/publications/evaluating-us-geological-survey-3d-elevation-program-lidar-data-a-source-building). USA Structures also carries H_ADJ_ELEV/L_ADJ_ELEV usable as a ground reference for depth-above-grade; its NC data were refreshed June 2025 under CC BY 4.0.
- Flood inundation libraries exist from three operators: FIMAN (state, built from effective FIS models at stage targets, Current/Scenario/Forecast modes, alerts, KMZ export; libraries not open, fiman.nc.gov blocks scripts, fimant.nc.gov DNS dead), NWS static FIM libraries at 17 NC gauges exposed through the NWPS gauge API with 1 ft depth-grid layers and shapefile/KMZ downloads (https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/bltn7), and NWC experimental HAND/NWM services (AnA hourly over a 30 % population domain, 5-day forecast, stage-based CatFIM over 60 %) at https://maps.water.noaa.gov/server/rest/services. The NCEM GAGES_ALL service (882 records) already carries NWM forecast peaks and FIMAN-T road-mile/bridge impacts per gauge.
- 3D visualisation options have cost/licence constraints: Cesium ion's free tier excludes government projects (Commercial $149/month individual, $524 team; self-hosted on request); Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles cost $6 per 1,000 root tile requests after 1,000 free per month and prohibit caching, offline use and any image analysis or geodata extraction; Cesium OSM Buildings heights are often estimated as levels x 3 m. For a no-paid-services product, self-generate 3D Tiles/EPT views from NC lidar and extrude NCEM/USA Structures footprints.
- NC OneMap Terms (https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/e60514c3b78542ef9902e3d7f7a671c1/data?f=pjson) and NC DEQ's disclaimer both state free, unrestricted use with no written release agreements, no warranty, and request citation ('NC OneMap (2019)…'); NCEM AGOL items carry no licence text, so a short confirmation with NCEM GIS is advisable before redistribution in exports.
- Storm surge: the NHC MOM GeoTIFF package was refreshed to v4 on 2026-06-18 (1.5 GB, 1 ft bins, Cat 1-5, not for parcel-level use) and P-Surge Potential Storm Surge Flooding Maps are issued 6-hourly as GeoTIFF/WMS during watches; SLOSH code itself is only available by request under licence (https://vlab.noaa.gov/web/mdl/slosh).
- Change-detection method guidance specific to Helene exists (NCSU/OpenPlains GRASS workshop at FOSS4G-NA 2026: DoD, vertical uncertainty, autocorrelation, co-registration, variable thresholds, temporal framework), but no published DoD raster for Helene was found; NC Recon would be producing the first operational statewide-ready DoD product from 2017 Phase 5 vs 2025 Collaboratory lidar.
### gaps
- sdd.nc.gov is a JavaScript application behind Cloudflare and Azure AD B2C; its live catalog (which counties/phases of 2024-2025 QL1 lidar are downloadable today, tile index, DSM availability) could not be read. No DSM product is published by NCEM; DSMs must be derived from all-return LAS.
- Point density, quality level, vertical accuracy report and vendor deliverable specs for the 2025 NC Collaboratory Helene lidar were not found on any fetched page (only the imagery specification and '12 counties with NCEM classification').
- No USGS 3DEP post-Helene lidar over North Carolina was found (3DEP index MapServer queries returned 'Failed to execute query'); the Bowman/Merrick Helene task order was confirmed only via search snippets covering GA/TN/AL/SC.
- FIMAN inundation library polygons/depth grids, the count of gauges with libraries, stage increments and any API were not obtainable: fiman.nc.gov returns a Cloudflare block to non-browser clients, the 2017 workshop PDF is mostly images, and fimant.nc.gov does not resolve. Access terms for libraries (MOU) are unknown.
- FRIS: per-county flood depth grids (FEMA Flood Risk Database style) were not confirmed; the list of counties with the 'Building Footprints with Risk' layer, the source of first-floor elevations (lidar vs survey) per building, and the model download base URLs (runtime config) were not retrievable. The FRIS API is undocumented and partly POST-only.
- Whether FFE, LIDAR_LAG/HAG, replacement values and roof attributes are actually populated in the NCEM 2020-2023 footprint services (schema inherited from the 2010 RISK dataset) was not verified; the composite statewide layer has only BLDG_ID.
- No open drone/sUAS point clouds, photogrammetric meshes or Gaussian-splat datasets for post-Helene NC were found; USGS NUSO Blue Ridge Parkway lidar and NCDOT/GPI US-19W corridor lidar have no public release located. Web search budget was exhausted before Gaussian-splat and OpenAerialMap-3D queries could run.
- Only one open 3D city model in NC was found (Mecklenburg Buildings3dMultipatch scene layer, cached 2023); the City of Raleigh 'Mirror City' model cited by NCEM has no public endpoint found. Google Photorealistic 3D Tiles coverage over rural WNC and public-sector/crisis credit eligibility were not verified.
- Overture's 3DEP-derived building-height whitepaper exceeded the fetch size limit; the number and location of NC buildings with lidar heights in Overture is unknown.
- SLOSH Display Program/basin pages returned no content; the NC SLOSH basin list and MEOW access were not confirmed. nowCOAST WMS layer URLs for P-Surge and NWM inundation were not fetched (JavaScript site).
- The NWS/NWC public FIM domain over western NC WFOs was not verified against the static_public_fim_domain layer; the NWC Visualization Services Public Handbook v2.3 URL returned 404; the NWS FIM story map is JavaScript-only.
