- NCGS county landslide hazard map completion list (which WNC counties have Slope Movement Outlines/Deposit Polygons/Stability Index) was not verified; the DEQ landslide program sub-page URLs returned 404.
- The GSA 'repeat-lidar analysis of Hickory Nut Gorge' and other Helene DoD studies appeared only in search snippets; the fetched abstract was the Wilson Creek study, so no published Helene DEM-of-Difference dataset or accuracy figures were obtained.
- NCEM AdvisoryFlood/Mosaic_AEG_0500 ImageServer (3 ft pixels, statewide extent) has no description; its content (possibly an advisory elevation grid) and intended use are unknown.
- No publicly released NCEM/FEMA 2D HEC-RAS model for the French Broad/Swannanoa Helene event was found; only an educational HydroLearn module exists.
- Mecklenburg County open mapping catalogue (lidar/DEM/3D downloads and licence) is JavaScript-only and was not read.
- Microsoft GlobalMLBuildingFootprints height coverage specifically for the United States/NC was not confirmed.

## State of the art in automated building damage assessment and what NC Recon should adopt (xBD lineage, Microsoft/Google/HASTE operational tools, geospatial foundation models, SAM footprints, SAR proxies, OPERA, blue tarps, per-structure flood depth, lidar/landslides, drone models, uncertainty/abstention, FEMA PDA acceptance, accuracy expectations, compute cost, Helene-specific data)
- (high/high) **xBD dataset and xView2 challenge (paper, baseline, scoring)** (Defense Innovation Unit / Carnegie Mellon SEI (xView2), Maxar Open Data imagery) https://arxiv.org/abs/1911.09296
  Benchmark of pre/post Maxar satellite image pairs with per-building Joint Damage Scale labels (no damage / minor / major / destroyed): 19 disasters, 22,068 images, 850,736 building polygons, 45,362 km2, GSD below 0.8 m. Baseline (U-Net localization IoU 0.66 buildings; ResNet50 ordinal classifier) reached weighted damage F1 0.2654 (per class: no damage 0.6631, minor 0.1435, major 0.0094, destroyed 0.4657). xView2 leaderboard baseline: overall 0.28408, localization F1 0.80482, damage F1 0.06091; score = 0.3*loc F1 + 0.7*damage F1.
  access: portal download (xview2.org, account); TorchGeo loader; baseline/scoring code on GitHub | auth: account (xview2.org login) | licence: Dataset CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0 (as recorded by TorchGeo docs); baseline and scoring code BSD-3-Clause | cadence: static (2019) | NC: None (no NC events). Hurricanes Harvey, Michael, Florence(?) not confirmed; paper lists Midwest floods, Harvey, Joplin etc.
  use (post): Training/benchmark lineage of NC Recon's xView2 CNN; use the published per-class F1 (minor/major near-random) to justify treating minor-vs-major as uncertain and routing to human review; re-score NC Recon on xView2 metric for comparability. Check NC-SA licence before any commercial redistribution of weights trained on xBD.
  caveats: Non-commercial ShareAlike licence on labels/imagery; class imbalance (no damage >8x others); 'major' F1 0.0094 in baseline shows the ordinal middle classes are the hard part.
- (high/high) **xView2 first-place solution (Durnov) and xView2-deploy** (Victor Durnov (winner) / DIUx-xView GitHub org) https://github.com/DIUx-xView/xView2_first_place
  Winning xView2 models: UNet-like encoders (ResNet34, se_resnext50_32x4d, SeNet154, DPN92) for localization, Siamese weight-shared pre/post network for damage; Dice+Focal(+CE) losses; trained ~7 days on 2x12GB GPUs; weights zip on S3. xView2-deploy runs these on arbitrary pre/post GeoTIFFs with AOI; README says CPU inference is 'wildly impractical' and recommends cloud GPU instances.
  access: GitHub repo + weights download (S3 zip) | auth: none | licence: MIT (code and weights) | cadence: static (2020) | NC: n/a (model, global)
  use (post): Reference ensemble for NC Recon's ONNX xView2 channel; export the 4 backbones to ONNX and benchmark against NC Recon's current model on Helene NOAA/Maxar pairs; adopt its TTA and Siamese design. Note final leaderboard scores are not published in the README (unknown).
  caveats: Trained on 0.3-0.8 m satellite; distribution shift to 15-30 cm NOAA aerial and to sUAS imagery is documented (Manzini 2025); GPU strongly recommended.
- (high/high) **Microsoft AI for Good building-damage-assessment toolkit (and Rolling Fork workflow paper)** (Microsoft AI for Good Research Lab) https://github.com/microsoft/building-damage-assessment
  End-to-end per-event fine-tuning workflow: labeling tool -> masks -> fine-tune -> inference; plus a Damage Proxy Map (SAR) workflow that intersects NASA JPL / EOS DPMs with footprints. Used for 2023 Turkey/Syria earthquakes (3,849 buildings damaged across four cities, with AFAD), Rolling Fork MS tornado, Maui fires, Libya floods, Hurricane Beryl 2024. Rolling Fork: human-in-the-loop fine-tuning of 'a U-Net with an ImageNet pre-trained ResNet-50 backbone' end-to-end in under 2 hours per scene, precision 0.86 / recall 0.80 for damaged buildings vs ground truth.
  access: GitHub (code); paper on arXiv | auth: none | licence: MIT (code); paper CC BY | cadence: per event | NC: No Helene use documented in the repo README (gap)
  use (post): Adopt the per-event fine-tune pattern: let NC Recon analysts label a few hundred buildings on the first post-event scene and fine-tune a light head, rather than relying only on frozen xBD weights; adopt the DPM-intersection workflow as a SAR evidence channel when ARIA/EOS products exist.
  caveats: Architecture, GSD and GPU requirements not stated in README; results depend on analyst labels per event.
- (medium/medium) **Microsoft HASTE (no-code rapid damage mapping platform)** (Microsoft) https://github.com/microsoft/haste
  Open-source web platform (React + Azure Functions + TiTiler + Azure Batch GPU pools) for analysts to produce per-building damage maps from post-disaster satellite imagery using (1) scene-level segmentation with polygon labels or (2) foundation-model embeddings + logistic regression needing only a handful of labeled buildings; paper reports use in 'more than thirty real-world disaster responses since 2023' and FM embeddings matching a supervised ResNet-50 with 1/20 of the labels on xBD.
  access: GitHub (azd deploy to Azure; docker compose locally) | auth: none for code; Azure subscription to run as designed | licence: MIT (code); paper CC BY 4.0 | cadence: per event | NC: No events named in repo; Helene not confirmed
  use (post): Evaluate: the embeddings+logistic-regression few-shot approach is the right design for NC Recon's review queue (analyst labels feed an online classifier); reuse the UI pattern (label polygons, train, map) but on NC Recon's single-container stack rather than Azure.
  caveats: Azure-centric (Cosmos DB, Blob, Batch); which foundation model is used was not stated in the README excerpt.
- (medium/high) **Google Research SKAI (with WFP)** (Google Research / Google.org; WFP Innovation Accelerator) https://github.com/google-research/skai
  ML tool for building damage assessment on pre/post COG imagery: footprints from Open Buildings, OSM or user CSV; 64x64 training / 256x256 labeling chips; Eagle Eye labeling with classes no_damage, minor_damage, major_damage, destroyed, bad_example; two-tower model fine-tuned on a small number of labels; runs on GCP (Cloud Storage, Dataflow, Vertex AI). Claims 85-98% agreement with experts; WFP deployments: Hurricane Ian 2022 (410,000 buildings scanned in days, 69,000 damaged), Turkiye-Syria 2023 (~600,000 buildings, accuracy >81%), Durban 2022 (precision 88%), Myanmar and Sri Lanka 2025.
  access: GitHub + GCP pipeline | auth: none for code; GCP account to run | licence: Apache-2.0 | cadence: per event | NC: None documented; no FEMA/US agency use found on WFP page
  use (post): Adopt SKAI's labeling protocol (4 FEMA-like classes + bad_example for misaligned/cloudy/no-building chips) for NC Recon's ground-truth UI; adopt its two-tower few-shot fine-tune idea; do not adopt the GCP dependency.
  caveats: Requires GCP Dataflow/Vertex; GSD requirements not stated; accuracy claims are WFP/Google self-reported.
- (medium/high) **BRIGHT multimodal (optical pre + VHR SAR post) damage dataset and DFC 2025 benchmark** (Chen et al. (Univ. of Tokyo/RIKEN); IEEE GRSS DFC 2025) https://github.com/ChenHongruixuan/BRIGHT
  4,246 1024x1024 optical-pre/SAR-post pairs (0.3-1 m; Capella/Umbra SAR), ~700k buildings, 14 events/23 regions, 3 classes (intact/damaged/destroyed). Benchmarks: ChangeMamba mIoU 67.63%, F1 loc 90.90%, F1 damage 72.70%; zero-shot to unseen event ~39.38% mIoU, one-shot ~43.23%; SAR-only 65.56% vs optical-only 69.76% vs fusion 70.79%; single-pol SAR 'lacks the fine-grained information needed to reliably distinguish partially damaged buildings'.
  access: Zenodo / Hugging Face download; code on GitHub | auth: none | licence: CC BY 4.0 (paper and most data); TorchGeo records 'CC-BY-4.0 AND CC-BY-NC-4.0' (Maxar-derived parts NC) | cadence: static (2025) | NC: No US hurricane events named; Maui 2023 is the only US event
  use (post): Evaluate as the all-weather path: if NC can task Capella/Umbra (paid) after a storm, BRIGHT-trained models give a cloud-independent damage channel; use its cross-event numbers (~10-pt drop) as the expected generalization penalty for any new NC event.
  caveats: VHR SAR post-event imagery is commercial; mixed licence components.
- (high/high) **Damage-TriageFormer and DamageTriage-Bench (labeled Hurricane Helene NOAA ERI)** (Xiao, Ho, Thasma, Ma, Mostafavi (Texas A&M UrbanResilience.AI)) https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12248
  Mono-temporal, footprint-conditioned damage model (DINOv3 ViT-L + simple feature pyramid + gated two-stage head) and a benchmark built from NOAA Emergency Response Imagery (0.3-0.5 m) for Hurricane Michael 2018, Hurricane Helene 2024 and 2025 LA fires: 7,472 tiles, 87,017 labeled polygon fragments, 5 typology classes (undamaged; partial/total roof damage; partial/total structural damage). Macro F1 0.619 test; undamaged 0.906, total collapse 0.836, total roof damage 0.326.
  access: Hugging Face dataset (Ymx1025/DamageTriage-Bench, 7.71 GB, PNG + masks) and GitHub code | auth: none (HF account for download) | licence: Dataset CC BY-NC 4.0; code MIT | cadence: static (2026) | NC: Includes Hurricane Helene 2024 NOAA ERI tiles (per-event counts/locations not stated; whether NC or FL scenes is unknown)
  use (post): Adopt as the first external validation set for NC Recon's NOAA-ERI pipeline and as the only found labeled Helene aerial dataset; evaluate its roof-vs-structural taxonomy as an explanation layer under FEMA levels; consider the post-only model as a channel when no pre-image aligns.
  caveats: Non-commercial licence; mono-temporal model struggles on roof-damage classes; Helene footprint geography not confirmed.
- (high/high) **CRASAR-U-DROIDs sUAS damage dataset** (CRASAR (Texas A&M / Florida State)) https://huggingface.co/datasets/CRASAR/CRASAR-U-DROIDs
  Largest labeled sUAS orthomosaic dataset: 52 (now 265 listed) orthomosaics, 68 km2, 70.6 gigapixels, 21,716 building polygons with Joint Damage Scale labels (+ un-classified, obscured) from 10 US disasters (Ian, Ida, Harvey, Idalia, Laura, Michael, Mussett Bayou fire, Mayfield tornado, Kilauea, Champlain Towers); GSD 1.77-12.7 cm/px (mean 3.74); 7,880 alignment adjustments = 36% of polygons had to be shifted to match imagery.
  access: Hugging Face download (219 GB) | auth: none | licence: CC-BY-4.0 | cadence: growing (labels added over time) | NC: None (no NC events; no Helene/Debby)
  use (post): Adopt as the training/validation set for a drone-imagery damage model; adopt its finding that footprint-to-image alignment is a first-class step (NC Recon already aligns outlines; make alignment offsets explicit and QA'd).
  caveats: 219 GB; variable GSD; sUAS label distribution differs from satellite labels.
- (high/high) **Operational sUAS damage-assessment deployment at Hurricanes Debby and Helene (Manzini, Perali, Murphy)** (CRASAR / Texas A&M with Florida FL-UAS1 task force) https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03132
  First documented operational ML damage assessment on drone imagery: Attention U-Net trained on CRASAR-U-DROIDs (test macro F1 0.554 aligned / 0.524 unaligned; ResNet101+DeepLabv3+ 0.493; ScaleMAE ViT-L variants 0.12-0.37) deployed on WingtraOne imagery; 415 buildings assessed in ~18 minutes total (Debby: 222 buildings, Suwannee Co. FL; Helene: 193 buildings, Lafayette and Taylor Cos. FL); all outputs manually inspected before delivery; 91 practitioners from 57 agencies trained. Challenges: GSD varied 1.65-25.3 cm/px; spatial misalignment degraded performance by at least 39.1%; limited connectivity; outputs had to be reissued as GeoJSON/CSV/ArcGIS-compatible formats.
  access: arXiv paper (IAAI-26); companion paper arXiv 2506.15890 | auth: none | licence: arXiv non-exclusive; dataset CC-BY-4.0 | cadence: per event | NC: Helene deployment was in Florida, not NC
  use (post): Adopt lessons: ship GeoJSON/CSV/ArcGIS-ready outputs (NC Recon already does), build an offline/edge inference path for poor connectivity, normalize GSD before inference, and keep human inspection before release; use macro F1 ~0.55 as the realistic sUAS five-class expectation.
  caveats: Practitioners cared less about accuracy than about integration into existing tools (ArcGIS).
- (medium/high) **RescueNet and FloodNet UAV datasets (BinaLab)** (Rahnemoonfar et al. (BinaLab) with CRASAR) https://doi.org/10.1038/s41597-023-02799-4
  RescueNet: 4,494 UAV images (3000x4000) after Hurricane Michael with pixel labels for 10 classes incl. building no/medium/major damage/total destruction, road clear/blocked, water, vehicle, tree, pool; baselines mIoU PSPNet 95.67%, Attention UNet 98.47%, Segmenter 85.78%; Figshare DOI 10.6084/m9.figshare.c.6647354.v1. FloodNet: 2,343 DJI Mavic images after Hurricane Harvey, 10 classes incl. building-flooded/non-flooded, road-flooded.
  access: Figshare (RescueNet), Dropbox link in GitHub (FloodNet) | auth: none | licence: RescueNet CC BY 4.0; FloodNet Community Data License Agreement (permissive) | cadence: static | NC: None
  use (post): Evaluate as pretraining/augmentation for a drone semantic-segmentation model (debris, blocked roads, flooded buildings) that complements per-building classification; FloodNet's building-flooded class is a direct flood-evidence channel for drone sorties.
  caveats: Single-event each (Michael, Harvey); very high reported mIoU suggests easy test split; GSD not stated.
- (medium/high) **NASA/IBM Prithvi-EO-2.0 and Sen1Floods11 fine-tunes (TerraTorch)** (IBM Research, NASA IMPACT, Juelich) https://huggingface.co/ibm-nasa-geospatial/Prithvi-EO-2.0-300M
  ViT MAE geospatial foundation model (5M-600M params) pretrained on 4.2M HLS 30 m samples (6 bands); example fine-tunes: multitemporal crop, landslide segmentation, carbon flux; flood fine-tunes: Prithvi-EO-1.0-100M-sen1floods11 (mIoU 88.68%, water IoU 80.46%, Bolivia holdout water IoU 77.95%) and Prithvi-EO-2.0-300M-TL-Sen1Floods11 (3-class water/no-water/cloud on Sentinel-2 L1C). No building-damage fine-tune exists.
  access: Hugging Face weights; TerraTorch | auth: none | licence: Apache 2.0 | cadence: static weights; HLS 2-3 day revisit | NC: Global (HLS covers NC)
  use (during,post): Evaluate for flood-extent mapping from HLS/Sentinel-2 at 30 m as a water-surface source feeding per-structure depth (NC Recon currently lacks one); skip for per-building damage (resolution too coarse). Landslide fine-tune pattern applicable to Helene-type debris flows.
  caveats: Optical only (cloud-limited in the days after a storm); 30 m.
- (medium/high) **SatlasPretrain backbones (incl. NAIP 0.5-2 m aerial)** (Allen Institute for AI) https://github.com/allenai/satlaspretrain_models
  Pretrained Swin-v2 (Base/Tiny) and ResNet50/152 backbones for Sentinel-2 (RGB and 9-band), Sentinel-1 (VV/VH), Landsat 8/9 and NAIP aerial RGB 0.5-2 m/px, with FPN and task heads; reported faster training and better downstream performance than other initializations.
  access: pip package + hosted weights | auth: none | licence: Code Apache-2.0; weights ODC-BY | cadence: static | NC: NAIP covers NC (pretraining domain matches NC OneMap/NOAA aerial orthos)
  use (post): Adopt as the encoder when fine-tuning a damage head on NOAA ERI / NC OneMap aerial pairs (aerial-RGB pretraining is closer to NC Recon's inputs than ImageNet or 30 m HLS models).
  caveats: No disaster/damage fine-tune provided; ODC-BY attribution on weights.
- (low/medium) **Other geospatial foundation models: Clay v1.5, DOFA, Copernicus-FM** (Clay Foundation; TUM zhu-xlab) https://github.com/Clay-foundation/model
  Clay v1.5: Apache-2.0 code and weights (HF made-with-clay/Clay), embeddings + segmentation/classification/regression tutorials (Chesapeake, EuroSAT, Biomasters); no damage example. DOFA: MIT code, HF XShadow/DOFA weights, accepts any number of channels (S1, S2, NAIP RGB, hyperspectral). Copernicus-FM: Apache-2.0 code, CC-BY-4.0 weights, S1/S2/S3/S5P pretraining (18.7M images), Copernicus-Bench includes a Flood-S1 change-detection task (ICCV 2025).
  access: GitHub + Hugging Face | auth: none | licence: Apache-2.0 / MIT code; Apache-2.0 or CC-BY-4.0 weights | cadence: static | NC: Global
  use (post): Skip for now for building damage (no damage fine-tunes exist, 10-30 m domain); note Copernicus-FM Flood-S1 for a later Sentinel-1 flood-extent channel and DOFA for mixed-sensor experiments.
  caveats: No published building-damage fine-tunes for any of them (confirmed absent in docs fetched).
- (medium/medium) **Segment Anything (SAM/SAM2) and samgeo for footprints** (Meta AI (SAM); Qiusheng Wu / opengeos (samgeo)) https://github.com/opengeos/segment-geospatial
  SAM (Apache 2.0; ViT-B/L/H; mask decoder exportable to ONNX) wrapped for GeoTIFF/COG/TMS inputs with SAM2, HQ-SAM, FastSAM and GroundingDINO text prompts; outputs GeoPackage/Shapefile/GeoJSON; QGIS and ArcGIS Pro integration; recommends GPU with 8 GB VRAM though CPU works. Zero-shot footprint literature is thin: a 2025 ISPRS paper reports 78.31% AP50 on Vaihingen with SAM + regularization.
  access: pip (samgeo), GitHub | auth: none | licence: SAM Apache 2.0; samgeo MIT | cadence: static | NC: n/a (model)
  use (pre,post): Evaluate for (a) prompt-based refinement of misaligned/missing footprints using the existing footprint centroid as the point prompt on the post-event ortho, and (b) analyst 'click to add building' in the review UI; skip as a primary footprint source (no accuracy evidence vs USA Structures/OSM).
  caveats: Over-segmentation of roofs; GPU recommended; peer-reviewed footprint accuracy evidence is weak.
- (medium/low) **NASA ARIA damage/flood proxy maps and Sentinel-1 coherence-change method (Tay et al. 2020)** (NASA JPL ARIA; Earth Observatory of Singapore (EOS-RS)) https://aria-share.jpl.nasa.gov/
  Event-driven Damage Proxy Maps and Flood Proxy Maps derived from SAR coherence/intensity change (Sentinel-1, ALOS-2), published per event on ARIA-share as GeoTIFF/KMZ; method documented in Tay et al. 2020 (Scientific Data, CC BY) for Typhoon Hagibis with outputs 'largely in agreement' with aerial/optical validation (no quantitative accuracy given). Microsoft's toolkit includes a workflow to intersect DPMs with footprints. A 2026 JAWRA Helene/Buncombe study validated flood models against 'NASA-JPL satellite-derived flood extents', implying a Helene flood product existed.
  access: portal download (ARIA-share directory listing; JS-rendered, could not be enumerated); NASA Disasters Mapping Portal (ArcGIS Hub) | auth: none | licence: NASA open data (unstated on page); contains Copernicus Sentinel data | cadence: per event (days after SAR pass) | NC: Unconfirmed: a Helene DPM/FPM directory could not be fetched; NASA Disasters Helene storymap URL returned 404
  use (during,post): Evaluate as a coarse (30 m) all-weather evidence channel and a triage layer in the first 48 h: intersect DPM pixels with footprints to raise prior damage probability, as Microsoft's DPM workflow does; do not use as per-building ground truth.
  caveats: Existence and location of Helene DPM/FPM not confirmed in this session; coherence change also fires on vegetation/flood, so it is a proxy only.
- (high/high) **OPERA DIST-ALERT-HLS (land surface disturbance)** (NASA JPL OPERA / UMD GLAD; distributed by LP DAAC (LPCLOUD)) https://www.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/catalog/lpcloud-opera-l3-dist-alert-hls-v1-1
  30 m vegetation and generic disturbance alerts from HLS (Landsat 8/9 + Sentinel-2) since 2023-01-01, daily cadence of inputs; layers VEG-ANOM, VEG-ANOM-MAX, VEG-DIST-STATUS, VEG-DIST-CONF, VEG-DIST-DATE, VEG-DIST-DUR, VEG-HIST, VEG-IND, GEN-ANOM, GEN-DIST-STATUS etc.; COG on MGRS tiles; CMR concept C2746980408-LPCLOUD; DOI 10.5067/SNWG/OPERA_L3_DIST-ALERT-HLS_V1.001. The DIST product page also lists a Sentinel-1 variant, but no DIST-S1 collection was found in CMR.
  access: Earthdata Search, CMR STAC, direct S3 (us-west-2) | auth: free key (Earthdata Login) | licence: NASA EOSDIS open data, no restrictions | cadence: with each HLS acquisition (2-3 days), cloud-dependent | NC: Global incl. NC; Jan 2023 onward (covers Helene)
  use (during,post): Adopt as a map layer and trigger: VEG-DIST-STATUS/GEN-DIST after a storm flags debris-flow scars, tree-fall and flood-stripped areas; use as a prior for landslide-affected structures and as a coverage-gap cue for where to task aerial imagery (the USGS Helene inventory itself was built from Sentinel-2 NDVI change).
  caveats: Optical, so cloud-limited; 30 m; forest canopy hides small slides.
- (medium/high) **OPERA DSWx-HLS and DSWx-S1 (dynamic surface water extent)** (NASA JPL OPERA; distributed by PO.DAAC (POCLOUD)) https://cmr.earthdata.nasa.gov/search/collections.json?short_name=OPERA_L3_DSWX-HLS_V1
  DSWx-HLS: 30 m water classification, confidence, land cover, terrain shadow, cloud layers and a 30 m DEM on 109.8 km UTM tiles from HLS; collection start 2016-01-01, validated from April 2023; Earthdata collection C2617126679-POCLOUD. DSWx-S1: Sentinel-1 radar surface water at 30 m on MGRS tiles regardless of clouds, maps inland water bodies >3 ha and >200 m wide, 6-12 day revisit; start 2023-12-01, operational Sept 2024; collection C2949811996-POCLOUD; spec D-108761.
  access: Earthdata Search, CMR, virtual directory, Harmony, podaac data-subscriber | auth: free key (Earthdata Login) | licence: NASA open data (DEM layer carries Copernicus disclaimer) | cadence: HLS 2-3 days (cloud-limited); S1 6-12 days | NC: Global incl. NC; DSWx-S1 operational since Sept 2024 (Helene landfall 26-27 Sept 2024)
  use (during,post): Evaluate as the missing water-surface/extent source: intersect DSWx water with the 3DEP/NC lidar DEM (FwDET-style) to estimate depth per structure; DSWx-S1 gives a cloud-independent extent during the event; both too coarse for narrow mountain streams, so use for Piedmont/coastal riverine flooding.
  caveats: 30 m; S1 misses water <3 ha or <200 m wide; terrain shadow in mountains; DIST-S1 not yet in CMR.
- (medium/medium) **Blue tarp / tarped-roof detection literature** (Miura, Aridome, Matsuoka (Remote Sensing 2020); Wolf and Takeuchi (AEJ: Economic Policy 2026)) https://doi.org/10.3390/rs12121924
  Miura et al.: CNN classifying post-disaster aerial photos into collapsed, non-collapsed and blue tarp-covered buildings, ~95% accuracy on Kumamoto/Kobe earthquakes and >90% on 2019 Chiba typhoon (CC BY). Wolf and Takeuchi: blue tarps detected from post-Irma photographs over Microsoft US Building Footprints for >500,000 Florida homes to measure building-code effectiveness (data/code at openICPSR 10.3886/E236942V1, could not be fetched). No peer-reviewed US hurricane blue-tarp detection paper with reported precision/recall was found via arXiv/OpenAlex/Crossref.
  access: journal articles; openICPSR data (403 on fetch) | auth: none | licence: Miura CC BY 4.0; AEA article proprietary; ICPSR terms unknown | cadence: static | NC: None
  use (post): Adopt tarps as a recovery-phase indicator (repairs under way, roof damage confirmed) and as weak Minor/Major evidence in later re-flights (NC OneMap Helene 2025 orthos), not as initial damage evidence; publish NC Recon's own tarp detector precision/recall since literature is thin.
  caveats: Tarps appear days-weeks after the event, so absent in first-pass imagery; colour-based detectors confuse pools, blue roofs.
- (high/high) **FwDET v2.0 Floodwater Depth Estimation Tool** (Cohen et al. (Univ. of Alabama SDML / CSDMS)) https://nhess.copernicus.org/articles/19/2053/2019/
  Estimates water depth from any flood-extent polygon plus a DEM by assigning each flooded cell the elevation of its nearest boundary cell (cost allocation), excluding boundary cells at/below sea level; mean absolute difference vs hydrodynamic models 0.18 m (Norfolk, 1 m lidar DEM) and 0.31 m (Brazos, 10 m DEM); 15x faster than v1; Python with ArcGIS and QGIS tools (GitHub csdms-contrib/fwdet).
  access: GitHub / CSDMS download | auth: none | licence: CC BY 4.0 | cadence: on demand | NC: Method; applicable with NC QL2/QL1 lidar DEMs
  use (during,post): Adopt as the per-structure depth engine: extent from DSWx/ARIA FPM/analyst-drawn or HWM-interpolated water surface + NC lidar DEM -> depth at footprint -> FEMA waterline thresholds (below/at-or-above outlets).
  caveats: Depth error 0.2-0.3 m is comparable to the FEMA Minor/Major waterline distinction; needs a clean extent boundary.
- (high/high) **USGS high-water-mark flood-inundation mapping precedent in NC (Hurricane Matthew OFR 2017-1047) and USGS FIM Program** (U.S. Geological Survey (South Atlantic Water Science Center; FIM Program)) https://pubs.usgs.gov/publication/ofr20171047
  After Matthew (Oct 2016) USGS surveyed 254 of 267 HWMs (NCEM added 353) and produced six community flood-inundation maps with 'digital datasets of the inundation areas, study reach boundary, and water-depth rasters' (data release DOI 10.5066/F75X276T). The USGS FIM Program publishes gauge-stage-indexed inundation map libraries (depth shown) through the FIM Mapper (apps.usgs.gov/fim) tied to real-time gauges; 2007 Tar River basin lidar-derived FIM library (SIR 2007-5032) is an NC precedent. No Helene flood-inundation/depth data release was found on ScienceBase.
  access: USGS publications + ScienceBase data release; FIM Mapper web app | auth: none | licence: US Gov public domain | cadence: per event (weeks-months after) | NC: Matthew 2016 (eastern NC, 6 communities); Tar River basin libraries; Helene depth rasters not found
  use (pre,during,post): Adopt the method (survey/STN HWMs + lidar DEM -> interpolated water surface -> depth raster) as NC Recon's documented flood-depth procedure; pre-load FIM Program libraries for NC gauges so the Conditions map can show forecast depth at structures during the event.
  caveats: USGS products arrive weeks later; FIM libraries exist only for selected reaches.
- (high/high) **NOAA OWP inundation-mapping (HAND FIM) and FEMA Hazus 7.0 Flood Model / FAST** (NOAA Office of Water Prediction; FEMA (Hazus)) https://github.com/NOAA-OWP/inundation-mapping
  OWP FIM: Apache-2.0 Docker pipeline producing National Water Model-driven inundation via Height Above Nearest Drainage, synthetic rating curves, 3DEP DEMs and NHDPlus HR; pre-generated HAND data ~1.7 TB on AWS S3 (ESIP). Hazus 7.0 Flood Model Technical Manual (June 2025) documents user-defined depth grids ('recent event captured with high water marks'), and depth-damage functions by occupancy/stories/foundation (FIA and USACE curves, e.g., RES1 1-story no basement = USACE); FAST/HazPy computes per-building losses from a UDF CSV + depth TIFF in pure Python (GitHub inactive; moved to FEMA MSC).
  access: GitHub (FIM, FAST); FEMA PDFs; Hazus via FEMA Map Service Center | auth: none (Hazus requires ArcGIS Pro licence to run GUI) | licence: FIM Apache-2.0; Hazus free US Gov; FAST licence per repo | cadence: OWP FIM per NWM cycle; Hazus static | NC: HAND covers CONUS incl. NC; Hazus depth-damage functions are national
  use (pre,during,post): Adopt Hazus depth-damage functions (FIA/USACE) to convert per-structure depth + NSI foundation/first-floor height into damage fraction and FEMA level; evaluate OWP FIM HAND outputs as a forecast depth layer during the event (NWM-driven).
  caveats: HAND FIM is coarse in mountain headwaters; Hazus GUI needs ArcGIS Pro; FAST GitHub no longer updated.
- (low/medium) **Hurricane Helene flood-model sensitivity study, Buncombe County (JAWRA 2026)** (US Army ERDC Coastal and Hydraulics Laboratory (Smallwood, Pennell, Ondich)) https://api.crossref.org/works/10.1111/1752-1688.70125
  AutoRoute large-scale flood inundation runs for Buncombe County during Helene with nine DEM/land-cover resolution combinations, validated against NASA-JPL satellite-derived flood extents; found DEM resolution dominates output and coarser inputs lower velocities and widen top-widths.
  access: journal (Wiley), CC BY-NC-ND | auth: none | licence: CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | cadence: static | NC: Buncombe County NC, Helene 2024
  use (post): Note only: supports using the highest-resolution NC lidar DEM for any depth estimation and confirms a NASA-JPL Helene flood extent product existed for validation.
  caveats: No HWM/gauge validation; AutoRoute is not open source in the same way as OWP FIM.
- (high/high) **USGS and NC Geological Survey Hurricane Helene landslide inventories** (USGS Landslide Hazards Program (LASER) and NC Geological Survey (NCDEQ DEMLR)) https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/item/674634a1d34e6d1dac3abddc
  USGS preliminary point inventory (DOI 10.5066/P14CHGKS): 2,217 landslides (1,155 by Oct 2024) across western NC, TN, VA, GA, SC mapped 28 Sept-23 Oct 2024 from Sentinel-2 NDVI change (10 m), PlanetScope, CAP/NOAA/USGS aerial reconnaissance, media and DOT reports; positional accuracy 'tens of meters'; GeoJSON/CSV/ZIP + WMS + ArcGIS FeatureServer (fields Source, Impact). NCGS has since mapped >2,800 landslide outlines using higher-resolution data, lidar and field visits, served as NC_Landslide_Inventory FeatureServer (Observations, Ground Ruptures, Landslide Features) with the earlier Post-Helene service deprecated (offline 2027); NCGS also serves a Channelized Debris Flow Model MapServer. USGS OFR 2025-1028 field report covers nine NC counties (Avery, Buncombe, Henderson, McDowell, Mitchell, Polk, Rutherford, Watauga, Yancey).
  access: ScienceBase download, WMS, ArcGIS FeatureServer (GeoJSON/PBF); NCDEQ ArcGIS services | auth: none | licence: USGS inventory CC0 1.0 / public domain; NCGS layers under NC DEQ custom disclaimer (free access, liability disclaimer) | cadence: USGS static (Jan 2025); NCGS ongoing | NC: Western NC, Helene 2024 (ongoing NCGS updates)
  use (post,pre): Adopt as a ground-truth and evidence layer: structures intersecting landslide points/outlines or debris-flow runout get a landslide hazard flag and elevated Destroyed prior; use the NCGS debris-flow susceptibility model pre-event for exposure; cite USGS NDVI-change method as the template for an NC Recon disturbance channel.
  caveats: USGS points under-represent canopy-hidden slides; NCGS schema 'incomplete' and changing; old service deprecated.
- (medium/medium) **Repeat (pre/post-Helene) airborne lidar change detection, Hickory Nut Gorge** (Scheip, Lang, Prince, Wegmann, Wooten et al. (NC State / NCGS), Earth Surface Processes and Landforms 2026) https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/pdfdirect/10.1002/esp.70228
  Uses repeat airborne lidar collected before and after Helene over Hickory Nut Gorge NC to document landslide reactivation, debris-flow coarse-sediment delivery, channel geometry and roughness change, and argues pre- and post-event lidar are both required because signatures fade with revegetation and repairs. Confirms post-Helene lidar exists for at least part of western NC; collector, date and point density were not stated in the abstract. No post-Helene lidar project was found in USGS 3DEP/TNM API searches as of 2026-10-07.
  access: journal (bronze OA PDF) | auth: none | licence: unstated (bronze OA) | cadence: per event | NC: Hickory Nut Gorge (Rutherford/Henderson Cos.), Helene 2024
  use (post): Evaluate a lidar DEM-of-difference channel (NC QL2 2017-2020 baseline vs post-event lidar) for debris-flow and channel-change detection and to flag structures within elevation-change polygons; depends on obtaining the post-Helene lidar (source unconfirmed, likely NCEM/NCGS).
  caveats: Post-event lidar distribution point not found (NC OneMap elevation page and DCAT feed returned no 2024/2025 lidar).
- (high/high) **FEMA 2025 Preliminary Damage Assessment Guide (effective 1 July 2025)** (FEMA Recovery Directorate) https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_rd_pda-guide_07012025.pdf
  Standard framework for IA/PA PDAs. Defines four IA damage levels: Affected (non-structural, safe to occupy); Minor (repairable non-structural or flood waterline below electrical outlets in essential living space; in floor system of a manufactured home); Major (structural damage or waterline at/above outlets; enters living space of manufactured home); Destroyed (total loss). Section 'Virtual Sensing' lists imagery specs: orthorectified satellite 0.41 m (20-inch) if possible, airborne 10-inch or better at nadir, 6-inch oblique for best results, SAR/LiDAR for flood extents and change detection; 'Advanced analytics could be used to automatically identify and categorize damaged structures from imagery.' Appendix E: Response Geospatial Office Geospatial Damage Assessment (GDA) tool; FEMA cannot fly drones but 'will accept imagery and damage information collected through a drone if the SLTT government submits it as part of the IDA'; 'An SLTT government may use Artificial Intelligence (AI) and machine learning... FEMA information developed through AI is an acceptable methodology for collecting and assessing damage information.' Virtual validation and desktop assessments allowed; required fields include address/GPS, degree of damage, peril, insurance, home type, owner/renter, primary residence; Hazus and NHC flood modeling cited as modeling inputs.
  access: PDF download (fema.gov/PDA); pocket guide PDF | auth: none | licence: US Gov public domain | cadence: annual-ish revision | NC: Applies to all states; NC Helene PDAs predate this version (operational draft June 2024-June 2025)
  use (pre,during,post): Adopt verbatim: (1) map NC Recon levels to these definitions and encode the flood waterline-vs-outlets rule in the depth channel; (2) make the export carry the required IDA fields so AI-derived levels can enter the state's Initial Damage Assessment; (3) record imagery GSD against the 0.41 m / 10-inch / 6-inch thresholds as a quality flag; (4) support drone imagery ingest since FEMA accepts SLTT-submitted drone data.
  caveats: Virtual/desktop assessment still requires FEMA validation; 'water line and other ground-based information is crucial' after floods, limiting pure remote calls for Minor vs Major.
- (high/medium) **Uncertainty calibration and abstention in EO/disaster ML (conformal prediction, MC dropout, segmentation UQ)** (Singh et al. 2024 (Scientific Reports); Rey et al. 2025 (Google DeepMind, arXiv); Bin et al. 2022 (Sensors)) https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-65954-w
  Singh et al.: survey found only 22.5% of EO datasets carry uncertainty; introduces conformal prediction giving statistically valid prediction sets for any model, with Google Earth Engine modules (CC BY). Rey et al.: benchmarks stochastic segmentation networks, ensembles and metrics on PASTIS/ForTy for their ability to flag prediction errors and corrupted inputs, with practical recommendations (CC BY 4.0). Bin et al.: pre/post aerial fusion with Fourier attention + Monte Carlo dropout; when uncertainty exceeds a threshold the case is routed to human experts (CC BY). Operational practice from Microsoft Rolling Fork and CRASAR deployments: all outputs manually inspected before delivery.
  access: open-access papers | auth: none | licence: CC BY (all three) | cadence: static | NC: n/a
  use (post): Adopt split-conformal calibration on NC Recon's fused per-building probabilities using analyst labels as the calibration set (report prediction sets, e.g., {Minor, Major}); route any set with >1 class or containing Destroyed to the review queue; log coverage per event; add MC-dropout/ensemble variance from the xView2 ONNX ensemble as an abstention signal.
  caveats: No paper applies conformal prediction specifically to xBD-style damage levels (arXiv and OpenAlex searches returned none); calibration sets must come from the same event/imagery to be valid.
- (medium/medium) **Published accuracy expectations and transferability (Nex et al. 2020; 2024 decadal review; operational numbers)** (Nex, Duarte, Tonolo, Kerle (Remote Sensing 2020); Buildings 2024 bibliometric review (370 papers)) https://www.mdpi.com/2072-4292/12/17/2839/pdf
  Nex et al.: CNN on 175,289 xBD buildings across 13 disasters; performance 'differed highly between occurrences of disasters' but reached high AUC on unseen disasters, not correlated with hazard type, region or satellite parameters. 2024 review: gaps are data fusion, real-time processing, generalization, UAV improvements and responder training. Operational benchmarks gathered in this session: satellite per-event fine-tune P 0.86/R 0.80 (Microsoft Rolling Fork); SKAI 70-88% precision, >81% accuracy Turkiye, 85-98% agreement claim; xBD baseline weighted F1 0.27 vs top models ~0.66-0.80 class; BRIGHT ~10-pt mIoU drop on unseen events; aerial 0.3-0.5 m five-class macro F1 0.62 (DamageTriage); sUAS five-class macro F1 0.55 (CRASAR). No peer-reviewed paper directly comparing aerial vs satellite accuracy against FEMA ground PDAs for the same hurricane was found.
  access: open-access papers | auth: none | licence: CC BY (MDPI) | cadence: static | NC: n/a
  use (post): Adopt as published expectation ranges in NC Recon's Help/brief: binary damaged/undamaged ~0.8-0.9 precision is achievable; four/five-level classification macro F1 ~0.55-0.65 with middle classes weakest; expect a further ~10-point drop on a new event until per-event labels are added.
  caveats: Numbers come from different events, label schemas and test splits; not directly comparable.
- (medium/high) **GPU vs CPU inference cost reference (AWS on-demand, us-east-1)** (AWS pricing as listed by Vantage instances.vantage.sh; xView2-deploy README) https://instances.vantage.sh/aws/ec2/g4dn.xlarge
  g4dn.xlarge (1x NVIDIA T4 16 GiB, 4 vCPU, 16 GiB): $0.526/h on-demand, $0.288/h spot. c6i.2xlarge (8 vCPU, 16 GiB, no GPU): $0.34/h on-demand, $0.159/h spot. xView2-deploy states CPU inference of the first-place ensemble is 'wildly impractical' and recommends GPU instances (P3/P4d). SAM/samgeo recommend >=8 GB VRAM. Prithvi/Clay/DOFA are ViT models that also expect GPUs for fine-tuning.
  access: public pricing pages | auth: none | licence: n/a | cadence: prices change | NC: n/a
  use (post): Keep CPU ONNX inference for the single-model path (NC Recon's current design) and document a burst option: a T4 at ~$0.53/h is ~1.5x the cost of an 8-vCPU CPU box, so an optional GPU container profile for the 4-backbone ensemble and SAM is cheap per event; measure buildings/hour on both and publish in OPERATIONS.md.
  caveats: No published buildings-per-hour throughput for xView2 models on CPU vs GPU was found; prices are third-party listings of AWS rates.
- (high/high) **Hurricane Helene open imagery for model validation: Maxar/Vantor Open Data and NOAA NGS ERI** (Vantor (formerly Maxar) Open Data Program; NOAA National Geodetic Survey) https://maxar-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/events/HurricaneHelene-Oct24/collection.json
  Maxar STAC collection HurricaneHelene-Oct24: 44 ARD acquisitions, bbox ~85.89W-81.56W / 29.09N-38.35N, temporal 2019-11-09 to 2024-10-06, licence CC-BY-NC-4.0 (program now at vantor-opendata STAC catalog). NOAA NGS Helene ERI: 15-30 cm GSD aerial mosaics (zoom 19-20) combined with NAIP, Sentinel and CAP imagery, 'for informational and planning purposes only... no accuracy or completeness guarantee'. These are the imagery sources behind the only labeled Helene aerial dataset (DamageTriage-Bench).
  access: STAC/S3 (Maxar); NOAA storms site tiles/downloads | auth: none | licence: Maxar CC-BY-NC-4.0; NOAA US Gov public domain (mixed attributions in mosaic) | cadence: per event | NC: Both include western NC Helene coverage (Oct 2024)
  use (post): Already integrated; use them to build an NC-specific labeled validation set (analyst labels + FEMA/NCEM PDA results) and to publish NC Recon's first confusion matrix; respect Maxar NC licence in any derived public products.
  caveats: Maxar non-commercial; NOAA mosaic mixes sources and dates; positional accuracy not guaranteed.
### insights
- FEMA's 2025 PDA Guide (effective 2025-07-01) explicitly states that information developed through AI/ML by an SLTT government is an acceptable methodology for the Initial Damage Assessment, that FEMA will accept drone imagery submitted by the SLTT, and that virtual sensing/desktop assessments are allowed; it also gives target imagery specs (0.41 m satellite, 10-inch nadir airborne, 6-inch oblique). Source: https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_rd_pda-guide_07012025.pdf
- FEMA IA damage levels hinge on flood waterline relative to electrical outlets in essential living spaces (Minor below, Major at/above), so a per-structure depth estimate with ~0.2-0.3 m error (FwDET-class) is the single most decision-relevant quantity NC Recon can add; the PDA Guide notes water-line and ground information remain 'crucial' after floods. Sources: FEMA PDA Guide; https://nhess.copernicus.org/articles/19/2053/2019/
- Every operational system found (Microsoft Rolling Fork/Turkey, Google SKAI/WFP, Microsoft HASTE, CRASAR sUAS at Debby/Helene) relies on per-event human labeling plus fine-tuning or few-shot classifiers and manual inspection before release; none deploys a frozen xBD model alone. Sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.12589, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/google-research/skai/main/docs/assessment_instructions.md, https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11838, https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.03132
- Realistic accuracy expectations: binary damaged/undamaged precision 0.8-0.9 (Microsoft P 0.86/R 0.80; SKAI 70-88% precision); multi-level macro F1 0.55-0.65 (DamageTriage 0.619 on 0.3-0.5 m NOAA ERI; CRASAR Attention U-Net 0.554 on sUAS); middle classes (minor/major, roof damage) are weakest (xBD baseline major F1 0.0094; DamageTriage total-roof F1 0.326); unseen events cost ~10 mIoU points (BRIGHT). Sources: arXiv 2606.12248, 2511.03132, 1911.09296, ESSD 17:6217 (2025)
- Models trained on 30-50 cm satellite imagery should not be applied to 2-5 cm sUAS imagery: label distributions differ and the sUAS papers report at least 39.1% performance degradation from footprint misalignment, with 36% of CRASAR polygons needing translation adjustments. NC Recon's existing outline-to-roof alignment is therefore essential and should be exposed/QA'd. Sources: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.03132, https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.17673
- Practitioners at Debby/Helene deployments were 'less interested in model accuracy than anticipated' and focused on output formats compatible with ArcGIS (GeoJSON, CSV) and on connectivity; NC Recon's existing exports fit, but an offline/edge inference path is needed. Source: https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.03132
- Microsoft's Rolling Fork workflow fine-tuned 'a U-Net with an ImageNet pre-trained ResNet-50 backbone' end-to-end in under 2 hours per scene, and HASTE reports foundation-model embeddings + logistic regression matching a supervised ResNet-50 with one-twentieth of the labels on xBD, used in 30+ responses since 2023; this is the design pattern for NC Recon's review-queue learning loop. Sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.12589, https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11838
- Licences: xBD is CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0 (per TorchGeo), DamageTriage-Bench CC BY-NC 4.0, Maxar/Vantor Open Data CC-BY-NC-4.0; first-place xView2 code/weights MIT, Microsoft toolkit/HASTE MIT, SKAI Apache-2.0, CRASAR-U-DROIDs and RescueNet CC-BY-4.0, BRIGHT CC BY 4.0 with NC components, USGS Helene inventory CC0. Government use is non-commercial, but any paid/commercial redistribution of models trained on NC-licensed data needs review. Sources: https://docs.torchgeo.org/en/stable/api/datasets.html, https://huggingface.co/datasets/Ymx1025/DamageTriage-Bench, https://maxar-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/events/HurricaneHelene-Oct24/collection.json
- Only one labeled Hurricane Helene imagery dataset was found (DamageTriage-Bench: NOAA ERI tiles for Michael, Helene, LA fires; 7,472 tiles, 87,017 polygon fragments, five typology classes); no Helene-labeled satellite (xBD-style) dataset and no Microsoft, Google/SKAI or Planet Helene building-damage case study pages were located. Sources: https://huggingface.co/datasets/Ymx1025/DamageTriage-Bench, https://arxiv.org/search/?query=Hurricane+Helene+building+damage&searchtype=all
- USGS mapped 2,217 Helene landslides mainly from Sentinel-2 NDVI change (10 m) validated with PlanetScope and aerial reconnaissance, with 'tens of meters' positional accuracy; NCGS has since mapped >2,800 outlines using higher-resolution data, lidar and field visits and serves them via ArcGIS FeatureServer under an NC DEQ disclaimer licence. This is both a ground-truth layer and a template for an OPERA DIST-ALERT-based disturbance channel. Sources: https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/item/674634a1d34e6d1dac3abddc?format=json, https://www.usgs.gov/programs/landslide-hazards/science/2024-hurricane-helene-landslide-hazards
- Geospatial foundation models (Prithvi-EO-2.0, Clay v1.5, SatlasPretrain, DOFA, Copernicus-FM) have no published building-damage fine-tunes; their proven disaster uses are flood extent (Sen1Floods11: water IoU ~80%) and landslide segmentation at 10-30 m. SatlasPretrain's NAIP 0.5-2 m backbone (ODC-BY weights) is the most relevant encoder for NC aerial orthos. Sources: Hugging Face/GitHub model cards fetched
- OPERA products confirmed available with free Earthdata login: DIST-ALERT-HLS (30 m, since 2023-01-01, LPCLOUD, COG), DSWx-HLS (POCLOUD, validated from Apr 2023) and DSWx-S1 (Sentinel-1, operational Sept 2024, water bodies >3 ha and >200 m wide, 6-12 day revisit). DIST-ALERT-S1 is not present in CMR as of 2026-10-07. Sources: CMR collection JSON fetched
- Compute: a T4 GPU instance (g4dn.xlarge, $0.526/h on-demand, $0.288 spot) costs about 1.5x an 8-vCPU CPU instance (c6i.2xlarge, $0.34/h); xView2-deploy calls CPU inference of the winning ensemble 'wildly impractical', so NC Recon should keep its single-model CPU ONNX path and offer an optional GPU profile for ensembles/SAM. Sources: https://instances.vantage.sh/aws/ec2/g4dn.xlarge, https://github.com/DIUx-xView/xView2-deploy
- SAR damage proxy maps (ARIA coherence/intensity change) are validated only qualitatively ('largely in agreement' with aerial/optical) and BRIGHT shows single-polarization SAR cannot reliably separate partially damaged buildings; treat SAR as a triage prior, not a per-building classifier. Sources: Tay et al. 2020 (Europe PMC record), ESSD 17:6217
- Conformal prediction gives model-agnostic, statistically valid prediction sets and has GEE-native implementations, but no published application to xBD-style damage levels exists; MC-dropout thresholding with routing to human experts (Bin et al. 2022) is the closest disaster-specific precedent. Sources: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-024-65954-w, https://doi.org/10.3390/s22197167
- Two-tier legacy of USGS NC flood mapping (Matthew 2016: 267 HWMs + NCEM 353, six inundation maps with water-depth rasters; Tar River 2007 lidar FIM libraries) shows the HWM + lidar DEM -> water surface -> depth raster method is already accepted practice in NC; Hazus 7.0 (June 2025) accepts user-defined depth grids and provides FIA/USACE depth-damage curves by occupancy/stories/foundation. Sources: https://pubs.usgs.gov/publication/ofr20171047, https://fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_rsl_hazus-7-fltm_06272025_0.pdf
### gaps
- xView2 final leaderboard scores for the first-place solution (overall/localization/damage F1) could not be confirmed: xview2.org pages are JS-only and the winner's README publishes no numbers; only the leaderboard baseline (0.28408 / 0.80482 / 0.06091) was confirmed via DeepDamageNet.
- No NASA ARIA damage proxy map or flood proxy map for Hurricane Helene could be located: aria-share.jpl.nasa.gov renders only a header, aria.jpl.nasa.gov did not resolve, the NASA Disasters Mapping Portal REST endpoints and the Helene storymap returned 404, and ArcGIS Online searches for ARIA/OPERA Helene items returned 0 (only a NASA Black Marble ZCTA power-loss FeatureServer for Helene was found). The JAWRA 2026 paper's use of 'NASA-JPL satellite-derived flood extents' implies a product exists.
- No Microsoft AI for Good Hurricane Helene building damage assessment publication or dataset was found (toolkit README lists Turkey, Rolling Fork, Maui, Libya, Beryl only); no Google SKAI use by FEMA or for Helene was found on the WFP or GitHub pages; no Planet Helene building-damage case study was fetched.
- OPERA DIST-ALERT-S1 (Sentinel-1 disturbance) is not in NASA CMR under any tried name as of 2026-10-07; status unknown.
- Post-Helene lidar over western NC: confirmed to exist via the ESP&L 2026 Hickory Nut Gorge paper, but no distribution point found (USGS TNM API returned no post-2024 LPC projects for the area; NC OneMap elevation page and DCAT feed returned nothing). Collector, dates, QL and licence unknown.
- No peer-reviewed US hurricane blue-tarp detection paper with reported precision/recall was found (arXiv, OpenAlex, Crossref searches); closest are Miura et al. 2020 (Japan, aerial, ~95%) and Wolf and Takeuchi 2026 (Irma tarps from photographs; openICPSR data returned 403).
- No paper applying conformal prediction or formal abstention to building-damage-level classification (xBD-style) was found; only general EO conformal/segmentation-UQ work and one MC-dropout routing paper.
- No peer-reviewed head-to-head comparison of aerial vs satellite damage classification accuracy against FEMA ground PDAs for the same hurricane was found; Manzini et al. 2025c (drone vs satellite label agreement) is cited in the sUAS paper but was not retrievable via arXiv search.
- No USGS Helene flood-inundation/depth raster data release was found on ScienceBase (query returned 0), unlike Matthew 2016.
- Google SKAI model internals (two-tower architecture, GSD requirements, SigLIP zero-shot mode) and HASTE's foundation model were not fully documented in fetched pages.
- MDPI article pages (Nex et al. 2020; Buildings 2024 review; Miura 2020) returned 403; details came from Semantic Scholar/Crossref metadata only, so numeric per-scenario accuracies from Nex et al. are unknown.
- Microsoft Turkey earthquake report accuracy/validation figures and Maui report were not fetched; only the published count (3,849 damaged buildings across four cities) was confirmed.
- WebSearch budget was exhausted before this subtask began; discovery relied on direct URLs and site-native APIs (arXiv, OpenAlex, Crossref, Semantic Scholar, CMR, ScienceBase, ArcGIS Online), so niche items (e.g., Copernicus EMS Helene activation, Planet case studies) may have been missed.

## UI and UX for emergency operations centres and field damage assessment, and what comparable tools look like
- (high/high) **FEMA Preliminary Damage Assessment Guide (July 1, 2025) and PDA Pocket Guide** (FEMA Recovery Directorate) https://www.fema.gov/disaster/how-declared/preliminary-damage-assessments/guide
  Doctrine for local Initial Damage Assessments and joint PDAs. Defines the residential degree-of-damage scale NC Recon already uses (Affected, Minor, Major, Destroyed) plus an 'Inaccessible' state, with flood waterline criteria in Appendix H matrices (e.g. Destroyed = waterline at/above ceiling or failure of two or more structural components; Major = waterline at/above electrical outlets in an essential living space; Inaccessible = damage cannot be visually validated because roads/bridges are blocked). Chapter 3 names 'Virtual Sensing' (aerial, satellite, lidar, SAR; 'advanced analytics could be used to automatically identify and categorize damaged structures from imagery'), GIS, modeling (FEMA POST scores 1 km / 5 km US National Grid cells by residential parcels) and 'Desktop Assessments' where FEMA validates virtually from photos, annotated maps and GIS data the state submits. Defines a state 'Geospatial and Data Analysis Lead' role and says IDA datasets can be uploaded as Excel or geodatabase.
  access: portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: US Government work (public domain) | cadence: static; 2025 edition supersedes 2020/2021 guides for incidents on/after 2025-07-01 | NC: Applies to every NC declaration with an incident period from 2025-07-01 onward
  use (post): Authoritative schema: keep the five categories but add an explicit 'Inaccessible' state for structures with no usable imagery/access; encode waterline thresholds as the flood-depth evidence rule; export a 'desktop PDA package' (annotated map, photos, USNG, Excel + GeoPackage/FileGDB) matching what FEMA accepts; aggregate counts by 1 km USNG cell like POST.
  caveats: PDF text extracted locally with pypdf; the guide does not name Survey123 or a FEMA data standard; it says ICS/PDA data may be submitted from state GIS as Excel or geodatabase.
- (high/high) **ICS 209 Incident Status Summary (NIMS ICS Forms Booklet v3)** (FEMA Emergency Management Institute (ICS Resource Center)) https://training.fema.gov/icsresource/icsforms.aspx
  The standard situation-report form used across ICS/EOCs. Key blocks: 3 Report Version (Initial/Update/Final, Rpt #); 6 Incident Start Date/Time with time zone; 11 'For Time Period From/To'; 12-14 Prepared/Submitted/Approved with date/time; 22 lat/long; 23 'US National Grid Reference'; 26 UTM; 27 'Note any electronic geospatial data included or attached (indicate data format, content, and collection time information and labels)'; 28 Significant Events; 30 Damage Assessment Information with table A Structural Summary, B # Threatened (72 hrs), C # Damaged, D # Destroyed by rows E Single Residences, F Nonresidential Commercial Property, Other Minor Structures, Other, with instruction to note whether numbers are estimates or confirmed; 31 Public Status Summary (fatalities, injured, trapped, missing, evacuated, sheltering in place, in temporary shelters); 36/38/39 projections at 12/24/48/72 hours.
  access: portal download | auth: none | licence: US Government work (public domain) | cadence: static (v3) | NC: Used statewide by NCEM/SERT and counties operating under NIMS
  use (during,post): Make the one-page brief and CSV export emit an ICS 209 block-30-compatible table (Damaged/Destroyed by Single Residences vs Nonresidential, plus an estimate-vs-confirmed flag), carry the operational period From/To and 'as of' timestamp, include a USNG reference and a block-27 style data-attachment note (format, content, collection time). Decide and document the mapping from FEMA's five levels to ICS 209's Damaged/Destroyed.
  caveats: ICS 209 collapses damage to Damaged/Destroyed/Threatened; the FEMA five-level scale does not map one-to-one.
- (high/high) **NIMS doctrine, 3rd edition (October 2017)** (FEMA) https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/2020-07/fema_nims_doctrine-2017.pdf
  Defines ICS Command and General Staff and the Planning Section's Situation Unit: it collects, processes and organizes situation information, prepares situation summaries and projections, produces SITREPs on schedule or on request of the Planning Section Chief/IC, and 'frequently includes Geographic/Geospatial Information Systems (GIS) Specialists, who produce maps' plus Field Observers. Describes three EOC structures: ICS-like, Incident Support Model (separates a Situational Awareness Section from planning and puts the EOC director in direct contact with information management) and Departmental. Operational periods are 'typically 12 to 24 hours'; the Planning P repeats each period.
  access: portal download | auth: none | licence: US Government work (public domain) | cadence: static | NC: NC EOP and SERT operate under NIMS
  use (pre,during,post): Role model for views: the Situation Unit / GIS Unit analyst is the primary editor; EOC director and Planning Section Chief get read-only summaries keyed to the operational period; PIO gets the suppressed public summary; logistics gets displaced-population and access layers. Time every product to a 12- or 24-hour operational period and show 'as of'.
  caveats: Extracted locally with pypdf; the phrase 'common operating picture' did not match in the extracted text, so quote NIMS for roles and periods, not for a COP definition.
- (high/high) **FEMA Community Lifelines Implementation Toolkit 2.1 (July 2023) and Lifelines Toolkit page** (FEMA) https://www.fema.gov/emergency-managers/practitioners/lifelines-toolkit
  Eight lifelines (Safety and Security; Food, Hydration, Shelter; Health and Medical; Energy; Communications; Transportation; Hazardous Materials; Water Systems). Condition colours, verbatim semantics: Grey = Unknown ('extent of disruption and impacts to lifeline services is unknown'); Red = Significant Impact (immediate attention required); Yellow = Moderate Impact (disruptions, restoration in progress, includes temporary means); Green = Minimal Impact (functioning at pre-incident levels); Blue = Administrative only. 'A color designation represents a snapshot in time for that response operational period.' Reporting products: Tier I and Tier II Senior Leadership Brief templates (PPTX), Lifelines card template, icons collection; FEMA Situation Reporting Construct frames reports as 'What? So What? Now What? And When?'. Toolkit page is published in 7 languages.
  access: portal download (PDF, PPTX, DOCX, XLSX) | auth: none | licence: US Government work (public domain) | cadence: static; update expected after Jan 2025 per lifelines page | NC: National doctrine; NCEM SERT uses lifelines in briefings (not independently verified)
  use (during,post): Use the exact lifeline colour semantics on the Conditions map and in the brief (grey for unknown/no coverage, not green); structure the one-page brief as What / So What / Now What / When with an operational-period 'as of' stamp; offer a PPTX/PDF export laid out like the Tier I SLB.
  caveats: Toolkit PDF extracted locally; SLB slide layout details not extracted.
- (medium/medium) **NIMS Emergency Operations Center How-To Quick Reference Guide (October 2022) and EOC Skillsets** (FEMA) https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_eoc-quick-reference_guide.pdf
  Guidance for building and running EOCs: EOCs may be physical, virtual or hybrid; 'Display capacity is an important part of a modern EOC'; many incident-management programs now push status information to individual users, 'which potentially reduces the need for elaborate display systems'; audiovisual systems should support hybrid staff and closed captioning / sign-language interpreters. Companion resources on the NIMS components page: EOC Skillsets (RTLT), EOC Toolkit (zip), Incident Action Planning Process, Senior Leaders Toolkit.
  access: portal download | auth: none | licence: US Government work (public domain) | cadence: static | NC: Applies to NC State EOC and county EOCs
  use (pre,during): Design for three surfaces at once: wall display (unattended, large type), individual analyst desktop, and remote/hybrid access over HTTPS with SSO; add captions/alt text to any auto-playing imagery.
  caveats: No explicit shift-handover or COP-display section surfaced in the extracted text.
- (high/high) **FEMA Geospatial Resource Center: Hurricane Helene incident page and Geospatial Damage Assessment (GDA) application** (FEMA Response Geospatial Office (RGO)) https://gis-fema.hub.arcgis.com/pages/incident-page-hurricane-helene-2024
  FEMA's own imagery-based damage assessment product: 'Trained analysts use the tool to compare pre- and post-incident aerial imagery collected from authoritative sources to indicate damages at the structure level', shown in a live-updating dashboard during activations. GDA dashboard (fema.maps.arcgis.com item 0ced24e8fda84bf293c98fa8f4f32c0f) is restricted to FEMA and Incident Coordination Group members (REST returns 403); emergency partners request access via FEMA-RGO@fema.dhs.gov. Public companions: Hazus impact dashboard, NIST windfield data, USACE flood inundation dashboard; imagery sources listed: NOAA NGS, Civil Air Patrol (oblique/nadir/ground), USGS HDDS (Earth Explorer login), USGS Collection Management Tool.
  access: ArcGIS Hub page (public); GDA layers restricted | auth: GDA: FEMA/ICG membership or email request (MOU-type); companions: none | licence: US Government; GDA sharing terms unstated | cadence: per event; live during activation | NC: Helene 2024 (DR-4827) was an active GDA incident
  use (post): NC Recon is the open analogue of GDA. Align the output schema (structure-level, FEMA damage levels, imagery source and date per call) so state/county results can be handed to RGO, and request GDA Helene results through FEMA-RGO@fema.dhs.gov as a validation set. Mirror the 'live dashboard during activation' pattern.
  caveats: Hub page is client-rendered; content read from the ArcGIS item data JSON. GDA category scheme and accuracy are not public. The hub DCAT feed (24 datasets) lists no Helene or damage-assessment datasets.
- (medium/high) **FEMA PDA Report, North Carolina Tropical Storm Helene, FEMA-4827-DR (expedited)** (FEMA) https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/PDAReport_FEMA4827DRexpedited-NC.pdf
  Two-page report: Governor requested an expedited declaration 2024-09-27; declared 2024-09-28; joint PDA requirement waived under 44 CFR 206.33(d)/206.36(d) because severity made field PDAs unnecessary. All IA residential counts (Destroyed, Major, Minor, Affected) and PA estimates are blank ('-'/'N/A'). Footnote 3 gives the FEMA degree-of-damage definitions (Major = substantial structural failure or >30 days to repair; Minor = uninhabitable but habitable with short repairs; Affected = some damage, still habitable). Initial IA counties: Alexander, Alleghany, Ashe, Avery, Buncombe, Burke, Caldwell, Catawba, Clay, Cleveland, Gaston, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, Lincoln, Macon, Madison, McDowell, Mitchell, Polk, Rutherford, Transylvania, Watauga, Wilkes, Yancey and the Eastern Band of Cherokee Indians.
  access: portal download | auth: none | licence: US Government work (public domain) | cadence: per declaration | NC: DR-4827-NC
  use (post): Evidence that for catastrophic NC events there is no official structure-level PDA count to validate against; ground truth must come from FEMA IA inspections, SAR observations or county data. Use the footnote definitions as the short glossary in the UI.
  caveats: Counts blank by design (expedited).
- (high/high) **North Carolina SERT Hurricane Helene After-Action Review (McChrystal Group, 2025)** (NC Emergency Management (NCDPS), prepared by McChrystal Group) https://www.ncdps.gov/division/emergency-management/ts-helene-after-action-review/open
  Third-party AAR of preparedness and response: 65 interviews and 100+ surveys (85% response), calibration workshop 2025-02-03. Three critical areas: SERT organization and staffing (could not sustain 24-hour operations; ICS and WebEOC proficiency gaps at state and county level); interoperability, communications and data; logistics. Data findings: 'absence of a centralized database that provides a common operating picture (COP) for all SERT partners'; operation-critical information spread across WebEOC, NCSPARTA (NC State Preparedness and Resource Tracking Application), Microsoft Teams channels, situation report templates, iCAM, SharePoint, Adobe Connect; agencies failed to track and close resource requests in WebEOC; external partners on guest Wi-Fi in the SEOC; LEO satellite internet 'a home run' but 'It took days to place [alternate] communications resources (Starlinks and satellite phones) into the impacted counties', which 'kept NCEM in the dark about ground truth'; VIPER radio overload ('bonking'). Survey asked staff to rate day/night shift turnover and to name most/least valuable tools (WebEOC, GIS mapping, Teams, iCAM, SharePoint, Adobe Connect, Linktree). Top workshop actions include 'Create a unified database for information sharing and coordination across all SERT partners' and permanent ESF liaisons at RCC West; NCDIT suggested as integration partner.
  access: portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state publication; licence unstated | cadence: static | NC: NC statewide; western NC Helene response
  use (pre,during,post): Design constraints straight from NC's own lessons: NC Recon must feed the eventual unified SERT COP rather than be another silo (export to WebEOC/NCSPARTA/ArcGIS, API with bearer token or SSO); run usefully over a single Starlink link and offline; support explicit shift handover (what changed since last period, who reviewed what); keep training to minutes (NC found WebEOC proficiency gaps); surface 'ground truth' from counties quickly.
  caveats: Report is anonymized and generalized; survey result charts are images and were not extracted; no explicit damage-assessment-UI findings.
- (high/high) **NC OSBM Hurricane Helene Revised Damage and Needs Assessment (December 13, 2024)** (NC Office of State Budget and Management) https://www.osbm.nc.gov/hurricane-helene-dna/open
  Statewide damage estimate $59.6B. Housing: 'approximately 73,700 homes are expected to be damaged' in the October method; later 'between 121,000 and 132,000 homes' based on IA claims; 274,900 households expected to apply for IA. Methodology combines a top-down estimate from FEMA IA applications/inspections (projected verified damage 155% of Florence) with a bottom-up estimate from Search and Rescue structure observations collected by FEMA and NCEM through 2024-11-19 that 'categorize properties they observe as destroyed, having major or minor damage, affected, or unaffected based on criteria established by FEMA', supplemented by NCEM flood-inundation modeling, NCDEQ/USGS landslide data and county parcel data from NC OneMap; notes SAR teams categorized only part of the structures. Primary data sources list NCEM (SAR structure damage, inundation, power outages), NCDEQ landslides, NCDOT closures, NC OneMap parcels, FEMA IA statistics and building-footprint occupancy types.
  access: portal download (PDF) | auth: none | licence: NC state publication; licence unstated | cadence: two editions (Oct 23 and Dec 13, 2024) | NC: All Helene-declared NC counties
  use (post): Replicates NC Recon's own fusion idea at state scale and names the ground-truth sets that exist for Helene (SAR structure observations, FEMA IA verified damage) which should be requested from NCEM/FEMA for validation; adopt its parcel-join and 'confirmed vs likely' distinction in exports; the 73,700 vs 121,000-132,000 spread shows why uncertainty must be displayed, not hidden.
  caveats: SAR structure observations are not published; OSBM says they cover only a subset of structures.
- (medium/high) **NCDPS Tropical Storm Helene page and NCEM 'Helene Retrospective' ArcGIS Experience** (NC Department of Public Safety / NC Emergency Management) https://www.ncdps.gov/our-organization/emergency-management/tropical-storm-helene
  NCEM's public Helene page links a 'Helene Retrospective' ArcGIS Experience Builder app (item 7892f669bb0e442981b15009c7f2b5f0, owner 'NCWebEOC', public, created January 2025, 'illustrate the impacts of the storm and the immediate response by the North Carolina State Emergency Response Team'), FIMAN, a Survey123 intake form for the SMART private-property debris program, the Private Road and Bridge program and Disaster Case Management; Spanish appears only for the Disaster Distress Helpline. The NCEM main page links ReadyNC, Know Your Zone, County EM directory, Annual Reports and a DPS 'Recursos en español' page; no public situation reports or damage-assessment guidance for counties are linked.
  access: web pages; ArcGIS Online public items | auth: none | licence: NC state content; ArcGIS items unstated | cadence: page updated through 2026 | NC: NC statewide
  use (during,post): NCEM publishes with Esri Experience Builder/Dashboards from an 'NCWebEOC' ArcGIS org, so NC Recon outputs should be directly consumable there (GeoJSON, FileGDB, optionally a hosted feature service) and visually consistent with Esri conventions; Spanish public content is thin, so a Spanish public summary fills a gap.
  caveats: State EOC activation-levels page returned 403 to the fetcher; its content known only from a search snippet (SERT Activation defined for multi-jurisdiction, multi-operational-period events).
- (high/high) **Esri ArcGIS Solutions: Damage Assessment (v6.0, Nov 2025)** (Esri) https://doc.arcgis.com/en/arcgis-solutions/latest/reference/introduction-to-damage-assessment.htm
  The de facto commercial field-assessment stack for US counties. Components: Survey123 forms 'Public Damage Report', 'Individual Assistance Survey' (dwelling type, owner/renter, insurance, address, structure details, damage level, water depth, photos; assignments via inbox), 'Public Assistance Survey' and 'PA Damage Inventory'; QuickCapture 'Windshield Damage Report' (QR-code project, big buttons for IA and PA damage categories, photo, comment); Experience Builder 'Damage Assessment Operations' with a Manage tab (review submitted/assigned/completed, assign personnel, move points, set 'Public View' on photos) and Monitor tab dashboards (counts by damage level, dwelling type, insurance, completion, filtered by incident); ArcGIS Hub site; Photo Viewer; Archive notebook that moves closed incidents to history layers. v6.0 adds alignment to 'FEMA 4.1.3 Public Assistance data standards', Spanish-language surveys and incident-name filtering. Requires ArcGIS Online, Survey123, Survey123 Connect, QuickCapture; Mobile Worker and Creator user types.
  access: ArcGIS Solutions deployment into an ArcGIS Online organization | auth: paid ArcGIS Online subscription (user types) | licence: Esri Master Agreement (proprietary) | cadence: solution releases roughly annually | NC: Used by NC agencies (e.g. NC National Guard in Buncombe/Rutherford for Helene) and available to any NC county with ArcGIS Online
  use (post): Make the Survey123 field sheet field-compatible with the IA Survey so county Survey123 data can be ingested as ground truth; copy the Manage/Monitor split, assignment inbox, 'Public View' photo flag and incident filter; provide Spanish form labels; note the FEMA PA 4.1.3 data standard as the schema to check for PA exports.
  caveats: Damage-level domain values and offline behaviour not extracted from these pages; the FEMA 'PA 4.1.3 data standard' document itself was not located.
- (medium/high) **Esri ArcGIS Solutions: Emergency Management Operations** (Esri) https://doc.arcgis.com/en/arcgis-solutions/latest/reference/introduction-to-emergency-management-operations.htm
  Five products mapped to roles: Community Lifelines Editor (Survey123, operations staff, Contributor role); Emergency Information Manager (Experience Builder, operations staff); Incident Status Dashboard (ArcGIS Dashboards, command staff, Viewer role); Public Message Editor (Survey123, PIO); Emergency Management Information Hub site (public). Requires ArcGIS Online; infographics need geoenrichment privileges.
  access: ArcGIS Solutions deployment | auth: paid ArcGIS Online subscription | licence: Esri (proprietary) | cadence: solution releases | NC: Available to NC agencies with ArcGIS Online
  use (during,post): Role split to copy: operations staff edit lifeline status, command staff get a read-only status dashboard, PIO authors public messages from the same data. NC Recon's EM-director view should be read-only and indicator-first.
  caveats: Offline support and layer details not documented on the intro page.
- (high/high) **ArcGIS Dashboards documentation: dashboard types, effective dashboards, accessibility, themes** (Esri) https://doc.arcgis.com/en/dashboards/latest/reference/author-effective-dashboards.htm
  Esri's conventions for EOC dashboards: operational / strategic / analytical / informative types; desktop view 'for unattended scenarios (operations centers on large screens) or attended'; mobile portrait view; 'When possible, it's best to make a dashboard for one audience'; 'if the dashboard will be viewed in a dimly lit office, consider using the dark theme to reduce eyestrain'; monitor-wall dashboards must be 'easy to read from a distance' and should be tested in the workspace; most important element largest and most prominent; indicators with reference values and conditional formatting; 'Only include information that users need to know'. Accessibility page: 'All dashboard themes meet minimum color contrast requirements' (Light, Dark, Enhanced contrast); 'Avoid relying solely on color to convey information. Use varying symbol size or shape'; built-in contrast checker warns when requirements are not met; match basemap to theme.
  access: public documentation | auth: none | licence: Esri documentation (proprietary); product requires ArcGIS Online | cadence: updated per release | NC: n/a (design guidance)
  use (pre,during,post): Adopt verbatim as NC Recon UI rules: one audience per view (analyst, director, PIO, wall), dark theme as an option for dim rooms with Enhanced-contrast variant, indicator tiles with reference values (e.g. change since last operational period), symbol shape/size in addition to colour, test on an actual wall display.
  caveats: Esri does not cite WCAG by number; refresh-interval options not captured.
- (high/high) **ArcGIS Field Maps: offline map areas and sync** (Esri) https://doc.arcgis.com/en/field-maps/latest/prepare-maps/prepare-maps-for-download.htm
  Offline pattern used by field assessors: predefined or ad hoc map areas ('There is a limit of 16 offline map areas that can be created in a single web map'); layers must be sync-enabled; basemaps via sideloaded .tpk/.tpkx/.vtpk, referenced tile packages, or downloadable Esri basemaps; 'Mobile workers only receive updates to downloaded maps on request'; captured data stays on device until synced; mobile map packages are read-only; tile package coordinate system must match the map.
  access: public documentation; product needs ArcGIS Online/Enterprise | auth: paid ArcGIS user types | licence: Esri (proprietary) | cadence: per release | NC: n/a
  use (pre,during,post): Blueprint for NC Recon offline packs: pre-cut per-AOI packages (imagery tiles + structures + current calls), sideloadable by USB/hard drive (NC lidar was distributed to counties on hard drives), explicit user-triggered sync with last-write and conflict display, and a hard cap on pack count/size.
  caveats: Esri documentation does not describe edit-conflict resolution here.
- (medium/high) **ArcGIS QuickCapture** (Esri) https://www.esri.com/en-us/arcgis/products/arcgis-quickcapture/overview
  'Big-button' rapid capture app for windshield surveys: single-tap points/lines/polygons with photo/video, auto-populated location, time and speed; 'keep the pace of field data capture even when offline' with automatic sync on reconnection; included with ArcGIS user types; cited uses include Hurricane Ian recovery and storm damage assessment by a Mississippi utility.
  access: app plus ArcGIS Online | auth: paid ArcGIS user types | licence: Esri (proprietary) | cadence: per release | NC: Used in Helene by partner agencies (SC National Guard per Esri news; NCNG case study names Survey123)
  use (post): Model for a field 'confirm/override' mode: one tap per structure to accept, raise or lower the predicted damage level while driving past, queued offline, synced later.
  caveats: Survey123 offline documentation URL could not be resolved (see gaps).
- (medium/high) **Esri case study: NC National Guard GIS in Hurricane Helene (Buncombe and Rutherford)** (Esri (about NC National Guard)) https://www.esri.com/en-us/lg/industry/public-safety/stories/nc-national-guard-uses-gis-in-hurricane-response
  From 2024-09-25 NCNG engineer assessment teams used Survey123 for field assessments, an Experience Builder 'NC National Guard Disaster Recovery Assessment Portal', Dashboards organized by fire district with KPIs, and Instant Apps aggregating 18 partner agencies (USACE, municipalities, NCDOT, contractors) into a 'Geospatial Common Operating Picture'. Over 1,000 field assessments in two months covering debris volume, bridges and roads; 'Data formatted to FEMA and North Carolina Office of Emergency Management standards'; training videos were essential for onboarding non-GIS personnel; started from Esri's Emergency Debris Management solution rather than from scratch.
  access: public case study | auth: none | licence: Esri marketing content | cadence: static | NC: Buncombe and Rutherford counties, Helene 2024
  use (post): Confirms NC partners aggregate by fire district and expect FEMA/NCEM-standard schemas; add fire-district and USNG aggregation, publish 2-3 minute training videos, and expose a feature-service-like endpoint so partner Instant Apps can consume NC Recon layers.
  caveats: Offline/connectivity problems not discussed in the case study.
- (low/high) **Esri Disaster Response Program** (Esri) https://www.esri.com/en-us/disaster-response/overview
  24/7 program offering temporary ArcGIS licence extensions, new access for non-customers, Living Atlas data, workflow implementation for situational awareness and damage assessment, and premium support during disasters; 'all requests should be fully justified and are subject to approval'.
  access: request form | auth: account; approval | licence: Esri (temporary licences) | cadence: per event | NC: Available to NC agencies
  use (during,post): Procurement note for counties without ArcGIS during an event; NC Recon should not depend on it but should document how to publish NC Recon layers into a DRP-provisioned org.
  caveats: No Helene-specific examples on the page.
- (medium/medium) **NAPSG Foundation Incident Symbology Framework and Symbol Library** (NAPSG Foundation with DHS) https://www.napsgfoundation.org/symbology
  Consistent incident symbology for maps and GIS: 'Incident Symbology Framework & Guideline' v4.0 (March 21, 2020) and 'Technical Implementation Guidance' v5 (June 12, 2024); symbols as PNG and SVG, ArcGIS Pro style sheets, TrueType fonts, AGOL vector web styles and an example geodatabase schema; licensed CC BY 4.0. Symbol library web tool 'will be deprecated on March 1st, 2027' with a new tool promised. GitHub NAPSG/DHS-Symbol-Server holds the SVG set (September 2021 snapshot) in categories such as access/hazards, HAZMAT, critical infrastructure, lifelines, NIMS positions, natural hazards, pre-planning features and USAR; damage assessment is not an explicit category.
  access: web downloads, ArcGIS Online groups, GitHub | auth: none | licence: CC BY 4.0 | cadence: guideline v4.0 2020; tech guidance v5 2024 | NC: National; used by FEMA and states (adoption not independently verified)
  use (pre,during,post): Use NAPSG SVGs for incident, lifeline and infrastructure points on the Conditions map (attribution required); keep a separate, documented damage ramp since NAPSG does not define structure-damage symbols.
  caveats: Both guideline PDF URLs listed on the page returned 404; content of the guideline not verified.
