- (high/high) **US National Grid (FGDC-STD-011-2001) and USNG Center** (Federal Geographic Data Committee; USNG Center (usngcenter.org)) https://www.fgdc.gov/usng
  The national grid reference standard. FGDC page links the standard PDF, a 2009 FEMA Disaster Operations memo making USNG the standard for FEMA operational maps, NAPSG's USNG implementation guide, and a 'Report on Implementing the USNG Standard in North Carolina'. USNG Center: '10-meter accuracy enables fast rescues and emergency response coordination' when addresses are gone. ICS 209 block 23 and the FEMA PDA Guide (debris locations, POST 1 km/5 km cells) both use USNG.
  access: portal download | auth: none | licence: US Government (public domain) | cadence: static | NC: NC implementation report exists (link on fgdc.gov articles-reports page)
  use (pre,during,post): Keep USNG in CSV; add a USNG grid overlay (1 km / 100 m) with cell labels, a USNG search box, and per-1 km-cell damage counts for ICS 209 and POST-style aggregation.
  caveats: NC implementation report PDF not fetched (index page link only).
- (high/high) **NWS hazard map colour and priority conventions** (NOAA National Weather Service) https://www.weather.gov/help-map
  Official colours and priority order (1-111) for watches/warnings; one event per zone shown, most significant first; map refreshes about every 5 minutes. Examples: Tornado Warning #FF0000 (priority 2), Flash Flood Warning #8B0000 (5), Hurricane Warning #DC143C (19), Tropical Storm Warning #B22222 (31), Flood Warning #00FF00 (39), Tornado Watch #FFFF00 (45), Wind Advisory #D2B48C (76), Flood Watch #2E8B57 (89).
  access: public page | auth: none | licence: US Government (public domain) | cadence: static table | NC: NWS Raleigh/GSP/Morristown/Wilmington/Blacksburg alerts for NC
  use (pre,during): The Conditions map must render NWS alerts in these exact colours and resolve overlaps by NWS priority so EOC staff recognize them instantly; use them unchanged in dark mode and check the 3:1 non-text contrast against the basemap.
  caveats: Several NWS colours (e.g. Flood Warning lime) have poor contrast on light basemaps; add outline or halo rather than recolouring.
- (high/high) **WCAG 2.2 Success Criteria 1.4.3 Contrast (Minimum) and 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast** (W3C Web Accessibility Initiative) https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-minimum.html
  Text contrast at least 4.5:1; large text (18pt, or 14pt bold; about 24px / 18.5px) at least 3:1; charts, graphs and UI components fall under 1.4.11 Non-text Contrast (3:1). Contrast is judged against the actual rendered background.
  access: public | auth: none | licence: W3C Document License | cadence: static | NC: n/a; US federal Section 508 references WCAG 2.0 AA
  use (pre,during,post): Add automated contrast tests for both themes (text 4.5:1, map symbols and damage ramp 3:1 against both imagery and basemap) to CI.
  caveats: No dark-background-specific guidance in the SC text.
- (high/high) **ColorBrewer 2.0 and Color Universal Design (Okabe-Ito) palette guidance** (Cynthia Brewer and Mark Harrower, Penn State (ColorBrewer); Masataka Okabe and Kei Ito (CUD page)) https://colorbrewer2.org/
  ColorBrewer: sequential, diverging and qualitative schemes for 3-12 classes with 'colorblind safe', 'print friendly' and 'photocopy safe' filters, exportable as JS arrays/CSS; open source on GitHub (Apache 2.0). CUD page: an eight-colour palette designed to be distinguishable for all common colour-vision deficiencies (orange, sky blue, bluish green, yellow, blue, vermilion, reddish purple, black), guidance to 'use not only different colors but also a combination of different shapes, positions, line types and coloring patterns', and prevalence figures of about 8% of Caucasian males, 5% of Asian males and 4% of African males.
  access: public web tools | auth: none | licence: ColorBrewer: Apache 2.0; CUD page: free to use, attribution requested | cadence: static | NC: n/a
  use (pre,during,post): Damage ramp: one colourblind-safe 5-class sequential scheme (ColorBrewer filter on) with distinct marker shapes and text labels per level; never red/green alone for damaged vs undamaged; qualitative Okabe-Ito colours for evidence channels (CNN, change, tarp, flood) and review states.
  caveats: The fetch summary returned inconsistent hex values for the Okabe-Ito colours; read hex values directly from the page before use.
- (medium/medium) **Piepenbrock et al., display polarity studies (Ergonomics 2013; Human Factors 2013)** (Cosima Piepenbrock et al. (peer-reviewed)) https://doi.org/10.1080/00140139.2013.790485
  'Positive display polarity is advantageous for both younger and older adults' (Ergonomics, 2013) and 'Positive Display Polarity Is Particularly Advantageous for Small Character Sizes' (Human Factors, 2013): dark text on light background improves proofreading/legibility versus light-on-dark, especially at small sizes.
  access: journal (paywalled); metadata via CrossRef API | auth: paid or institutional access for full text | licence: publisher copyright | cadence: static | NC: n/a
  use (pre,during,post): Default to a light (positive-polarity) theme for dense reading tasks (review queue, tables) and offer dark theme for wall displays in dim EOCs as Esri recommends; increase base font size in dark mode.
  caveats: Only bibliographic metadata was fetched, not the full papers.
- (medium/medium) **Crisis Cleanup** (Crisis Cleanup (nonprofit) and open-source community) https://www.crisiscleanup.org/
  Collaborative disaster work-order management platform connecting volunteer organizations with survivors (muck-out, trees, tarps, debris). GitHub org (25 repos): web app v4 (Vue 3/TypeScript, Apache-2.0, launched March 2023; README cites '600,000+ volunteers from 2,500+ organizations with 140,000+ households'), native iOS (Swift) and Android (Kotlin) apps, infrastructure as code. Helene hotline 844-965-1386 was promoted by NC DPS and the Governor's office; a Reddit post reported about 9,000 cases logged early (unverified); the hotline ran through 2024-10-11 per Chronicle Online.
  access: web app and mobile apps; account required for relief organizations; public hotline | auth: account (organizations); none for survivors via hotline | licence: Apache-2.0 (code); platform data not open | cadence: live per disaster | NC: Active for Helene across western NC
  use (post): Distribution channel and UI precedent: NC Recon could export destroyed/major clusters as suppressed, aggregate 'where to canvass' layers for VOADs; do not ingest Crisis Cleanup survivor PII. Study its work-order status and claim model to avoid duplicate field visits in NC Recon's review/field assignments.
  caveats: crisiscleanup.org is client-rendered; feature, API and offline details not confirmed from primary docs.
- (medium/high) **HOT Tasking Manager** (Humanitarian OpenStreetMap Team) https://github.com/hotosm/tasking-manager
  Open-source (BSD-2-Clause) coordination tool: splits an area of interest into task squares that mappers lock and complete, then validators review; FastAPI/Python backend, React frontend, PostgreSQL/PostGIS; multiple national instances besides tasks.hotosm.org.
  access: GitHub; hosted instances | auth: OSM account on hosted instances; none to self-host | licence: BSD-2-Clause | cadence: active | NC: n/a
  use (post): Reference design for the human review queue: per-cell task locking, mapped/validated/invalidated states with a progress bar, validator role distinct from reviewer, and per-task comments; reuse the concept with USNG 1 km cells as tasks.
  caveats: Designed for OSM editing, not damage labelling.
- (low/medium) **SKAI (Google Research / WFP Innovation Accelerator / InstaDeep)** (Google Research with UN World Food Programme) https://github.com/google-research/skai
  Open-source (Apache-2.0) ML pipeline that performs automatic building damage assessment from before/after aerial or satellite imagery; README claims 'model-generated assessments match expert generated assessments on between 85% and 98% of buildings assessed'. Runs on Google Cloud.
  access: GitHub | auth: Google Cloud account to run | licence: Apache-2.0 | cadence: active | NC: n/a
  use (post): Accuracy benchmark and a comparable human-labelling-then-fine-tune workflow; its labelling UI was not inspected.
  caveats: README raw fetch failed on main/master; setup docs not read.
- (high/high) **Microsoft AI for Good Lab: building-damage-assessment toolkit, satellite-imagery-labeling-tool and HASTE platform** (Microsoft AI for Good Research Lab) https://github.com/microsoft/building-damage-assessment
  MIT-licensed end-to-end workflow: label post-event imagery in the web 'satellite-imagery-labeling-tool' (classes Building, Damaged Building, background; label only damaged portions), export GeoJSON, build masks, fine-tune (20-30 epochs, TensorBoard review), infer a per-pixel GeoTIFF, then merge_with_building_footprints.py (OSM/Google/Microsoft footprints) outputs a GeoPackage with 'the percentage of each building that is predicted to be damaged'; a second workflow intersects SAR damage proxy maps with footprints. Used for Turkey/Syria 2023, Rolling Fork tornado, Maui, Libya, Hurricane Beryl; Lahaina assessment delivered within four hours at 97% accuracy to the American Red Cross. HASTE (arXiv 2607.11838): no-code web platform; analysts label a handful of buildings, foundation-model embeddings per footprint are scored with in-browser logistic regression, results 'within hours to days'; 30+ responses since 2023; matches a ResNet-50 baseline with one-twentieth of the labels on xBD; open source. Outputs are delivered as before/after visualizers (catalog at visualizers.aiforgood.ai).
  access: GitHub; visualizer catalog website | auth: none | licence: MIT (toolkit); HASTE stated open source | cadence: active; per-event visualizers | NC: No Helene-specific deployment confirmed on fetched pages
  use (post): Closest open comparable to NC Recon's fused approach: adopt few-shot per-event recalibration from reviewer labels, percent-of-footprint-damaged as an attribute, and a swipe before/after visualizer as the public/PIO artefact.
  caveats: Visualizer catalog page rendered empty to the fetcher; HASTE GitHub repository name not identified.
- (high/high) **Texas A&M CRASAR: Deploying Rapid Damage Assessments from sUAS Imagery (AAAI 2025)** (Texas A&M University (Manzini, Perali, Murphy)) https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/download/41474/45435
  First operational AI/ML building damage assessment on small-UAS imagery during federally declared disasters (Hurricanes Debby and Helene). Trained on 21,716 building damage labels (CRASAR-U-DROIDs) annotated by 130 annotators in a LabelBox interface; 91 practitioners trained; deployed model 'assessed a combined 415 buildings in approximately 18 minutes'. Documents that sUAS teams deliver 47-369 GB of imagery per day which 'often had to be physically transported to decision makers instead of transmitted wirelessly'.
  access: open-access PDF | auth: none | licence: AAAI copyright (open access) | cadence: static | NC: Helene deployment imagery shown from Dekle Beach, FL; NC deployment not stated
  use (post): Drone imagery must be ingestible from local disk/USB (sneakernet) and processed on-prem; plan storage and tiling for tens to hundreds of GB per day; follow the practitioner-training model (operators trained before the event).
  caveats: Deployed UI specifics not extracted; damage scale in the dataset not captured here.

  Planet Disaster Data publishes select imagery for major disasters on Source Cooperative (source.coop/planet/disasterdata) under CC-BY-NC for qualified NGOs, government authorities and crisis responders. Separately (press release 2021-11-17) Planet and NLT integrate near-daily PlanetScope imagery into FEMA's disaster response program via API/cloud to assess damage and plan recovery.
  access: Source Cooperative repository; FEMA integration via contractor | auth: qualifying organization for Disaster Data; FEMA internal for NLT feed | licence: CC-BY-NC (Disaster Data); commercial otherwise | cadence: per event | NC: Helene 2024 release not confirmed on fetched pages
  use (post): Potential evidence channel if the non-commercial licence is acceptable for government use; also shows that FEMA's own GDA analysts have daily 3 m imagery that NC Recon lacks.
  caveats: No contract vehicle or terms disclosed; event list not shown on the page.

- (high/high) **FCC Hurricane Helene Communications Status Reports (DIRS) and outage cause analysis** (FCC Public Safety and Homeland Security Bureau; arXiv preprint (Olukola et al.)) https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DOC-406055A1.pdf
  Daily DIRS reports Sept 26 to Oct 19, 2024. October 1, 2024 (9:00 EDT): NC DIRS counties Ashe, Avery, Buncombe, Burke, Caldwell, Cherokee, Clay, Graham, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, Macon, Madison, McDowell, Mitchell, Polk, Rutherford, Swain, Transylvania, Watauga, Yancey; NC total 1,452 cell sites served, 707 out (48.69%, down from 54.0%): 3 out due to damage, 303 due to transport, 338 due to power, 102 up on backup power; county extremes Yancey 90.91%, Mitchell 90.00%, Transylvania 70.00%, Polk 55.56%; PSAPs: Yancey rerouted without ALI; Beech Mountain PD, Cherokee, Clay, EBCI, Madison, Mitchell 911, Swain rerouted with ALI; 796,999 cable/wireline subscribers out across the disaster area. Preprint 'The Towers Were Standing' (arXiv 2609.10944) decomposes DIRS: physical damage 1.1% of attributed site-days; power loss 63.2% pooled; in NC transport/backhaul 52.2% vs power 47.3%, transport share rising from 7.0% to 85.0% over the event.
  access: portal download (PDF/TXT per day) | auth: none | licence: US Government (public domain); arXiv preprint CC/arXiv licence | cadence: daily during activation (deactivated May 2025 for NC) | NC: 21 WNC counties
  use (during,post): Quantitative basis for low-bandwidth design: assume half the cell sites in the AOI are down for days and that backhaul, not towers, is the failure; design for Starlink-class links and intermittent sync; use DIRS county percentages as a 'connectivity' context layer during events.
  caveats: fcc.gov/helene index returned 403; Sept 29 figure (66.4%, down from 74.3%) known only from a search snippet of a mirrored PDF.

  Oct 6: 'An additional 30 Starlink satellite systems have arrived' for the NC Emergency Response Center and mobile locations; about 80% of cellular access restored; ~142,000 customers without power (peak over 1 million); ~650 roads closed (100+ primary); 22 shelters with 825 people. Nov 6 blog: 24 counties received Starlink kits via NC National Guard and law enforcement; 900+ cache VIPER radios distributed; 911 centers rerouted; Newton Communication Center handled 6,000+ calls Sept 27-Oct 1; JIC handled ~600 inquiries and 35 statements; misinformation 'confusing to people trying to get help'.

  caveats: none

  use (pre,post): Pre-event vulnerability context alongside SVI; the mobile-home share justifies a manufactured-home branch in damage logic and in the displacement estimate.
  caveats: No limited-English or Hispanic figures in the story.
- (medium/high) **GAO-23-104750 FEMA housing inspections (Oct 2022) and FEMA home-inspection page** (US Government Accountability Office; FEMA) https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-23-104750
  FEMA uses contracted housing inspectors; since April 2020 'inspectors estimated the overall damage level of a home based on a smaller set of key indicators (e.g., height of floodwater in a home)' instead of itemizing; mean awards fell 35% and 'FEMA has not assessed this new approach to determine if it accurately estimates damages'; 43% of applicants who self-assessed minor damage had eligible damage; 2.7M applicants, ~1.4M inspections, ~710k approved (Jan 2018-Nov 2021); some inspectors lacked agency tablets. FEMA page: inspections take up to 45 minutes, inspector photographs interior and exterior, carries official photo ID.
  access: public reports | auth: none | licence: US Government (public domain) | cadence: static | NC: national; applies to NC IA inspections
  use (post): FEMA's own inspection logic is key-indicator based (water height first), which validates NC Recon's flood-depth channel as the primary flood evidence; IA verified-damage statistics (OpenFEMA) remain the scalable ground truth even though applicant-level data are aggregated.
  caveats: The inspector mobile application itself (vendor, UI) is not described in fetched sources.
- (high/high) **PMTiles (Protomaps)** (Protomaps (Brandon Liu)) https://docs.protomaps.com/pmtiles/
  Single-file archive for tile pyramids served by HTTP range requests from static storage with no tile server; MapLibre GL is the recommended client; the pmtiles CLI makes regional extracts from larger archives; software BSD-licensed, basemap data ODbL where derived from OpenStreetMap; suited to offline and bandwidth-constrained use because a file is downloaded once.
  access: open-source library and CLI | auth: none | licence: BSD (software); data licence follows source (ODbL for OSM-derived basemaps) | cadence: active | NC: n/a; extracts for any NC AOI
  use (pre,during,post): Mechanism for offline PWA packs: ship basemap + before/after imagery + structures as PMTiles extracts per AOI, served from the container or copied to a laptop/USB; MapLibre already in use.
  caveats: Imagery licences still govern redistribution of tile extracts.
- (low/medium) **NASA Earthdata Hurricane Helene Recovery Data Dashboard and 'North Carolina Helene Data' hub (Northeastern University)** (NASA-funded researchers; Northeastern University) https://www.earthdata.nasa.gov/data/tools/hurricane-helene-recovery-data-dashboard
  ArcGIS-based 'Emergency Response Decision Support System' letting users navigate by county to flooding, community effects and recovery needs; researchers estimated ~19,600 private wells, 34,300 businesses and 500 fire stations affected by flooding. The companion hub (nc-helene-data-nu.hub.arcgis.com) lists 25 items including 'Helene Flooding Extents Vector Tile', HAND (height above nearest drainage) intersections with Helene counties, well-water guidance pages and a Survey123 'Hurricane Helene Damage Assessment' survey; no licences declared.
  access: public ArcGIS Hub; some NASA tools need Earthdata login | auth: none (hub); Earthdata login for some tools | licence: NASA data open; hub items unstated | cadence: items modified Oct 2024 to Jul 2025 | NC: Helene disaster counties
  use (post): UI comparable (county-first navigation for recovery) and a candidate flood-extent layer to evaluate (licence unstated).
  caveats: Hub items carry no licence statements.
- (high/high) **NC Collaboratory / AECOM / NCDPS post-Helene lidar and aerial imagery (2025) via NC OneMap** (North Carolina Collaboratory (UNC) with AECOM and NC Department of Public Safety) https://www.unc.edu/posts/2025/10/02/collaboratory-maps-helene-damage-to-speed-recovery/
  $4M program flew high-resolution aerial photography and lidar in leaf-off conditions January to early March 2025 over 13 priority counties (Ashe, Avery, Buncombe, Burke, Davie, Haywood, Henderson, Jackson, McDowell, Mitchell, Rutherford, Watauga, Yancey), with the state covering the rest of western NC; counties received regional datasets on hard drives and full collections are public through NC OneMap; intended for FEMA recovery requests, NCDOT, geological survey, floodplain remapping, landslide risk and flood warning.
  access: NC OneMap download/services; hard drives to counties | auth: none | licence: NC state data (NC OneMap terms) | cadence: one-time 2025 collection | NC: 13 priority counties plus broader WNC
  use (post,pre): Post-event 3D source for lidar DEM-of-difference and roof/height evidence, and pre-event baseline for the next WNC event; the hard-drive distribution confirms offline ingest is a real workflow.
  caveats: NC OneMap landing pages for these datasets were not fetched (site is client-rendered).
- (low/medium) **DHS SAVER Highlight: Common Operating Picture for Emergency Responders (Sept 2008)** (DHS Science and Technology Directorate, SAVER program) https://www.dhs.gov/sites/default/files/publications/CommonOpER_HLT_0908-508.pdf
  Procurement-oriented highlight defining a COP per the National Response Framework and describing SAVER's practitioner-relevant assessments of commercial COP systems ('What equipment is available?' and 'How does it perform?').
  access: portal download | auth: none | licence: US Government (public domain) | cadence: static (2008) | NC: n/a
  use (pre): Historical context only; SAVER's criteria (usability, capability, deployability, maintainability, affordability) are a reasonable rubric for NC Recon's own acceptance testing.
  caveats: Single-page highlight; the full SAVER report was not fetched.
### insights
- FEMA's five-level residential scale is defined by waterline thresholds, not by visual roof damage: Destroyed = waterline at or above the ceiling or failure of two or more structural components; Major = waterline at or above electrical outlets in an essential living space (or >30 days to repair per the DR-4827 PDA report footnote); Minor = uninhabitable but repairable quickly; Affected = habitable with some damage; plus 'Inaccessible' when damage cannot be visually validated because roads/bridges are blocked (FEMA PDA Guide 2025, Appendix H; https://fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_rd_pda-guide_07012025.pdf; https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/PDAReport_FEMA4827DRexpedited-NC.pdf).
- The FEMA PDA Guide 2025 explicitly recognizes 'Virtual Sensing' (aerial, satellite, lidar, SAR) and says 'Advanced analytics could be used to automatically identify and categorize damaged structures from imagery', and allows 'Desktop Assessments' in which FEMA validates state-submitted photos, annotated maps and GIS data without field visits; it also defines a state 'Geospatial and Data Analysis Lead' role (https://fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_rd_pda-guide_07012025.pdf).
- ICS 209 block 30 reports structures only as # Threatened (72 hrs), # Damaged and # Destroyed by Single Residences / Nonresidential Commercial / Other Minor Structures, and asks whether numbers are estimates or confirmed; block 11 carries the reporting period, block 23 a US National Grid reference, block 27 a note on attached geospatial data with 'collection time information' (NIMS ICS Forms Booklet v3, https://training.fema.gov/emiweb/is/icsresource/assets/nims%20ics%20forms%20booklet.pdf).
- NIMS places map production inside the Planning Section's Situation Unit, which 'frequently includes Geographic/Geospatial Information Systems (GIS) Specialists, who produce maps' and issues SITREPs on schedule; operational periods are 'typically 12 to 24 hours' (NIMS 2017, https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/2020-07/fema_nims_doctrine-2017.pdf).
- FEMA Community Lifelines colours have fixed meanings: Grey = Unknown, Red = Significant Impact, Yellow = Moderate Impact (restoration in progress, including temporary means), Green = Minimal Impact (pre-incident levels), Blue = Administrative only; 'A color designation represents a snapshot in time for that response operational period' (Toolkit 2.1, https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/fema_lifelines-toolkit-v2.1_2023.pdf).
- FEMA's Situation Reporting Construct frames every report as 'What? So What? Now What? And When?' for decision makers (Lifelines Toolkit 2.1, same URL).
- FEMA's Response Geospatial Office runs a Geospatial Damage Assessment application in which 'Trained analysts use the tool to compare pre- and post-incident aerial imagery collected from authoritative sources to indicate damages at the structure level'; the Helene GDA dashboard is restricted to FEMA/ICG members and the item returns HTTP 403 publicly; partners request access at FEMA-RGO@fema.dhs.gov (https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/ff4eb86beb974387b3e26f9bb020d7f0/data?f=json).
- For Helene NC the joint PDA was waived (44 CFR 206.33(d)/206.36(d)) and the FEMA PDA report lists no residential damage counts, so no official structure-level PDA numbers exist for validation (https://www.fema.gov/sites/default/files/documents/PDAReport_FEMA4827DRexpedited-NC.pdf).
- NC OSBM's Helene housing estimate moved from about 73,700 damaged homes (October 2024 method) to 121,000-132,000 (IA-claims method, December 2024); its bottom-up method used FEMA/NCEM Search and Rescue structure observations categorized as destroyed/major/minor/affected/unaffected per FEMA criteria through 2024-11-19, fused with NCEM inundation modeling, NCDEQ/USGS landslide data and NC OneMap parcels (https://www.osbm.nc.gov/hurricane-helene-dna/open).
- NC's own Helene after-action review found 'the absence of a centralized database that provides a common operating picture (COP) for all SERT partners', with critical information split across WebEOC, NCSPARTA, Microsoft Teams, situation report templates, iCAM and SharePoint; agencies 'failed to track and close resource requests in WebEOC'; there were 'Gaps in ICS and WebEOC proficiency' at state and county levels; external partners worked over guest Wi-Fi; and Starlink/satellite phones took 'days' to reach counties, which 'kept NCEM in the dark about ground truth' (https://www.ncdps.gov/division/emergency-management/ts-helene-after-action-review/open).
- The AAR's top workshop actions include 'Create a unified database for information sharing and coordination across all SERT partners' and permanent ESF liaison positions at RCC West, with NCDIT proposed as the integration partner; the staff survey rated day/night shift turnover and asked which tools were most and least valuable (WebEOC, GIS mapping, Teams, Adobe Connect, iCAM, SharePoint, Linktree) (same URL).
- Esri's EOC dashboard guidance: build one dashboard per audience; 'if the dashboard will be viewed in a dimly lit office, consider using the dark theme to reduce eyestrain'; wall dashboards must be 'easy to read from a distance' and tested in the actual workspace; the largest element carries the most important information; 'Avoid relying solely on color to convey information. Use varying symbol size or shape'; all Esri themes (Light, Dark, Enhanced contrast) meet minimum contrast and a checker warns when custom colours fail (https://doc.arcgis.com/en/dashboards/latest/reference/author-effective-dashboards.htm; https://doc.arcgis.com/en/dashboards/latest/reference/accessibility-best-practices.htm).
- Peer-reviewed display research finds positive polarity (dark text on light) 'advantageous for both younger and older adults' and especially for small characters (Piepenbrock et al., Ergonomics 2013, DOI 10.1080/00140139.2013.790485; Human Factors 2013, DOI 10.1177/0018720813515509), so dark themes should be reserved for dim-room wall displays and paired with larger type.
- WCAG 2.2 requires 4.5:1 contrast for body text, 3:1 for large text (18pt or 14pt bold, about 24px/18.5px) and 3:1 for non-text graphics and UI components (SC 1.4.11) (https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/contrast-minimum.html).
- Colour-vision deficiency affects roughly 8% of Caucasian males, 5% of Asian males and 4% of African males; Color Universal Design guidance is to 'use not only different colors but also a combination of different shapes, positions, line types and coloring patterns' (https://jfly.uni-koeln.de/color/); ColorBrewer offers colourblind-safe, print-friendly and photocopy-safe filters for 3-12 classes under Apache 2.0 (https://colorbrewer2.org/).
- NWS alert colours are standardized and priority-ordered (e.g. Tornado Warning #FF0000, Flash Flood Warning #8B0000, Hurricane Warning #DC143C, Flood Warning #00FF00, Flood Watch #2E8B57); only the highest-priority event per zone is drawn and the map refreshes about every 5 minutes (https://www.weather.gov/help-map).
- Esri's Damage Assessment solution v6.0 (Nov 2025) aligns to 'FEMA 4.1.3 Public Assistance data standards' and adds Spanish surveys; its IA survey fields are dwelling type, owner/renter, insurance, address, structure details, damage level, water depth and photos; its operations app separates a Manage tab (assign, relocate, 'Public View' photo flag) from a Monitor tab (counts by damage level, dwelling type, insurance, completion) (https://doc.arcgis.com/en/arcgis-solutions/latest/reference/use-damage-assessment.htm).
- ArcGIS Field Maps caps offline map areas at 16 per web map, requires sync-enabled layers, supports sideloaded .tpk/.tpkx/.vtpk, and 'Mobile workers only receive updates to downloaded maps on request'; MMPKs are read-only (https://doc.arcgis.com/en/field-maps/latest/prepare-maps/prepare-maps-for-download.htm).
- NC National Guard's Helene GIS effort in Buncombe and Rutherford produced over 1,000 field assessments in two months using Survey123, Experience Builder, Dashboards organized by fire district and Instant Apps aggregating 18 partner agencies into a 'Geospatial Common Operating Picture', with data 'formatted to FEMA and North Carolina Office of Emergency Management standards' and training videos essential for non-GIS staff (https://www.esri.com/en-us/lg/industry/public-safety/stories/nc-national-guard-uses-gis-in-hurricane-response).
- Operational sUAS damage assessment at Hurricanes Debby and Helene assessed 415 buildings in about 18 minutes after training on 21,716 labels annotated by 130 people in LabelBox; drone teams generate 47-369 GB of imagery per day that 'often had to be physically transported to decision makers instead of transmitted wirelessly' (Texas A&M, AAAI 2025, https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/download/41474/45435).
- Microsoft's open toolkit outputs 'the percentage of each building that is predicted to be damaged' as a GeoPackage after merging per-pixel predictions with OSM/Google/Microsoft footprints, and its HASTE platform lets analysts label 'a handful of buildings' and fit a per-footprint classifier in-browser, delivering results 'within hours to days' across 30+ responses since 2023 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/building-damage-assessment/main/SATELLITE_WORKFLOW.md; https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11838).
- On 2024-10-01 the FCC reported 707 of 1,452 NC cell sites out (48.69%) in 21 WNC counties: 3 due to physical damage, 303 due to transport/backhaul, 338 due to power, 102 up on backup power; Yancey 90.9% and Mitchell 90.0% out; several PSAPs rerouted (Yancey without ALI); 796,999 cable/wireline subscribers out across the disaster area (https://docs.fcc.gov/public/attachments/DOC-406055A1.pdf). A DIRS-based study attributes only 1.1% of outage site-days to physical damage and finds NC outages split 52.2% transport vs 47.3% power, with transport's share rising from 7% to 85% over the event (https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10944).
- NC deployed Starlink to 24 county EOCs via NC National Guard and law enforcement and received 30 additional terminals for the State Emergency Response Center and mobile sites by Oct 6, 2024; ~80% of cellular access was restored by that date and ~650 roads remained closed (https://www.ncdps.gov/blog/2024/11/06/helene-response-communications; https://governor.nc.gov/news/press-releases/2024/10/06/rescue-teams-continue-locate-survivors-communications-improve-and-more-military-assets-join-storm).
- FEMA publishes the NC Helene disaster page in 11 languages (English, Spanish, French, Arabic, Simplified Chinese, Haitian Creole, Korean, Russian, Vietnamese, Hmong, Ukrainian) (https://www.fema.gov/disaster/4827); NCDPS's Helene page offers Spanish only for the Disaster Distress Helpline (https://www.ncdps.gov/our-organization/emergency-management/tropical-storm-helene).
- In the 27 NC Helene counties about 577,000 people (20.2%) had high social vulnerability; mobile homes were 15.4% of housing (138,000 units, one in four of NC's mobile homes); 10 of 27 counties were at least 80% rural (https://www.census.gov/library/stories/2024/10/hurricane-helene.html).
- FEMA IA inspectors since April 2020 estimate 'the overall damage level of a home based on a smaller set of key indicators (e.g., height of floodwater in a home)'; GAO found mean awards dropped 35% and FEMA had not assessed the approach's accuracy; 43% of applicants self-reporting minor damage had eligible damage (GAO-23-104750, https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-23-104750).
- NCEM publishes its public Helene Retrospective as an ArcGIS Experience Builder app owned by an 'NCWebEOC' ArcGIS account (created January 2025), confirming Esri + WebEOC as NCEM's publishing stack (https://www.arcgis.com/sharing/rest/content/items/7892f669bb0e442981b15009c7f2b5f0?f=json).
- Juvare marketed 'WebEOC for daily usage and emergency management' and 'Crisis Track damage assessment' to NC emergency managers at the NCEMA 2025 Spring Conference (Cherokee, NC, March 23-26, 2025) (https://www.juvare.com/ncema-north-carolina-emergency-management-association-2025/).
- NAPSG's DHS-funded incident symbology is CC BY 4.0 and ships as SVG/PNG, ArcGIS styles and web styles; its current symbol-library web tool 'will be deprecated on March 1st, 2027' (https://www.napsgfoundation.org/symbology; https://napsg-web.s3.amazonaws.com/symbology/index.html).
- Post-Helene leaf-off lidar and aerial imagery for 13 WNC counties were flown January to early March 2025 by the NC Collaboratory/AECOM/NCDPS ($4M), delivered to counties on hard drives and published on NC OneMap (https://www.unc.edu/posts/2025/10/02/collaboratory-maps-helene-damage-to-speed-recovery/).
- PMTiles lets a MapLibre app read tiles from a single static file over HTTP range requests with no tile server, and 'pmtiles extract' cuts regional archives, making it a fit for per-AOI offline packs (https://docs.protomaps.com/pmtiles/).
### gaps
- WebSearch budget was exhausted before this task; DuckDuckGo returned CAPTCHAs, Bing returned irrelevant results for multi-word queries, Brave worked but rate-limited (HTTP 429) after ~20 queries, Semantic Scholar returned 429; several planned searches (Spanish/LEP figures for WNC, FEMA inspector app vendor, DisasterAWARE Pro docs, 'NC Hazards' app, EOC dashboard usability studies, NCEM sitrep format) could not be completed.
- ArcGIS Survey123 offline documentation: every current URL tried (desktop/get-answers/prepareforoffline.htm redirect, capture/field-app/prepareforoffline.htm, get-started/prepareforoffline.htm, create/main/preparebasemaps.htm, get-started/offlinesurveys.htm) returned 404; Survey123 offline behaviour therefore not confirmed from primary docs.
- NAPSG Incident Symbology Framework & Guideline v4.0 and Technical Implementation Guidance v5 PDFs returned 404 at the URLs linked from napsgfoundation.org/symbology; whether NAPSG defines structure-damage symbols is unconfirmed (the GitHub SVG set lists no damage-assessment category).
- NCEM situation report format and any public SERT sitreps: none found on ncdps.gov; the AAR mentions 'situation report templates' in Microsoft Teams only. NC State EOC activation-levels page and the 2020 NC Emergency Operations Plan PDF returned 403 to the fetcher.
- NCSPARTA (NC State Preparedness and Resource Tracking Application): no public documentation located; only named in the AAR.
- FIMAN (fiman.nc.gov) returned 403; its UI, inundation-library API and licence were not inspected (flood.nc.gov description only).
- PDC DisasterAWARE / EMOPS: pdc.org returned 403 on all paths; only the Disaster Alert App Store listing was readable.
- Palantir: no primary evidence of any FEMA Hurricane Helene damage-assessment role; the Jacobs IRDR page rendered only a title.
- Microsoft AI for Good: a Hurricane Helene-specific deployment was not confirmed (Fundamental Rights page describes Turkey/Syria, Afghanistan and Lahaina); the visualizer catalog page rendered empty; HASTE's GitHub repository name was not identified.
- SKAI: README could not be fetched on main or master branches, so its labelling UI, damage classes and Vertex AI requirements are unverified.
- Juvare WebEOC Nexus and Crisis Track: product pages are client-rendered; boards, ArcGIS integration, mobile/offline capability, FEMA PDA form support and pricing could not be read; the Harnett County case study body did not load.
- Western NC Spanish-speaking and limited-English-proficiency counts: the Census API now rejects keyless requests ('A valid key must be included') and QuickFacts returned 403; variables to use with a key are ACS 5-year profile DP02_0115PE (Spanish at home), DP02_0116PE (speak English less than very well) and DP05_0076PE (Hispanic or Latino). FEMA's language-access policy page URL returned 404.
- 'NC Hazards' app: no product by that name was found on ncdps.gov, readync.gov or via search; the NC Risk Management Portal (NCID login) and ReadyNC are the closest state tools located.
- Peer-reviewed EOC dashboard / COP usability research: the two ISCRAM papers identified (Opach et al. 2020 'Map-based Interfaces for Common Operational Picture'; Steen-Tveit & Radianti 2019) were unreachable (idl.iscram.org HTTP 503 on four attempts); CrossRef results for 'EOC dashboard usability' were not relevant; NIST PSCR usability page returned 404. No fetched academic usability study of damage-assessment UI with analysts could be reported.
- FEMA 'PA 4.1.3 data standard' referenced by Esri's Damage Assessment solution, and any FEMA IA/PDA damage-assessment data schema, were not located.
- FEMA 'verified damage maps' for Helene NC (Jan 7, 2025) reported by the Asheville Citizen-Times could not be fetched (site blocked) and the underlying FEMA product was not located.
- Esri Damage Assessment solution damage-level domain values and QuickCapture button labels were not extracted; the configure page was not fetched.
- FEMA Tier I Senior Leadership Brief slide layout (PPTX) and the Lifelines icons collection were not opened; only their URLs were confirmed.
- Shift-handover-specific guidance for EOCs: NIMS gives operational periods and the AAR surveyed shift turnover, but no fetched document prescribes a handover display or checklist.
- Official low-bandwidth/offline design guidance for public-safety apps was not found; FCC DIRS outage data and PMTiles documentation are the only primary bases reported.
- Planet Disaster Data: whether a Helene 2024 release exists on Source Cooperative was not confirmed.
- NC OneMap landing pages for the 2025 post-Helene lidar/imagery were not fetched (client-rendered site); only the UNC announcement confirms publication there.

## Post-disaster recovery data and long-term tracking (weeks to years): FEMA IA/PA/NFIP/HMGP flows, SBA, debris and Blue Roof, NCDOT, temporary housing, NC recovery programmes (GROW NC, Renew NC/DCR, NCORR), county permits and parcels, imagery time series, blue-tarp literature, displacement, economic and public-health indicators, landslides, recovery reporting metrics, and hand-off to case management
- (high/high) **OpenFEMA Individuals and Households Program (IHP) Valid Registrations v1** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v1/IndividualsAndHouseholdsProgramValidRegistrations
  Applicant-level IHP registrations (PII removed) for every declared disaster since 2002, including inspection and damage fields (rpfvl, ppfvl, destroyed, habitabilityRepairsRequired, waterLevel in inches, floodDamage, roofDamage, foundationDamage, homeDamage, autoDamage), eligibility/award fields (ihpAmount, haAmount, onaAmount, tsaEligible, tsaCheckedIn, rentalAssistanceEligible, repairAssistanceEligible, replacementAssistanceEligible, sbaApproved), household composition and ownRent/residenceType. Geography: damagedStateAbbreviation, damagedCity, damagedZipCode, county, fips, censusGeoid (12-character census block group).
  access: REST API (OData filters, $top max 10,000 per call) and bulk download; also CSV (>10 GB) and Parquet (500 MB-10 GB) files | auth: none (no API key) | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions: attribution statement required ('This product uses the Federal Emergency Management Agency's OpenFEMA API, but is not endorsed by FEMA'), no re-identification of individuals, not to be used to make determinations affecting individual rights or benefits | cadence: Daily refresh for recent disasters (R/P1D); annual full refresh | NC: Statewide for every NC declaration including DR-4827 (Helene); dataset last refreshed 2026-10-06
  use (post): Ground-truth proxy and recovery dashboard channel: aggregate by censusGeoid/zip to compare NC Recon damage-level counts with FEMA-inspected damage (rpfvl bands, destroyed, waterLevel); seed re-assessment schedules where tsaEligible/destroyed clusters persist; never use at individual level (license).
  caveats: Raw NEMIS data 'subject to a small percentage of human error'; financial figures differ from official federal reporting. Field 'inspected' does not exist (use inspnIssued/inspnReturned); 'sbaEligible' does not exist (sbaApproved only). Very large files; use OData filters on disasterNumber.
- (high/high) **OpenFEMA Housing Assistance Program Data - Owners v2 and Renters v2** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v2/HousingAssistanceOwners
  Aggregated (non-PII) housing-assistance counts by state/county/city/zip and disaster: validRegistrations, totalInspected, averageFemaInspectedDamage, damage brackets ($1-10k, $10-20k, $20-30k, >$30k), approvedForFemaAssistance, totalApprovedIhpAmount, repairReplaceAmount, rentalAmount, otherNeedsAmount, approval brackets. Renters version has damage severity classes none/moderate/major/substantial.
  access: REST API and bulk download | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions (attribution; no re-identification) | cadence: Daily (R/P1D) | NC: All NC declarations since 2002 incl. DR-4827; last refreshed 2026-10-06; 160,459 (owners) and 148,018 (renters) rows nationally
  use (post): Zip/county-level recovery dashboard tiles (inspected vs damaged vs approved) and a lightweight validation of NC Recon's tract-level damage totals without touching applicant-level data.
  caveats: Self-reported and 'subject to human error'; zip-level aggregates only.
- (low/high) **OpenFEMA Individual Assistance Housing Registrants - Large Disasters v1** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v1/IndividualAssistanceHousingRegistrantsLargeDisasters
  Curated applicant-level dataset for selected large disasters (Sandy, 2017 hurricanes) with 15-character census block IDs and damage fields (rpfvl, ppfvl, destroyed, waterLevel, roofDamageAmount, foundationDamageAmount). FEMA states 'No new disasters will be added to this dataset'.
  access: REST API and CSV/Parquet download | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions | cadence: Irregular; frozen for new disasters | NC: Does not include Helene (DR-4827); only historical disasters
  use (post): Note only: useful as a training/benchmark set for block-level damage-vs-inspection relationships from past hurricanes, not for Helene tracking.
  caveats: Frozen; floodDamage field 'being deprecated'.
- (high/high) **OpenFEMA Public Assistance Funded Projects Details v2** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v2/PublicAssistanceFundedProjectsDetails
  Every obligated PA project worksheet: pwNumber, applicantId, county/countyCode, damageCategoryCode/damageCategoryDescrip (A debris, B emergency protective measures, C-G permanent work), projectAmount, federalShareObligated, totalObligated, first/lastObligationDate. 849,813 records nationally.
  access: REST API and bulk download | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions; citation required | cadence: Daily (R/P1D); last refresh 2026-10-05 | NC: All NC declarations; DR-4827 PA obligations ($2.38B as of 2026-07-14 per FEMA disaster page)
  use (post): Recovery dashboard: county-level Category A (debris) and C-G (permanent work) obligations over time as a proxy for infrastructure recovery; join to NC Recon tract rankings to show where public rebuild money has/has not landed.
  caveats: v1 deprecated 2025-10-06; applicantName not in v1; figures may differ from usaspending.gov.
- (medium/high) **OpenFEMA FEMA Web Disaster Summaries v1** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v1/FemaWebDisasterSummaries
  Per-declaration headline totals: totalNumberIaApproved, totalAmountIhpApproved, totalAmountHaApproved, totalAmountOnaApproved, totalObligatedAmountPa, totalObligatedAmountCatAb, totalObligatedAmountCatC2g, totalObligatedAmountHmgp, paLoadDate, iaLoadDate.
  access: REST API and download | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions | cadence: Every 20 minutes (R/PT20M) | NC: All NC declarations incl. DR-4827
  use (post): Cheap top-line tiles on the recovery dashboard (IA approved count and dollars, PA and HMGP obligations) refreshed near-real-time.
  caveats: Raw NEMIS; may differ from official financial reporting.
- (high/high) **OpenFEMA NFIP Redacted Claims v3 (NfipClaims) and NFIP Redacted Policies v3** (FEMA / FIMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v3/NfipClaims
  2.73M NFIP claim transactions (dateOfLoss, amountPaidOnBuildingClaim/Contents, buildingDamageAmount, waterDepth, ratedFloodZone, baseFloodElevation, elevationDifference) and 74.7M policy transactions (coverage, premiums, occupancy, flood zone). Geography: state, county FIPS, census block group FIPS, approximate lat/lon to 1 decimal place.
  access: REST API and CSV/Parquet download | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions; citation required | cadence: Approximately monthly (R/P1M) | NC: Statewide; Helene losses (Sept 2024) present as dateOfLoss; v3 claims last refreshed 2026-09-09, policies 2026-07-27
  use (pre,post): Pre: block-group insurance take-up (policies) to flag uninsured-flood-exposure tracts. Post: block-group claim counts and waterDepth distribution as an independent flood-depth evidence calibration and a long-term recovery metric (paid vs damage amount).
  caveats: FimaNfipClaims v2 and FimaNfipPolicies v2 are deprecated and frozen as of 2026-06-01, removed 2026-10-15: use v3 endpoints. Coordinates at 0.1 degree cannot be joined to structures; use censusBlockGroup. Related: NfipResidentialPenetrationRates v1 (quarterly) and NfipMultipleLossProperties v1 (annual) also listed in the metadata API.
- (high/high) **OpenFEMA HMA Subapplications Project Site Inventories v1 (replaces HMA Mitigated Properties v4)** (FEMA (OpenFEMA)) https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v1/HmaSubapplicationsProjectSiteInventories
  235,434 project-site records from Hazard Mitigation Assistance subapplications: mitigation activity (acquisition/buyout, elevation, relocation, etc.), hazard type, structure type/size/foundation/year built, BFE/flood zone, ownership/insurance, benefit-cost ratio; geography county, city, zip (no lat/lon).
  access: REST API and download | auth: none | licence: OpenFEMA Terms and Conditions | cadence: Daily (R/P1D) | NC: Statewide; Helene HMGP ($1.543B lock-in per GROW NC; 835 acquisitions submitted/446 approved as of June 2026) will appear as sites are approved
  use (post,pre): Buyout/elevation tracking layer on the recovery dashboard (city/zip counts); mark NC Recon structures in buyout zips as 'pending acquisition' for re-assessment scheduling; pre-disaster: identify previously mitigated areas.
  caveats: HazardMitigationAssistanceMitigatedProperties v4 deprecated (removal 2026-10-15); no coordinates, so parcel-level join requires county HMGP layers (see Buncombe).

News/media tab holds 192 releases, e.g. 2026-05-11 buyouts: 162 properties ($59M; Buncombe 142/$51.7M, Haywood 11/$2.4M, Mitchell 9/$2.4M), cumulative HMGP approvals >$146M.

  State Mission Assigned Recovery Task programme for private-property, waterway, C&D, white goods, HHW debris and demolition of substantially damaged structures in 13 counties (Avery, Burke, Caldwell, Clay, Gaston, Haywood, Henderson, Madison, McDowell, Mitchell, Polk, Rutherford, Transylvania) plus Weaverville and Woodfin; Right-of-Entry based. Statewide volumes exist only in reports: OSBM DNA (Dec 2024) 10,445,000 cubic yards cleared from 27 counties, 108 active TDSRS in 20 counties, estimate of 50M cubic yards total (10M curbside); GROW NC Q4 FY26 cites MountainTrue ~2M lbs over 364 river miles, Haywood Waterways 147,000 lbs.

- (high/high) **NC OSBM Hurricane Helene Damage and Needs Assessment (revised 2024-12-13)** (NC Office of State Budget and Management) https://www.osbm.nc.gov/hurricane-helene-dna/open
  133-page statewide assessment: $59.6B damage and needs ($44.4B direct, $9.4B indirect, $5.8B mitigation); housing: 73,700 homes expected damaged (p.32) but 121,000-132,000 homes damaged based on IA claims (p.42), 274,900 households expected to apply, $12.7B residential damage, 5.2% of households with flood insurance; transportation: ~5,000 miles of state roads impacted, 674 bridges and 712 culverts damaged, >7,000 private roads/bridges/culverts; 1,171 landslides cited; debris 10,445,000 cy cleared, 108 TDSRS. Housing data sources: FEMA IA claims through 2024-12-02, preliminary DOI/NFIP claim payments, ACS 1- and 5-year estimates.

- (high/high) **NC Geological Survey Landslide Inventory of North Carolina (and deprecated Post-Helene inventory) plus DEMLR hazard models** (NC DEQ Division of Energy, Mineral and Land Resources, NC Geological Survey) https://services2.arcgis.com/kCu40SDxsCGcuUWO/arcgis/rest/services/NC_Landslide_Inventory/FeatureServer
  Current-schema inventory with layers 0 Observations (points), 1 Ground Ruptures (lines), 2 Landslide Features (polygons: source, transport, deposit), including Helene events; max 1000 records/query; 'updated as landslides occur'. Deprecated 'North Carolina Post Helene Landslide Inventory' FeatureServer stays online until 2027. DEMLR MapServers: North_Carolina_Probability_of_Sliding_Model and North_Carolina_Channelized_Debris_Flow_Model (maps.deq.nc.gov/arcgis/rest/services/DEMLR). Landslide Hazards Data Viewer holds 4,500+ landslides; NCGS responded to 400+ Helene reports; GROW NC notes DEQ is updating susceptibility and debris-flow runout models.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer/MapServer; ArcGIS Online viewer; download | auth: none | licence: DEQ: geospatial data at no charge, no warranty; Hub listing shows 'custom' licence restricting commercial sale | cadence: As landslides occur / mapping progresses | NC: Western NC; statewide inventory under construction
  use (pre,post): Live landslide evidence layer (pulled on each run) and pre-event susceptibility layer for the collection planner and tract risk context.
  caveats: Inventory 'currently incomplete'; deprecated Helene service offline in 2027; no editingInfo lastEditDate exposed.
- (medium/high) **NASA Harmonized Landsat Sentinel-2 (HLS) v2.0 via CMR STAC** (NASA LP DAAC) https://cmr.earthdata.nasa.gov/stac/LPCLOUD/collections
  HLSL30_2.0 and HLSS30_2.0 30 m surface reflectance, ~1.6-day combined revisit (1.4-day with five satellites), 2-3 day latency, global land, COG with XML metadata; STAC search open, assets require Earthdata Login (S3 via credentials endpoint or HTTPS).
  access: STAC API (CMR LPCLOUD) + S3/HTTPS | auth: free Earthdata Login account for assets | licence: NASA open data (not stated on page; US Government) | cadence: Daily tiles; 2-3 day latency | NC: Full NC, continuous since 2013 (Landsat) / 2015 (S2)
  use (pre,post): Recovery time series: monthly NDVI/NDBI change per tract and per structure cluster (vegetation regrowth, roof reflectance return) for weeks-to-years monitoring and re-assessment triggers.
  caveats: 30 m is sub-structure; use at block/tract level. Earthdata is currently 'unconfigured' in NC Recon.
- (high/high) **Sentinel-2 L2A Cloud-Optimized GeoTIFFs on AWS (Earth Search STAC)** (Element 84 (AWS Open Data); ESA Copernicus data) https://earth-search.aws.element84.com/v1
  Global Sentinel-2 L2A COGs (bucket sentinel-cogs, us-west-2; also e84-earth-search-sentinel-data), 5-day revisit, since Dec 2018 globally, new scenes within hours of Copernicus publication; free, no requester-pays.
  access: STAC API + S3 (public) | auth: none | licence: Copernicus free and open data terms | cadence: ~5-day revisit; hours latency | NC: Full NC
  use (pre,post): Primary free 10 m optical time series for recovery monitoring (blue-tarp persistence at cluster scale, vegetation/debris recovery, river channel migration) and as the baseline for coverage-gap analysis.
  caveats: Cloud cover in the mountains; 10 m limits per-structure inference.
- (medium/medium) **NC OneMap orthoimagery services (Orthoimagery_20242027, Orthoimagery_Helene_2025, Orthoimagery_Latest)** (NC CGIA / NC 911 Board (statewide program); NC Collaboratory at UNC Chapel Hill (Helene 2025)) https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/Imagery?f=pjson
  ImageServers: Orthoimagery_20242027 (most recent program imagery per area, 0.5 ft, 3-band; sample tiles dated Feb 2024), Orthoimagery_Helene_2025 (spring 2025, 6-inch, 4-band RGBIR source, leaf-off, 13 counties: Alexander, Ashe, Watauga, Avery, Mitchell, Burke, Yancey, McDowell, Rutherford, Buncombe, Henderson, Haywood, Jackson; ~5,323 sq mi; 6,768 tiles), Orthoimagery_Latest, CIR/NDVI variants, annual services 1995-2023.

  use (pre,post): Already used for before/after; for recovery add Orthoimagery_Latest as the rolling 'most recent' layer and watch the 2024-2027 service catalogue for the next western NC acquisition to schedule the first structure-level re-assessment pass.
  caveats: Flight schedule for western NC in 2026/2027 not found (nconemap.gov pages are JS-only; it.nc.gov page 404).
- (low/high) **Google Earth / Google Maps imagery usage rules (Geo Guidelines)** (Google) https://about.google/brand-resource-center/products-and-services/geo-guidelines/
  Permits research, education, film, nonprofit and limited print/broadcast use with attribution; prohibits bulk download, creating derivative datasets, tracing/digitising, and application-based analysis/extraction; commercial integration only via Google Maps Platform APIs; no allowance for government decision-support redistribution.
  access: Not an API for imagery analysis; Maps Platform APIs are commercial | auth: n/a | licence: Proprietary; Geo Guidelines terms | cadence: n/a | NC: Historical imagery exists for NC but cannot be analysed/extracted under the terms
  use (post): Note only: Google Earth historical imagery is not a permissible evidence channel or data source for NC Recon; analysts may view it manually for context but outputs must not be derived from it.
  caveats: Terms do not address historical imagery specifically; treat as same restrictions.
- (medium/medium) **Blue-tarp detection and roof-recovery literature** (Various (MDPI Remote Sensing; American Economic Journal: Economic Policy; PE&RS; ICPSR)) https://doi.org/10.3390/rs12121924
  Miura et al. 2020, Remote Sensing 12(12):1924: CNN identifies collapsed, non-collapsed and blue-tarp-covered buildings from post-disaster aerial images (Japan). Wolf et al. 2026, AEJ: Economic Policy (doi 10.1257/pol.20240544; SSRN 10.2139/ssrn.5670590): 'remotely detected blue tarps' after Hurricane Irma over 500,000 Florida homes; code-compliant homes 22% lower damage probability, 27% lower severity, unrepaired properties sold 13.3% lower; replication data ICPSR 10.3886/E236942V1. Gonsoroski et al. 2023, PE&RS (10.14358/pers.22-00106r2): roof damage classification from high-resolution imagery for recovery. Xu et al. 2022, Applied Sciences (10.3390/app12104912): typhoon roof-damage levels from aerial photos. No paper explicitly modelling tarp persistence as a recovery indicator over time was found.
  access: Journal pages (some paywalled); ICPSR data (account) | auth: none for abstracts; ICPSR account for data | licence: Varies (MDPI CC BY; AEA/ICPSR terms) | cadence: n/a | NC: None NC-specific; methods transferable to NC OneMap/NOAA 6-inch imagery
  use (post): Justifies the blue-tarp detector as a recovery indicator: schedule repeat tarp detection on each new ortho/Sentinel pass and report 'tarp persisting >N months' per structure; the Wolf et al. design (tarp = damage proxy, later sale discount) supports using tarp persistence to prioritise case-management hand-off.
  caveats: Imagery source and detection method of Wolf et al. not visible in the abstract (MDPI and ICPSR pages returned 403); Semantic Scholar and arXiv rate-limited during research.
- (medium/low) **Crisis Cleanup (volunteer work-order coordination platform)** (Crisis Cleanup (nonprofit); open-source code on GitHub) https://api.crisiscleanup.org/docs/
  Collaborative platform connecting relief-organisation volunteers with survivors' property work orders; 'Modern, versioned API with an interactive playground'; web client crisiscleanup-4-web (Vue 3, Apache-2.0), native iOS/Android apps, infrastructure-as-code (Apache-2.0), analytics orchestrator (MIT); contributions require a CLA.

  use (post): Hand-off export: produce a Crisis Cleanup-compatible address/work-type CSV (muck-out, debris, tarp) for LTRGs that hold accounts; ingestion of Crisis Cleanup statuses only if an organisation shares API credentials under their terms.
  caveats: Site and help centre are JS-rendered or 403; work-order field list, privacy constraints and Helene statistics unconfirmed.

  caveats: nc.211counts.org and 211counts.org returned HTTP 403; data export terms unknown.

  caveats: Contains personal contact details; store only organisational contacts in NC Recon. Case-management systems used by LTRGs not stated.

OSBM: ~5,000 miles state roads impacted, 674 bridges, 712 culverts damaged; GROW NC: NCDOT spent $1.7B, ~$2.9B FHWA Emergency Relief authorised (June 2026).
