### insights
- WebSearch was unavailable for this task (session budget exhausted before start); all findings come from direct WebFetch of agency pages, OpenFEMA metadata/field APIs, ArcGIS REST endpoints, Federal Register API, and local text extraction of two state PDFs (GROW NC Q4 FY2026 quarterly report, 81 pp; OSBM revised Damage and Needs Assessment, 133 pp).
- What NC actually reports in its statutory Helene recovery reporting (GROW NC quarterly report to the General Assembly, https://www.wncrecovery.nc.gov/grow-nc-q4qr-2026/open): per-programme appropriations and expenditures, Renew NC single-family applications/eligible/completed (7,924 / 2,455 / 113 homes as of 2026-08-07), nonprofit repairs (Baptists on Mission 472 homes; Habitat 117 projects), business grants ($55M to 2,182 businesses), HMGP acquisitions submitted/approved (835/446) and elevations (466/0), private road and bridge cost estimate (~$380M), NCDOT spend ($1.7B) and FHWA ER authorisation (~$2.9B). It contains no debris cubic yards, no Blue Roof counts, no FEMA Direct Housing unit counts, no case-management counts and no structure-level damage counts: NC Recon's structure-level status and re-assessment history fills a reporting gap the state does not cover.
- Housing damage baselines are estimates derived from FEMA IA registrations, not observed structure counts: OSBM (https://www.osbm.nc.gov/hurricane-helene-dna/open) gives 73,700 homes expected damaged (p.32) and, elsewhere, 121,000-132,000 homes damaged based on IA claims (p.42), with primary data sources listed as FEMA IA claims through 2024-12-02, DOI/NFIP claim payments and ACS. Total damage and needs $59.6B ($44.4B direct). Transportation: ~5,000 miles of state roads impacted, 674 bridges and 712 culverts damaged, >7,000 private roads/bridges/culverts (pp.63-65).
- FEMA DR-4827 totals as of 2026-07-14 (https://www.fema.gov/disaster/4827): IA approved $567,412,314 across 161,042 approved applications; PA obligated $2,379,760,937 (Cat A-B $1,314,843,115; Cat C-G $877,170,738); HMGP obligated $201,485,386. GROW NC reports an HMGP lock-in of $1.543B (state 25% share ~$513M) and that the first voluntary acquisition was approved by FEMA in January 2026, 16 months after the storm.
- CDBG-DR timeline for recovery-dashboard planning: HUD allocated $1,428,120,000 to the State of NC and $225,010,000 to the City of Asheville on 2025-01-16 (https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/full_text/text/2025/01/16/2025-00943.txt); Renew NC launched 2025-06-16; first HUD drawdown October 2025; 113 homes completed by 2026-08-07 (GROW NC). ReBuild NC for 2016/2018 storms closed 2026-09-30 after 4,242 families (https://www.rebuild.nc.gov/). Recovery tracking must therefore be designed for a 5-10 year horizon.
- HUD Universal Notice (https://www.federalregister.gov/documents/full_text/text/2025/01/08/2024-31621.txt) obliges CDBG-DR grantees to keep a public disaster-recovery website updated at least quarterly with action plan, numbered amendments and performance information (Sections I.C.2.b, I.C.6, II.A.1.c, III.B.8), so commercerecovery.nc.gov and Asheville's CDBG-DR page are the places where structured programme performance will appear; the fetched Renew NC pages reference six programme dashboards but did not expose their URLs.
- OpenFEMA licence constraints that shape NC Recon design (https://www.fema.gov/about/openfema/terms-conditions): required attribution statement; no attempt to re-identify individuals; data may not be used to make determinations affecting individual rights or benefits; FEMA may ask users to cease use. Implication: use IHP applicant-level data only in aggregate (censusGeoid block group, zip) for validation and dashboards, never to label an individual structure.
- OpenFEMA deprecation calendar relevant to the deployment plan: FimaNfipClaims v2 and FimaNfipPolicies v2 frozen 2026-06-01 and removed 2026-10-15 (use NfipClaims v3 and NfipPolicies v3); HazardMitigationAssistanceMitigatedProperties v4 and HazardMitigationAssistanceProjects v4 deprecated with removal 2026-10-15 (use HmaSubapplicationsProjectSiteInventories v1); PublicAssistanceFundedProjectsDetails v1 deprecated 2025-10-06 (use v2). Source: https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v1/OpenFemaDataSets metadata.
- IHP Valid Registrations field dictionary (https://www.fema.gov/api/open/v1/OpenFemaDataSetFields?$filter=openFemaDataSet%20eq%20%27IndividualsAndHouseholdsProgramValidRegistrations%27) confirms censusGeoid (12-character block group), waterLevel (inches), destroyed, habitabilityRepairsRequired, roofDamage, foundationDamage, rpfvl/ppfvl, tsaEligible/tsaCheckedIn, sbaApproved, inspnIssued/inspnReturned: these map directly onto NC Recon's damage level, flood-depth and displacement outputs for block-group-level validation.
- NFIP claims coordinates are rounded to 0.1 degree (~11 km), so claims cannot be joined to structures; census block group is the finest reliable key (https://www.fema.gov/openfema-data-page/nfip-redacted-claims-v3). OSBM notes only 5.2% of households in the declared counties hold flood insurance, so NFIP claims under-represent flood damage in western NC.
- Buncombe County publishes a 10,973-point combined Helene damage-assessment layer (sources: ATC-45 survey, Property Damage Form, NCEM SAR Data Collector, Red Cross survey, NEMAC damage assessment, SARCOP; posting green/yellow/red; substantial-damage yes/no; parcel pinnum; last edited 2024-12-17) and a 296-point HMGP applications layer with an Approved flag and Acquisition_Group (https://services6.arcgis.com/VLA0ImJ33zhtGEaP/arcgis/rest/services?f=pjson). These are the best available structure-level ground truth for validating NC Recon's Helene outputs; no licence text is attached, so terms must be confirmed with the county.
- Parcel reassessment as a recovery indicator: Haywood County postponed its revaluation from 2025 to effective 2027-01-01 because of Helene and collects damage report forms (https://www.haywoodcountync.gov/CivicAlerts.aspx?AID=1078); McDowell's reappraisal takes effect for tax year 2027 (https://mcdowellnc.gov/2027); NC OneMap statewide parcels carry parval/landval/improvval/saledate/structyear for all 100 counties (https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/NC1Map_Parcels/FeatureServer/0?f=pjson), enabling pre/post assessed-value comparisons once 2027 values publish. Buncombe's reappraisal page returned 403.
- Debris: the only confirmed public volumes are in the OSBM DNA (Dec 2024): 10,445,000 cubic yards cleared from 27 counties, 108 active Temporary Debris Storage and Reduction Sites in 20 counties, an estimate of 50 million cubic yards total including 10 million curbside, and 13,513 of an estimated 22,000 cubic yards removed from Lake Lure waterways by USACE. NCEM's SMART programme (https://ncdebriscleanup.com/) covers private-property and waterway debris in 13 counties plus Weaverville and Woodfin but publishes no statistics. All USACE domains returned HTTP 403, so no ADMS or Blue Roof dataset could be confirmed.
- Landslides: USGS reviewed inventory counts 2,217 Helene-triggered landslides (OFR 2025-1028, https://doi.org/10.3133/ofr20251028); the preliminary point inventory is CC0 with shapefile/GeoJSON/CSV and 'none planned' updates (https://www.sciencebase.gov/catalog/item/674634a1d34e6d1dac3abddc). NCGS maintains a live statewide inventory FeatureServer and is updating susceptibility and debris-flow runout models; GROW NC states 31 North Carolinians died in Helene landslides. NCDHHS counts 107 storm-related deaths as of 2025-06-17 (Buncombe 42).
- Imagery for recovery monitoring: NC OneMap 'Orthoimagery_Helene_2025' is a spring-2025, 6-inch, leaf-off, 4-band collection over 13 western counties (~5,323 sq mi) by the NC Collaboratory; the statewide program service 'Orthoimagery_20242027' holds the most recent program imagery per area (sample tiles Feb 2024) and 'Orthoimagery_Latest' is a rolling layer (https://services.nconemap.gov/secure/rest/services/Imagery?f=pjson). Henderson County additionally serves LIDAR_Elevation_2025 hillshade, Orthos2025_NC and Pictometry_Orthos_2026 (https://gisweb.hendersoncountync.gov/arcgis/rest/services?f=pjson). Free satellite time series: Sentinel-2 L2A COGs via Earth Search STAC (no auth) and HLS 30 m via CMR STAC (Earthdata Login for assets). Google Earth historical imagery cannot be used as an evidence channel under Google's Geo Guidelines (no bulk download, derivative datasets or extraction).
- Blue-tarp literature supports tarp persistence as a damage/recovery proxy but no paper models persistence over time explicitly: Miura et al. 2020 (CNN for blue-tarp-covered buildings in aerial imagery, doi 10.3390/rs12121924); Wolf et al. 2026 AEJ: Economic Policy used remotely detected blue tarps after Hurricane Irma over 500,000 Florida homes and found unrepaired properties sold for 13.3% less (doi 10.1257/pol.20240544; data 10.3886/E236942V1); Gonsoroski et al. 2023 PE&RS classified roof damage for recovery (doi 10.14358/pers.22-00106r2).
- Hand-off landscape after the first damage call: NC Disaster Case Management (NCDPS) closed new enrolments 2026-08-31 and sunsets 2027-01-22 with intake via 844-746-2326 (https://www.ncdps.gov/helene/dcm); NC 211 is the statewide referral line with zip/county need data in 211 Counts; NC VOAD lists LTRGs for 15+ western counties (https://www.ncvoad.org/coads-ltrgs/); Crisis Cleanup offers a versioned API for relief organisations (details not retrievable). NC Recon's exports should therefore carry DCM/211 contacts now and route to county LTRGs after January 2027.
- Displacement indicators with confirmed access: Census PEP API (county components of change, key required), IRS SOI county migration (2022-2023 latest, ~2-year lag), USPS FOIA change-of-address CSVs 2018-2023 and the licensed ZIP-level Population Mobility Trends service (monthly), NC DPI monthly ADM by school (SBS DART/Statistical Profile; both blocked automated fetch). Economic indicators: NCDOR monthly gross collections and taxable sales by county (xlsx, ~1-month lag), BLS QCEW county CSV slices (Q2 2026 releases 2026-12-02), NC Commerce D4 LAUS (non-commercial-use licence).
### gaps
- USACE Operation Blue Roof and USACE debris mission data for NC Helene: usace.army.mil, saw.usace.army.mil and blueroof.gov all returned HTTP 403; no installation counts, ADMS ticket data or dashboards could be confirmed from primary sources.
- FEMA Direct Temporary Housing Assistance for DR-4827 (travel trailers/MHUs, direct lease, households housed): FEMA direct-housing pages returned 404, the DR-4827 page has no housing counts, and neither OSBM nor GROW NC reports Direct Housing unit counts; no dataset found.
- NC state temporary housing programmes (state travel trailers, Back@Home Helene outcomes): NCDPS /helene/housing returned 404; OSBM lists Back@Home-Helene $20M and MAST team enrolments (65 households in Buncombe, Dec 2024) but no ongoing data source was found.
- Renew NC programme dashboards: the Commerce Recovery site names six programme dashboards and a plans/policies/reports section, but the dashboard URLs, Action Plan PDF and any QPR downloads were not exposed in fetched HTML.
- NCDOT Helene road/bridge rebuild tracker: six URL variants under ncdot.gov returned 404 and the news pages rendered without listings; only generic quarterly GIS layers and 2024 Helene ArcGIS items were confirmed.
- County building-permit open data: no permit layers found for Buncombe (ArcGIS Online catalogue), Haywood (SmartGov portal only), Watauga or McDowell (viewer-only vendors); Asheville's AccelaPermitsView exists but its layer-field endpoint returned HTTP 500, so fields and update cadence are unconfirmed.
- SBA disaster loan data for Helene: data.sba.gov files end at FY2022 (last modified 2024-05-08); FY2023-FY2025 files and the SBA FOIA frequently-requested-records page (404) could not be confirmed.
- Planet NICFI programme status and coverage: planet.com/nicfi redirected to a generic product page and docs.planet.com NICFI paths returned 404; status, end date and latitude bounds were not verified (program design covers the tropics, which would exclude NC, but this is unverified here).
- Post-Helene lidar for western NC: TNM Access and ScienceBase searches returned no 2025+ lidar products; GROW NC says DPS is incorporating 'new post-Helene LIDAR data' for floodplain remapping and Henderson County serves a 2025 lidar hillshade, but the statewide release location, licence and date are unknown.
- River channel change / geomorphic change studies after Helene: no USGS publications or data releases found (pubs warehouse and ScienceBase searches); only landslide products confirmed.
- Blue-tarp persistence as a time-series recovery indicator: no paper found that explicitly models tarp duration vs recovery; Semantic Scholar and arXiv APIs were rate-limited (429) and MDPI/ICPSR/SSRN pages returned 403, so imagery sources and accuracies of the key papers are partly unconfirmed.
- NC 211 data access: nc.211counts.org and 211counts.org returned HTTP 403; whether call/need counts can be downloaded or accessed via API is unknown.
- Crisis Cleanup API: schema endpoints (/openapi.json, /api/schema/) returned 404 and the help centre 403; authentication, credential eligibility, work-order fields and Helene statistics are unconfirmed.
- Buncombe County reappraisal and Helene damage adjustments: buncombenc.gov reappraisal page returned 403; number of damage-adjusted parcels and 2025 reappraisal handling unknown.
- NC OneMap statewide orthoimagery flight schedule for western NC in 2026-2027: nconemap.gov pages are JS-rendered and it.nc.gov program page returned 404; the Orthoimagery_20242027 service exposes dates but not phase/county plans.
- NC General Assembly reports with structure counts beyond OSBM/GROW NC: no NCGA Fiscal Research or committee report with county structure counts was located; the quarterly GROW NC reports contain programme metrics but no structure-level damage tallies.
- NC DEQ Helene debris management site data and water-system status datasets: deq.nc.gov/helene returned 404; no TDSRS geodata was found (OSBM cites 108 sites but no file).
- Census PEP latest vintage and CRE API endpoint: the PEP developer page shows Vintage 2023 and may be stale; CRE API access was not verified.
- BLS QCEW county release lag: page text implies ~3 months but the stated schedule (Q2 2026 on 2026-12-02) implies ~5 months; exact lag not confirmed.
- Licences missing or unstated on several key sources: Buncombe damage-assessment and HMGP layers (empty copyrightText), NC OneMap parcels and imagery (reuse terms not explicit), NCDOT FGDB downloads, NC 211, NC VOAD, GROW NC/OSBM PDFs; each needs a written confirmation before redistribution in exports.
- FEMA Disaster Case Management programme page and HUD CDBG-DR programme page did not render (404/empty); DCM delivery details were taken from the NCDPS page only.

## Pre-disaster readiness data and functionality: what NC Recon should hold before the storm so it is useful the hour after (hazard/exposure baselines, vulnerability data, pre-event imagery baselines, collection planning, pre-positioning, training, mitigation data, building code context, historical calibration events, forecast-driven triggers)
- (high/high) **FEMA National Risk Index (NRI) v1.20 - census tract and county feature layers** (FEMA (owner FEMA_NationalRiskIndex on ArcGIS Online; distributed via RAPT)) https://services.arcgis.com/XG15cJAlne2vxtgt/arcgis/rest/services/National_Risk_Index_Census_Tracts/FeatureServer
  Tract- and county-level Risk Index, Expected Annual Loss (building/population/agriculture), Social Vulnerability and Community Resilience scores for 18 natural hazards. The standalone hazards.fema.gov/nri site and its static zip downloads now 301-redirect to the FEMA RAPT page; the data lives on FEMA's ArcGIS Online feature services and inside RAPT.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer (query JSON/PBF; supportedExportFormats csv, shapefile, sqlite, geoPackage, filegdb, geojson, kml, excel); companion county service National_Risk_Index_Counties; RAPT web app | auth: none | licence: FEMA Terms & Conditions in item licenseInfo: attribution required and disclaimer 'uses FEMA's National Risk Index dataset ... but is not endorsed by FEMA'; US Government work | cadence: versioned releases (v1.20 per RAPT page); layer dataLastEditDate 2025-12-16; static between versions | NC: 2,660 NC census tracts returned by STATEABBRV='NC' count query; all 100 counties
  use (pre): Pre-load per-tract RISK_SCORE, EAL_VALB/EAL_VALP, SOVI_SCORE and RESL_SCORE as a static baseline layer; use hurricane/riverine/coastal-flood EAL to pre-rank tracts for AOI pre-arming and to complement CDC SVI in the need ranking; BUILDVALUE as sanity check on NSI replacement values.
  caveats: hazards.fema.gov/nri/* (including NRI_Table_CensusTracts.zip) returns 301 to the RAPT page, so any hard-coded NRI download URL in docs is dead; the feature view is maxRecordCount 2000 so paginate by NC tract list.
- (medium/medium) **Hazus 7.2 state inventory databases (general building stock, essential facilities)** (FEMA (Flood Map Service Center distribution)) https://msc.fema.gov/portal/resources/hazus
  Hazus loss-estimation software plus per-state inventory databases (building stock by census block and occupancy, essential facilities, demographics) used for hurricane wind, flood and earthquake loss modelling; Hazus 7.2 released September 2025, Hazus 7.1 hurricane-only databases offered for large study areas.
  access: portal download (per-state database table; reCAPTCHA gate); requires ArcGIS Pro 3.4-3.6 and installs SQL Server Express 2022 | auth: none (reCAPTCHA only) | licence: US Government work, free; no licence text on page | cadence: with Hazus releases (7.0, 7.1, 7.2); Hazus 6.1 downloads ended 2026-03-03 | NC: North Carolina state database listed among all 50 states and territories
  use (pre): Offline pre-season import of NC general building stock counts and hurricane-wind damage functions to (a) cross-check NSI occupancy/structure counts per block and (b) provide wind-fragility priors by building type once a wind channel exists; not a runtime dependency because it needs ArcGIS Pro to open natively.
  caveats: Database format and schema not confirmed from fetched pages; extraction to open formats would need a one-off ArcGIS Pro or SQL Server step.
- (high/high) **FEMA National Flood Hazard Layer (NFHL) web services and state/county downloads** (FEMA) https://hazards.fema.gov/arcgis/rest/services/public/NFHL/MapServer
  Effective flood hazard data from FIRM databases: flood hazard zones, FIRM panels, LOMRs, LOMAs, Base Flood Elevations, cross-sections, levees, political jurisdictions, NFHL availability; plus Preliminary, Pending and Draft NFHL services and state/county downloads from the Map Service Center.
  access: ArcGIS REST MapServer (public/NFHL), OGC WMS (public/NFHLWMS/MapServer/WMSServer), OGC WFS (hazards.fema.gov/arcgis/services/public/NFHL/MapServer/WFSServer); state/county zip downloads via msc.fema.gov Search All Products; NFHL viewer msc.fema.gov/nfhl | auth: none | licence: US Government public domain (not stated on page); preliminary data 'for review and guidance purposes only', not for insurance rating | cadence: 'new data are added continually' | NC: Statewide; NC is also served by NCEM's own FRIS services (see NC FRIS entry) which carry the state's effective and preliminary studies
  use (pre,post): Pre-cache SFHA zones and BFE lines per county so every structure carries FLD_ZONE/BFE before the event; in post, SFHA membership and BFE drive the flood-depth prior and the FEMA IA waterline-based category logic; download the NC state NFHL zip for the offline pack.
- (high/high) **NC Flood Risk Information System (FRIS) flood hazard services (NC Floodplain Mapping Program)** (NCEM Risk Management / NC Floodplain Mapping Program (spartagis.ncem.org)) https://spartagis.ncem.org/arcgis/rest/services/Public/FRIS_FloodZones/MapServer
  North Carolina's own effective and preliminary flood hazard layers (Stream Centerlines, Flood Hazard Lines, Flood Hazard Areas), FIRM panels (FRIS_Panels), hydraulic model footprints (FRIS_HydraModel) and preliminary study status; FRIS web app (fris.nc.gov) adds address-level 'flood hazard, structural and content impacts, potential insurance rates, mitigation opportunities'.
  access: ArcGIS REST MapServers under spartagis.ncem.org/arcgis/rest/services/Public (FRIS_FloodZones, FRIS_FloodZones_Prelim, FRIS_Panels, FRIS_HydraModel, PrelimStatus); NC OneMap items with shapefile/CSV download API (e.g. https://www.nconemap.gov/api/download/v1/items/4a76b66114ae431f95eb1de7d0673ff8/shapefile?layers=1); FRIS web viewer | auth: none | licence: NCEM/Floodplain Mapping Program statement: no warranty, 'Acknowledgement of the North Carolina Floodplain Mapping Program is appreciated', derived data should carry federal-standard metadata | cadence: as studies move to preliminary/effective; NC OneMap 'North Carolina Flood Hazard Area Effective' modified 2026-06-11, Prelim 2026-07-12 | NC: Statewide (NC is a Cooperating Technical State producing its own FIRMs)
  use (pre,post): Prefer NC FRIS effective + preliminary zones over NFHL where they differ (NC preliminary studies appear here first); the FRIS 'structural and content impacts' per address are only exposed in the web app, so NC Recon should instead use the NCEM building footprint layer (next entry) for building-level flood risk fields.
  caveats: No public API found for FRIS building-level risk results; fris.nc.gov is a JS application that returned only a title to the fetch tool.
- (high/high) **NCEM Risk Management Building Footprints (2010) with first-floor elevations and flood attributes** (NCEM Risk Management (NC Floodplain Mapping Program), published on NCEM ArcGIS Online and NC OneMap) https://services1.arcgis.com/YBWrN5qiESVpqi92/ArcGIS/rest/services/NC_Risk_Building_Footprints/FeatureServer/0
  5,193,564 statewide building footprints heads-up digitized from 2009-2012 orthos with FEMA-style risk attributes: OCCUP_TYPE, BUILD_TYPE, FL_SCHEME, FLD_ZONE, STATIC_BFE, WIND_ZONE, YEAR_BUILT, BLDG_VALUE, BLDGREPVAL, CONTREPVAL, HTD_SQ_FT, FFE (first-floor elevation; field-collected inside SFHAs, lidar lowest-adjacent-grade derived elsewhere), LIDAR_LAG, LIDAR_HAG, FOUND_TYPE, BASMENT_TY, NUM_STORY, NUM_UNITS, ISCOASTAL, ISLIMWA. This is the inventory behind FRIS building-level flood risk.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer (public, anonymous query works); NC OneMap item 2d07f32b93184a758be29dc1f41344bd | auth: none | licence: NCEM disclaimer (accuracy caveats, no warranty); accessInformation 'NCEM Risk Management'; no explicit open licence | cadence: static; dataLastEditDate 2021-01-05 | NC: Statewide, circa 2010 structures
  use (pre,post): Core of the standing per-structure baseline: join FFE, LIDAR_HAG, FLD_ZONE, STATIC_BFE, YEAR_BUILT, BLDGREPVAL, CONTREPVAL and NUM_UNITS onto each structure; when a water-surface or gauge-derived depth exists, depth-above-FFE maps directly to FEMA Table 23 waterline criteria (Affected/Minor/Major/Destroyed); replacement values feed loss estimates; NUM_UNITS improves displacement estimates over NSI alone.
  caveats: 2010 vintage: new construction since 2012 absent, demolished/acquired structures persist; redistribution terms unstated so confirm with NCEM before bundling in offline packs.
- (high/high) **North Carolina Composite Building Footprints (2020-2023 orthoimagery)** (NCEM (NC Department of Public Safety - Emergency Management), ArcGIS Online owner DJMadding_NCEM_GIS) https://services1.arcgis.com/YBWrN5qiESVpqi92/arcgis/rest/services/North_Carolina_Composite_Building_Footprints/FeatureServer/0
  5,435,529 roofline polygons for primary structures >800 sq ft compiled from the four regional 2020-2023 Statewide Orthoimagery projects; estimated 85-90% capture; nine counties (Cherokee, Swain, Macon, Cleveland, Lincoln, Gaston, Cabarrus, Union, Montgomery) still based on circa-2010 imagery. Fields: BLDG_ID only.
  access: ArcGIS FeatureServer (public) plus Vector Tile Service https://tiles.arcgis.com/tiles/YBWrN5qiESVpqi92/arcgis/rest/services/North_Carolina_Composite_Building_Footprints_Vector/VectorTileServer | auth: none | licence: unstated (copyright text 'NC Department of Public Safety - Emergency Management'; item licenseInfo empty) | cadence: dataLastEditDate 2026-09-23; refreshed as regional ortho-derived footprints are delivered | NC: Statewide; 91 counties on 2020-2023 imagery, 9 counties circa 2010
  use (pre,post): Use as the NC-native footprint layer in the structure-finding step because it is drawn on the same NC OneMap 2020-2023 orthos NC Recon uses as 'before', giving better roof alignment than USA Structures/OSM; adopt BLDG_ID as the persistent per-structure key for the standing baseline, and union with NSI/USA Structures for buildings under 800 sq ft (manufactured homes, outbuildings).
  caveats: No attributes beyond BLDG_ID; excludes structures under 800 sq ft (many manufactured homes); licence unstated.

  access: ArcGIS ImageServer/FeatureServer (public) | auth: none observed | licence: NC OneMap Terms for the flood-extent rasters (academic attribution requested); NCEM layers unstated | cadence: static | NC: Matthew/Florence: Piedmont and Coastal Plain; Helene: western NC
  use (pre): Calibration and regression suite: Matthew/Florence extents against NOAA ERI and 2016/2018 baselines for the pixel-change and flood channels; Helene inundation and peak stage against the Helene orthos and review-queue labels; isolated-community polygons as ground truth for the planned road-isolation module.
  caveats: No Tropical Storm Fred (2021) imagery or damage layer was found in NOAA ERI, NC OneMap or NCEM AGOL.
- (medium/medium) **NCDOT Structures (bridges, culverts, railroad bridges, tunnels, pedestrian structures)** (North Carolina Department of Transportation) https://gis11.services.ncdot.gov/arcgis/rest/services/NCDOT_Structures/MapServer
  Statewide inventory of transportation structures (Bridge Structures, Railroad Bridge Structures, Tunnel Structures, Pedestrian Structures, Other Structures) published 'to support emergency response and recovery' per the NC OneMap catalog entry (modified 2026-08-25).
  access: ArcGIS MapServer | auth: none | licence: NCDOT statement in catalog ('provided to support emergency response and recovery efforts'); full terms not fetched | cadence: catalog modified 2026-08-25 | NC: Statewide
  use (pre,post): Pre-season baseline for the planned road-isolation module: bridge locations crossed with flood extents and DriveNC closures identify cut-off communities.
  caveats: Service metadata not fetched directly; details come from the NC OneMap DCAT feed.
### insights
- WebSearch was unavailable for this entire session (budget exhausted before the first query), so every finding comes from direct fetches of agency pages, ArcGIS REST/STAC endpoints and catalog feeds; URLs that could not be guessed are listed under gaps.
- FEMA's standalone National Risk Index site is gone: every hazards.fema.gov/nri path, including the static NRI_Table_CensusTracts.zip and NRI_Table_Counties.zip downloads, 301-redirects to https://www.fema.gov/emergency-managers/practitioners/resilience-analysis-and-planning-tool, which says 'The National Risk Index v1.20 data update is now available on RAPT'. The data remains queryable on FEMA's ArcGIS Online feature services (owner FEMA_NationalRiskIndex, tracts layer last edited 2025-12-16, 2,660 NC tracts, 469 fields).
- DHS shut down the HIFLD Open portal (https://hifld-geoplatform.opendata.arcgis.com/ now returns an ArcGIS Hub 'site: null, status 404' payload). Public Environmental Data Partners with Fulton Ring, Data Rescue Project and Niyam IT restored a Data Rescue Project snapshot as 'HIFLD Next' at https://hifld.publicenvirodata.org (526 STAC collections, GeoParquet/PMTiles/GeoPackage/GeoJSON/Shapefile/FGDB, cursor-paginated 50 per page, no feature query API). Mobile Home Parks, National Shelter System Facilities, Cellular Towers, Antenna Structure Registration, Nursing Homes, Dialysis Centers, Hospitals, Public/Private Schools, Child Care Centers, Fire and EMS Stations, Power Plants, Wastewater Treatment Plants and National Inventory of Dams are present; Electric Substations and Transmission Lines are not. Source: https://screening-tools.com/blog/hifld-next-restoring-americas-infrastructure-datasets
- CEJST's federal domain (screeningtool.geoplatform.gov) no longer resolves; PEDP says public access ended 2025-01-22, the usds/justice40-tool repo was archived 2025-03-27, and PEDP's mirror serves CEJST v2.0 (released 2024-12-20) tract data as CSV 42 MB, XLSX 35.6 MB and shapefile 356.8 MB (https://public-environmental-data-partners.github.io/j40-cejst-2/en/downloads).
- NC OneMap Terms (read from the Hub page item e60514c3b78542ef9902e3d7f7a671c1): CGIA offers access 'at no charge', partners accept a 'free and unrestricted use policy', 'Written release agreements ... are not required and will not be issued', no warranty, 'Any sale of this data must not violate applicable state laws', citation 'NC OneMap (2019). North Carolina Department of Information Technology, Government Data Analytics Center, Center for Geographic Information and Analysis. Available at www.nconemap.gov.' This covers parcels, address points, orthoimagery, elevation and the CGIA facility layers.
- NC statewide orthoimagery rotation confirmed from the NC_Orthoimagery_Regions layer: 2020 Coastal Plain (CO20), 2021 Eastern Piedmont (EP21), 2022 Northern Piedmont and Mountains (NPM22), 2023 Southern Piedmont and Mountains (SPM23). Phase 4 project areas are 2024, 2025 and 2026 polygons whose extents match Coastal Plain, Eastern Piedmont and Northern Piedmont and Mountains respectively, so 2027 should be Southern Piedmont and Mountains (inferred). Products are 6-inch, 3-band JPEG for display (RMSE 1.0 ft) with separate 4-band 'analysis', CIR and NDVI services for pixel work; copyright 'NC Center for Geographic Information and Analysis, NC 911 Board'.
- NC Recon's structure layer can be upgraded with two NCEM products that are already public on ArcGIS Online: North_Carolina_Composite_Building_Footprints (5,435,529 rooflines >800 sq ft from the 2020-2023 orthos, last edited 2026-09-23, nine western/piedmont counties still circa-2010, BLDG_ID only) and NC_Risk_Building_Footprints (5,193,564 footprints digitized 2009-2012 carrying FFE, LIDAR_LAG/HAG, FLD_ZONE, STATIC_BFE, WIND_ZONE, YEAR_BUILT, BLDG_VALUE, BLDGREPVAL, CONTREPVAL, HTD_SQ_FT, FOUND_TYPE, NUM_STORY, NUM_UNITS, ISCOASTAL). First-floor elevations were field-collected inside SFHAs and lidar-derived elsewhere; this is the inventory behind FRIS building-level flood risk, for which no public API exists.
- NOAA ERI timing from the noaa-eri-pds bucket: Florence first sortie 2018-09-15 (landfall 2018-09-14) with 15 sorties through 2018-09-22 at ~25 cm; Helene first sortie 2024-09-27 (Florida landfall late 2024-09-26) with 21 sorties through 2024-10-08 at 15-30 cm; Matthew obliques 2016-10-07 to 10-11 then RGB tiles 2016-10-11 to 10-16. NOAA also flies Pre_Event collections (folders 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025, 2026_Pre_Event). The bucket is anonymous (aws s3 ls --no-sign-request s3://noaa-eri-pds/), GeoTIFF plus COG for recent events, attribution requested. No 2021 Fred folder exists.
- FEMA 2025 PDA Guide (effective 2025-07-01) explicitly allows SLTT governments to use AI/machine learning for the Initial Damage Assessment, accepts SLTT drone imagery (FEMA itself may not fly drones), expects IDA data as Excel or geodatabase, specifies virtual-sensing imagery of about 0.41 m satellite or 10-inch-or-better nadir airborne with 6-inch obliques preferred, and runs a Response Geospatial Office Geospatial Damage Assessment (GDA) tool whose results must be matched with occupancy data to separate dwellings from outbuildings. Table 23 gives waterline-based criteria for Affected/Minor/Major/Destroyed for conventional and manufactured homes.
- Maxar's open data moved under the Vantor name: https://www.maxar.com/open-data redirects to https://vantor.com/company/open-data-program; the live STAC root https://vantor-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/events/catalog.json lists only 10 events (2026) while the legacy https://maxar-opendata.s3.amazonaws.com/events/catalog.json still lists 55 events including HurricaneHelene-Oct24 (42 acquisition collections, pre-event imagery back to 2019-11-09, last 2024-10-06), Milton, Ian and Idalia; Florence and Matthew are no longer present. Licence CC BY-NC 4.0; activation requires a Vantor FirstLook event; tasking lead times are not published.
- NAIP coverage for NC in the Planetary Computer STAC: 2018, 2020 and 2022 at 0.6 m GSD; no NC items for 2019, 2021 or 2023, and both the AWS registry and Planetary Computer catalogs end at 2023. AWS NAIP buckets are Requester Pays.
- Sentinel-2 acquisition plans are published as KML per satellite (S2A/S2B/S2C), each covering 10-15 days, new files roughly weekly, currently through October 2026 (pattern s2a_mp_acq__kml_20261001t150000_20261019t180000), with an archive back to November 2015; combined with the USGS Landsat acquisition tool (16-day cycles, 8-day offset, CSV export) this is enough to build a satellite-pass predictor without any commercial API.
- HLS S30 v2.0 is CC0-1.0, 30 m, global every ~1.6 days with Landsat, COG, 22 million granules, but requires an Earthdata Login for download; latency is not stated on the catalog page.
- emPOWER's public FeatureServer (https://services2.arcgis.com/ZQ4jTQn6k7VPXEwO/arcgis/rest/services/HHS_emPOWER_REST_Service_Public/FeatureServer) exposes 18 layers: electricity-dependent DME counts and ESRD dialysis, oxygen, home health, hospice and any-service combinations at ZIP, county and state level, monthly, de-identified; the ZIP-level Emergency Planning dataset is restricted to public health authorities, so NC DHHS would be the conduit.
- CMS nursing-home provider records (dataset 4pq5-n9py) carry latitude and longitude fields; dialysis facility records (23ew-n7w9) carry address only; both are queryable without a key through https://data.cms.gov/provider-data/api/1/datastore/query/{id}/0 with state=NC conditions (CSV refreshes dated 2026-08-01 and 2026-06-16).
- NC DHSR publishes 17 licensed-facility XLSX listings at https://info.ncdhhs.gov/dhsr/reports.htm including Adult Care Homes and Family Care Homes (07/30/2026), Nursing Homes (09/09/2026), Hospitals (10/01/2026), Mental Health facilities (10/06/2026), Home Care and Hospice (08/20/2026); adult care and family care homes are not in CMS and are the main NC-only addition.
- NC Office of State Fire Marshal publishes 2,126 active fire stations with coordinates (NC_Fire_Stations FeatureServer, last edited 2026-07-06), and CGIA publishes 2,447 Potential Emergency Shelters with capacity and contact fields (dataset modified 2023-03-22). NCEM's ArcGIS Online org (services1.arcgis.com/YBWrN5qiESVpqi92) also exposes Shelters_WebEOC (978), NC_Evacuation_Zones (66), Historic_EM_Mitigation_Properties (4,608), NC_Hazard_Mitigation_Parcels_Final_August25 (4,318), HeleneEstimatedFloodInundation_Jan2025 (2,082, with ICEYE), Peak_Stage_Helene (222) and Helene_NC2024_Isolated_Communities (109) to anonymous queries, but none carries licence text.
- NC NG911 GIS: counties and PSAPs submit road centerlines, address points, PSAP boundaries, emergency service boundaries and provisioning boundaries through GeoComm's GIS Data Hub in the NENA GIS Data Model (CLDXF), processed within 1-2 hours of upload, with monthly ALI validation recommended; public access is through the AddressNC derivative services and status dashboards on NC OneMap, not the raw NG911 database (https://it.nc.gov/about/boards-councils/nc-911-board/next-generation-911-gis-services).
- OpenFEMA HMA Projects v4 is deprecated as of 2026-08-31 and will be discontinued 2026-10-15; the replacement is HMA Subapplications v2 (daily, 63,593 records, county/zip granularity, no coordinates). OpenFEMA requires no key but demands citation of endpoint, version and access date plus a 'not endorsed by FEMA' disclaimer.
- NC building code status: the 2018 NC State Building Codes (based on the 2015 I-codes) have been effective since 2019-01-01; the 2024 NC codes were approved but, per a 2025-03-31 OSFM notice, their effective date was still pending. No statewide permit or inspection dataset is published by OSFM. FEMA BCAT tracks adoption at jurisdiction level and refreshes quarterly.
- Hazard mitigation planning in NC runs through 30 regional multi-jurisdictional plans (Eastern 9, Central 10, Western 11) plus two UNC System plans, each a PDF with expiration date on ncdps.gov, updated on the five-year cycle at no cost to local governments.
- xBD (arXiv:1911.09296) contains 850,736 building annotations over 45,362 km2 at <0.8 m GSD for 19 events, including Hurricane Florence (Sep 10-19, 2018), Hurricane Matthew (2016), Michael and Harvey, sourced from the Maxar Open Data Program; the Florence subset is the only NC-area labelled damage set found.
- Pre-positioning data on laptops is feasible entirely from open endpoints: NC OneMap download API (https://www.nconemap.gov/api/download/v1/items/{itemId}/shapefile?layers=N) and county parcel zips tracked by NC1Map_Parcels_Transform_Dates; NFHL state/county zips from the Map Service Center; NRI exports in GeoPackage/FileGDB from the FeatureServer; HIFLD Next PMTiles and GeoParquet; CEJST and SVI CSVs; Meta 30 m GeoTIFF tiles. The per-structure baseline (footprint, FFE, flood zone, parcel attributes, imagery year, baseline chip) is the natural unit for such packs.
- NCEM training registers through NC TERMS at https://terms.ncem.gov/TRS/ (terms.ncem.org does not resolve); the NCEM training page lists damage assessment among its subjects and states no direct instructional cost for eligible participants. FEMA IS-559 Local Damage Assessment (2 h) and IS-922.A Applications of GIS for Emergency Management (3 h) are free online with a FEMA SID.
- Copernicus Data Space terms confirm Sentinel data is 'available on a free, full and open basis'; the Earth Search STAC 'proprietary' licence tag is a metadata placeholder, not a restriction.
### gaps
- WebSearch quota was exhausted before this task began, so discovery relied on known or guessed URLs; several agency pages (FEMA Geospatial Resource Center 503, disasters.geoplatform.gov 500, FCC ASR pages 403/404, NC DHHS data portal 403, LandScan 403/AccessDenied, FIMAN 403, HDX dataset page 403) could not be read.
- ORNL LandScan USA/Global: could not confirm resolution, day/night layers, licence, account requirement or latest version; all LandScan pages were blocked.
- No open, current source for electric substations or transmission lines was found: HIFLD Next has no substation/transmission collection, and the EIA Energy Atlas DCAT feed and its ArcGIS org listing did not expose power plant or substation layers in this session.
- FCC Antenna Structure Registration direct download pages were blocked; only the HIFLD Next ASR and Cellular Towers snapshots (unknown vintage) were confirmed.
- Civil Air Patrol imagery hosting portal and access terms could not be confirmed (FEMA GeoPlatform endpoints returned 500/503); only NOAA's credit line and the PDA Guide description were fetched.
- Maxar/Vantor tasking lead times and FirstLook activation thresholds are not published on the open-data page.
- International Charter Space and Major Disasters: the US authorized user and whether Helene 2024 was an activation were not confirmed from the homepage.
- NC FRIS building-level flood risk ('structural and content impacts') is only in the fris.nc.gov web app; no REST or download endpoint for those results was found.
- FIMAN: no public API documentation found; the spartagis gauge service is labelled 'Test Gages NC FIMAN'; inundation library access would need an NCEM arrangement.
- NC DHSR XLSX facility lists: whether they include street addresses or coordinates was not confirmed (page lists names, dates, sizes only); the DHSR data directory returns 403.
- Tropical Storm Fred (2021): no post-event imagery or damage dataset found in NOAA ERI, NC OneMap, NCEM ArcGIS Online or Maxar catalogs.
- NAIP 2024 for NC: neither the AWS registry nor Planetary Computer catalogs extend past 2023, so the latest NC NAIP year could not be confirmed.
- NC lidar: QL2 collection years by phase and the distribution path for the post-Helene 'Priority 1 Lidar' were not confirmed; only the DEM03 services and a 2014-2015 eastern NC canopy product were seen.
- NC OneMap 2027 orthoimagery region is inferred from Phase 4 extents, not stated in metadata; the Phase 4 layer contains only 2024, 2025 and 2026 polygons.
- HIFLD Next collections have null temporal extents and licence 'other', so the vintage and reuse terms of each rescued layer (e.g., Mobile Home Parks, National Shelter System) remain unverified.
- NC 2024 State Building Code effective date after the 2025-03-31 OSFM notice is unknown; no statewide permit/inspection dataset exists.
- FEMA BCAT portal URL and whether BCAT data is downloadable or served as an ArcGIS layer were not found (bcat-portal path 404).
- USFA National Fire Department Registry page returned no content; EPA SDWIS facility coordinate availability via Envirofacts was not confirmed.
- NC OneMap health, EMS, EOC, law-enforcement and potential-shelter layers show item modified dates of 2023-03-22 but no source vintage.
- NCEM ArcGIS Online layers (shelters, evacuation zones, Helene inundation, mitigation properties) are anonymously queryable but carry no licence or intended-use statement; an MOU or written confirmation from NCEM GIS is needed before NC Recon depends on or redistributes them.
- FEMA Response Geospatial Office GDA tool access for SLTT partners and its data schema were not retrievable (503).
- NCEM exercise programme details (statewide hurricane exercise schedule, damage assessment drills) were not found; the training page lists subject areas only.
- OpenFEMA rate limits and the Hazus state database file format/schema are unstated on the fetched pages.
- xBD licence (reported elsewhere as CC BY-NC-SA 4.0) was not confirmed from the fetched paper text; xview2.org is JS-rendered.
- American Red Cross open-shelter map host (maps.redcross.org) does not resolve; no open Red Cross shelter feed was confirmed.
- No NC-specific mobile home park inventory was found; only the HIFLD Next national snapshot and the ACS B25024 mobile-home unit counts (block group) are available.
- HIFLD's own ArcGIS Online org (services1.arcgis.com/Hp6G80Pky0om7QvQ) still lists 527 services but with hashed names and empty metadata on the former Hospitals_1 layer, so it cannot be relied on.
